MotoRacer PC Reverse Engineering                           Analysis Note


                            THE RACER ARRAY


Abstract

   Twenty-five racers, 12809 bytes each, in one static array. This document
   records what is known about that array and how a race's roster is
   built, which is as far as the current work has gone. The record
   itself is not yet reconstructed.

   +-------------------------+------------+
   | Item                    | Address    |
   +=========================+============+
   | g_racers                | 0x00581218 |
   +-------------------------+------------+
   | g_racerCount            | 0x00509bac |
   +-------------------------+------------+
   | g_localRacerIndex       | 0x004ea4c0 |
   +-------------------------+------------+
   | g_viewedRacerIndex      | 0x00508834 |
   +-------------------------+------------+
   | g_playerName            | 0x005161b0 |
   +-------------------------+------------+
   | maybe_Game_CreateRacers | 0x00410a50 |
   +-------------------------+------------+
   | maybe_Racer_Create      | 0x0045bf70 |
   +-------------------------+------------+

The port convergence log is a separate document

   THE A/B RUNS, THE TRANSCRIPTION RECORDS AND THE DEFECT HUNTS ARE IN
   subsystems/racers-convergence.txt. This file is what the BINARY does;
   that one is how the reconstruction was brought onto it.

   Section numbers were kept across the split, so the numbering here has
   gaps and they are not omissions - each gap is a run that moved:

   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | Sections            | Subject                                   |
   +=====================+===========================================+
   | 9.17                | LIVE VERIFICATION - the model measured    |

   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.18.24 - 9.18.41.2 | the six drive states, and the defect hunt |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.20                | TRANSCRIBED INTO src/ - physics.c         |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.25                | THE SCAFFOLDING TRANSCRIBED               |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.27                | DRIVE AND STEERING TRANSCRIBED            |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.32                | THE BIKE COULD NOT PULL AWAY              |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.46.9 - 9.46.24.4  | the jump, shift, donut and drift campaign |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.59.1 - 9.59.3     | the automatic shift, measured             |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | 9.59.5 - 9.59.6     | what the port implements, and placement   |

   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+

   A BARE SECTION NUMBER THAT IS NOT IN THIS FILE IS IN THE OTHER ONE.
   The numbers partition the pair - none appears in both - so any 9.x
   reference resolves in exactly one of the two documents.

   10. Open questions


1.  Twenty-five slots of 0x3209

   The stride is 0x3209, 12809 bytes, and it turns up in almost every
   function this project has read that touches a racer. The slot count
   is 25, confirmed three independent ways [verified]:

   +---------------------------------------------------+---------------+
   | Evidence                                          | Gives         |
   +===================================================+===============+
   | roster loop 0x00583fec to 0x005d22cc, step 0x3209 | 25            |
   +---------------------------------------------------+---------------+
   | collision bitmap on the stack, int[625]           | 25 x 25       |
   +---------------------------------------------------+---------------+
   | loop bounds at 0x005cf4f9 and 0x005cf561          | the array end |
   +---------------------------------------------------+---------------+

   Twenty-five slots at 12809 bytes is 313 KB of static data, which for
   1997 is a substantial fraction of the working set. It is also
   consistent with the maximum opponent count of 23 that
   maybe_Game_LoadRace sets - 23 opponents plus the player plus one
   spare slot.

   The base address of 0x00581218 is DEDUCED, not read from an
   allocation: the array is static, so there is no operator new to bound
   it. It comes from taking the lowest per-racer address seen and
   checking that every other one lands at a consistent offset under a
   0x3209 stride [inferred]. Treat the absolute offsets in section 3 as
   provisional until a function is found that indexes the array from its
   true base.

2.  Building the roster

   maybe_Game_CreateRacers at 0x00410a50 is step 5 of
   maybe_Game_LoadRace. It returns 0 if any racer cannot be created,
   which propagates all the way out to MenuPage01_Loading_Tick
   [verified].

       1  for all 25 slots: copy the default name from DAT_0053b8c4,
          uppercase it with __strupr, and clear several fields
       2  maybe_Game_LoadBikeMeshes
       3  network off: count the active entries in the table at
          0x00508054 into g_racerCount, then create one racer each
       4  otherwise: create the local player, then g_opponentCount
          opponents
       5  find the local racer, copy g_playerName into its name, and
          point g_viewedRacerIndex at it

   Step 1 is worth noting for what it implies about the data: every slot
   gets a default name before any racer is created, so an unused slot is
   not blank but carries a placeholder. Anyone reading this array at
   runtime should test the active flag rather than the name.

   Step 4 has a special case. When g_opponentCount is 0 and g_dwReplayMode
   is 2 it forces two racers. g_dwReplayMode is the mode selector that
   maybe_Game_ReadInput uses to choose between live input and replay, so
   mode 2 is neither - most likely the attract demo, which would need
   racers without a player [inferred].

   g_playerName is CONFIG.BPT record 0, DefName. Settings_LoadAll and
   Settings_SaveAll are its other users, which is what identifies it -
   see subsystems/config-file.txt [verified].

3.  Fields known so far

   Accumulated from every function read so far rather than from any
   single pass over the record:

   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | Offset  | Holds                                       | Confidence |
   +=========+=============================================+============+
   | +0x0004 | NOT AN INDEX - Racer_Create's third arg,    | verified   |
   |         | 1 for every racer in a solo game and 0 for  |            |
   |         | every racer in a network one. A mode flag.  |            |
   |         | Read live, racers 1, 2 and 3 all hold 1     |            |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0008 | HUMAN-PLAYER FLAG - Racer_Create's second   | verified   |
   |         | arg. 1 for the local player in solo and for |            |
   |         | each occupied slot in a network game; 0 for |            |
   |         | the AI. It gates the speedometer needle     |            |
   |         | smoothing - see MR_Racer.txt 23.1           |            |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0018 | FLT_MAX                                     | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x001c | FLT_MAX                                     | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0068 | active flag                                 | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x006c | 1                                           | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0078 | bike id; indexes g_bikeCarac                | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x00b4 | performance, from carac[16] plus a handicap | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x00bc | 12 dwords, position and orientation         | inferred   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0108 | a pointer to the racer itself               | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0170 | livery id, param & 7, or -1                 | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x02f4 | the same value, or -1                       | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0478 | the same value, or -1                       | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x05fc | the same value                              | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0638 | skeleton slot, 0 or 1                       | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0684 | per-skeleton block, stride 2000             | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x069c | 20 slots x 100 bytes, skeleton 0            | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x0e6c | 20 slots x 100 bytes, skeleton 1            | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x2d4c | per-skeleton counts                         | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x2d74 | an owned pointer                            | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x2d78 | an owned pointer                            | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x2dac | flags; collision ORs 4, 8, 0x10, 0x20       | inferred   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x2dd4 | name[8]                                     | inferred   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x2e21 | zeroed                                      | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x31bd | zeroed                                      | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x31c1 | zeroed                                      | verified   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x31e9 | input buttons                               | inferred   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x31ed | a second button word                        | inferred   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x31f1 | an axis, centred at 0x8000                  | inferred   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+
   | +0x31f5 | an axis, centred at 0x8000                  | inferred   |
   +---------+---------------------------------------------+------------+

   The input group at +0x31e9 is the best understood, because it is
   written from two directions: Input_OnKeyDown ORs a driving action's
   bit into +0x31e9, and maybe_Game_ReadInput resets all four each step,
   centring the last two at 0x8000. See subsystems/game-object.txt
   section 9.

4.  What Racer_Create sets up

   Racer_Create at 0x0045bf70 is __thiscall, so its first argument IS
   the racer record. That matters more than the fields it writes: every
   offset it touches is a real field offset rather than one deduced from
   a stride.

   It also settles the array base that section 1 could only infer.
   maybe_Game_CreateRacers writes racer+0x68 through &DAT_00581218 + i *
   0x3209; Racer_Create writes +0x68 through its own this pointer. Two
   functions agreeing on the same field at the same offset from opposite
   directions confirms g_racers at 0x00581218 [verified].

   TWO SKELETONS PER RACER. It sets the slot at +0x638 to 1, loads the
   hand skeleton from g_pathSklHand, then sets the slot to 0 and loads
   the body skeleton from g_pathSkl - both manifest keys from
   subsystems/bike-assets.txt. Each occupies a 2000-byte block at racer
   + slot * 2000 + 0x684, and after loading, every vertex is re-centred
   on the block's first vec3 [verified].

   Each skeleton also owns 20 slots of 100 bytes - at +0x69c and +0xe6c
   - and Racer_Create frees and zeroes both sets before loading. Those
   are almost certainly animation channels [inferred].

   It attaches the wheels by calling FUN_0048bce0 with g_meshRoueArriere
   and g_meshRoueAvant. That answers an open question from
   subsystems/bike-assets.txt about why each wheel is loaded twice: the
   two copies are attachment sources, one per wheel position [inferred].

   THE PLAYER'S racer+0xb4 IS SET HERE, and it is RealSpeedMax:

       0045c572  MOV ECX,[EAX*4 + 0x5092c0]   0x509278 + 18 dwords
       0045c579  MOV [ESI+0xb4],ECX

   Measured live, racer 1 holds 80.0 on the road bike and 100.0 on
   the pocket bike, each equal to its own carac's RealSpeedMax.  So
   the port substituting c->realSpeedMax for r->realSpeedMax is
   EXACTLY right for the player rather than a stand-in [verified].

   A BONUS FOLLOWS, AND IT GOES TO THE PLAYER, NOT THE OPPONENTS.
   EDI is zeroed at 0x0045c538 and the three tests at 0x0045c57f
   .. 0x0045c592 read against it, so 2.0 (0x004d0e88) is added when

       humanPlayer != 0  AND  soloMode != 0  AND  0x004dc0a0 == 0

   0x004dc0a0 reads 1 in our build, so it never fires [verified].

5.  The bike characteristic table

   Racer_Create indexes a table at 0x00509278 by the racer's bike id,
   with a stride of 0x124 - 292 bytes, or 73 dwords [verified].

   73 dwords is exactly the CARAC record that formats/bpt-carac.txt
   describes from the file side: 73 values, index 0 an integer and the
   rest floats. Each bike manifest names eight carac files under the
   MotoCarac key, and the id Racer_Create stores is masked to 0..7. So
   this is where carac1.spt through carac8.spt end up in memory.

   That note had the file format fully worked out but no in-memory
   address. This is the join between them.

   Fields observed in use, as dword indices into the record:

   +------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   | Index      | Used by                   | As                                  |
   +============+===========================+=====================================+
   | 12, 13, 14 | maybe_Game_CollideRacers  | floats                              |
   +------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   | 18         | Racer_Create              | RealSpeedMax, copied to racer+0xb4  |
   +------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   | 70         | maybe_Game_LoadBikeMeshes | a boolean face-kind switch          |
   +------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------------+

   CORRECTED. The base was first put at 0x00509280 by scanning for the
   lowest referenced address, and the scan started two fields too high.
   Carac_Load settles it: the first field it writes is at 0x00509278
   [verified].

   Every one of the 73 fields is now named, because Carac_Load reads
   each by its setting key. The full map is in formats/bpt-carac.txt,
   and the record is typed in the database as MR_BikeCarac.

6.  Three embedded model instances

   Racer_Create calls maybe_ModelInstance_Init three times, and the this
   pointer each time is racer+0x24, racer+0x1a8 and racer+0x32c. Those
   are 0x184 apart, so the racer EMBEDS three objects of 0x184 = 388
   bytes [verified].

   The size is confirmed from outside the racer as well.
   MenuPage14_Title_Enter initialises three standalone ones at 0x540500,
   0x540684 and 0x540808 - the same spacing - and
   MenuPage25_DsiLogo_Enter walks an array with EBX += 0x184. The
   initialiser's highest write is index 0x60, which is +0x180, the last
   dword of such a record.

   +-----------------+-------------------+
   | Offset in racer | Filled from       |
   +=================+===================+
   | +0x0024         | DAT_0053a6a8      |
   +-----------------+-------------------+
   | +0x01a8         | g_meshRoueAvant   |
   +-----------------+-------------------+
   | +0x032c         | g_meshRoueArriere |
   +-----------------+-------------------+

   Body plus two wheels. That is the same trio subsystems/bike-
   assets.txt saw from the loading side, and it confirms what those two
   loaded wheel copies are for: each racer binds one of them into an
   instance slot.

   The menu pages use the same object standalone, for the title-screen
   and logo models, so it is the engine's general renderable-instance
   type rather than anything racer-specific [inferred].

   CAUTION. The argument roles are not resolved. The function is
   __thiscall with RET 0xc, so three stack arguments, but the call sites
   push a mix of stack vec3s and - in Racer_Create - the address of an
   MR_Mesh, and the third stack argument was not located. The size and
   the embedding are solid; the parameter list is not, and nothing
   should be built on it yet.

6.1.  The five-mesh run at 0x0053a6a8

   The mesh bound into the racer's first instance slot is element 0 of a
   contiguous run of five MR_MeshBase records, 0x70 apart [verified]:

   +-----+------------+-------------------+--------------------------------+
   | Idx | Address    | Name              | Loaded from                    |
   +=====+============+===================+================================+
   | 0   | 0x0053a6a8 | g_meshSkl         | FileSkl, the body skeleton     |
   +-----+------------+-------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 1   | 0x0053a718 | g_meshSklHand     | FileSklHand, the hand skeleton |
   +-----+------------+-------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 2   | 0x0053a788 | g_meshMotoMed     | FileMotoMed                    |
   +-----+------------+-------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 3   | 0x0053a7f8 | g_meshMotoLow     | FileMotoLow                    |
   +-----+------------+-------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 4   | 0x0053a868 | g_meshMotoVeryLow | FileMotoVeryLow                |
   +-----+------------+-------------------+--------------------------------+

   What identifies element 0 is how Racer_Create reads it. It takes the
   vertex pointer from &DAT_0053a6ac + slot * 0x70 and the vertex count
   from &DAT_0053a6d8 + slot * 0x70. Those are +0x04 and +0x30 of a mesh
   - vertices and vertexCount - so the base is a mesh and the array
   steps by 0x70.

   +0x10 is vertexNormals, and it is NULL on all five of these.
   maybe_Mesh_ShadeVertices (0x00422540) walks it one vec3 per
   vertex and returns at once when it is null; its only writer,
   Mesh_ComputeVertexNormals, is called on the four WHEEL meshes
   and nothing else.  So the far LOD's per-vertex lighting does
   nothing in MOTO either.  See structs/MR_Mesh.txt 5.

   Element 1 has NO direct reference anywhere in the image. That is not
   a sign it is unused: it is only ever reached as base + slot * 0x70,
   with the slot at racer+0x638 that Racer_Create sets to 1 before
   loading g_pathSklHand and to 0 before loading g_pathSkl. A raw-
   address scan alone would have called it dead.

   The run ends at element 4. The next 0x70 boundary, 0x0053a8d8, is
   g_resourceDescriptors, an unrelated table used by Lang_SetLanguage.

   So the racer's three model instances are the rider's body skeleton
   and the two wheels - not the bike body, which is instead one of the
   three LODs at elements 2 to 4 and must be bound somewhere else
   [inferred].

7.  Level of detail

   The bike body LOD is bound at DRAW time, not load time, by
   maybe_Racer_SelectLod at 0x0045f160 [verified]:

       dist = |racer.pos - camera[racer+0x31dd].pos|
              / FUN_0049a150()
       racer+0x634 = dist

   +--------------------------------------------+-------------+-------------------+
   | Distance                                   | racer+0x638 | Mesh              |
   +============================================+=============+===================+
   | 3000 and above                             | 4           | g_meshMotoVeryLow |
   +--------------------------------------------+-------------+-------------------+
   | 2500 to 3000                               | 3           | g_meshMotoLow     |
   +--------------------------------------------+-------------+-------------------+
   | outside 1000..2500, when DAT_004ea4e0 is 0 | 0           | g_meshSkl         |
   +--------------------------------------------+-------------+-------------------+
   | otherwise                                  | 2           | g_meshMotoMed     |
   +--------------------------------------------+-------------+-------------------+

   AND IT ONLY RUNS IN A RACE. maybe_Racer_SelectLod has exactly
   one caller, FUN_00467b30, reached only from maybe_Game_Tick and
   three racer-update functions - all gameplay [verified]. A menu
   page that draws a racer never re-selects the level of detail,
   so racer+0x638 keeps what Racer_Create left: 0, the articulated
   model. That is what page 10 shows, and it is why the bike
   select screen has a posed rider rather than the moulded one of
   the medium LOD.

   DAT_004ea4e0, the gate on the slot-0 branch, has ONE reference
   in the whole binary - the read here - and no writer. Its
   initialiser is 0, so the branch is always live [verified].

   maybe_Racer_Draw at 0x0045f290 then reads racer+0x638 in more than
   twenty places, each time multiplying by 0x70 to index g_meshSkl[],
   and pulls faces, vertices and both counts out of the chosen mesh.

   This resolves what the five-mesh run is for. Slot 0 is not merely a
   skeleton: it is the full skeleton-animated model drawn at the nearest
   distances, and slots 2 to 4 are progressively cheaper static
   approximations. FileSkl is the articulated model; FileMotoMed,
   FileMotoLow and FileMotoVeryLow are its stand-ins. Slot 1, the hands,
   is never selected here - it is for the cockpit view [inferred].

   racer+0x638 therefore serves two purposes. Racer_Create uses it as a
   LOAD DESTINATION, setting it to 1 to load FileSklHand and 0 to load
   FileSkl; maybe_Racer_SelectLod uses it as a DRAW SELECTION. Same
   field, same array, different phase [verified].

   Two oddities recorded as they stand. The inner test assigns 0 on both
   of its branches, so one comparison against 200.0 has no effect. And
   the 3000.0 far threshold appears twice - once as the literal bound of
   the 2500..3000 band and once as _DAT_004d1040 - so the two are only
   equal by coincidence of the data, not by construction.

   New fields, all from this pair [verified]:

   +---------+-------------------------------------------+
   | Offset  | Holds                                     |
   +=========+===========================================+
   | +0x003c | position x                                |
   +---------+-------------------------------------------+
   | +0x0040 | position y                                |
   +---------+-------------------------------------------+
   | +0x0044 | position z                                |
   +---------+-------------------------------------------+
   | +0x0634 | distance to the camera                    |
   +---------+-------------------------------------------+
   | +0x0638 | mesh slot index into g_meshSkl[5]         |
   +---------+-------------------------------------------+
   | +0x31dd | which of the four cameras this racer uses |
   +---------+-------------------------------------------+

7.1.  The per-part record, and how it slices the mesh

   rec = racer + 0x63C + (lod * 20 + part) * 100, and the two ints
   at the end are NOT a first/count pair, which is the reading they
   invite:

   +---------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | Offset  | Meaning                                         |
   +=========+=================================================+
   | +0x18   | M, a 3x3 - the part's pose matrix               |
   | +0x3c   | t, its translation                              |
   | +0x58   | this part's VERTEX count                        |
   | +0x5c   | this part's FACE count                          |
   +---------+-------------------------------------------------+

   THE SLICES RUN CONSECUTIVELY. Part 0 takes the first +0x58
   vertices, part 1 the next, and so on. The proof is arithmetic:
   read live from racer 1, the vertex counts sum to exactly 240 and
   the face counts to exactly 340, which are corps.skl's own totals,
   and every vertex is consumed with none left over.  [verified]

   o READING THEM AS (first, count) LOOKS PLAUSIBLE AND IS WRONG.
     Part 0 gives 71 and 90, which passes as "vertices 71..160".
     It produces garbage part extents and, being garbage of roughly
     the right magnitude, does not announce itself. The sums are
     what catch it.

   THE VERTICES ARE DRAWN WITH THE INVERSE OF M - see
   runtime-capture.txt 13.1 - so a part's drawn geometry is

       drawn_j = M_row_j . (v - t)

   over its slice of the BIND pool. maybe_Racer_Draw never poses the
   pool itself; g_meshSkl[0] holds the bind pose at all times.

7.2.  A racer's position is its CONTACT POINT, not its model origin

   Measured with MOTO.EXE paused on the countdown, ile01:

       racer 1 position   (-128.00, 0.00, 2058.00)   racer+0x30
       road beneath it     y = 0.00                  mesh 0 face 7
       drawn bike extent   y [-63.11, 113.97]

   Taken together those say the bike's lower third is under the
   tarmac, which is plainly not what ships. Every global says
   otherwise: racer+0x30 reads zero, the racer matrix at +0xbc is
   the identity with a zero translation, and nothing in the racer's
   first 1024 bytes holds a lifted position.

   A TRACE SETTLED IT. Hooking maybe_Racer_Draw's per-part transform
   at 0x0046196e and capturing the composed matrix off the stack
   gave part 2 a camera-space translation of

       (2.74, -138.75, 500.85)

   where drawing at the racer position would have given

       (0, -203.28, 503.66)

   a difference of 64.5 units of camera Y. THE GAME DRAWS THE BIKE
   64.5 UNITS ABOVE ITS RACER POSITION, which is the ride height
   that stands the wheels on the road.  [verified]

   o WHERE THAT NUMBER IS KEPT IS STILL UNFOUND. It is applied
     inside the draw and appears in no global that was read. Almost
     certainly suspension or ground contact producing a per-frame
     ride height, which would also explain why it is 64.5 and not
     exactly the model's own 62.55 bottom.

   o AND IT IS WHY STATIC READS COULD NOT SOLVE IT. The position,
     the road height and the mesh extent were all measured
     correctly and all agreed; the answer simply was not in any of
     them. A trace was the only instrument that could reach it, and
     it should have been reached for sooner.

7.3.  staticPied IS the resting pose, and the note that says
      otherwise is retracted

   A race at rest shows the same stance as the bike-select page: one
   foot down, hands on the bars, the foot lifting to the peg once
   the bike moves [observed]. bikes.h labels
   BIKE_PATH_ROT_STATICPIED "feet down, the grid", and page 10
   applies it through Rot_ApplyToRacer(&g_menuRacer, 0) with
   g_pilotRot[4] [verified].

   THE "staticPied IS OUT BY 42.5" CLAIM IN runtime-capture.txt 3.1
   IS RETRACTED IN THAT SAME DOCUMENT, and the retraction is easy to
   miss. The capture behind it compared g_meshSkl's vertices against
   the bind pose, and maybe_Racer_Draw never poses that pool, so the
   test could not fail.

   A SECOND WEAK TEST REACHED THE SAME WRONG PLACE. Comparing the
   drawn VERTICAL EXTENT against the bind pool's - [-63.11, 113.97]
   against [-62.55, 120.14] - and concluding "the race barely poses
   the bike" is not evidence: A RANGE CANNOT DISTINGUISH A RIDER
   WITH HANDS ON THE BARS FROM ONE WITH ARMS OUT FRONT. Removing
   the pose on that basis produced the unposed stance immediately,
   which is what a stronger test would have predicted.

   WHAT IS STILL OPEN: our staticPied bake is CLOSE BUT NOT EQUAL to
   what the race draws.

       ours     y [-58.89, 102.56]    height 161.4
       retail   y [-63.11, 113.97]    height 177.1

   so retail's rider sits about eleven units taller and four lower,
   a 10 per cent difference in overall height. Close enough to look
   right, far enough to mean the pose or its application is not
   exactly the race's. Worth resolving once the wheels are drawn and
   the stance can be judged by eye.

8.  THE RACER BASE IS 0x00581218, AND GETTING IT WRONG COSTS 0x24

   The array base is 0x00581218 with a stride of 0x3209 [verified].
   Racer_Create is __thiscall, so its offsets are real field offsets,
   and maybe_Game_CreateRacers writes the same +0x68 through
   0x00581218 + i * 0x3209. Two directions, one answer.

   +---------+----------+--------------------------------------------+
   | Offset  | Type     | What                                       |
   +=========+==========+============================================+
   | +0x0030 | float[3] | position                                   |
   +---------+----------+--------------------------------------------+
   | +0x00bc | float[12]| 3x4 matrix, rows then translation. Reads   |
   |         |          | orthonormal to 1.00005 a row, translation  |
   |         |          | ZERO                                       |
   +---------+----------+--------------------------------------------+

   THIS SECTION PREVIOUSLY SAID +0x0c AND +0x98 AND WAS WRONG. The
   error is worth keeping because of how convincing it looked.

   Camera_UpdateChase computes its racer pointer as 0x0058123c +
   i * 0x3209 - which is the racer's BODY MODEL INSTANCE, because
   Racer_Create initialises one at this+0x24 and 0x0058123c is
   0x00581218 + 0x24. So the camera's +0x0c and +0x98 are offsets
   inside an MR_ModelInstance, and adding them to the racer base
   lands 0x24 short.

   o THREE CONFIRMATIONS THAT WERE ONE. The wrong version cited
     Camera_UpdateChase's +0x0c, Racer_GetYaw's +0x98, and mode 3's
     copy from 0x00581248 - three functions, three offsets, all
     agreeing. Every one of them reaches the racer through the SAME
     +0x24 instance pointer. Correlated evidence reads exactly like
     independent evidence and is worth nothing.

   o AND LIVE READS AGREED WITH BOTH. Reading "+0x30" from the wrong
     base hits the same bytes as "+0x0c" from the right one, so the
     dump that appeared to disprove +0x30 was measuring a different
     field entirely and looked like a clean refutation.

   THE CHECK THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT: 0x00581218 has twelve xrefs
   including maybe_Game_CreateRacers; 0x0058123c has nine and no
   creator. An array base that nothing allocates or initialises is
   not an array base.

   o AND SECTION 6 ALREADY HAD THE INSTANCES. The three at +0x24,
     +0x1a8 and +0x32c, the 0x184 spacing, and the size confirmed
     from outside the racer by two menu pages - all of it was
     written up before this section existed. It was restated here as
     a fresh discovery anyway, which is the same failure to grep
     docs/ first that CLAUDE.md warns about, one paragraph after a
     section about believing correlated evidence.

9.  The race tick chain

   Read top-down with MR_Racer applied, from the game object's vtable
   down to one racer's physics. Everything here is out of the
   decompiler and the disassembly [verified].

9.1.  maybe_Game_Tick's in-race step, in order

   0x0047d660. The branch runs when FUN_004126a0 returns non-zero -
   in a race rather than a menu - and then only if FUN_004121e0 and
   vtable slot +0x10 both agree.

       DAT_004e9810 = __ftol()             the delta as an integer
       if (DAT_00519fec > 0)               a countdown, decremented
           DAT_00519fec -= DAT_004e9810      by that integer
       FUN_0047dae0()
       Race_TickAllRacers()                0x0047df40  PHYSICS AND AI
       vtable+0x18 maybe_Game_CollideRacers(dt)
       [net] replicate and broadcast the local input word
       FUN_0048b7a0(dt)
       FUN_00457590(dt)                    profiled into _DAT_005429cc
       maybe_Race_UpdateProgress()         0x0047e3d0
       Race_ComputeStandings()             0x0047e1e0
       DAT_004dc0f8 = 0
       Camera_SelectView(dt)               0x004583e0
       FUN_00458620(dt)
       [if DAT_004dc138] FUN_00467b30(1) for every active racer
       [if DAT_004db65c] FUN_004079e0()
       FUN_00405f30()
       [net] the race-finish check - 9.6
       if (FUN_00412a50())
           vtable+0x20 maybe_Game_RenderFrame(dt, 0xfffffffe)
       Frame_LimitAndMeasure()

   o PHYSICS RUNS BEFORE COLLISION AND BOTH RUN BEFORE THE CAMERA.
     So Camera_SelectView reads positions this frame's physics has
     already committed. There is no one-frame lag to reproduce, and
     a port that updates the camera first will not match.

   o AND THE RENDER IS GATED. FUN_00412a50 returning 0 simulates the
     frame and does not draw it, while Frame_LimitAndMeasure still
     runs. Whatever that gate is, it does not stop the clock.

9.2.  Race_TickAllRacers, and what the frame delta actually is

   0x0047df40, renamed from FUN_0047df40. It opens by rescaling the
   step in place:

       g_frameDeltaScaled *= 0.1f;        FLT_TICK_DT_SCALE 0x004d1970
       if (g_frameDeltaScaled < 0.0f)     the constant at 0x004d1900
           g_frameDeltaScaled = 1.0f;       is 0.0f

   o THAT SECOND LINE IS A NEGATIVE GUARD, NOT A FLOOR. It compares
     against zero, and the replacement value of 1.0 is two orders of
     magnitude larger than any step the game actually sees. So it
     fires only when the delta has gone backwards, and when it does
     the tick deliberately takes a whole unit rather than a small
     one.

   o THE SCALE IS APPLIED IN PLACE, which is only safe because
     maybe_Game_ReadInput writes g_frameDeltaScaled fresh every frame
     - the store at 0x00411809. Everything downstream of this
     function, collision and camera and render alike, therefore reads
     the SCALED value and not the raw one.

   The loop walks the array from racer 1 by 0x3209, testing the
   active flag at +0x68:

       slot = 1;  racer = &g_racers[1];
       do {
           if (g_nSoloMode) goto drive;
           if (Net_FindPlayerSlot(...) == slot ||
               (DAT_004dbff8 && g_netPlayerStates[slot] == 3)) {
       drive:  if (racer->active)
                   Racer_TickDispatch(racer,
                                      racer->inputWord |
                                          racer->inputWord2,
                                      racer->axis0, racer->axis1,
                                      g_frameDeltaScaled);
           } else if (racer->active) {
               maybe_Racer_TickRemote(racer, g_frameDeltaScaled);
           }
           racer++;  slot++;
       } while (slot <= 24);

   o THE INPUTS COME OUT OF THE RACER, NOT OUT OF THE INPUT LAYER.
     maybe_Game_ReadInput has already deposited this step's buttons
     and axes into +0x31e9, +0x31ed, +0x31f1 and +0x31f5, and the
     tick reads them straight back out. So the reconstruction's input
     code writes the racer record and stops; nothing threads a
     controller object down into the physics.

   o IN SOLO, EVERY RACER GOES THROUGH THE DISPATCH. The network
     branch is the only thing that splits them, and it splits them by
     OWNERSHIP: this machine's player, plus any slot whose
     g_netPlayerStates entry is 3, are simulated from inputs, and
     everyone else gets maybe_Racer_TickRemote and a bare dt.

   o g_netPlayerStates IS NOT THE RACER. It is a separate table at
     0x0050806c with a stride of 36, bounded at 0x005083cc, and it is
     what both `== 3` tests in this chain read - the other being the
     one in maybe_Game_Tick that decides whose state to broadcast.
     Anything in this project that has wondered about "race state 3"
     needs to check which of the two arrays it meant: racer->raceState
     is +0x2de5 and is a different field entirely.

9.3.  Three sibling ticks, not one

   +--------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | Function                 | Address      | The one callee it does not     |
   |                          |              | share                          |
   +==========================+==============+================================+
   | maybe_Racer_Tick         | 0x0045d580   | seven of its own, among them   |
   |                          |              | FUN_0048d0f0 and FUN_0048d620  |
   +--------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_TickAI       | 0x0046bf80   | FUN_0046b120                   |
   +--------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_TickRemote   | 0x0045e940   | FUN_0045e560                   |
   +--------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+

   o THEY SHARE A LIBRARY, NOT A BODY. All three call
     Racer_SavePrevFrame, Racer_UpdateSuspension,
     maybe_Racer_BuildShadow, maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash,
     maybe_Racer_UpdateLean, FUN_004660f0, FUN_00466940,
     FUN_00466d70, FUN_00467930, FUN_00467b30 and FUN_0046c7a0. What
     separates them is the decision layer each one adds on top.

   o maybe_Racer_TickRemote AND maybe_Racer_TickAI ARE NEARLY THE
     SAME FUNCTION - their callee sets differ by two entries. That is
     what a remote tick should look like: the same integrator with no
     decision layer, run on locally held state. It is support for
     replay.txt section 6's determinism argument arriving from a
     completely different direction.

   o maybe_Racer_TickRemote HAS EXACTLY ONE CALLER, which is why it
     could be identified at all. Race_TickAllRacers reaches it from
     two places and only two: a network game's non-local racers, and
     the time-attack ghost.

9.4.  Racer_TickDispatch is eight instructions

   0x0045d540, renamed from FUN_0045d540, now __thiscall on
   MR_Racer. In full:

       void __thiscall MR_Racer::Racer_TickDispatch(
               MR_Racer *this, uint buttons, uint axis0,
               uint axis1, float dt)
       {
           g_frameDelta = dt;
           if (this->humanPlayer == 0) {
               maybe_Racer_TickAI(this, dt);
               return;
           }
           maybe_Racer_Tick(this, buttons, axis0, axis1, dt);
       }

   o THE HUMAN AND THE AI DO NOT SHARE A PHYSICS TICK, and the AI is
     handed ONLY the frame delta. It never sees buttons or axes, so
     the AI does not drive through a synthetic controller - a port
     that routed it through the player tick would be a different
     game.

   o AND THAT EXPLAINS A CLASS OF CONFUSION IN structs/MR_Racer.txt.
     Several fields chased there as "sometimes written, sometimes
     not" are simply on one side of this branch.

   o THE CONVENTION IS VERIFIED, not inferred: MOV EDX,[ECX+8] at
     0x0045d54a reads humanPlayer through ECX, and RET 0x10 accounts
     for exactly four stack arguments.

   g_frameDelta (0x00581014) is then copied by Racer_SavePrevFrame
   into racer+0x54, and subtracted from timers in
   maybe_Racer_UpdateLean. That is what confirms it is a time STEP
   and not a scale, and racer+0x54 is named frameDelta on the
   strength of it.

9.5.  Time attack does not run the loop at all

   The first thing Race_TickAllRacers does after rescaling the delta
   is test three globals, and if all three agree it ticks one racer,
   services a ghost, and returns:

       if (g_nSoloMode && g_nSelectedMode == 0 && g_nTimeAttack) {
           Racer_TickDispatch(&g_racers[1], ...);
           if (g_racers[2].active) {
               maybe_Ghost_PlaybackFrame(dt, &g_racers[2].speed,
                                         &g_racers[2].placeWheels0,
                                         ..., &crashed, vec);
               ... the prev-frame copies ...
               if (crashed)
                   maybe_Racer_BeginCrash(&g_racers[2], vec);
               maybe_Racer_TickRemote(&g_racers[2], dt);
           }
           maybe_Ghost_RecordFrame(dt, <player fields>);
           g_racers[1].ghostEvent = 0;
           return;
       }

   o SO RACER SLOT 2 IS THE GHOST. Its base is 0x0058762a, exactly
     one stride above racer 1, and it is driven by
     maybe_Racer_TickRemote - the same tick a network peer gets.

   o AND IT IS ARMED IN maybe_Game_InitRace, at 0x0047c02a, before
     anybody is placed:

         if (g_nSelectedMode == 0) {
             if (g_nTimeAttack == 0)  g_racers[2].active = 0;
             else {
                 FUN_004191b0();
                 g_racers[2].active = (DAT_0052c0d8 != 0);
                 FUN_004191a0();
             }
         }

     DAT_00587692 in the decompilation is that active field -
     g_racers + 2 * 0x3209 + 0x68.  Practice is given ONE opponent
     by maybe_Game_LoadRace, so the record is built like any
     other; this is what stops it being ridden.  Left set with no
     stream behind it the AI tick takes the slot and rides it.

     TWO VISIBLE CONSEQUENCES, both of which look like separate
     bugs until this is read.  DAT_004dc100 counts ACTIVE racers,
     so practice has 1 and Hud_DrawPosOnly's `1 < DAT_004dc100`
     fails - no position block at all, and "1/1" on the time
     attack arm (hud-race.txt 1).  And the grid loop gates its
     segment step and lateral offset on g_nSelectedMode != 0, so
     racers 1 and 2 share a segment and a side: the ghost starts
     exactly where the player does, by design.

   o AND NO AI RUNS IN TIME ATTACK. Not "the AI are present and
     idle": the loop that would tick them is not entered at all.

   o &g_menuRacer IS RACER 1. The decompiler writes
     Racer_TickDispatch((MR_Racer *)&g_menuRacer, ...) here, which
     reads like a bug and is not - 0x00584421 is the player's slot,
     and the menu happens to reuse it. Already on record in
     subsystems/game-object.txt.

9.5.1.  The ghost record is 52 bytes, and it is not the replay buffer

   maybe_Ghost_RecordFrame (0x004191c0, renamed from FUN_004191c0) is
   __thiscall on a small recorder object and appends one 0x34-byte
   record per frame:

   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+
   | Offset   | Size   | From the player racer                        |
   +==========+========+==============================================+
   | +0x00    | 4      | the running sum of dt - a timestamp          |
   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x04    | 4      | speed, +0x124                                |
   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x08    | 12     | position, +0x30                              |
   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x14    | 12     | matOrientation row 2 plus row 3, that is     |
   |          |        | +0xd4 and +0xe0 added componentwise          |
   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x20    | 4      | stuntOrOverspeed, +0x2e85                    |
   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x24    | 4      | ghostEvent, +0x31ad - cleared to 0 straight  |
   |          |        | after the call                               |
   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x28    | 12     | +0x31a1, the second impact vector            |
   +----------+--------+----------------------------------------------+

   The recorder object in ECX is four fields: +0x00 the write index,
   +0x08 the capacity, +0x0c the base of the record array, and +0x14
   the accumulated time that becomes record +0x00.

   o IT SILENTLY STOPS. The whole body is wrapped in an
     index-less-than-capacity test with no else. A long enough
     time-attack lap simply stops recording, which is exactly what
     replay.txt section 1 found for the input buffer - the same
     design decision made twice.

   o THIS IS NOT THE REPLAY MECHANISM. replay.txt describes an
     8-byte INPUT record played back by re-simulation. This is a
     52-byte STATE record whose playback writes the ghost's position
     and speed directly. Two different systems that both look like
     "recording a lap", with different buffers, record sizes and
     consumers. Do not conflate them. [verified]

9.6.  The race-finish check, and three open notes closed

   The finish test at the end of maybe_Game_Tick reads:

       for every racer:
           if (racer->active &&
               (racer->raceState == 1 || racer->raceState == 0))
               notEveryoneHasFinished;

   o SO 0 AND 1 BOTH COUNT AS STILL RACING. The note in
     mr_dump_racer.py reads "2 finished, 1 racing, 3/0 out", and the
     0 half of that needs softening: whatever 0 means elsewhere, this
     test treats it as unfinished. [verified]

   When everyone has finished it sets DAT_00509ba0, calls
   Race_ComputeStandings a second time, and stores each human racer's
   finishing position. That store is the answer to a question this
   project had been carrying:

   +------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Offset     | Was open, now answered                                       |
   +============+==============================================================+
   | +0x2dc8    | THE NET INPUT WORD. maybe_Game_Tick writes                   |
   |            | g_racers[localSlot].netInputWord =                           |
   |            | g_racers[g_inputRacerIndex].inputWord and then sends net     |
   |            | message 0x66. The note said "read by the net"; this is the   |
   |            | writer.                                                      |
   +------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x2f85    | THE FINISHING POSITION. The note said "read in a dozen       |
   |            | places, no writer found". The writer is the race-finish      |
   |            | block of maybe_Game_Tick, one store per human racer, from    |
   |            | maybe_Race_FinishingPosition(slot). 0 means did not finish - |
   |            | the jingle chosen for 0 is the unfinished one.               |
   +------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x31ad    | A ONE-SHOT THE GHOST RECORDER CONSUMES. The note said "reads |
   |            | 1". It goes into ghost record +0x24 and is cleared to 0 by   |
   |            | the next statement, so 1 is what it reads between the event  |
   |            | and the following tick.                                      |
   +------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+

   o THE +0x2dc8 ROW ABOVE IS NARROWER THAN THE TRUTH. It is not
     a net-only field - maybe_Racer_Tick writes it every tick for
     every racer. Corrected, with the evidence, in 9.7.4.

   o ALL THREE WERE FOUND BY READING ONE CHAIN TOP-DOWN, not by
     hunting the fields. Two of them are in maybe_Game_Tick, a
     function this project had already read once for its vtable
     slots - the offsets were on the page and nobody had matched them
     against the racer map.

9.7.  Inside maybe_Racer_Tick

   0x0045d580, the human branch of 9.4. With MR_Racer applied and
   its callees typed it decompiles to about forty readable lines, so
   this section is what typing the callees bought rather than a
   transcription of the body.

9.7.1.  Twelve prototypes, and the rule that found them

   Every callee reached with ECX holding the racer was showing up
   with no this at all, which hides both the receiver and the real
   argument list. The test for "is this a racer method" is the first
   few instructions: an offset off ECX that only makes sense on a
   racer.

   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | Address      | Now                        | Signature, this omitted        |
   +==============+============================+================================+
   | 0x0045d580   | maybe_Racer_Tick           | (uint buttons, uint axis0,     |
   |              |                            | uint axis1, float dt)          |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x00465da0   | -                          | (uint buttons, uint axis0,     |
   |              |                            | uint axis1, float dt)          |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x00466240   | -                          | (uint buttons, uint axis0,     |
   |              |                            | uint axis1, float dt)          |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x004660f0   | maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie  | (float dt)                     |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x0045d110   | -                          | (float dt)                     |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x00466660   | -                          | (uint buttons)                 |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x00466940   | -                          | (void)                         |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x00467930   | -                          | (void)                         |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x0046c7a0   | -                          | (int segment, int param_2)     |
   |              |                            | returns int                    |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x0046d340   | maybe_Trk_LaneOfRacer      | (void) returns int             |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x00467b30   | maybe_Racer_UpdateSkeleton | (int bAnimate)                 |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | 0x00468ca0   | maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundCo | was void, now returns int      |
   |              | ntact                      |                                |
   +--------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------+

   o TWO OF THEM ARE NOT RACER METHODS AND THAT MATTERS.
     FUN_0048d0f0 and FUN_0048d620 are called with LEA ECX,[ESI+0x24]
     - MODEL INSTANCE 0, not the racer. Typing them onto MR_Racer
     would have produced confident nonsense at every offset. The
     call site, not the callee, is what settles the receiver.

   o AND FUN_0046c7a0 IS a racer method even though its first
     instructions never touch ECX. It reads its stack arguments
     first; MOV ECX,ESI at the call site is the evidence. A
     first-instruction heuristic gets this one wrong in both
     directions, so the call site has to be checked either way.

9.7.2.  maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact returns a flag

   It was declared void. It is not:

       0045d79c  CALL maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact
       0045d7a1  MOV  EDI,EAX
       ...
       0045d7d5  PUSH EDI
       0045d7d8  CALL maybe_Racer_UpdateSkeleton
       0045d7dd  TEST EDI,EDI
       0045d7df  JZ   0045d7e9                    skips FUN_00466d70

   o SO ONE FLAG GATES TWO THINGS. It is passed to the skeleton
     update as bAnimate, and tested to decide whether FUN_00466d70
     runs at all.

   o THE DECOMPILER WAS ACTIVELY MISLEADING BEFORE THE FIX. With a
     void return it invented extraout_EAX and used it for BOTH the
     argument and the test, which reads as two unrelated unknowns.
     They are one value. A void return type on a function that
     returns something is worse than an unknown one, because the
     output stays plausible.

9.7.3.  The wheelie is a self-contained subsystem

   maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie (0x004660f0) owns three fields and
   nothing else writes them:

   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Offset     | Named this pass                                                |
   +============+================================================================+
   | +0x00b8    | wheelieScale. NEW - it filled the last 4-byte gap before       |
   |            | matOrientation. 1.0 normally, 1.6 while the wheelie angle is   |
   |            | non-zero, 2.0 instead of 1.6 when DAT_004dc1f8 is set.         |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x307d    | wheelieAngle. The note said "a float Camera_Update and         |
   |            | CollideRacers read" - true, and now it is known what they are  |
   |            | reading. Radians, clamped at pi/2.                             |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x3081    | wheelieTimer. The note said "beside +0x307d [open]". It is the |
   |            | hold timer, and it carries the duration cap.                   |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x2dc8    | tickInputWord, RENAMED from netInputWord - see 9.7.4.          |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+

   The shape, with the constants read out of .rdata:

       if (crashLatch) { angle = timer = stuntOrOverspeed = 0;
                         scale = 1.0f;
                         if (humanPlayer && soloMode) stuntHeld = 0;
                         return; }
       if (stuntOrOverspeed && timer <= 0x4347ffff) {
           angle += dt * K_UP;  if (angle > LIMIT) angle = PI/2;
           timer += dt;
       } else if (angle > 0) {
           angle += dt * K_DOWN;                   K_DOWN is negative
           if (angle < 0) { springCompression = -32.0f; angle = 0; }
       }
       scale = angle ? (DAT_004dc1f8 ? 2.0f : 1.6f) : 1.0f;

   o THERE IS A HARD CAP ON WHEELIE DURATION. 0x4347ffff is a float
     compared as an integer, about 199.99998. Past it the ramp stops
     and the angle decays WITH THE BUTTON STILL HELD. That is a
     gameplay rule, not a physical clamp, and a port that leaves it
     out gives the player an infinite wheelie.

   o AND THE LANDING THUMP IS HERE, not in the suspension code.
     springCompression = -32.0 fires exactly once, on the frame the
     angle crosses zero from above.

   o IT ALSO CONFIRMS THE STUNT LATCH FROM THE OTHER SIDE. The crash
     path clears stuntHeld only for humanPlayer && soloMode, which is
     the same pair maybe_Racer_Tick tests one line earlier to copy
     stuntHeld into stuntOrOverspeed. structs/MR_Racer.txt section 19
     worked that latch out from the stunt end; this is its other
     half.

9.7.4.  A correction: +0x2dc8 is not net-specific

   9.6 recorded +0x2dc8 as "the net input word" on the strength of
   maybe_Game_Tick's broadcast path. maybe_Racer_Tick's LAST
   statement is

       this->tickInputWord = buttons;

   so every racer gets it every tick, network game or not. The field
   is THE BUTTON WORD THIS TICK RAN WITH; the net code preloads it
   for the slot it is about to send, which is a use of the field
   rather than its meaning. Renamed netInputWord -> tickInputWord in
   the map and in the DB.

   o THE ORIGINAL READING CAME FROM THE ONLY WRITER THAT HAD BEEN
     SEEN. It was not wrong about the mechanism, only about the
     scope, and one more reader settled it. Worth noting because the
     same shape - a general field first met in a special-case caller
     - is how several names in this project have had to be widened.

9.7.5.  Float sign tests done in integer registers

   The body is full of comparisons that look wrong and are not:

       if ((int)dt < 1)                dt = 1.0f;     dt <= 0
       if ((uint)headingRef < 0x80000001)             headingRef >= 0
       if (0 < (int)forkDive)                         forkDive > 0
       if (0x80000000 < (uint)forkDive)               forkDive < 0

   These are IEEE bit-pattern tests on floats held in general
   registers. They agree with the float comparisons everywhere
   except at -0.0.

   o SO THE dt GUARD IS THE SAME ONE Race_TickAllRacers APPLIES, one
     level up, done a different way - see 9.2. A reconstruction that
     transcribes `if (dt < 1) dt = 1.0f` literally is wrong; the
     original means `if (dt <= 0.0f)`.

   o AND THIS IS WHY THE DECOMPILATION LOOKED SO BAD BEFORE THE
     TYPES WENT ON. Half the strangeness was the missing struct and
     the missing prototypes; the other half is the binary genuinely
     doing this, and only the first half goes away.

9.7.6.  Still unnamed inside the chain

   Reading them is the next step down:

       0x00465da0   DONE in 9.8 - maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive
       0x00466240   DONE in 9.9 - maybe_Racer_ApplySteering
       0x0045d110  1046 bytes   dt only
       0x00466660   382 bytes   buttons; opens on onGround
       0x00466940   309 bytes   reads humanPlayer and the brake bit
       0x00467930   499 bytes   opens on rearWheelContact minus its
                                previous value
       0x00466d70    -          buttons; runs only when the ground
                                contact flag is set

9.8.  maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive, and the race clock behind it

   0x00465da0, renamed from FUN_00465da0. 9.7.6 listed it as the
   next thing to read. It is the throttle and brake path, and
   reading it forced the function above it open as well.

9.8.1.  What it actually does

   It rewrites this tick's button word, builds a direction, turns
   the input into a signed fraction, and hands both to FUN_0048ef30.
   The overrides come first and they are the interesting half:

       raceState not in {0,1} and humanPlayer -> buttons = 2
       g_bPreRace                             -> buttons |= 2
       else if (wheelieAngle != 0)            -> buttons |= 1
       if (buttons & 2) { stuntOrOverspeed = 0;
                          if (humanPlayer && soloMode)
                              stuntHeld = 0; }

   o A HUMAN WHO HAS FINISHED IS FORCED TO BRAKE. Not "input
     ignored" - buttons is SET to 2, so the brake is held down. That
     is what stops the bike after the line, and it is a behaviour a
     port has to reproduce deliberately; simply dropping input
     leaves the bike coasting forever.

   o A WHEELIE IMPLIES THROTTLE. The bike cannot be on its back
     wheel and coasting, so the accel bit is forced while the angle
     is non-zero.

   o AND BRAKING CANCELS THE WHEELIE - the third place this project
     has found that latch enforced, after maybe_Racer_Tick's copy of
     stuntHeld into stuntOrOverspeed and maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie's
     crash path. Three separate functions guard the same pair of
     fields, which is worth knowing before deciding one of them is
     redundant.

9.8.2.  The analog axis is not symmetric, and that is verified

   The drive value goes out as a numerator and a denominator:

       analog, axis0 != 0x8000, racing:
           n = 0x8000 - axis0
           n >= 0  ->  (n,        0x8000)
           n <  0  ->  (n / 2000, 0x8000)
       digital:
           neither bit             ->  ( 0, 1)
           brake, and !g_bPreRace  ->  (-1, 1)
           accel                   ->  ( 1, 1)

   The divide is real and was checked in the disassembly rather than
   taken from the decompiler [verified]:

       00465ed5  JS   0x00465ef9
       ...
       00465ef9  CDQ
       00465efa  MOV  ESI,0x7d0
       00465f03  IDIV ESI

   o SO ONE HALF OF THE AXIS IS ATTENUATED 2000 TIMES AND THE OTHER
     IS NOT. A full deflection on the divided side reaches about
     0.0005 of one digital unit, which is indistinguishable from
     nothing. Whether that is a design decision - the axis drives
     only forward, braking being a button - or a defect is OPEN.
     It is recorded because a reconstruction has to choose, and
     choosing silently is how a port stops matching.

   o THE DIRECTION IS matOrientation[9..11] + matOrientation[6..8],
     normalised unless its length equals _DAT_004d0e54. The
     UNNORMALISED sum is exactly what maybe_Ghost_RecordFrame writes
     to ghost record +0x14 (9.5.1), so two unrelated functions agree
     on the quantity. WHAT IT MEANS GEOMETRICALLY IS NOT SETTLED -
     adding two rows of a 4x3 matrix is not an obvious "forward
     vector", and it is not being named one on a guess. [open]

     [PARTLY ANSWERED IN 9.10.4. The drivetrain uses the Y
     component as a gravity term - downhill accelerates - which
     only makes sense for a world-space forward vector. Still an
     inference, but one with a consumer behind it.]

   o FUN_0048ef30 IS NOT A RACER METHOD. Every call site does
     ADD ECX,0x24 first, so the receiver is MODEL INSTANCE 0. It is
     4771 bytes and reads g_bPreRace itself - the largest thing the
     tick chain reaches, and the obvious place the actual vehicle
     dynamics live.

9.8.3.  raceState 3 is TIMED OUT

   +----------+------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Value    | racer->raceState at +0x2de5                                      |
   +==========+==================================================================+
   | 0        | Still racing. maybe_Game_Tick's finish test accepts it as        |
   |          | unfinished, and maybe_Race_TickClock advances the clock for it.  |
   +----------+------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | 1        | Racing. Assigned at the start to racers whose segmentStart       |
   |          | passes a half-track test.                                        |
   +----------+------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | 2        | Finished. maybe_Racer_UpdateSkeleton pairs it with               |
   |          | finishPosition == 0 to run a post-finish timer.                  |
   +----------+------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | 3        | TIMED OUT. One writer, one condition, local racer only - see     |
   |          | 9.8.3.                                                           |
   +----------+------------------------------------------------------------------+

   maybe_Race_TickClock (0x0047dae0, renamed from FUN_0047dae0) is
   the step maybe_Game_Tick runs immediately before
   Race_TickAllRacers - it was the one unnamed entry in 9.1's list.
   It owns three globals:

   The 2 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/race.h.

   Its shape:

       1  if (g_bPreRace && !solo) run the DirectPlay start
          handshake - mark our g_netPlayerStates entry 4, wait for
          every active peer to reach 4, ping each, qsort by latency
          and timeGetTime-busy-wait so all machines leave the grid
          together
       2  g_raceTimer -= g_frameDeltaMs
       3  if (!g_bPreRace) for every racer that is active, has
          raceState 0 or 1, and is the local player or not human:
              racer->raceClockMs += g_frameDeltaMs;
              g_lapTimes[slot*8 + racer->lapCounter] +=
                                                  g_frameDeltaMs;
       4  when g_raceTimer goes negative:
          a  ON THE GRID -> g_bPreRace = 0, the race starts.
             g_raceTimer is RELOADED as the time limit, but only
             if segment 0's gridShort is >= 0:
                 network  track+0xda + 120000
                 solo     track+0xda +
                          g_difficultyBonus[g_cfgDifficulty], the
                          table at 0x00515fe0
             then raceClockMs and the eight g_lapTimes entries are
             zeroed for each eligible racer, and raceState 1 is
             assigned from segmentStart against half the segment
             count, with DAT_004dc144 inverting the test.  Both of
             those happen whether the reload did or not
          b  ALREADY RACING -> the limit expired.  Gated on three
             terms, then past a 500 unit grace: DAT_0052c238 = 1,
             jingle 0x39, and
                 g_racers[g_localRacerIndex].raceState = 3
             Before that, a warning is redisplayed and jingle 0x42
             plays no more often than every 2000 units

   THE GATE ON 4b IS THREE TERMS at 0x0047de56, and it is the part
   easiest to read past because it sits on the arm rather than on
   the timer:

       DAT_004e9830 == 0                      attract demo
       local raceState == 1 || == 0           racing, not finished
       g_nSelectedMode != 0 || g_nTimeAttack   NOT PRACTICE

   o THE MODE TERM IS WHAT MAKES A PRACTICE RACE UNTIMED.  Mode 0
     is practice in a solo game, and in it this arm never runs at
     all: g_raceTimer goes on down into deep negatives and nothing
     reads it.  THE gridShort SKIP IN 4a IS NOT THAT MECHANISM - a
     negative gridShort in a TIMED mode still times the player out
     about 500 units after the grid clears, because 4b only asks
     whether the timer is negative.

   o THE raceState TERM IS NOT MERELY A RE-ENTRY LATCH.  It also
     keeps a racer who has FINISHED - state 2 - from being
     overwritten as timed out.  A write-once flag substituted for
     it covers only the re-entry half.

   The same mode test picks the HUD's position block - see
   hud-race.txt section 1.

   o WHY IT WAS NEVER OBSERVED. One writer, one condition, and only
     the LOCAL racer - a lap that finishes never reaches it. No
     amount of watching a good replay would have produced it; it had
     to be read.

   o AND IT SETTLES THE WARNING IN 9.2 FROM THE OTHER SIDE. The
     `== 3` tests in Race_TickAllRacers and maybe_Game_Tick are on
     g_netPlayerStates, an unrelated 36-byte-stride table. Two
     different threes. The one in the racer means timed out; the one
     in the net table means a slot this host simulates.

   o g_lapTimes IS int[racer][8], indexed by the 16-bit lapCounter
     at +0x2f7b. That is the first time the array's shape has been
     read rather than assumed, and it caps a race at eight recorded
     laps.

   o THE PORT CAN HOLD THE TIMER OFF ZERO, via [OPTIONS_CUSTOM]
     FreezeRaceTimer - a DEBUG override, off by default. Step 4b then
     never runs, so raceState 3 is never written at all rather than
     being written and ignored. Gated on preRace so step 4a still
     starts the race. config-file.txt 4.1.

   o THE PORT OMITS ONLY THE ATTRACT-DEMO TERM, which it has no
     attract demo to make false.  DAT_004e9830's identity is not
     settled; its writers point that way but the polarity is
     unverified.

9.8.4.  Two timers in one variable

   g_raceTimer is the grid countdown before the start and the race
   time limit after it, with g_bPreRace as the only thing that says
   which. A reader that fixes on one meaning is wrong for the other
   phase, and the transition rewrites the variable in the same
   branch that clears the flag.

   o THE RELOAD VALUE COMES OFF THE TRACK RECORD, at track+0xda,
     plus a per-difficulty bonus in solo or a flat 120000 in
     network. So the time limit is per-track data, not a constant -
     one more field for whenever MR_TrkHeader is worked.

9.9.  maybe_Racer_ApplySteering - the writer of racer+0x110

   0x00466240, renamed from FUN_00466240, the largest thing left in
   9.7.6's list. racer-draw-pipeline.txt 7 found the steering angle
   at racer+0x110 from the DRAW side and measured its speed
   fade. THIS IS THE OTHER END: the function that writes it.

   o WHICH MEANS THE FIELD IS NOW CLOSED FROM BOTH DIRECTIONS. A
     field known only from its reader is a field whose units and
     range are inferred; known from its writer, they are read. The
     clamp is g_bikeCarac RoueLimiteAngulaire, so the range is per
     bike and comes out of the .bpt.

9.9.1.  axis1 is steering, axis0 is throttle

   9.8 established axis0 as the drive axis without being able to say
   what axis1 was. This function answers it: axis1 is compared
   against 0x8000 and mapped straight onto the steering angle. The
   pair is settled. [verified]

9.9.2.  Three paths

       ANALOG    axis1 != 0x8000
           leanMode = 1;
           n = (speed < _DAT_004d10c0) ? 0 : axis1 - 0x8000;
           steering = n * RoueLimiteAngulaire * _DAT_004d10c8;

       DIGITAL   axis1 == 0x8000, left or right held,
                 and speed > _DAT_004d10c0
           leanMode = 0;
           if steering has the wrong sign, snap it to 0
           steerRate += RoueAccelerationAngulaire * dt;
           steering  += steerRate * dt,
                        clamped to +-RoueLimiteAngulaire;

       CENTRING  otherwise
           steerRate = 0;
           k = max(steerReturnScale * RoueVitesseRetour * K,
                   RoueVitesseRetour);
           step steering toward 0 by k*dt, snapping at the crossing.

   o YOU CANNOT STEER BELOW A MINIMUM SPEED. _DAT_004d10c0 gates all
     three paths - the analog one zeroes its own input, the digital
     ones fall through to centring. A stationary bike does not turn,
     which is a behaviour a port will otherwise get wrong in the
     pits and on the grid.

   o AND A REVERSAL IS INSTANT, NOT A COAST-THROUGH. Pressing right
     while the angle is still left snaps it to zero first, then
     ramps. So the digital steering has no momentum across centre,
     only within a side.

   o leanMode (+0x2e29) IS THE ANALOG/DIGITAL FLAG.
     structs/MR_Racer.txt 21.4 recorded it as set at 0x00466293 and
     cleared at 0x004662ec and 0x0046636f, all in this function,
     with "what distinguishes the two modes is not established".
     It is the steering path: 1 analog, 0 digital. CLOSED.

9.9.3.  The bike characteristics do the work

   Six already-decoded fields carry the whole model, which is what a
   data-driven handling model looks like from the code side:

   +------------------------------+----------+----------------------------------------+
   | g_bikeCarac field            | bpt      | Role in the steering model             |
   +==============================+==========+========================================+
   | RoueLimiteAngulaire          | 13       | the lock stop - steering is clamped to |
   |                              |          | plus or minus this                     |
   +------------------------------+----------+----------------------------------------+
   | RoueAccelerationAngulaire    | 9        | the rate the bars are pulled over,     |
   |                              |          | integrated into steerRate              |
   +------------------------------+----------+----------------------------------------+
   | RoueVitesseRetour            | 11       | the self-centring speed, and the FLOOR |
   |                              |          | on the scaled centring rate            |
   +------------------------------+----------+----------------------------------------+
   | RotationTransfert            | -        | the torque scale, before the speed     |
   |                              |          | fade                                   |
   +------------------------------+----------+----------------------------------------+
   | VitesseMaxBoite              | -        | divisor of the physics-side speed fade |
   +------------------------------+----------+----------------------------------------+
   | MotoType                     | -        | gates turnBrakeFlag; also clamps       |
   |                              |          | RoueAccelerationAngulaire at load time |
   +------------------------------+----------+----------------------------------------+

   o THIS IS TWO APPLIED TYPES PAYING OFF AT ONCE. MR_Racer's
     bikeIndex indexes MR_BikeCarac by a named field, and the
     decompiler prints g_bikeCarac[this->bikeIndex].RoueLimiteAngulaire
     without either type knowing about the other. The signature pass
     predicted this; it is what makes the function readable at all.

9.9.4.  Three more fields named

   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Offset     | Named here                                                     |
   +============+================================================================+
   | +0x00ac    | turnBrakeFlag. Written 1 or 0, and ONLY for a human on a bike  |
   |            | whose MotoType is 1. Cleared while braking and turning, or     |
   |            | with axis1 outside 0x4000..0xc000; forced back to 1 below      |
   |            | speed 2.0. What consumes it is not read yet. [open]            |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x00f4    | prevLongAccel. The two-tap low pass inside                     |
   |            | maybe_Racer_UpdateForkDive keeps its previous output here, and |
   |            | the function both reads and writes it. PER RACER. See 9.31.    |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x00f8    | steerReturnScale. Multiplied by RoueVitesseRetour to get the   |
   |            | centring rate, which is then floored at RoueVitesseRetour so   |
   |            | the scale can only ever speed centring up. Written by          |
   |            | maybe_Racer_UpdateForkDive @ 0x004667e0 [verified].            |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x010c    | steerRate. The steering angular rate, integrated at            |
   |            | RoueAccelerationAngulaire * dt while a turn is held and zeroed |
   |            | when neither button is down. It sits immediately below         |
   |            | steering at +0x110, which is what a rate/angle pair should     |
   |            | look like.                                                     |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+

9.9.5.  A SECOND speed fade, not the draw-side one

   The torque handed to the integrator is

       factor = humanPlayer
                  ? RotationTransfert * (1 - fade(speed))
                  : 0.004f
       if (wheelieAngle != 0) factor *= _DAT_004d1018;
       if (fxGate) grip = clamp(|FUN_0048d0f0()| * k + k2,
                                1.0, _DAT_004d10e0),
                   and a flat 1.6 while braking AND turning
       FUN_0040fd20(0, grip * factor * dt * K * steering, 0)

   o TWO FADES ON TWO DIFFERENT QUANTITIES. The draw-side one,
     racer-draw-pipeline.txt 7, divides by RealSpeedMax and scales
     the VISUAL angle - the forks stop at half top speed. This
     one is in the physics, divides by VitesseMaxBoite, and scales
     the TORQUE. They are not the same fade and must not be merged
     in the reconstruction. [verified - different functions,
     different divisors, different consumers]

   o THE AI STEERS WITH A CONSTANT. humanPlayer == 0 takes
     factor = 0.004f flat: no RotationTransfert, no speed fade, no
     wheelie term. Consistent with 9.4 - the AI is a separate
     integrator, not a synthetic player - and it means the AI's
     cornering does not vary with the bike it is riding.

   o A WHEELIE COSTS STEERING AUTHORITY, by one more multiply. That
     is the physics counterpart of replay.txt 2.1's observation that
     one of three recorded crashes had the stunt bit up.

   o THE VECTOR IS (0, y, 0) - PURE YAW. Which is what a steering
     torque must be, and what four sweeps of the pose failed to
     find because they were looking in the wrong place. 3.8.10.2's
     closing claim - "there is no separate scalar for it anywhere in
     the racer record" - was superseded by 3.8.18 and is doubly
     superseded now: there is a scalar, this function writes it, and
     the pose angle at racer+0x6b0 is downstream of it.

9.9.6.  Still unnamed inside the chain

       0x0045d110  1046 bytes   dt only
       0x00466660   382 bytes   buttons; opens on onGround
       0x00466940   309 bytes   reads humanPlayer and the brake bit
       0x00467930   499 bytes   opens on rearWheelContact minus its
                                previous value
       0x00466d70    -          buttons; runs only when the ground
                                contact flag is set
       0x0048ef30   DONE in 9.10 - maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain

9.10.  maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain - where the engine lives

   0x0048ef30, renamed from FUN_0048ef30. 4771 bytes, the largest
   thing the racer tick chain reaches. Both 9.8's drive fraction and
   the longitudinal half of the handling model end up here.

       __thiscall(inst, float dirX, float dirY, float dirZ,
                  int num, int den, float dt)

   num/den is maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive's fraction - (n, 0x8000) analog
   or (-1|0|1, 1) digital - and dirX/Y/Z its normalised direction.

9.10.1.  The receiver is model instance 0, and the map confirms
         eight fields

   Every call site does ADD ECX,0x24, so instance+N is racer+N+0x24.
   Reading the function against the racer map is therefore a check
   of that map by a completely different route, and it passes:

   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | instance     | racer          | Field                                        |
   +==============+================+==============================================+
   | +0x54        | +0x78          | bikeIndex - CONFIRMS                         |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x58        | +0x7c          | gear - CONFIRMS                              |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x60        | +0x84          | shiftState - CONFIRMS                        |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x88        | +0xac          | turnBrakeFlag - CONFIRMS, and it was named   |
   |              |                | from the other side an hour earlier          |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x8c        | +0xb0          | a brake-hold counter, run through __ftol.    |
   |              |                | NEW                                          |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x90        | +0xb4          | realSpeedMax - CONFIRMS                      |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x98        | +0xbc          | matOrientation, float[12] - CONFIRMS         |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
