   | +0xc8        | +0xec          | engineSpeed - CONFIRMS                       |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0xcc        | +0xf0          | gates the slip path. NEW                     |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0xe4        | +0x108         | a pointer. NEW                               |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0xe8        | +0x10c         | steerRate - CONFIRMS                         |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0xec        | +0x110         | steering - CONFIRMS                          |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x100       | +0x124         | speed - CONFIRMS                             |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x104       | +0x128         | speedPrev. NEW                               |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x108       | +0x12c         | gearboxRoadSpeed - CONFIRMS                  |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x10c       | +0x130         | velDir, float[3]. NEW                        |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | +0x118       | +0x13c         | driveDisengaged. NEW                         |
   +--------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+

   o EIGHT INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATIONS IN ONE FUNCTION. Every one of
     those was named from a racer-relative displacement in some
     other function; here they are reached through a different base
     register at a different offset and they all land on the same
     meanings. That is the strongest single check the racer map has
     had.

   o AND ONE OF THEM WAS NAMED THE SAME DAY. turnBrakeFlag came out
     of maybe_Racer_ApplySteering (9.9.4) with "what consumes it is
     not read yet". inst+0x88 is the consumer, in the coast branch
     of this function. It stays [open] only in the sense that what
     the flag MEANS is still an interpretation.

9.10.2.  The tyre-slip block

   Repeated six times through the function, gated on airTime
   (racer+0x0f0) being plus or minus zero - WHICH IS TO SAY, ON
   THE BIKE BEING ON THE GROUND. 9.18 identifies the field; the
   sign-bit-masked test is what makes -0.0f count as grounded
   too. So the tyre-slip model simply does not run in the air,
   which is why the six copies all sit behind it:

       d = normalize(velDir);   v = d * speed;
       v = v * inverse(matOrientation);          world -> body
       grip = (MotoType == 1) ? f(|lateral|)
                              : max(1.0f, MinAdherenceWheel);
       gearboxRoadSpeed = lerp(gearboxRoadSpeed, v.z, grip);

   o THE DRIVETRAIN DOES NOT READ speed DIRECTLY. It reads the
     LONGITUDINAL COMPONENT of the velocity in body space and
     follows it at a grip-dependent rate. The difference between
     the two is wheelspin, and this lerp is where it lives.

   o MotoType 1 GETS A DIFFERENT GRIP CURVE from every other bike -
     a ramp between two thresholds rather than the
     flat max(1.0, MinAdherenceWheel). That is the third place
     MotoType 1 is special-cased, after turnBrakeFlag (9.9.4) and
     driveDisengaged below, and
     formats/bpt-carac.txt already records it clamping
     RoueAccelerationAngulaire at load time. Whatever MotoType 1
     is, it has its own handling model.

   o THE MotoType 1 GRIP CURVE, with its constants read [verified]:

         grip = 1.0                       |lat| <= 0.01
         grip = 1.0 - 0.05 * |lat|        0.01 < |lat| < 20.0
         grip = 1.0                       |lat| >= 20.0

     0x004d1d48 = 0.01, 0x004d1d50 = 20.0, 0x004d1d58 = -0.05 and
     0x004d1c00 = 1.0, all doubles. Grip falls to zero at a lateral
     magnitude of 20 - and then SNAPS BACK to 1.0 above it, because
     the ramp is bounded by an AND rather than clamped. Whether
     that discontinuity is reachable in play is open.

9.10.3.  The engine

       demand = (num/den) * MoteurVitesseMax
       demand < engineSpeed, or num == 0  ->  decay engineSpeed
       otherwise ->
           torque = FUN_0048ee30(engineSpeed)
                    * RegimeMax / MoteurVitesseMax * K
           a      = torque / (gearboxRoadSpeed, or a constant
                              below a speed threshold)
           gearboxRoadSpeed += clamp(a * dt / MotoMasse, ...)

   o MoteurVitesseMax IS THE REDLINE the throttle fraction scales
     against, and MotoMasse divides the torque - so a heavier bike
     accelerates more slowly, from data, exactly as the .bpt field
     names promise.

   o FUN_0048ee30 IS A TORQUE CURVE keyed on engine speed. It is
     one small unread function standing between this project and
     the complete engine map. That is the next thing worth reading
     in this subsystem.

   Braking, when num < 0 or g_bPreRace: Frein clamped to [10, 140],
   scaled down while the brake-hold counter at racer+0xb0 is small,
   and integrated to pull gearboxRoadSpeed toward zero from either
   sign. Drag: speed -= speed*speed*dt*K / MotoMasse, floored at 0.

9.10.4.  dirY is a slope term - which answers 9.8.2

   9.8.2 recorded the direction handed in from maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive
   as matOrientation[9..11] + matOrientation[6..8], normalised, and
   had to leave what it MEANS open because there was no consumer to
   point at. There is now, and it is a single use:

       t = -dirY * dt * K,  clamped at 0.55
       v.z += t                        the body-space longitudinal

   o DOWNHILL ACCELERATES, UPHILL RETARDS. That is gravity resolved
     along the direction, which only makes sense if the direction is
     the bike's FORWARD VECTOR IN WORLD SPACE. Still an inference -
     nothing names it - but an inference with a consumer behind it
     rather than a shape argument. [inferred]

   o AND IT EXPLAINS WHY THE SUM IS NOT NORMALISED WHEN THE GHOST
     RECORDS IT. maybe_Ghost_RecordFrame stores the raw sum at
     record +0x14 (9.5.1); normalisation happens per-use here.

9.10.5.  Still unnamed inside the chain

       0x0045d110  1046 bytes   dt only
       0x00466660   382 bytes   buttons; opens on onGround
       0x00466940   309 bytes   reads humanPlayer and the brake bit
       0x00467930   499 bytes   opens on rearWheelContact minus its
                                previous value
       0x00466d70    -          buttons; runs only when the ground
                                contact flag is set
       0x0048ee30   DONE in 9.11 - Engine_TorqueAt
       0x0048cbe0   DONE in 9.14 - and it is NOT the
                    gearboxRoadSpeed writer
       0x0048d0f0    -          returns the magnitude the grip curve
                                and the fxGate test both read

9.11.  Engine_TorqueAt - the whole engine map in twenty lines

   0x0048ee30, renamed from FUN_0048ee30. 9.10.3 called this "the
   one small unread function standing between this project and the
   complete engine map". It is read now.

       rpm = (RegimeMax / MoteurVitesseMax) * engineSpeed;

       if (rpm >= RegimeCoupleMax) {              UPPER SEGMENT
           m = (CoupleRegimeMax - CoupleMax)
               / (RegimeMax - RegimeCoupleMax);
           b = CoupleMax - RegimeCoupleMax * m;
       } else {                                   LOWER SEGMENT
           m = (CoupleMax - Couple1000)
               / (RegimeCoupleMax - 1000.0f);
           b = Couple1000 + m * -1000.0;
       }
       t = (m * rpm + b) * 9.81;
       if (t < 0.2) t = 0.2;
       if (g_codeCheckPassed && racer+0x88 == 0) t *= 1.1;
       return wheelieScale * t;

   o A TWO-SEGMENT PIECEWISE-LINEAR CURVE through three points -
     (1000, Couple1000), (RegimeCoupleMax, CoupleMax) and
     (RegimeMax, CoupleRegimeMax). Peak torque is CoupleMax at
     RegimeCoupleMax BY CONSTRUCTION, so the .bpt field names
     describe the geometry exactly. "Couple" is torque and "Regime"
     is rpm; formats/bpt-carac.txt had the names and this is what
     they are for.

   o engineSpeed IS NOT IN rpm. The first line converts it, by
     RegimeMax/MoteurVitesseMax, which also pins those two fields
     as the two ends of the same scale.

9.11.1.  The constants are doubles, and one of them is gravity

   Read out of .rdata rather than inferred [verified]:

   The 5 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/physics.c.

   o 9.81 MEANS THE .bpt STORES TORQUE IN kgf.m. That is a unit
     choice, not a fudge factor, and it is the kind of thing that
     silently ruins a reconstruction if it is copied as "some
     scale". 1000.0 being the lower anchor is what makes the field
     name Couple1000 literal.

   o A 0.2 FLOOR MEANS THE ENGINE NEVER PRODUCES ZERO TORQUE, so a
     bike always creeps forward with the throttle open, whatever
     the curve says.

9.11.2.  wheelieScale multiplies the torque - the loop closes

   The return value is wheelieScale * t. That is what racer+0xb8 is
   FOR, and it was named from its writer alone.

   o A WHEELIE DOUBLES ENGINE TORQUE on a stock copy - the
     multiplier is 2.0 when g_codeCheckPassed is set and 1.2
     when it is not. So the wheelie is not decoration: it is
     the game's boost mechanic.

     CORRECTED - THIS SAID 1.6 AND THAT WAS WRONG. The
     immediate at 0x004661e8 is 0x3f99999a, which is 1.2; 1.6
     would be 0x3fcccccd. It survived because "60 per cent
     more torque" is a plausible-sounding number that nothing
     downstream contradicted. See 9.23.

   o AND THE WHOLE GAMEPLAY LOOP IS NOW READABLE END TO END:

       maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie   ramps the angle, caps the
                                   duration at ~200, sets the scale
       maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive      forces the throttle bit on while
                                   the angle is up
       maybe_Racer_ApplySteering   multiplies steering authority
                                   DOWN while the angle is up
       Engine_TorqueAt             multiplies torque UP by the same
                                   scale

     Pull a wheelie for 60 per cent more drive, at the cost of
     steering and stability, for at most about two seconds. Four
     functions, found in four different reads, describing one
     mechanic. [verified]

   o IT ALSO EXPLAINS THE USER'S CARAC CHEAT. tools/mr_cheats.py
     raises RegimeCoupleMax, CoupleMax and CoupleRegimeMax among
     others - which is to say it reshapes exactly the three points
     this curve interpolates.

9.11.3.  A FIFTH protection layer, and a second silent penalty

   g_codeCheckPassed (0x004dc1f8) sits FOUR BYTES after
   g_protectionPassed (0x004dc1f4), and it is a different check.
   FUN_0045d110 sets it by reading the game's OWN CODE:

       0045d187  MOV ECX,[0x004dc094]
       0045d18d  CMP ECX,0x2
       0045d190  JNZ 0045d270              only in mode 2
       0045d196  MOV EAX,[0x00509ba8]
       0045d19b  CMP EAX,0x3
       0045d19e  JL  0045d270              and only past state 3
       0045d1a4  MOV [0x004dc1f8],0x0      assume FAILED
       0045d1ae  MOV AL,[0x00442761]       <- READS ITS OWN .text
       0045d1b3  CMP AL,0x75
       0045d1b5  JNZ 0045d1cf
       0045d1b7  MOV AL,[0x00442762]
       0045d1bc  CMP AL,0x22
       0045d1be  JNZ 0045d1cf
       0045d1c0  MOV [0x004dc1f8],0x1      bytes intact -> PASSED

   o IT IS A CODE-INTEGRITY CHECK ON THE DISC-CHECK ROUTINE.
     0x00442761 is inside the same function as the patched site at
     0x00442785, so a cracker working on the disc check is likely
     to disturb it. Fail it and every bike quietly loses the 1.1x
     torque here and drops from 2.0 to 1.6 on the wheelie.

   o OUR COPY PASSES. The bytes at 0x00442761 read 75 22
     [verified], and they are not among the seven patched sites in
     binary-modifications.txt. So the penalty never fires here -
     but a reconstruction that omits the mechanism is not
     reproducing the binary, and one that gets the SENSE backwards
     would nerf every bike.

   o THE SHAPE IS THE SAME AS THE GEARBOX PENALTY.
     copy-protection.txt 5 records g_protectionPassed subtracting
     1.0 from NbSpeed. This is its neighbour in memory and its
     sibling in design: no message, no refusal, just a slower bike.
     Anything measured against a cracked binary has to account for
     BOTH.

9.11.4.  Still unnamed inside the chain

       0x0045d110  1046 bytes   dt only - and it is the writer of
                                g_codeCheckPassed, so it is doing
                                more than physics
       0x00466660   382 bytes   buttons; opens on onGround
       0x00466940   309 bytes   reads humanPlayer and the brake bit
       0x00467930   499 bytes   opens on rearWheelContact minus its
                                previous value
       0x00466d70   DONE in 9.12 - maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx
       0x0048cbe0   DONE in 9.14 - and it is NOT the
                    gearboxRoadSpeed writer
       0x0048d0f0    -          returns the magnitude the grip curve
                                and the fxGate test both read

9.12.  maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx - skid marks, trail and spray

   0x00466d70, renamed from FUN_00466d70. The last of 9.7.6's list
   read here, and the one that turned out to have
   nothing to do with physics.

   It opens with a COPY-PROTECTION CHECK that has nothing to do with
   FX either - copy-protection.txt 5.2, check B, the eight-entry byte
   table against a library routine. Unlike the other two live sites
   it draws ILLEGAL VERSION and falls straight through with NO field
   payload. Everything below is what the function is actually for.

9.12.1.  FX exist only near the camera

   Nothing is emitted unless this racer's segmentStart lies in

       [ viewed - 10, viewed + 30 ]

   wrapped through Trk_WrapSegmentIndex against the segment of
   g_viewedRacerIndex.

   o THE FORWARD EXTENT IS 30 SEGMENTS, which is the same number
     the proximity flag vanishes at - MR_Racer.txt 23.3 has
     drawnThisFrame going to 0 "between a gap of 30 and one of
     46". Two independent range limits keyed to the viewed racer's
     segment, and this one is a literal in the code rather than a
     measurement. Whether they are the same list is not established,
     but 30 turning up twice is worth recording.

9.12.2.  Three effects, three emitters

       1  THE TRAIL. onGround and FUN_0040ec60 returning a positive
          surface id -> normalise
          rearWheelContact - rearWheelContactPrev and pass it, the
          position, two packed greys - Color_Pack16(9,9,9) and
          (8,8,8) - speed * 0.55 and the surface id to FUN_004576a0.

       2  THE TWO SKID RIBBONS. On a RISING EDGE of the brake bit or
          of fxGate, two slots come from maybe_Fx_AllocSlot into
          fxSlot0 and fxSlot1. Each frame the vector
          (+-skidWidth, 0, 0) is transformed through matOrientation
          and added to rearWheelContact and frontWheelContact, giving
          FUN_00457390 one quad per wheel.

       3  THE SPRAY. Gated on (a wheelie with wheelieTimer < 25.0) OR
          the brake OR fxGate-with-lateral, and onGround.

   o THE SKID WIDTH TRACKS THE SLIDE. It grows by a constant while
     |lateral| exceeds skidWidthPrev and shrinks by 1.0 otherwise,
     clamped to 1.0..12.0. So a mark that starts narrow and fattens
     as the bike steps further out - and the whole update is skipped
     for an AI, whose width is a flat 5.0.

   o PARTICLE COUNT IS A STRAIGHT LOD CUT:

         humanPlayer ? 24 : 4

     computed with the branchless
     (-(humanPlayer == 0) & 0xffffffec) + 0x18. Six times the spray
     for the player as for any AI.

   o AND THE SPREAD FLIPS WITH THE STEERING. buttons & 4 - steer
     left - negates the random lateral offset, so the spray is
     thrown to the correct side of the bike rather than symmetrically.

9.12.3.  A decompiler trap: the "seeds" are not arguments

   The particle loop appeared to call a random function with distinct
   seeds - 0x169e, 0x169f, 0x16a2, 0x16a6 - which reads like four
   separate noise streams and is a tempting thing to write down.

   IT IS AN ARTEFACT. 0x00458cd0 is a TEN-BYTE THUNK and takes no
   arguments at all; the constants were phantom parameters Ghidra
   invented for an undeclared prototype. Renamed maybe_Rand and given
   int __cdecl (void), after which they vanish.

   o WHAT IT THUNKS TO DOES TAKE AN ARGUMENT, and that is the part
     worth keeping. The body is Rand_Next(1) - the per-stream
     Park-Miller generator formats/bpt.txt records at 0x004b1160 -
     so maybe_Rand is STREAM 1, fixed. The audio mixer draws from
     STREAM 4 instead. See audio-cues.txt 9.6 for why separate
     streams matter to replay determinism.

   o WHICH IS THE SAME CLASS OF MISTAKE AS 9.7.2's extraout_EAX, in
     the opposite direction. There a missing return type merged one
     value into two; here a missing parameter list split zero values
     into four. Both look like findings until the prototype is set.

9.12.4.  Fields

   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Offset     | Named here                                                     |
   +============+================================================================+
   | +0x0184    | skidWidthPrev. The previous frame's |lateral|, kept only to    |
   |            | decide whether the skid is widening.                           |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x0188    | skidWidth. Clamped to 1.0..12.0, and PINNED AT 5.0 for an AI,  |
   |            | which never runs the update.                                   |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x2dd0    | slotIndex. The note said "referenced by App_Init, read by the  |
   |            | net [open]". The test this->slotIndex == g_viewedRacerIndex    |
   |            | only makes sense if it holds this racer's own index.           |
   |            | [inferred]                                                     |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x0180    | still open, but now has a second reader - maybe_Racer_Tick     |
   |            | clears +0x000c and +0x0010 when it is plus or minus zero.      |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------------------------+

9.12.5.  Still unnamed inside the chain

   9.7.6's list is now empty except for the FX back ends, which are
   a different subsystem and are the natural next thread:

       0x00457390   DONE in 9.16 - maybe_Fx_UpdateRibbon
       0x004576a0   DONE in 9.16 - maybe_Fx_SpawnTrail
       0x00457620   DONE in 9.16 - maybe_Fx_SpawnParticle
       0x0040ec60   157 bytes   returns a surface id from a segment
                                and a position
       0x0048cbe0   DONE in 9.14 - and it is NOT the
                    gearboxRoadSpeed writer
       0x0048d0f0   DONE in 9.13 - maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed

9.13.  maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed - the number the slide model turns on

   0x0048d0f0, renamed from FUN_0048d0f0. Six callers, more than
   anything else the racer tick chain reaches, and 9.12.5 flagged it
   as the last thing worth reading here.

   __fastcall on MODEL INSTANCE 0, returning a float on the x87
   stack.

9.13.1.  What it computes, from the disassembly

   THE DECOMPILER LOSES THE TAIL. It reuses one local for both a
   matrix element and a vector component and emits an expression that
   cannot be read as arithmetic. What the instructions say:

       d = normalize(inst->velDir);        inst+0x10c = racer+0x130
       v = d * inst->speed;                inst+0x100 = racer+0x124
       M = inverse(inst->matOrientation);  inst+0x98  = racer+0xbc
       return dot(column0(M), v) + M.translation[0];

   The five instructions that settle it [verified]:

       0048d286  CALL Matrix34_Copy        stores M at [ESP+0x14]
       0048d28b  FLD  [ESP+0x2c]  FMUL [ESP+0x8]     M+0x18 * v0
       0048d293  FLD  [ESP+0x20]  FMUL [ESP+0xc]     M+0x0c * v1
       0048d29b  FLD  [ESP+0x14]  FMUL [ESP+0x10]    M+0x00 * v2
       0048d2a9  FADD [ESP+0x38]                     M+0x24

   M+0x00, +0x0c, +0x18 and +0x24 is a stride of 0x0c - ONE COLUMN of
   a 4x3 matrix, plus the matching translation component. So the
   return value is ONE COMPONENT OF THE VELOCITY EXPRESSED IN THE
   BIKE'S OWN FRAME.

   o WHICH COMPONENT IS NOT SETTLED, and is not being guessed. 9.8.2
     left the matrix row/column convention open and nothing since has
     closed it. Calling it "the lateral velocity" would be an
     assertion this project has not earned - see the front-end pitch
     at racer+0x2e39, which was called steering for a while on
     exactly this kind of reasoning.

   o WHAT IS SETTLED is that EVERY consumer takes the absolute value
     and treats it as a slip magnitude. The name records the role,
     not the axis.

9.13.2.  Everything in the slide model reads this one value

   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | Caller                         | What it does with |slip|                   |
   +================================+============================================+
   | maybe_Racer_Tick               | below a threshold it clears fxGate, which  |
   |                                | gates the whole FX and grip path           |
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_ApplySteering      | scales the steering torque while fxGate is |
   |                                | set, and a flat 1.6 while braking and      |
   |                                | turning                                    |
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx        | drives skidWidth up or down, and gates the |
   |                                | spray                                      |
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain   | the MotoType 1 grip curve's only input:    |
   |                                | grip = 1.0 - 0.05 * |slip| between 0.01    |
   |                                | and 20.0                                   |
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering     | squared and divided by the turn radius -   |
   |                                | the centripetal term                       |
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | FUN_00406290                   | unread                                     |
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+

   o SO GRIP, STEERING AUTHORITY, SKID WIDTH, SPRAY AND THE
     CENTRIPETAL TERM ARE ALL FUNCTIONS OF ONE SCALAR. That is worth
     knowing before reconstructing any of them separately: they are
     not five independent models, they are five curves over the same
     input.

   o AND IT IS RECOMPUTED FROM SCRATCH AT EVERY CALL SITE. Nothing
     caches it in the racer, so a frame that touches all five paths
     builds the inverse matrix five times. A reconstruction is free
     to compute it once per tick - the value cannot change between
     calls within a frame - but should say so rather than doing it
     silently.

9.13.3.  The inverse-matrix block is duplicated, not shared

   maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain builds the same inverse inline SIX
   times (9.10.2), and this function is the factored version of the
   same arithmetic. They agree.

   o WHICH IS A USEFUL CROSS-CHECK RATHER THAN A COMPLAINT. Two
     independently written copies of one computation, reached from
     different call sites, reading the same offsets, is about as
     strong as static confirmation of a field's meaning gets -
     velDir, speed and matOrientation are all re-confirmed here.

9.13.4.  What is left

   The racer tick chain is now read end to end. What remains is a
   different subsystem - the FX back ends 9.12.5 lists - plus:

       0x0048cbe0    DONE in 9.14 - and it is NOT the
                     gearboxRoadSpeed writer
       0x00406290    the sixth caller of maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed, and
                     the only one outside the tick chain
       0x0045cd10    DONE in 9.15 - maybe_Math_TurnRadius3Pt
       0x0040ec60    the surface id lookup

9.14.  maybe_Racer_SetLongSpeed - the write side, and a correction

   0x0048cbe0, renamed from FUN_0048cbe0. 9.10.5, 9.11.4 and 9.12.5
   all listed this as "the write path for gearboxRoadSpeed".

   THAT WAS WRONG. It does not touch gearboxRoadSpeed at all. It
   writes speed and velDir, and it is the exact inverse of
   maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed. The mistake came from where it is CALLED -
   maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain invokes it in the same statement
   that updates gearboxRoadSpeed - rather than from reading it, and
   it survived three passes because each pass copied the previous
   list forward.

9.14.1.  What it does

       v = clamp(v, -realSpeedMax, +realSpeedMax);

       b = (normalize(velDir) * speed) * inverse(matOrientation);

       world = row0*b[0] + row1*b[1] + row2*v + translation;

       speed  = |world|;
       velDir = (|world| > 1e-5) ? world/|world| : (0,0,0);

   o IT REPLACES ONE BODY-SPACE COMPONENT AND KEEPS THE OTHER TWO.
     b[0] and b[1] survive; only the component paired with ROW 2 is
     overwritten. That is a "set the longitudinal speed" primitive,
     not a velocity setter, and it is why seven different callers -
     the drivetrain, ground contact, the crash, the gearbox, racer
     collision and two unread functions - can all shove the bike
     forwards or backwards without disturbing its slide.

   o speed IS DERIVED. It is recomputed as |world| on every call, so
     a reconstruction that stores speed and velDir independently has
     to re-derive it here or the two drift apart.

   o BELOW 1e-5 velDir IS ZEROED rather than left stale. A stopped
     bike has velDir == (0,0,0), which is what every normalize()
     guard in this subsystem is protecting against.

9.14.2.  It narrows 9.13.1's open axis question

   9.13.1 could only say that maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed returns "one
   component of the velocity in the bike's own frame" and refused to
   name which. This pins it down by elimination [verified]:

       SlipSpeed dots COLUMN 0 of the inverse matrix. For a rotation
       the inverse is the transpose, so column 0 of the inverse is
       ROW 0 of the forward matrix - it reads b[0].

       SetLongSpeed overwrites the component paired with ROW 2 with
       a value clamped to +-realSpeedMax. A quantity bounded by the
       bike's top speed is a forward speed - it writes b[2].

   o SO THEY TOUCH DIFFERENT AXES, and SlipSpeed is NOT the
     longitudinal component. "One of three" becomes "one of two".
     Whether b[0] is lateral or vertical still is not settled, but
     lateral is now the only reading consistent with every consumer
     treating |b[0]| as a slide magnitude.

   o AND THE READ AND WRITE SIDES AGREE. maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain
     lerps gearboxRoadSpeed toward the THIRD component of the same
     transform (9.10.2) - the same b[2] this function writes back.
     Two independently written paths, one axis convention.

9.14.3.  A to-do label is still an assertion

   The "write path for gearboxRoadSpeed" label was invented from a
   call site and copied unread through three sections. Rule 4 applies
   to placeholders: an entry that has not been read should say so
   rather than describe what it probably does.

9.15.  maybe_Math_TurnRadius3Pt - the fit behind turnRadius

   0x0045cd10, renamed from FUN_0045cd10. A pure geometry helper -
   no this, eight floats in - and the producer of racer+0x18.

       maybe_Math_TurnRadius3Pt(ax, az, bx, bz, cx, cz,
                                float *pCentreX, float *pCentreZ)

   THREE PLAN-VIEW POINTS. X and Z only; Y is never read, so a jump
   does not disturb the fit. The centre out-params exist and THE
   ONLY CALLER PASSES NULL FOR BOTH, so the circle centre has never
   been used by anything.

9.15.1.  The algorithm

       1  degenerate check, epsilon 1e-4: b==c or a==b -> write 0 to
          the out-params, return FLT_MAX
       2  chord lengths d1 = |b-a|, d2 = |c-b|
       3  CLAMP EACH CHORD TO [128.0, 800.0]
       4  rebuild two points either side of b, at half the clamped
          chord length along each original chord direction
       5  intersect the two perpendicular bisectors -> the centre
       6  r = |b - centre|, NEGATED when the 2D cross product of the
          rebuilt chords is negative
       7  return r * 2.0
       8  bisectors parallel - a straight line -> 0 and FLT_MAX

9.15.2.  The chord clamp is frame-rate independence in geometry

   Rescaling both chords into [128, 800] before fitting makes the
   result depend on the SHAPE of the three points and not on how far
   the bike travelled between them.

   o SO THE RADIUS DOES NOT CHANGE WITH FRAMERATE, and it is done
     without dividing by dt anywhere. A port that drops the clamp
     because it looks like a sanity check gets a cornering force
     that varies with frame time - which is exactly the class of bug
     that is invisible on the machine it was written on.

   o AND IT BOUNDS THE INPUT, NOT THE OUTPUT. The radius itself is
     unclamped; only the chords are. Three points 2 units apart and
     three points 2000 apart with the same curvature give the same
     answer.

9.15.3.  FLT_MAX means straight, confirmed from the producer

   Both degenerate exits return FLT_MAX, and maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering
   initialises racer+0x18 to FLT_MAX and only overwrites it when this
   function returns.

   o WHICH CLOSES THE clipMin CORRECTION FROM THE OTHER END. The
     field was called clipMin on the strength of two FLT_MAX values
     seen in a dump, before anything that wrote it had been read.
     Now both the writer and the value's meaning are known: FLT_MAX
     is the straight-line case, not a sentinel for "unset".

9.15.4.  What is not settled

   o WHETHER IT RETURNS A RADIUS OR A DIAMETER. Step 4 halves and
     step 7 doubles, so the fit runs at half scale; the maths alone
     does not say which quantity comes out. It is called a radius
     because maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering computes v*v divided by it
     as a centripetal acceleration, which only works for a radius.
     [inferred - hence the maybe_ on the name]

   o THE CENTRE IS COMPUTED AND THROWN AWAY. Both out-params are
     NULL at the one call site, so either there was a second caller
     once, or the centre was for debugging. Nothing else references
     the function. [open]

9.16.  The FX back ends - two pools and one appender

   9.12.5 listed three FX functions as the next thread. All three are
   thin dispatchers: they compute a this pointer and forward every
   argument. The work is one level further down.

   +------------------+----------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | Pool             | Address        | Shape                                      |
   +==================+================+============================================+
   | g_fxRibbons      | 0x00542a28     | 16 slots x 5640 bytes. Indexed -           |
   |                  |                | maybe_Fx_UpdateRibbon bounds-checks 0..15  |
   |                  |                | and computes the pointer. These are what   |
   |                  |                | maybe_Fx_AllocSlot hands out.              |
   +------------------+----------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | g_fxParticles    | 0x0052c498     | 128 slots x 260 bytes. Pooled -            |
   |                  |                | maybe_Fx_SpawnTrail and                    |
   |                  |                | maybe_Fx_SpawnParticle both linear-scan    |
   |                  |                | for the first slot whose +0xfc is 0.       |
   +------------------+----------------+--------------------------------------------+

   o THE TWO POOLS ARE ALLOCATED DIFFERENTLY ON PURPOSE. A ribbon is
     owned - a racer holds fxSlot0 and fxSlot1 across frames and
     keeps appending to them - so it is indexed. A particle is
     fire-and-forget, so it is scanned for. That is why
     maybe_Fx_AllocSlot exists for one and not the other.

   o AND THE PARTICLE SCAN IS UNBOUNDED IN COST. Both spawners walk
     up to 128 records looking for a free one and SILENTLY DO
     NOTHING if all are busy. With 24 particles per player frame
     (9.12.2) the pool can fill, and the failure mode is missing
     spray rather than an error.

9.16.1.  maybe_Fx_AppendRibbonQuad, and the ribbon record

   0x00457200, renamed from FUN_00457200, is the only one of the
   three back ends that is game code this pass could read end to end.
   The record it writes accounts for all 5640 bytes - 5640 is exactly
   0x1608:

   +--------------+----------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | Offset       | Size           | Field                                          |
   +==============+================+================================================+
   | +0x0000      | 4096 bytes     | NOT touched by the appender. 4096 / 128 = 32   |
   |              |                | bytes per vertex, which would fit a parallel   |
   |              |                | transformed or coloured array, but nothing     |
   |              |                | read so far uses it. [open]                    |
   +--------------+----------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x1000      | 128 x 12       | the vertex array, xyz floats                   |
   +--------------+----------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x1600      | int            | QUAD COUNT, capped at 0x3e = 62                |
   +--------------+----------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | +0x1604      | int            | vertex write index                             |
   +--------------+----------------+------------------------------------------------+

       if (quadCount >= 62 || g_nTrkSegmentCount == 0) return;
       seg  = Trk_FindSegmentForPoint(segHint, &a, 0);
       a.y  = FUN_0040c8b0(seg, &a) + 2.0f;
       seg  = Trk_FindSegmentForPoint(segHint, &b, 0);
       b.y  = FUN_0040c8b0(seg, &b) + 2.0f;
       vertex[writeIndex++] = a;
       vertex[writeIndex++] = b;
       if (writeIndex > 2) quadCount += 2;

   o BOTH INCOMING Y VALUES ARE THROWN AWAY. maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx
     passes the wheel contact points plus a lateral offset; this
     replaces each Y with the TRACK HEIGHT there plus 2.0. So the
     skid mark lies on the ground whatever the bike was doing, and
     the 2.0 is a z-fight lift rather than a physical offset.
     _DAT_004d0dcc = 2.0f [verified].

   o 62 QUADS AND 128 VERTICES AGREE. Two vertices per call, and the
     count only advances once there is a previous pair to build a
     quad against, so 62 quads consume 124 of the 128 slots.

   o A RIBBON IS A FIXED-LENGTH STRIP THAT SIMPLY STOPS. There is no
     wrap and no oldest-vertex eviction: at 62 quads the function
     returns having done nothing. So a long slide draws a mark of
     bounded length and then stops extending, which is a behaviour a
     port has to reproduce rather than a limit to raise.

9.16.2.  FUN_0040c8b0 returns a ground height, and its first
         argument's type does not add up

   Nine callers, and this pass found two that disagree:

       maybe_Fx_AppendRibbonQuad  passes Trk_FindSegmentForPoint's
                                  RETURN VALUE
       maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering passes racer->segmentStart

   racer->segmentStart is definitely an INDEX - maybe_Race_TickClock
   compares it against g_nTrkSegmentCount and maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx
   wraps it through Trk_WrapSegmentIndex. But the function's first
   instruction is MOV EAX,[ECX+4], which suits a POINTER.

   o SETTLED, AND THERE WAS NO CONTRADICTION. It is
     Trk_GroundHeightAt, __thiscall on the TRACK OBJECT: ECX is the
     track, and both stack arguments are exactly what the callers
     pass. The [ECX+4] that looked like a pointer dereference is the
     track's SEGMENT COUNT. racer->segmentStart is an index and so
     is Trk_FindSegmentForPoint's return. See formats/trk.txt 2.4.

     A HIDDEN this SHIFTS A DECOMPILED ARGUMENT LIST BY ONE, and an
     argument list that does not add up is more often a missing
     prototype than a real inconsistency. That is the same shape as
     9.7.2 and 9.12.3.

   o IT IS WORTH SETTLING BECAUSE NINE CALLERS DEPEND ON IT, and
     because "Trk_FindSegmentForPoint returns a pointer" would change
     how several already-written notes read.

9.16.3.  maybe_Fx_InitParticle, and the particle record

   0x004afe90, renamed from FUN_004afe90. The constructor
   maybe_Fx_SpawnParticle hands its found slot to. 260 bytes of
   record, and this writes about a third of them.

   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | Dword index      | Byte         | Field                                        |
   +==================+==============+==============================================+
   | [0x00]           | +0x000       | a KIND TAG, set to 5                         |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x01..0x06]     | +0x004       | position then velocity, from the caller      |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x07..0x09]     | +0x01c       | zeroed                                       |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x0a][0x0b][0x0 | +0x028       | the caller's three colour or fade arguments  |
   | c]               |              |                                              |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x0e]           | +0x038       | lifetime, as an int                          |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x0f]           | +0x03c       | the SAME lifetime as a float                 |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x30..0x3b]     | +0x0c0       | the four billboard corners, +-2 * size, Z =  |
   |                  |              | 0                                            |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x3c]           | +0x0f0       | size                                         |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [0x3f]           | +0x0fc       | THE IN-USE FLAG, set to 1                    |
   +------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------------+

   o [0x3f] IS AT BYTE OFFSET 0xfc - exactly the field both spawners
     scan for zero when hunting a free slot (9.16). So this is where
     a slot is marked busy, and the pool reading is now confirmed
     from the allocator's other end rather than inferred from the
     scan alone.

   o THE LIFETIME IS STORED TWICE, as an int at [0x0e] and as a
     float at [0x0f], from the same argument. A countdown and its
     original, or an int for logic and a float for fading - not
     settled. [open]

   o THE RECORD CARRIES ITS OWN BILLBOARD CORNERS at +-2 * size
     rather than generating them at draw time, which is why the
     record is 260 bytes for what is conceptually a point and a
     velocity.

9.16.4.  The billboard is double-sided, and that is deliberate

   It builds the quad TWICE through FUN_00422d20, with vertex orders
   0,1,2,3 and then 3,2,1,0, both with UVs

       (225,0) (255,0) (255,31) (225,31)

   o REVERSED WINDING MEANS FRONT AND BACK. The spray is visible
     whichever side the camera passes it, and a port that relies on
     backface culling alone loses half of every particle. This is
     the kind of decision that is invisible until it is wrong.

   o THE UV PATCH IS 30 x 31 TEXELS at u 225..255, v 0..31 - the
     top-right corner of a 256-wide atlas.

   o BOTH PRIMITIVES TAKE Color_Pack16(0x10,0x10,0x10) through
     Prim_SetColorIndex before FUN_004232e0 commits them.

   o THE PRIMITIVE API IS THE NEXT LAYER AND IS UNREAD:
     FUN_00422d20 with 5 callers, FUN_00423060 with 14, FUN_004232e0
     with 9. 0x44 is the first argument to FUN_00422d20 in both
     calls here and is presumably a primitive type or a texture id.
     [open]

   o AND THAT IS WHERE THE RACER TICK CHAIN FINALLY LEAVES ITS OWN
     SUBSYSTEM. Everything from maybe_Game_Tick down has now been
     read; below this point the calls are the renderer's, which is
     the replace-list and a different kind of work - CLAUDE.md on
     the spine.

9.18.  THE AIR TIMER - racer+0x0f0 identified, and a branch
       9.17.8.3 called unreachable

   9.17.4 found two writers for racer+0x0f0 and reported that BOTH
   STORE ZERO. 9.17.8.3 then observed that the 1.6 drag coefficient
   depends on that field and concluded the branch "may be
   unreachable". Both observations were true and both conclusions
   were wrong, and one run on a dirt bike shows why.

9.18.1.  What it actually is

   maybe_Racer_UpdateAirborne (0x00466660, was FUN_00466660) opens
   with:

       00466665  MOV EAX,[ECX+0x2db8]      onGround
       00466670  JNZ 0046668d
       00466672  FLD  [EBX+0xf0]           AIRBORNE: accumulate
       00466678  FADD [0x004d0f20]         + 2.592
       00466682  FSTP [EBX+0xf0]
       00466688  JMP  00466735
       0046668d  MOV  [EBX+0xf0],0         GROUNDED: reset

   THE TWO WRITERS ARE THE TWO ARMS OF ONE IF, and the field is an
   AIR TIMER: it accumulates while the bike is off the ground and is
   reset the instant it lands. Renamed airTime. [verified]

9.18.2.  How 9.17.4 came to the opposite conclusion

   The capture was on tarmac and the bike never left the ground, so
   the accumulate arm never ran and the field genuinely was always
   zero - for that track. 9.17.4 said "not seen on this track", which
   was honest; 9.17.8.3 then cited it as though it were stronger and
   wrote "may be unreachable" about live code.

   A NEGATIVE OBSERVATION DOES NOT GET STRONGER BY BEING CITED.

9.18.3.  The ladder, verified

   A value_ne 00 watch on the low byte of airTime caught eight
   consecutive hits. The step is a constant, so the whole sequence
   is predictable in advance and every entry is a check on the
   model [verified]:

   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | n     | airTime     | float bits   | predicted   | captured   |
   +=======+=============+==============+=============+============+
   | 1     | 2.592       | 4025e354     | 54          | 54         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | 2     | 5.184       | 40a5e354     | 54          | 54         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | 3     | 7.776       | 40f8d4fe     | fe          | fe         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | 4     | 10.368      | 4125e354     | 54          | 54         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | 5     | 12.960      | 414f5c29     | 29          | 29         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | 6     | 15.552      | 4178d4fe     | fe          | fe         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | 7     | 18.144      | 419126e9     | e9          | e9         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+
   | 8     | 20.736      | 41a5e354     | 54          | 53         |
   +-------+-------------+--------------+-------------+------------+

   o EIGHT CONSECUTIVE AIRBORNE FRAMES, n = 1 to 8. About 264 ms of
     air - one jump.

   o n=8 CAME BACK 0x53 RATHER THAN 0x54 because the game
     accumulates by repeated FADD into a float rather than
     multiplying, so rounding drifts one ULP low by the eighth
     addition. Predicting the exact bits and then seeing them is
     what makes this verified rather than plausible.

9.18.4.  The step is per-FRAME, not per-second

   _DAT_004d0f20 = 2.592, added whole with no dt anywhere near it.
   And 3.3 * PI/4 = 2.5918, where 3.3 is g_frameDeltaScaled at 33 ms
   (9.17.2). So the constant was tuned for a fixed 33 ms frame and
   folded the dt in at authoring time. [inferred]

   o THAT IS A REAL FRAMERATE DEPENDENCY and it goes on the port's
     list. At double the framerate a bike ages through the air twice
     as fast, so anything gated on airTime crossing a threshold
     fires after half the real time. A reconstruction that
     "helpfully" multiplies by dt here changes the game's feel; one
     that keeps the constant reproduces it, quirk and all (rule 5).

9.18.5.  What consumes it - the drag coefficient

   maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain reads it once, at entry:

       0048ef61  XOR  EBX,EBX
       0048ef63  MOV  EAX,[ESI+0xcc]        airTime
       0048ef70  TEST EAX,0x7fffffff        mask the SIGN BIT
       0048ef75  SETNZ BL                   EBX = airborne

   and EBX survives, untouched, all the way to the drag block:

       00490040  JZ   0049005d              grounded
       00490042  FLD/FMUL ... [0x004d1e28]  AIRBORNE: 1.6
       0049005d  FLD/FMUL ... [0x004d1e2c]  GROUNDED: 0.3

   o SO LEAVING THE GROUND MULTIPLIES DRAG BY 5.33. speed^2 * dt *
     1.6 / MotoMasse * 0.001 instead of the 0.3 form 9.17.9.3
     verified. At 25 m/s on a 150 kg bike that is 0.11 per frame
     against 0.021 - the difference between coasting and braking.

   o THE SIGN-BIT MASK IS DELIBERATE. TEST EAX,0x7fffffff treats
     -0.0f as grounded, which a plain TEST EAX,EAX would not. The
     same idiom appears at 0x0048efd8 gating the tyre-slip path.

   o 9.17.8.3 IS CORRECTED. The 1.6 branch is not unreachable; it
     runs on every jump, which on a dirt track is most of the lap.

9.18.6.  The dirt bike, for the record

   Read live out of g_bikeCarac[0] at 0x00509278 [verified], and
   these are stock rather than the hacked CARAC2 values:

   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | Field                | Dirt bike   | Against the road bike of 9.17.1          |
   +======================+=============+==========================================+
   | MotoType             | 1           | THE ONE WE COULD NOT REACH BEFORE - 2 on |
   |                      |             | the road bike                            |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | MoteurVitesseMax     | 8.0         | up from 7.0                              |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | MotoMasse            | 150.0       | down from 280.0 - barely half            |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | RealSpeedMax         | 46.0        | down from 80.0. 46 m/s = 165.6 km/h on   |
   |                      |             | the speedo, against 288                  |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | RegimeMax            | 10000.0     | down from 12000                          |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | RegimeCoupleMax      | 6500.0      | peak torque much earlier - 10500 on the  |
   |                      |             | road bike                                |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | CoupleMax            | 7.0         | down from 15.0                           |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | Couple1000           | 1.0         | down from 6.0                            |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | CoupleRegimeMax      | 1.0         | down from 2.0 - flat rather than falling |
   +----------------------+-------------+------------------------------------------+

   o SO MotoType 1 IS FINALLY REACHABLE and the three MotoType-1-only
     paths of 9.10.2 and 9.11.1 can be measured. This run confirmed
     two of them fire; neither is yet decoded.

9.18.7.  turnBrakeFlag and driveDisengaged both fire

   Write watchpoints on racer+0x0ac and racer+0x13c, same run
   [verified]:

   o turnBrakeFlag (+0x0ac) TOGGLES 1 -> 0 -> 1 repeatedly, written
     from two sites INSIDE maybe_Racer_ApplySteering: set to 1 at
     0x0046650e, cleared at 0x0046656c. Every hit carried EDX =
     0x8000, the centred steering axis, so the toggle is driven by
     the button word rather than by the axis. WHAT READS IT WAS
     read by inst+0x88 (9.10.1). 9.21.4
     pins it exactly: 0x0048f915, where it gates the whole
     deceleration block, so clearing it disables braking.

   o driveDisengaged (+0x13c) TOGGLES 0 -> 1 -> 0 every frame,
     written from two sites inside maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain:
     cleared unconditionally at 0x0048f8fa - the common tail entry
     of 9.17.9.1 - and set to 1 at 0x0048f95d in the coast branch.
     So it is a PER-FRAME flag rebuilt from scratch, not a latch,
     and 9.10.2's reading of it as MotoType-1-only is confirmed
     behaviourally: it never moved on the road bike.

9.18.8.  What this run did NOT settle

   The grip curve was unobserved here and is measured in 9.19. The
   instrument that got it was the DONUT - throttle and brake together,
   spinning on the spot, the largest sustained lateral slip available
   on demand and holdable indefinitely in one place.

   THE AIR TIMER STILL HAS NO READER BUT THE DRIVETRAIN. A field this
   cheap to maintain probably feeds the trick or landing logic too.
   [open]

9.18.9.  The rest of the function - the BRAKE DIVE

   maybe_Racer_UpdateAirborne is now imported whole as
   racer_update_airborne.  Beyond the air timer it does two things,
   and the first is far more important than it looks.

   THE BRAKE DIVE.  Gated on input bit 0x2 and on
   maybe_Racer_LongSpeed exceeding 0.1, it folds the euler triple
   (PI/128, 0, 0) into matOrientation through
   Racer_ComposeOrientation.  The angle passes through
   Racer_DiveAngleOrZero (0x0045d0c0), which returns 0 once the
   chassis attitude leaves +-(PI/2 - PI/32) - so the dive saturates
   at ONE step rather than accumulating while the brake is held.

   IT IS THE ONLY SOURCE OF PITCH IN matOrientation ON A GROUNDED
   BIKE, and that reaches much further than the visual.  row0 . row2
   is zero by construction, so a yaw-only matrix makes
   maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed return a STRUCTURAL zero - which pins the
   skid ribbon at minimum width and holds the 9.19 grip curve at 1.0.
   A port can lose both by omitting one 382-byte function.

   [verified] live on MOTO.EXE, gas alone against four brake frames:

       +-------------+---------------------------------------+
       | gas only    | row1 = (0, 1.000000, 0.000000)        |
       | brake x4    | row1 = (0, 0.999699, 0.024541)        |
       +-------------+---------------------------------------+

   and 0.024541229 is sin(PI/128) to eight places.

   THE MANUAL GEAR LATCH, on input bits 0x10 and 0x20 through
   DAT_004ea4c8 (now g_nRacerShiftLatch), gated on racer+0x88.  Both
   bits held arms the latch and shifts nothing.  It is ONE GLOBAL for
   the whole game rather than a per-racer field.

   THE AIRBORNE ARM SKIPS ONLY THE GROUNDED BLOCK: the JMP at
   0x00466688 lands past it on the gear latch, so the latch and the
   shift tick still run in the air.

   Addresses and instruction evidence are in the plate comment.

9.18.16.  The longitudinal assembly, and Racer_SetLongSpeed's caller

   maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain does not write speed directly.  It
   assembles a LONGITUDINAL SCALAR in the stack local [ESP+0x10] -
   drive, brake, drag and roll all land on that one number - snaps it
   at the zero crossing, and hands it to Racer_SetLongSpeed:

       0048fdfb   [ESP+0x10] built from the drive/brake chain
       0049008c   drag  = v*v*dtMs*(1.6 air | 0.3 ground)
                          / motoMasse / 1000            carac index 7
       0048fe4a   roll  = 0.02 * dtMs * 0.00981
                          (* 0.5 for MotoType 1, and * speed * 0.05
                           below 20 m/s - 0x0048fe5e)
       0048fe82   v -= roll, sign preserved
       0048feb6   snapped to 0 if it crossed
       0048fec5   Racer_SetLongSpeed(instance, v)

   THAT LAST CALL IS THE POINT.  SetLongSpeed replaces only the ROW-2
   component of the body-space velocity and keeps the other two, then
   rebuilds speed as |world| and rewrites velDir.  So the bike's
   lateral and vertical motion survive the drive step instead of
   being flattened by it.

   THE PORT HAD physics_set_long_speed TRANSCRIBED AND NEVER CALLED
   IT - fourteen sites in the original, none here.  In its place was
   a straight-line stand-in, `speed = gearboxRoadSpeed` one way and
   `gearboxRoadSpeed = speed` the other.

   WHY THE STAND-IN SURVIVED EVERY STRAIGHT-LINE TEST: with velDir
   along row 2 the other two body components are zero, so |world| is
   just |v| and the two forms agree exactly.  They differ only once
   the bike has velocity it is not pointing along - which is a turn.

   Now wired: race.c applies the roll to the scalar and ends the
   frame with physics_set_long_speed.  The drag moved onto
   gearboxRoadSpeed with it, since that is the scalar SetLongSpeed
   consumes.

   THE CLAMP MUST COME FROM THE CARAC.  SetLongSpeed clamps to
   instance+0x90, racer+0xb4 - and r->realSpeedMax HAS NO WRITER in
   the port.  MOTO holds 80.0 there and the port holds 0.0, so the
   first wiring clamped v to zero and the bike would not move at all.
   Every other limiter here already reads c->realSpeedMax, because
   racer+0xb4 is exactly the field the copy protection zeroes to pin
   a pirated bike (copy-protection.txt 5.1).  Passing the
   protection's own target reproduced the protection's symptom.

   ONE WRITER IS NOW IDENTIFIED, AND IT IS THE AI's.  FUN_0047bc60
   fills racer+0xb4 for the OPPONENTS from a per-difficulty table:

       factor = pocket ? (1.0 / FactorPocket) : 1.0
       racer[i]+0xb4 = table[i] * (1.0/3.6) * factor * difficultyMul

       0x004d1908  1.0f        0x004d1938  0.277778 = 1/3.6, km/h
       0x004d1928  0.9   easy  0x004d1930  1.05  hard, solo
       0x004d1940  1.1   hard, network
       0x004dbfd8  FactorPocket, 0.95 on ile01, from the .spt

   The solo arm starts at 0x005876de and strides 0x3209, which is
   racer[2]+0xb4, so it writes the opponents and never racer 1.  The
   PLAYER's copy comes from Racer_Create, which puts the carac's
   RealSpeedMax there - see section 4 - so c->realSpeedMax is the
   right value for racer 1 and the substitution is exact.  Measured
   with the pocket bike loaded:

       racer 1  human  100.0000   = carac RealSpeedMax
       racer 2  AI      70.1754
       racer 3  AI      64.3275      the table above, in m/s
       racer 4  AI      78.0702
       racer 5  AI      77.7778

   SO THE TWO DIFFER PER RACER and c->realSpeedMax is NOT a valid
   substitution once the AI is ticked.

   VERIFIED AGAINST MOTO, all eleven values.  With g_opponentCount
   11 the stride is sel = 22 - 2k, which picks ComputerSpeed entries
   22, 20, 18, 16, 14, 12, 10, 8, 6, 4, 2 - that is 270, 270, 267,
   266, 265, 264, 260, 250, 240, 220, 200 km/h, and divided by 3.6:

       75.00  75.00  74.17  73.89  73.61  73.33
       72.22  69.44  66.67  61.11  55.56

   MOTO's eleven opponents hold exactly that multiset.  THE
   TWO-STRIDE RULE AND THE km/h CONVERSION ARE BOTH CONFIRMED, and
   so is the shuffle - the values are the same eleven in a different
   order.

   THE 23-OPPONENT ROSTER IS A USEFUL CROSS-CHECK.  sel = 22 - k
   reaches entries 22, 21 and 20, which are all 270, so three racers
   share 75.00 exactly.  Seen in the port when the roster was 23.

   TRANSCRIBED as race_set_opponent_speeds in race.c, the SOLO arm:

       racers[2 + k].realSpeedMax =
           ComputerSpeed[sel] * (1/3.6) * pocket * difficulty

       sel = (opponents == 23) ? 22 - k : 22 - 2 * k

   TWO STRIDES FOR THE TWO ROSTER SIZES the game ships - 23 walks the
   table one entry at a time, the cut-down 11 walks it two at a time,
   so both spread across the same 23 entries.  The table is at
   0x0051be98, is ComputerSpeed out of the .spt in km/h, and reads
   180, 190, 200, 210, 220, 230, 240, 245, 250, 255, 260, 263.5, 264,
   264.5 ... 267.5, 270, 270, 270 on ile01.

   AND THEN IT SHUFFLES.  opponents/2 rounds swap realSpeedMax
   between two randomly chosen opponents at 0x0047bdb1, the address
   arithmetic being racers[2 + rand() % opponents] + 0xb4.  SO WHICH
   BIKE GETS WHICH SPEED IS NOT REPRODUCIBLE AGAINST MOTO - only the
   multiset is, and a per-racer diff of this field is not meant to
   match.  That is also why racer 2 was measured holding 72.222 m/s
   (= 260 km/h, entry 10) where the unshuffled loop would have given
   it entry 22.

   IT CHANGES NOTHING IN A GENTLE TURN, and that is expected.  With
   slip at 0.01 m/s against 30 m/s of speed the lateral body
   component is 0.03 per cent, so |world| is |v| to four figures and
   SetLongSpeed agrees with the stand-in it replaced.  The two part
   company only in a real slide.

9.18.17.  What the last 0.0055 a frame actually is

   After the assembly landed, the port still loses about 0.0055 m/s a
   frame more than MOTO - and it does so on a STRAIGHT as well as in
   a turn, so it was never a cornering effect.

   THE ROLL IS BYTE-EXACT.  A hook at 0x0048fe58 capturing [ESP+0x1c]
   reads 0.0064746 every braking frame, and
   physics_rolling_resistance computes 0.02*33*0.00981 = 0.0064746.
   Identical.

   WHAT THE HOOK SHOWS IS THAT MOTO LOSES LESS THAN ITS OWN THREE
   TERMS.  In the ramped phase at 22.2 m/s the longitudinal scalar
   falls 0.1729, 0.1726, 0.1723 a frame, against

       ramped brake  0.15278
       drag          0.01743
       roll          0.00647
       sum           0.17668

   so MOTO comes in about 0.0038 BELOW its own sum while the port
   applies all three and lands above it.  Something in the original
   is ADDING a small amount back on a braking frame.

   ENGINE TORQUE IS NOT IT.  Engine_TorqueAt has exactly one caller
   inside the drivetrain, 0x0048f3ba, and a hook on the instruction
   before it took ZERO hits in four braking frames and FOUR in four
   throttle frames.  The original branches drive OR brake, never
   both, which is what the port already does.

   NOR IS IT A MISSING TERM.  Hooks on the two drag paths -
   0x00490042 airborne, 0x0049005d grounded - took 0 and 4 hits
   respectively in four braking frames, so the drag runs on a
   braking frame by the ground formula.  The roll runs too, at its
   byte-exact value.  All three terms fire in the original exactly as
   they do in the port, with the same constants.

   SO ONE OF THE THREE IS NOT LANDING AT FULL VALUE IN MOTO.  Summed
   at 23.2 m/s the three predict 0.17828 a frame and the longitudinal
   scalar actually falls about 0.1744.  The drag is the one whose
   APPLICATION has not been traced: it is computed into [ESP+0x4c] at
   0x0049008c and where that goes is still unread.

   FOLLOWED, AND IT IS AN ORDERING DIFFERENCE:

       0049008c  FSTP [ESP+0x4c]        drag
       00490090  FLD  [ESI+0x100]       speed
       00490096  FSUB [ESP+0x4c]        - drag
       0049009a  FSTP [ESI+0x100]       speed -= drag
       004900ad  clamped to 0 if negative

   THE ADDRESS ORDER IS THE WHOLE ANSWER.  0x00490090 is PAST
   Racer_SetLongSpeed at 0x0048fec5, so the drag lands on the speed
   that call has just REBUILT, and gearboxRoadSpeed is never touched
   by it.  In the original the drag is therefore a per-frame loss on
   the speed the integrator is about to use, and it is wiped next
   frame when SetLongSpeed writes speed again from the road speed.

   THE PORT HAD IT COMPOUNDING.  It subtracted the drag from
   gearboxRoadSpeed - which persists - so every frame's drag was
   still in the road speed on the next one.  That is why the port
   lost MORE than the sum of the three terms while the original lost
   LESS: the original's drag never accumulates.

   BUT MOVING IT THERE MADE THE PORT WORSE, and that is the useful
   part.  Measured straight-line braking with the drag on speed after
   SetLongSpeed, per two-frame row:

       +0.0354  +0.0264  +0.0253  +0.0243  +0.0194  +0.0142  +0.0097

   the port now losing TOO LITTLE and by a margin that decays with
   speed - drag-shaped, and the opposite sign to the 0.0055 it had
   when the drag went onto gearboxRoadSpeed.

   SO 0x00490090 CANNOT BE THE ONLY APPLICATION.  A hook on the
   longitudinal scalar shows it falling 0.1729 a frame against a
   ramped brake of 0.15278 - an extra 0.020, which is the drag.  The
   ROAD SPEED carries the drag as well, by a path that is still not
   read.  The site at 0x00490090 is real and is additional to it.

   The port keeps the gearboxRoadSpeed placement, which measures
   about three times closer, and physics.c says plainly that it is
   not the original's structure.

9.18.18.  The scalar is built from gearboxRoadSpeed, not from speed

   [ESP+0x10] is assembled at 0x0048fdf0..0x0048fdfb as

       [ESP+0x10] = [ESP+0x4c] + [ESP+0x84]

   and a hook at 0x0048fdf4 capturing all four quantities settles
   what those are:

   +---+-------------+---------------+-------------+-------------+
   | n | [ESP+0x84]  | [ESP+0x4c]    | speed +100  | gbox +108   |
   +===+=============+===============+=============+=============+
   | 0 |   22.32125  |  -0.00000000  |   22.32125  |   22.32125  |
   | 1 |   22.14438  |  +0.00397237  |   22.15114  |   22.14438  |
   | 2 |   21.97176  |  +0.00397237  |   21.97176  |   21.97176  |
   +---+-------------+---------------+-------------+-------------+

   [ESP+0x84] IS gearboxRoadSpeed, exactly, in every row - and row 1
   shows speed differing from it by 0.00676, so the two are genuinely
   distinct at that moment rather than coincidentally equal.

   SO THE DRAG DOES NOT FEED BACK.  The 0x00490090 subtraction lands
   on speed, the scalar is rebuilt from gearboxRoadSpeed, and speed
   is overwritten by the next SetLongSpeed.  That closes the loop the
   wrong way for the earlier guess and it is now ruled out.

   AND [ESP+0x4c] HERE IS NOT THE DRAG EITHER.  It is a small
   POSITIVE term, +0.00397, ADDED to the base - the stack slot is
   simply reused, and the drag does not reach it until 0x0049008c,
   long after this read.  Its own build is at 0x0048fdc1:
   -[ESP+0x98] * dtMs * 0x004d1d60, clamped to 0.55 at 0x0048fde8.

9.18.19.  How the drag reaches the road speed - the last SpeedToEngine

   maybe_Gearbox_SpeedToEngine (0x0048deb0) is called from SIX sites
   inside the drivetrain and takes &gearboxRoadSpeed BY POINTER, so
   any of them can move it.  Hooked either side, on braking frames:

   +---------------------------------------+-----------------------+
   | SpeedToEngine #1, 0x0048fa68          | +0.000000 no change   |
   | 0x0048fdf4 -> 0x00490038              | -0.002502 CONSTANT    |
   |   (spans SetLongSpeed and STE #2)     |                       |
   | 0x00490038 -> next frame's 0x0048fdf4 | -0.169310             |
   |   of which the ramped brake is        |  0.152780             |
   |   leaving                             |  0.016530             |
   |   and the drag at 21.5 m/s is         |  0.016344             |
   +---------------------------------------+-----------------------+

   THAT LAST WINDOW IS WHERE THE DRAG ENTERS THE ROAD SPEED.  It
   contains the drag being applied to speed at 0x00490090 AND the
   final SpeedToEngine at 0x004901ae, which re-derives
   gearboxRoadSpeed from the speed that call has just reduced.

   SO BOTH EARLIER READINGS WERE RIGHT AND NEITHER WAS COMPLETE.  The
   drag is subtracted from speed ONLY, exactly as 0x00490090 shows -
   and the LAST SpeedToEngine then propagates it into the road speed
   for the next frame.  The port's `gearboxRoadSpeed -= drag` is a
   one-line shortcut across that loop, which is why it measures close
   and is not the structure.

   THE -0.002502 IS UNIDENTIFIED.  Constant per frame, so not the
   v-squared drag; not the roll either, which is 0.0064746.  It
   appears across SetLongSpeed plus SpeedToEngine #2.

   IT IS NOT SpeedToEngine.  0x0048deb0 writes the road speed it is
   handed ONLY when the rev limiter trips - the plate comment on it
   says so and the arithmetic agrees: in second gear at 21.5 m/s the
   engine works out at 7.0 / (0.6 * 61.0) * 21.5 = 4.11 against a
   MoteurVitesseMax of 7.0, so the limiter never fires and *speed is
   never written.  physics_speed_to_engine already has that shape.
   Transcribing it further would not let the shortcut go.

   IT IS ModelInstance_Integrate.  0x0048dd05 runs when the frame's
   speed changed by more than 0.001 (0x004d1c80) and LERPS the road
   speed toward the actual longitudinal velocity:

       if (|speed - speedAtEntry| > 0.001 && !airborne) {
           long = maybe_Racer_LongSpeed()
           k    = FUN_0048eb40()
           instance+0x108 += (long - instance+0x108) * k
       }

   maybe_Racer_LongSpeed reads velDir and speed - and speed HAS by
   then been reduced by the drag at 0x00490090.  So the drag reaches
   gearboxRoadSpeed through this lerp, one tick later, which is
   exactly the round trip the port's one-line shortcut stands in for.

   racer_integrate already carries this as a known hole - "NOT
   TRANSCRIBED: the LongSpeed / gearbox resync at 0x0048dd05" - and
   it turns out to be the same gap as the 0.6 per cent.

   BOTH ARE NOW TRANSCRIBED.  FUN_0048eb40 turned out to be the GRIP
   CURVE - the function physics_grip already is.  It transforms
   velDir * speed into body space, takes the LATERAL component, and
   returns 1 - 0.05*|slip| between 0.01 and 20 for MotoType 1, or
   max(1.0, MinAdherenceWheel) for every other bike.

   SO ON A ROAD BIKE THE LERP FACTOR IS 1.0 AND THE LERP IS A PLAIN
   COPY - gearboxRoadSpeed becomes the longitudinal velocity exactly.
   That is precisely what the stand-in was approximating, which is
   why it measured close, and it also explains the shape of the
   error: the copy is exact only while the bike goes where it points.

   The chain now runs as the original's does:

       race.c    assembly -> physics_set_long_speed        0048fec5
       race.c    speed -= drag                             00490090
       racer.c   resync: gearboxRoadSpeed lerps toward
                 LongSpeed by the grip factor              0048dd05

   AND IT MADE THE PORT WORSE AGAIN.  Measured braking in a turn with
   the resync in and the shortcut out, the port went FLAT at -1.8464 a
   two-frame row against MOTO's -1.9039 decaying, a d-diff of +0.074
   falling to +0.029.  -1.8464 is exactly 0.91667 + 0.006475, the full
   brake plus the roll: THE DRAG WAS NOT REACHING THE ROAD SPEED AT
   ALL.

   THE GATE IS WHY.  speedBefore is taken at the TOP of
   ModelInstance_Integrate and compared at the bottom, so the test is
   on the change WITHIN Integrate - and on a grounded frame Integrate
   does not move speed, so the resync never fires.  It is transcribed
   correctly and it is simply not the path the drag takes.

   THREE PLACEMENTS, ALL MEASURED:

       gearboxRoadSpeed -= drag          -0.0055 a frame
       speed -= drag after SetLongSpeed  +0.017  a frame
       the same plus the resync          +0.037  a frame

   The first is kept.  It is three to six times closer than either
   faithful attempt and physics.c marks it an approximation.  The
   resync stays transcribed - it is the original's code and costs
   nothing while its gate is shut.

9.18.20.  Every writer of gearboxRoadSpeed, enumerated

   A hardware data watchpoint on instance+0x108 (racer+0x12c,
   0x0058454d - byte-misaligned, so length 1) over three braking
   frames gives THREE writers a frame and no others:

   +------------+--------------------------------------------------+
   | eip after  | what                                             |
   +============+==================================================+
   | 0048fa10   | the brake apply, FSTP at 0x0048fa0a - known      |
   | 0048ffdd   | a LERP, FSTP at 0x0048ffd7                       |
   | 00490158   | the SAME LERP again, FSTP at 0x00490152          |
   +------------+--------------------------------------------------+

   BOTH UNKNOWN WRITERS ARE THE SAME FIVE INSTRUCTIONS:

       FLD  [ESP+0x18]        the target
       FSUB [ESI+0x108]       - gearboxRoadSpeed
       FMUL k                 * the factor
       FADD [ESI+0x108]       + gearboxRoadSpeed
       FSTP [ESI+0x108]       gearbox += (target - gearbox) * k

   and the second takes its factor from a CALL to 0x0048eb40 - the
   GRIP CURVE - at 0x0049013b.

   THE SECOND ONE IS THE ANSWER.  0x00490152 runs AFTER the drag is
   subtracted from speed at 0x0049009a, so it is what carries the
   drag into the road speed.  Both are inside
   maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain, in the span physics.c already marks
   HOLE.

   AND THE 0x0048dd05 RESYNC IS A THIRD LERP OF THE SAME SHAPE, in
   ModelInstance_Integrate.  That is why it read as the mechanism and
   why transcribing it changed nothing: its gate is on the speed
   change WITHIN Integrate, which is zero on a grounded frame.  Three
   copies of one idiom in two functions, and the port had been
   chasing the wrong copy.

   THE LESSON IS THE METHOD.  Two candidates were named from reading
   call graphs and both were wrong; the watchpoint named all three
   writers in one run, including two at addresses no search had
   surfaced.  A displacement search cannot find a store through a
   pointer, and neither can a hook on a site nobody suspects.

   THE TARGET IS THE LONGITUDINAL VELOCITY.  [ESP+0x10] is built at
   0x004900c8..0x00490134 as normalize(velDir) * speed, put through
   the inverse of matOrientation (0x0049011a, 0x0049012a), so
   [ESP+0x18] is its Z - the body-space forward speed, which is what
   maybe_Racer_LongSpeed returns.  The whole lerp is therefore

       long = LongSpeed()                 on the POST-DRAG speed
       k    = grip()                      FUN_0048eb40
       gearboxRoadSpeed += (long - gearboxRoadSpeed) * k

   and on a road bike k is 1.0, so it is a plain copy: the road speed
   becomes the longitudinal velocity, drag included.

   TRANSCRIBED into race.c's tail, ungated, after the drag.  The
   shortcut is gone.  The identical lerp at 0x0048dd05 stays where it
   is in racer_integrate - it is the original's code and its gate
   simply keeps it shut on a grounded frame.

   MEASURED, AND IT IS A TRADE.

       braking in a turn   -0.0055 a frame -> -0.00275, and the
                           decaying shape is kept.  HALVED.
       acceleration        0.04 per cent -> 0.35 per cent at
                           15 m/s.  WORSE.

   The lerp is not firing when it should not: a hook at 0x00490144
   takes four hits in four THROTTLE frames, so the original runs it
   on a drive frame exactly as the port now does.  The acceleration
   regression is therefore in what the lerp is fed on a drive frame -
   physics_long_speed of a velDir the port builds differently - and
   not in whether it runs.

   BOTH NUMBERS ARE NOW ABOUT 0.3 PER CENT.  The structure is the
   original's rather than a stand-in, which is the reason to keep it,
   but it is not a clean win and is recorded as one.

9.18.23.  Braking in a turn: the scalar is not gearboxRoadSpeed

   With the clock stepped and the placement complete, braking into a
   left-hand turn from 20 m/s runs:

   +-------+----------+----------+---------+-----------+
   | frame | MOTO     | port     | speed % | head deg  |
   +=======+==========+==========+=========+===========+
   |     2 | 19.66401 | 19.65788 | -0.031  |   -0.022  |
   |     8 | 17.88809 | 17.86374 | -0.136  |    0.000  |
   |    14 | 12.33724 | 12.28764 | -0.402  |   +0.088  |
   |    20 |  6.82512 |  6.75032 | -1.096  |   +0.132  |
   +-------+----------+----------+---------+-----------+

   HEADING IS ESSENTIALLY EXACT - 0.13 degrees after twenty frames of
   a turn.  Speed is not, and the error is a CONSTANT 0.00419 m/s per
   frame from the moment the 200 ms brake ramp saturates, over speeds
   from 19.7 down to 6.8.

   THAT CONSTANCY IS THE EVIDENCE.  Roll goes as speed below the 20.0
   cut and drag goes as speed squared, so neither can produce a flat
   error; only a term of fixed size can.  Every input to the brake
   step was then checked against the binary and all of them agree -
   frein 100.0 and motoType 2 on both, dtMs 32.9999995 against 33.0,
   and the saturated step 0.91666669 captured at 0x0048f9f6.

   THE SOURCE IS THE SCALAR.  Hooking 0x0048fe7b and reading the
   stack local the roll block operates on:

   +-----+----------------+----------+--------------+
   | hit | scalar ESP+10  | gbox     | scalar-gbox  |
   +=====+================+==========+==============+
   |   1 |        19.8472 |  19.8472 |     +0.00000 |
   |   2 |        19.6781 |  19.6741 |     +0.00397 |
   |   5 |        19.1723 |  19.1683 |     +0.00395 |
   |   8 |        17.9052 |  17.9012 |     +0.00397 |
   +-----+----------------+----------+--------------+

   MOTO's longitudinal scalar sits a CONSTANT 0.00397 above
   gearboxRoadSpeed, and race.c feeds Racer_SetLongSpeed the raw
   gearboxRoadSpeed instead - so every frame starts 0.004 low, which
   is the 0.00419 measured.  The two agree in size and in being flat.

9.18.23.1.  What 0x0048fc00..0x0048ff00 actually assembles

   Read out.  The block transforms the instance's velocity vector
   (instance+0xbc..0xc4) into body space through the 3x3 at
   instance+0x98 - five helper calls, 0x0040fcd0, 0x0040ff80,
   0x00403710, 0x004039c0 and 0x00410010 - and takes the z of the
   result as the longitudinal scalar at 0x0048fd91.  Then:

       0048fdb4  TEST [ESI+0xcc]     airborne -> the term is 0
       0048fdc1  FLD  [ESP+0x98]     dirY
       0048fdc8  FCHS
       0048fdca  FMUL [ESP+0xa8]     * dtMs
       0048fdd1  FMUL [0x004d1d60]   * 0.004905
       0048fdd7  FCOM [0x004d1d68]   > 0.55 ?
       0048fde8  MOV  [ESP+0x4c], 0x3f0ccccd    ...then 0.55
       0048fdf0  FLD  [ESP+0x4c]
       0048fdf4  FADD [ESP+0x84]     + body z
       0048fdfb  FSTP [ESP+0x10]     scalar = z + slope

   The whole block is skipped when a flag at [ESP+0x88] is set and
   speed is at or below 0.1 (0x004d1ca0).

   THE TRANSFORM LANDS BACK ON gearboxRoadSpeed.  The hook at
   0x0048fe7b measured the scalar a constant 0.00397 above it, and
   scalar minus 0.00397 equalled gearboxRoadSpeed to every digit -
   so the body z the five helpers compute is the value the port
   already holds, and only the slope term was missing.  That is why
   the fix is two lines and not an import of the helper chain.

   IT IS THE BRAKE DIVE, NOT A HILL.  dirY is the forward vector's
   Y, so a nose-down pitch under braking feeds a positive term:
   0.00397 back-solves to dirY = -0.0245, about 1.4 degrees of dive.
   A coast test on the same spot showed both games losing 0.0025 a
   frame with neither accelerating, which is what confirmed the
   ground there is flat and the term is attitude, not terrain.

   FIXED, AND MEASURED: race.c adds physics_slope into the scalar
   before the roll, at the original's 0x0048fdf0, and no longer adds
   it to speed at the ground-contact seam - that seam was applying
   the same term at the wrong place in the frame.

   The same twenty frames after the move:

   +-------+----------+----------+---------+-----------+
   | frame | MOTO     | port     | speed % | abs       |
   +=======+==========+==========+=========+===========+
   |     2 | 19.66402 | 19.65791 | -0.031  |  -0.00612 |
   |     8 | 17.88803 | 17.88729 | -0.004  |  -0.00074 |
   |    14 | 12.33715 | 12.33459 | -0.021  |  -0.00256 |
   |    20 |  6.82498 |  6.82063 | -0.064  |  -0.00435 |
   +-------+----------+----------+---------+-----------+

   -1.096 per cent to -0.064 at frame 20, and the flat per-frame
   error from 0.00419 to about 0.00030.  Heading ends 0.176 degrees
   apart over the same twenty frames.  [verified]

   TWO RESIDUALS REMAIN, both small and both unattributed:

   o THE FIRST-FRAME TRANSIENT WAS speed AND gearboxRoadSpeed BEING
     WELDED TOGETHER.  Stepping the port one frame at a time against
     MOTO's per-frame hook capture:

     +-----+-------------+-------------+-------------+
     | frm | MOTO gbox   | MOTO speed  | ours speed  |
     +=====+=============+=============+=============+
     |   2 |    19.82688 |    19.82688 |    19.82681 |
     |   3 |    19.65801 |    19.66401 |    19.65787 |
     |   4 |    19.48942 |    19.48942 |    19.48922 |
     +-----+-------------+-------------+-------------+

     MOTO's two values are equal on every frame but one, where the
     turn takes hold and they come apart by 0.006 before
     re-converging.  The brake ramp is not involved - brakeHoldMs
     ran 0, 33, 66, 99, 132, 165 on both sides and the step was
     0.152778 on every frame of it.

     The cause was the surviving half of the straight-line stand-in,
     `gearboxRoadSpeed = speed` at the ground-contact seam, which
     made them identical at the end of every frame.  The original
     lets the lerp at 0x00490140 converge them by the grip factor,
     and that factor is 1.0 only in a straight line.  Removed.

   o A RESIDUAL 0.0003 A FRAME, which accumulates to the -0.00435 at
     frame 20.  An order below the slope term that was just fixed
     and two below the doubled drag before it.  [open]

9.18.23.2.  The frame-2 decoupling - three dead ends, written down

   MOTO's speed and gearboxRoadSpeed come apart by 0.006 for exactly
   one frame as the turn takes hold.  The port's never come apart.
   Still unexplained; what it is NOT is now settled, so the next
   reader does not repeat these.

   NOT THE LERP FACTOR.  FUN_0048eb40 was read out and it IS
   physics_grip: the same band 0.01 to 20 with rate -0.05 for
   MotoType 1, and max(1.0, MinAdherenceWheel) for everything else.
   Our bikes are MotoType 2 with MinAdherenceWheel 0.2, so the
   factor is 1.0 on both sides and the lerp fully converges on both.
   Three comments calling that function unread were stale and are
   corrected.

   NOT AN UNKNOWN WRITER.  A hardware watchpoint on racer+0x124
   lists every writer of speed, and there are exactly three a frame:

   +------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | eip        | site                                         |
   +============+==============================================+
   | 0x0048cea5 | inside Racer_SetLongSpeed, twice per frame   |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | 0x004900a0 | the tail's drag - physics_drivetrain_tail    |
