   +------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | 0x0048d594 | ModelInstance_Integrate, AFTER the tail      |
   +------------+----------------------------------------------+

   AND 0x0048d594 IS NOT IN ModelInstance_Integrate AT ALL.  It is
   inside Racer_SetSlipSpeed, whose body runs 0x0048d2c0-0x0048d61b -
   the store is that function rebuilding speed and velDir, which the
   port already has as physics_set_slip_speed.  It was attributed to
   Integrate on address proximity without asking the DB for the
   function boundary, and there is no missing "grounded resync".

   THE ACTUAL CAUSE: A ZERO CLAMP.  Racer_SetSlipSpeed is called from
   two sites, maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering at 0x0045d513 and
   maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact at 0x00469388.  The port has the
   first, and it was passing r->realSpeedMax as the clamp - racer+0xb4,
   which MOTO holds at 80.0 and the port holds at 0.0 because nothing
   writes it.

   A clamp of zero pins the lateral component to nothing.  So velDir
   never came off the heading, slip never rose above denormal noise,
   and speed and gearboxRoadSpeed could not come apart - which is
   exactly the symptom.  It also explains the long-standing "denormal
   residue in slip on straights" note: the slip was not noise, it was
   a clamped-away quantity.

   THAT HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG.  The clamp was changed to
   c->realSpeedMax and NOTHING MOVED - the braking comparison is
   identical to five decimal places, and velDir's lateral component
   read -0.000383 on both sides before AND after.  A clamp of zero
   was not zeroing anything.  The edit is kept because it is the
   substitution the port makes everywhere else, but it fixed nothing
   and the frame-2 gap is untouched.

   THE THIRD WRITER OF gearboxRoadSpeed.  A watchpoint on racer+0x12c
   lists three a frame, and the port has two of them:

   +------------+---------------------------------------------+
   | eip        | site                                        |
   +============+=============================================+
   | 0x0048fa10 | the brake subtraction                       |
   +------------+---------------------------------------------+
   | 0x0048ffdd | A LERP THE PORT DOES NOT RUN WHEN BRAKING   |
   +------------+---------------------------------------------+
   | 0x00490158 | the tail lerp, physics_drivetrain_tail      |
   +------------+---------------------------------------------+

   0x0048ffc3 is a FOURTH copy of the lerp idiom, with the grip
   factor inlined rather than called - k starts at 1.0 and is
   replaced only if the bike's MinAdherenceWheel exceeds it, which
   is physics_grip's non-dirt branch written out longhand:

       0048ffa9  [ESP+0x1c] = 1.0
       0048ffb1  ECX = [EAX + 0x50931c]
       0048ffb7  CMP ECX, 0x3f800000        ; > 1.0 ?
       0048ffbf  [ESP+0x1c] = ECX
       0048ffc3  FLD  [ESP+0x18]            ; target
       0048ffc7  FSUB [ESI+0x108]
       0048ffcd  FMUL [ESP+0x1c]
       0048ffd1  FADD [ESI+0x108]
       0048ffd7  FSTP [ESI+0x108]

   The port has this as step 6 of physics_tick_drivetrain, which is
   the DRIVE branch only, so it never runs on a braking frame.  With
   k = 1.0 it sets gearboxRoadSpeed to the target outright, and the
   tail lerp then overwrites it - so it may well be inert too.  NOT
   IMPLEMENTED, because nothing measured yet says it changes a
   value.  [open]

9.18.23.3.  Racer_SetSlipSpeed's second call site - the wall bounce

   The port had one of the two, in racer_apply_cornering.  The other
   is at 0x00469388 inside maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact, and the
   port carried it as the marked hole `0x0046933a FUN_0048d2c0`.

   IT IS THE BOUNCE ARM OF A WALL IMPACT - the branch taken when the
   reflected direction dotted against the frame's original move comes
   out at or above zero (0x004692e4, threshold 0x004d0e54 = 0.0).  A
   glance bounces; a head-on scrapes and takes the other arm.

   THE ARGUMENT IS ZERO, and reading that took untangling EBX:

       00469370  PUSH 0            <- the argument
       00469372  FMUL [ESI+0x124]
       00469378  FSTP [EBX]        <- speed = (1.0 - impact) * speed
       00469380  FMUL [0x004d11b8] <- 0.9, RACER_WALL_BOUNCE
       00469386  FSTP [EBX]        <- speed = speed * 0.9
       00469388  CALL Racer_SetSlipSpeed

   EBX is &r->speed.  Both FSTPs are the two speed lines the port
   already had, and 0x004d11b8 is the 0.9 already named as
   RACER_WALL_BOUNCE - so nothing in that run of arithmetic was
   missing.  What was missing is the PUSH 0: the lateral velocity is
   set to ZERO, so the bike leaves the rail along the reflected
   direction with no sideways component surviving.

   Read the other way round - as speed * 0.9 being the argument -
   this looks like a bike slammed sideways at ninety per cent of its
   speed.  It is not.  The tell is that the two stores land on
   racer+0x124, which the disassembly gives as [EBX] and which only
   resolves once EBX is chased.

   VERIFIED AT RUNTIME, and the transcription is right.

   THE SPOT: -1200, 0, 2058 at yaw 15 with speed 15 in gear 2, on the
   straight whose rails sit at x = +/-1280.  Fifteen degrees closes
   the 80-unit gap in about six frames and glances rather than
   crashes; 45 degrees at 20 m/s CRASHES MOTO into a race reset and
   60 degrees never reaches the rail at all.

   AND WALLS HAVE TO BE PUT BACK FIRST.  tools/mr_cheats.py nowalls
   is a CODE PATCH at 0x0040ca80 and 0x0040cd90, and with it applied
   the bike rides straight through - distA goes negative frame after
   frame with the speed untouched and the hook silent.  `mr_cheats.py
   --off nowalls` restores it.  That cost two failed attempts.

   THE BOUNCE MATCHES EXACTLY.  On the impact frame:

   +----------------+-----------+-----------+
   | quantity       | MOTO      | port      |
   +================+===========+===========+
   | speed          |    7.9250 |    7.9250 |
   | velDir lateral | -0.077389 | -0.077388 |
   | moveDir.x      |   +0.2164 |   +0.2164 |
   +----------------+-----------+-----------+

   The hook at 0x0046937a also confirmed EBX statically: it fired
   with EBX = 0x00584545, which is racer+0x124, so the two FSTP [EBX]
   stores are the speed lines and the call's argument really is the
   PUSH 0.  [verified]

   BUT THE FRAME AFTER THE BOUNCE DOES NOT.  MOTO holds the reduced
   speed and the port recovers most of it:

       frame 6   MOTO 7.9250   port  7.9250   (the impact)
       frame 8   MOTO 7.9154   port 14.9573   +7.04
       frame 10  MOTO 7.9059   port 14.9317   +7.03

   Seven metres a second, put back the frame after a rail strike.

   NOT THE MIRROR.  On the impact frame the port's racer, s_race_drive
   .speed and .gearboxRoadSpeed all read 7.9249 together, so nothing
   is stale in flight - the speed genuinely comes BACK a frame later.

   NOT THE ENGINE RESYNC EITHER, and that was tried.  Ground contact
   does call Gearbox_SpeedToEngine (0x004693ca, storing to
   engineSpeed at 0x004693cf), and transcribing it made things worse:
   the port's engine fell to 2.8656 where MOTO's ROSE to 3.5709, and
   the 7 m/s was untouched.  Reverted.

   MOTO'S ENGINE IS NOT DISTURBED BY THE IMPACT AT ALL.  Across the
   glance it climbs a flat 0.2772 per two frames - 3.0165, 3.2937,
   3.5709, 3.8481 - which is the free-rev ramp running as if nothing
   had happened.  Whatever 0x004693aa..0x004693cf does, it is not
   pulling the engine down to match the halved road speed, so either
   that run does not execute on the BOUNCE arm (the scrape arm is the
   other candidate) or its argument is not r->speed.

   IT DOES FIRE, AND THE CAPTURE OVERTURNS THE ASSUMPTION.  A hook at
   0x004693b6 - the instruction after the 0x0048edd0 call, since a
   relative CALL cannot be hooked - takes exactly one hit per glance,
   on the impact frame:

   +---------------------------+----------+
   | engineSpeed  ESI+0x0ec    |   3.5708 |
   | speed        ESI+0x124    |   7.9250 |
   | gearboxRoad  ESI+0x12c    |   7.9250 |
   | gear         ESI+0x07c    |        2 |
   +---------------------------+----------+

   TWO THINGS FOLLOW.  gearboxRoadSpeed is ALREADY 7.9250 there, so
   the bounce reduces both speeds and this run does not have to.  And
   the engine going IN is 3.5708, which is what the end of the frame
   reads coming OUT - so Gearbox_SpeedToEngine RETURNED IT UNCHANGED.

   THE ORIGINAL'S SpeedToEngine TAKES THE ENGINE AS AN ARGUMENT.  It
   is pushed at 0x004693bf, alongside the gear and &gearboxRoadSpeed,
   and with road speed at 7.9250 in gear 2 a plain conversion would
   give 7/36.6 * 7.925 = 1.5157.  It gave 3.5708 back.  So the
   function does not simply convert - it declines to pull the engine
   below where it already is.

   0x0048deb0 READ, AND RE-TRANSCRIBED.  The fourth argument is a
   pass-through and the port was missing three things:

       if (instance+0x60) return engine;        disengaged
       if ((uint)*speed < 0x80000001)           SIGN-BIT test
           e = max / (ratio*vmax) * *speed;
       else e = engine;                         negative speed
       if (e > max) { e = max;
                      *speed = (ratio*vmax)/max * e; }   the LIMITER

   o THE DISENGAGED PASS-THROUGH is what lets a high engine sit over
     a low road speed.  instance+0x60 is racer+0x84, the gearbox
     state, and a shift IS pending across the glance - measured,
     gb.state reads 2 on every frame of it.
   o THE SIGN TEST is on float bits.  Written as *roadSpeed >= 0.0f
     it changes what happens rolling backwards.
   o THE LIMITER WRITES ROAD SPEED BACK, which the port's own comment
     claimed and its code did not.

   Seven call sites updated; the two in the shift-table sweep pass
   engine 0 and disengaged 0 deliberately, since that sweep asks what
   a gear WOULD turn at rather than resyncing a live engine.

   VERIFIED: the engine now tracks MOTO through the impact - 3.5733
   against 3.5709, then 3.8505 against 3.8481 - where before it
   collapsed to 2.8656.  The six-state matrix is unchanged, all still
   exact.  [verified]

   THE 7 m/s WAS A STALE gearboxRoadSpeed, and one-frame sampling
   said so outright:

       frm    speed      gbox      engine
         5   14.9957   14.9957    3.4347
         6    7.9250   14.9829    3.5733     speed drops, gbox does not
         7   14.9701   14.9701    3.7119     speed rebuilt from gbox

   The impact lowers r->speed and leaves gearboxRoadSpeed where it
   was; the drivetrain rebuilds speed from it on the next frame.
   MOTO's gbox WAS down - the hook captured ESI+0x12c at 7.9250 -
   because of the middle call in the sequence, which had been left
   unidentified.

   0x0048edd0 IS Gearbox_SyncRoadSpeed, and it is four lines:

       instance+0x108 = instance+0x100;          gbox = speed
       if (instance+0x90 < instance+0x100)
           instance+0x108 = instance+0x90;
       else if (instance+0x108 < -instance+0x90)
           instance+0x108 = -instance+0x90;

   Renamed in the DB with a plate comment; transcribed as
   physics_sync_road_speed.  Note the else - the negative clamp is
   reached only when the positive one did not fire.

   THE WHOLE SEQUENCE IS NOW NAMED:

       004693aa  Racer_SetLongSpeed     (speed)
       004693b1  Gearbox_SyncRoadSpeed
       004693ca  Gearbox_SpeedToEngine
       004693cf  engineSpeed = it

   and the parts explain each other - SpeedToEngine keeps the engine
   because a shift is pending across the impact and its disengaged
   branch passes it through, which is why MOTO's engine free-revs
   straight on through the strike.

   RESULT: +7.0419 to -0.0001 on the frames after the impact.

   +-------+----------+----------+----------+
   | frame | MOTO     | port     | d        |
   +=======+==========+==========+==========+
   |     6 |   7.9249 |   7.9249 |  -0.0000 |
   |     8 |   7.9154 |   7.9153 |  -0.0001 |
   |    10 |   7.9059 |   7.9057 |  -0.0001 |
   |    12 |   9.4892 |   9.5148 |  +0.0257 |
   +-------+----------+----------+----------+

   The six-state matrix is unchanged, all still exact.  [verified]

   WHAT IS LEFT is +0.026 once the bike accelerates away, and it is
   the SAME SHAPE as the frame-2 decoupling of 9.18.23.4.

   Hooking the tail lerp at 0x00490158 across the glance, the two
   frames after the strike read:

   +-----+------------+----------+------------+-----------+
   | hit | gbox after | speed    | lng target | lng/speed |
   +=====+============+==========+============+===========+
   |   7 |    7.89641 |  7.92017 |    7.89641 | 0.9969991 |
   |   8 |    7.89165 |  7.91540 |    7.89165 | 0.9969991 |
   +-----+------------+----------+------------+-----------+

   The lerp copies its target, as it must with k = 1, and the target
   is 0.0238 below speed.  A ratio of 0.9969991 is a tilt of 0.0774
   off row 2 - which is EXACTLY the velDir lateral both sides read at
   the frame boundary, -0.077389 on MOTO and -0.077388 on the port.

   TWO COUPLERS FOUND AND REMOVED, AND THE NUMBER DID NOT MOVE.

   o race.c had `s_race_drive.gearboxRoadSpeed = r->speed` at the end
     of the tick, right after a drive_load that already carries the
     racer's own gearboxRoadSpeed across.  It was the last fragment
     of the straight-line stand-in and it threw away the tail lerp's
     result every frame.  Removed.
   o The imported post-impact resync was placed OUTSIDE the wall
     block, so physics_sync_road_speed - which sets gbox = speed -
     ran on every frame and re-welded them exactly as the deleted
     line had.  In the original that run sits inside the impact path,
     after the 1 km/h floor.  Moved in.

   THE THIRD WAS A BARE CLAMP, and the per-stage trace named it in one
   run:

   +------------------+----------+----------+----------+
   | stage            | gbox     | speed    | sp-gb    |
   +==================+==========+==========+==========+
   | after the tail   |  7.92490 |  7.92490 | +0.00000 |
   | after drive_store|  7.92010 |  7.94379 | +0.02370 |
   | after steering   |  7.92010 |  7.94379 | +0.02370 |
   | after integrate  |  7.92010 |  7.92010 | +0.00000 |
   +------------------+----------+----------+----------+

   The TAIL WAS PRODUCING THE SPLIT CORRECTLY ALL ALONG - +0.02370
   against MOTO's +0.0238 - and it survived drive_store and the
   steering.  Between there and racer_integrate sat

       if (s_race_drive.speed > s_race_drive.gearboxRoadSpeed)
               s_race_drive.speed = s_race_drive.gearboxRoadSpeed;

   pinning speed so it could never exceed road speed.  THE ORIGINAL
   RUNS WITH speed ABOVE gearboxRoadSpeed - 7.9154 against 7.8916 two
   frames after the glance - so the rule is simply not the game's.
   Removed; the six-state matrix stays exact.

   THREE COUPLERS, ALL PORT-SIDE, EACH MASKING THE NEXT: the stand-in
   at the end of the tick, a misplaced Gearbox_SyncRoadSpeed, and this
   clamp.  Removing the first two showed no change at all, which is
   why the trace was worth re-adding rather than reasoning further.

   WHAT IS LEFT, and it is a different shape: the GAP now matches
   exactly, +0.0238 on both, but the port's speed sits one tilt-step
   HIGH - ours 7.9391 where MOTO reads 7.9153, and our gearboxRoadSpeed
   equals MOTO's speed.  The port is one step behind the original in
   the same chain rather than missing a term.  The end-of-run figure
   is unchanged at +0.026.

   HOOKING BOTH SetLongSpeed CALLS across the approach shows what the
   original's chain looks like on a normal frame:

   +-----+----------+-----------+----------+-----------+
   | hit | arg      | speed in  | gbox in  | arg-speed |
   +=====+==========+===========+==========+===========+
   |   3 |  15.0422 |   15.0471 |  15.0471 |  -0.00487 |
   |   4 |  15.0293 |   15.0342 |  15.0342 |  -0.00487 |
   |   7 |  14.9908 |   14.9957 |  14.9957 |  -0.00485 |
   |   8 |   7.9249 |    7.9249 |  14.9829 |  +0.00000 |
   +-----+----------+-----------+----------+-----------+

   On every ordinary frame MOTO enters the call with speed EQUAL to
   gearboxRoadSpeed and an argument a flat 0.00486 below - roll plus
   drag - so the two never drift on a straight.  Hit 8 is the impact
   call from ground contact: speed already halved, gearboxRoadSpeed
   not yet, argument equal to the reduced speed.  That is the shape
   physics_sync_road_speed then resolves.

   STOPPING HERE.  The residual is 0.3 per cent on a path the game
   reaches only by hitting a rail, and three consecutive attempts at
   it have each been a correct structural fix that left the number
   where it was.  What it needs is not another edit but a frame-by-
   frame diff of the whole SetLongSpeed chain on BOTH sides, which is
   a session of its own.  [open]

   MIND THE WALLS WHEN MEASURING.  A matrix run with nowalls OFF is
   worthless: the turn states corner hard for twenty frames, MOTO
   hits the rails and the MOTO column goes to 0.0000 and 6.7508.  The
   glance needs walls ON and everything else needs them OFF, and the
   MOTO column is the tell - if its numbers moved, the environment
   did.

   SO THE TILT EXISTS ON BOTH AND ONLY MOTO'S REACHES THE LERP.  The
   port's lng comes out equal to speed, so its gbox and speed stay
   welded and it carries 0.026 more speed away from the rail.  That
   is the same open question as 9.18.23.4 - a tilt present at the
   frame boundary that the port's tail does not consume - and it is
   now seen on two unrelated paths, braking turn-in and a wall
   glance.  Whatever the answer is, it is one answer.  [open]

   THE GENERAL RULE THIS LEAVES: anything that moves r->speed WITHOUT
   going through the gearbox must sync gearboxRoadSpeed too, or the
   drivetrain hands the speed straight back a frame later.

9.18.23.4.  The frame-2 0.006 is a TILT the tail does not consume

   Hooking 0x00490158 - the instruction after the tail lerp stores
   gearboxRoadSpeed - and capturing the lerp TARGET alongside speed
   shows what the decoupling actually is:

   +-----+------------+------------+------------+
   | hit | gbox after | speed      | lng target |
   +=====+============+============+============+
   |   1 |   19.82688 |   19.82688 |   19.82688 |
   |   2 |   19.65801 |   19.66402 |   19.65801 |
   |   3 |   19.48944 |   19.48944 |   19.48944 |
   +-----+------------+------------+------------+

   The lerp does exactly what it should - with k = 1 it copies the
   target - and on hit 2 the TARGET itself is 0.006 below speed.  So
   the question was never the lerp: it is that lng != speed, which
   happens precisely when velDir is not parallel to row 2.

   lng/speed = 0.999694 gives a tilt of 0.0245, about 1.4 degrees.

   THE PORT PRODUCES THE SAME TILT.  Stepping one frame at a time,
   the port's velDir at the end of frame ONE reads longitudinal
   0.999699 against MOTO's 0.999694 - the same number - and its
   LATERAL part is only -0.000386, so the tilt is almost entirely
   VERTICAL, not a slip angle.  By the end of frame two both games
   are flat again at 1.000000.

   So both sides create the tilt, both lose it a frame later, and the
   only difference is that MOTO's tail CONSUMES it on frame two and
   the port's does not.  The tilt is gone by the time our
   physics_drivetrain_tail computes lng.

   RULED OUT while narrowing: physics_set_long_speed does preserve
   the other two body components (it replaces only b[2]), and its
   stray + m[9+i] translation term is inert because matOrientation
   [9..11] reads 0,0,0 on BOTH sides.

   NOT THE GROUND FIT.  racer_update_ground_contact touches velDir in
   exactly one place - the wall-bounce set_slip_speed added in
   9.18.23.3 - and that branch does not run while braking in a turn.

   A WATCHPOINT ON velDir NAMES THE WRITERS.  racer+0x130 on MOTO,
   six braking-in-turn frames, and the same three fire every frame in
   the same order:

   +------------+--------------------------+-------------+
   | eip        | function                 | per frame   |
   +============+==========================+=============+
   | 0x0048d6cb | ModelInstance_Integrate  | once        |
   +------------+--------------------------+-------------+
   | 0x0048d5ee | Racer_SetSlipSpeed       | once        |
   +------------+--------------------------+-------------+
   | 0x0048ceee | Racer_SetLongSpeed       | TWICE       |
   +------------+--------------------------+-------------+

   TWICE.  The port calls physics_set_long_speed ONCE, from the
   drivetrain tail, and physics.c has carried a note about the second
   call for as long as the tail has existed: "A SECOND SetLongSpeed
   CALL SITS HERE, worth about -0.0065 a frame on both speeds at 24
   m/s - roughly a quarter of the drag.  The call is located; its
   argument is not quantified, so it is left out rather than guessed
   at."

   That omission is the strongest remaining candidate for the 0.006.
   SetLongSpeed is precisely the function that rebuilds velDir while
   PRESERVING the other two body components, so a missing second call
   is a missing rebuild - and the symptom is a tilt that MOTO still
   has when its tail reads velDir and the port does not.  The sizes
   agree too: the note says 0.0065 a frame and the gap is 0.006.

   BOTH CALLS READ.  Hooking 0x0048cbe5 - six bytes into
   Racer_SetLongSpeed, where the argument sits at ESP+0x84 (the very
   next instruction compares against it) - and capturing the argument
   with the instance state:

   +------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------------+
   | call | arg v     | speed in  | gbox in   | arg - gbox |
   +======+===========+===========+===========+============+
   | 1st  |  19.67410 |  19.82687 |  19.67410 |   +0.00000 |
   | 2nd  |  19.67170 |  19.68011 |  19.67410 |   -0.00240 |
   +------+-----------+-----------+-----------+------------+

   THE FIRST CALL IS THE TAIL and its argument is gearboxRoadSpeed
   exactly.  Look at what it PRODUCES: the second call comes in with
   speed 19.68011 against a longitudinal of 19.67410 - the 0.006.  So
   the decoupling is Racer_SetLongSpeed itself rebuilding speed as
   |w| while preserving the other two body components; it only shows
   when velDir carries a tilt.

   THE SECOND CALL IS 0x004693aa, AND IT IS ALREADY A MARKED HOLE.
   racer.c carries the line `0x004693aa the drivetrain resync. A
   marked hole.` inside racer_update_ground_contact.  The site:

       0046938d  FLD  [EBX]
       0046938f  FCOMP double [0x004d0f70]     ; 0.2777778, 1 km/h
       0046939c  MOV  [EBX], 0x3e8e38e4        ; ...floored to it
       004693a5  MOV  EAX,[EBX]                ; the argument
       004693a7  PUSH EAX
       004693aa  CALL Racer_SetLongSpeed

   THE GUARD IS ALREADY TRANSCRIBED.  racer.c does exactly that
   floor, against RACER_SPEED_1KMH = 0.2777778 at 0x004d0f70,
   immediately before the hole - so the port has the clamp and is
   missing only the call that follows it.

   AND IT IS NOT THE GROUND-CONTACT SITE AFTER ALL.  Capturing the
   RETURN ADDRESS at the callee hook - ESP+0x80, one slot below the
   argument - names both callers outright:

   +------+-------------+------------------------------------------+
   | call | returns to  | site                                     |
   +======+=============+==========================================+
   | 1st  |  0x0048fbc1 | 0x0048fbbc, maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain |
   | 2nd  |  0x0048feca | 0x0048fec5, the tail the port HAS        |
   +------+-------------+------------------------------------------+

   Both are inside TickDrivetrain.  0x004693aa in ground contact does
   not fire while braking in a turn at all, so tracing EBX there was
   chasing the wrong site - EBX is &r->speed (0x004692a7, LEA EBX,
   [ESI+0x124]) and that call simply is not on this path.

   THE MISSING CALL IS 0x0048fbbc, and physics.c has been pointing at
   it the whole time: "A SECOND SetLongSpeed CALL SITS HERE, worth
   about -0.0065 a frame".  Its argument:

       0048fba8  FSUB [ESP+0x18]     ; X - A
       0048fbac  SUB  ESP,4
       0048fbb1  FMUL [ESP+0x20]     ; * k    (pre-SUB [ESP+0x1c])
       0048fbb5  FADD [ESP+0x1c]     ; + A    (pre-SUB [ESP+0x18])
       0048fbb9  FSTP [ESP]
       0048fbbc  CALL Racer_SetLongSpeed

   arg = (X - A) * k + A - the grip-lerp idiom for the fifth time in
   this function.  With k = 1.0, which is what physics_grip returns
   for MotoType 2, arg = X, and X measured EXACTLY equal to
   gearboxRoadSpeed (19.67410 against 19.67410).

   ONE CORRECTION TO THE PLACEMENT: the port's note sat inside
   physics_tick_drivetrain, the DRIVE branch, but 0x0048fbbc fires on
   every braking frame too.  It belongs in the shared part of the
   function, not the drive-only slice.

   ADDED at the head of physics_drivetrain_tail, which is where
   0x0048fbbc sits relative to the slope at 0x0048fdf0, the roll at
   0x0048fe30 and the second call at 0x0048fec5.  Transcribed at
   k = 1 with A named as unread.

   RESULT: BOTH BRAKE STATES WENT EXACT.

   +-----------------+----------+----------+
   | state           | was      | now      |
   +=================+==========+==========+
   | straight brake  |  -0.0610 |  +0.0000 |
   | turn brake      |  -0.0611 |  -0.0000 |
   | straight coast  |  -0.5231 |  -0.5208 |
   | straight accel  |  +0.0000 |  +0.0000 |
   | turn accel      |  +0.0000 |  +0.0000 |
   +-----------------+----------+----------+

   6.8254 against 6.8254 and 6.8250 against 6.8250 after twenty
   frames of braking.  Nothing regressed - the call fires every frame
   and both acceleration states held exactly.  [verified]

   PER FRAME the run is now exact from frame four on: +0.000006,
   then 0.000000 at every sample to the end.

   THE FRAME-2 EVENT ITSELF STILL DOES NOT REPRODUCE - MOTO reads
   sp-gb +0.00601 there and the port -0.00000 - but it no longer
   ACCUMULATES.  The port is 0.006 low for that one frame and then
   tracks to six decimals, which is why the endpoint is exact.  It is
   the first tick after a teleport, the same frame that carries the
   coast placement transient of 9.18.25, and it is very likely the
   same cause: a frame the game never runs.  [open, low value]

   THE 0.006 REMAINS UNEXPLAINED after four eliminations.  It is one
   frame in twenty, it largely cancels by frame 4 (-0.031 per cent
   then -0.001), and the end-of-run -0.065 per cent is accumulation
   rather than this.  Recorded rather than chased further.


9.19.  THE MotoType 1 GRIP CURVE, MEASURED

   The last unmeasured piece of the drivetrain, and the one that
   needed a dirt bike. 9.10.2 decoded it statically; this is it
   running.

9.19.1.  The curve is exact

   Two hooks at the JOIN POINTS of the grip block - the address all
   four arms fall through to, so a hit is recorded whether or not
   the curve was applied - capturing grip, |slip| as a full double,
   and speed [verified]:

   +--------------+-------------+---------------+-----------+----------+
   | |slip|       | grip seen   | 1-0.05*slip   | speed     | km/h     |
   +==============+=============+===============+===========+==========+
   | 0.576639     | 0.971168    | 0.971168      | 27.403    | 98.7     |
   +--------------+-------------+---------------+-----------+----------+
   | 1.17717      | 0.941142    | 0.941142      | 3.201     | 11.5     |
   +--------------+-------------+---------------+-----------+----------+
   | 3.05132      | 0.847434    | 0.847434      | 6.722     | 24.2     |
   +--------------+-------------+---------------+-----------+----------+
   | 3.05132      | 0.847434    | 0.847434      | 7.082     | 25.5     |
   +--------------+-------------+---------------+-----------+----------+
   | 1.95e-07     | 1.000000    | not applied   | 0.209     | 0.8      |
   +--------------+-------------+---------------+-----------+----------+
   | 5.39e-06     | 1.000000    | not applied   | 2.219     | 8.0      |
   +--------------+-------------+---------------+-----------+----------+

   o SIX DECIMAL PLACES ON EVERY IN-BAND SAMPLE. grip = 1.0 -
     0.05 * |slip| is confirmed, and with it the constants
     _DAT_004d1d58 = -0.05 and _DAT_004d1c00 = 1.0.

   o AND THE LOWER BOUND BEHAVES AS READ. At |slip| around 1e-7 -
     which is what "straight ahead" looks like in floating point -
     grip is exactly 1.0, left at the value stored before the block
     at 0x0048f7ef and never overwritten. The 0.01 test at
     _DAT_004d1d48 is doing its job.

   o SLIP RISES AS SPEED FALLS in a donut: 0.577 at 98.7 km/h,
     3.05 at 25 km/h. That is the geometry of the manoeuvre rather
     than anything in the model - a tight low-speed spin puts a
     larger fraction of the velocity sideways.

9.19.2.  Which copy runs is not fixed

   The drivetrain holds SIX copies of this block and the donut
   capture caught copy 3 and copy 6 ON CONSECUTIVE FRAMES, never
   copy 4.

   o SO A HOOK ON ONE COPY PROVES NOTHING WHEN IT IS SILENT. A first
     attempt hooked copy 4's join, ran a full donut and recorded
     ZERO hits. Read on its own that looks like "the grip curve did
     not run", which is exactly the false negative 9.18.2 is about.
     What settled it was hooking maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed itself and
     capturing the RETURN ADDRESS off ESP, which names the caller in
     one run.

   o THAT TECHNIQUE GENERALISES. When a helper is called from
     several places and the question is WHICH, one hook at its entry
     capturing ESP+0 beats N hooks at the call sites, and costs one
     slot instead of four.

9.19.3.  The 20.0 bound, and why it is probably a guard

   NOT OBSERVED. The largest |slip| a donut produced was 3.05, and
   the bound is 20.0 - so this run says nothing about it either way,
   so this run says nothing about it either way. [open]

   What can be said from the code:

   o PAST |slip| = 20 THE LINEAR FORM WOULD GO NEGATIVE, and grip is
     the lerp factor in gearboxRoadSpeed = lerp(gearboxRoadSpeed,
     bodyForwardVel, grip). A negative lerp factor EXTRAPOLATES
     instead of interpolating, which runs away. So an upper bound
     has to exist. [inferred]

   o WHAT IS ODD IS THE VALUE IT FALLS BACK TO. Clamping to 0 would
     mean "no grip at all", the natural continuation of a curve that
     has just reached zero. Instead grip keeps its pre-initialised
     1.0, so the bike goes from ZERO grip to FULL grip the instant
     slip crosses 20 - the wheels snap to real velocity precisely
     when the bike is most sideways. Read as physics that is
     backwards; read as a guard written to stop a divergence it is
     ordinary. [inferred]

   o REACHING IT NEEDS ABOUT 20 m/s OF PURELY LATERAL VELOCITY -
     72 km/h sideways - because |slip| is a body-space velocity
     component in m/s like every other speed in this subsystem
     (9.17.10). A donut cannot get there; it is a low-speed
     manoeuvre by construction. A high-speed broadside slide is the
     scenario that would, and the dirt bike tops out at 46 m/s.

9.19.4.  maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed has six callers, not one

   Hooking its entry and capturing the return address, one frame
   [verified]:

   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | Return       | Function                       | Why it wants lateral slip        |
   +==============+================================+==================================+
   | 0045d67e     | maybe_Racer_Tick               | unread                           |
   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 0045d161     | maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering     | unread                           |
   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 00466251     | maybe_Racer_ApplySteering      | unread                           |
   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 00466f39     | FUN_00466d70                   | unread - and this is one of the  |
   |              |                                | four ILLEGAL VERSION sites       |
   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 00406733     | maybe_Snd_TickRacer            | AUDIO - but the RESULT IS        |
   |              |                                | DISCARDED, so it is dead code    |
   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 0048f5e4     | maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain   | grip block, copy 3               |
   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 0048ff6f     | maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain   | grip block, copy 6               |
   +--------------+--------------------------------+----------------------------------+

   o SIX CALLS PER FRAME, and it had been treated throughout 9.10
     as a drivetrain helper. It is a general lateral-slip utility
     the whole racer tick leans on.

   o THE AUDIO CALLER LOOKS LIKE THE INTERESTING ONE AND IS NOT.
     maybe_Snd_TickRacer appears to take lateral slip as a tyre-skid
     input, connecting physics to the mixer. IT DOES NOT: the call
     at 0x0040672e is followed immediately by
     FSTP ST0 - the result is popped and thrown away - and
     maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed never writes its receiver, so there is
     no side effect either. IT IS DEAD CODE. [verified]
     audio-cues.txt 9.7 had already recorded the discard; this
     note was written without checking it, and a guess that
     contradicts an existing document should have been caught
     before it was written down rather than after.

   o AND FUN_00466d70 IS AN ILLEGAL VERSION SITE
     (copy-protection.txt 5.3). Its use of slip is unread and the
     function is on the do-not-disturb list, so read it, do not
     rename or patch anything in it.

9.21.  THE BRAKE PATH - measured, and it has a ramp

   The last unmeasured piece of the drivetrain, and 9.10.3's outline
   turned out to be right as far as it went and to be missing the
   most interesting thing in it.

9.21.1.  The model

       force = clamp(Frein, 10.0, 140.0)
       if (brakeHoldMs < 200 && rampOverride == 0)
           force *= 1.0/6.0
       step = force * (1/3.6) * dtMs * 0.001
       roadSpeed -= step        toward zero from either sign,
                                clamped to exactly 0 on overshoot
       brakeHoldMs += dtMs      zeroed whenever the block is skipped

   o THE 1/3.6 IS THE km/h TO m/s CONVERSION, the same constant the
     speedometer uses in the other direction (9.17.10). So Frein is
     authored in km/h like the numbers a player sees, and this bike's
     80 means 80 km/h of speed shed per second at full strength.

   o THE SIGN HANDLING IS DONE ON FLOAT BITS AS SIGNED INTEGERS -
     TEST EAX,EAX at 0x0048f9fc and CMP EAX,0x80000000 at 0x0048fa16.
     That is the compiler's idiom for a float sign test, not a trick
     worth reproducing, and 0x80000000 is there so -0.0f sorts as
     zero.

9.21.2.  There is a 200 millisecond ramp-in at one sixth strength

   ONE HARD STOP IN A STRAIGHT LINE, eight consecutive frames
   [verified]:

   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | Frame | holdMs  | dt ms   | force      | step        | roadSpeed   |
   +=======+=========+=========+============+=============+=============+
   | 1     | 0       | 36      | 13.3333    | 0.133333    | 12.7854     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | 2     | 36      | 33      | 13.3333    | 0.122222    | 12.6383     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | 3     | 69      | 33      | 13.3333    | 0.122222    | 12.5077     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | 4     | 102     | 34      | 13.3333    | 0.125926    | 12.3773     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | 5     | 136     | 33      | 13.3333    | 0.122222    | 12.2432     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | 6     | 169     | 33      | 13.3333    | 0.122222    | 12.1133     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | 7     | 202     | 33      | 80.0000    | 0.733333    | 11.9836     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+
   | 8     | 235     | 33      | 80.0000    | 0.733333    | 11.2441     |
   +-------+---------+---------+------------+-------------+-------------+

   o THE FORCE SWITCHES FROM 13.3333 TO 80.0 BETWEEN FRAME 6 AND
     FRAME 7 - from Frein/6 to Frein - and 13.3333 is exactly 80/6.
     A brake that has just been applied is SIX TIMES WEAKER than one
     that has been held for a fifth of a second.

   o WHAT THE COUNTER COUNTS IS MILLISECONDS. A write watchpoint on
     racer+0x0b0 caught the ladder 0, 36, 69, 102, 136, 169, 202,
     235, 268 - increments of 36, 33, 33, 34, 33, 33, 33, 33, which
     are the per-frame dt values recovered independently from the
     step. It crosses 200 on frame 6 and the force changes on frame
     7, which is the ordering the code implies: the counter is read
     at 0x0048f9aa near the top of the block and advanced at
     0x0048fbe1 near the bottom.

   o SO IT IS A TIME RAMP, NOT A FRAME COUNT, and that matters for a
     port in the opposite direction to the air timer of 9.18.4. The
     air step is a hardcoded per-frame constant and is framerate
     dependent; this one accumulates real dt and is not. Two
     counters, twenty lines apart, on opposite sides of the same
     question.

   o AND THE STEP TRACKS dt EXACTLY. Frames 1 and 4 ran 36 ms and
     34 ms and their steps are 0.133333 and 0.125926, both exactly
     force * (1/3.6) * dt * 0.001. The dt was not assumed - it was
     recovered from the step and then confirmed against the
     counter's increments.

9.21.3.  The integration is brake plus drag, and nothing else large

   Predicting the next frame's road speed as
   road - step - drag, with drag from 9.17.9.3 at the ground
   coefficient:

       frame 2 to 7    predicted low by +0.0022 every time
       frame 8         low by +0.0033

   o SO BRAKE AND DRAG TOGETHER ACCOUNT FOR THE WHOLE
     DECELERATION to within a fifth of a per cent, and the residual
     is a small CONSTANT ADDITION rather than a proportional error.

   o THE SLOPE TERM FITS IT. 9.10.4 has gravity resolved along the
     bike's forward vector as -dirY * dt * 0.004905. A residual of
     +0.0022 at dt 33 needs dirY = -0.0136 - a very slight downhill,
     which is what a dirt track straight would be. NOT CONFIRMED:
     dirY was not captured on this run and the arithmetic is a fit,
     not a reading. [open]

9.21.4.  You cannot brake in a corner on a dirt bike

   The block is not always reached. From 0x0048f904:

       if (!braking && !preRace)                  skip entirely
       if (turnBrakeFlag || rampOverride || preRace)  run it
       otherwise      zero the counter and skip

   o turnBrakeFlag IS racer+0x0ac, is written ONLY for a human on a
     MotoType 1 bike, and 9.18.7 caught it toggling. THIS IS ITS
     CONSUMER, at 0x0048f915. It is cleared while braking and
     steering at once, so on a dirt bike leaning into a turn and
     grabbing the brake SKIPS DECELERATION ALTOGETHER.

   o 9.10.1 HAD ALREADY SAID SO - "inst+0x88 is the consumer, in the
     coast branch of this function" - and 9.18.7 then recorded "what
     READS it is still unread [open]" without checking. The open
     question was answered before it was asked. Corrected there.

   o AND THE BLOCK RUNS EVERY FRAME OF THE GRID COUNTDOWN, because
     the pre-race flag at 0x004dc0b4 is one of the three ways in and
     the throttle demand is zero. TWO CAPTURE RUNS WERE SPENT
     FINDING THAT OUT - the first was written off as "the hook only
     fires while braking", which was wrong. A hook budget is spent
     by whatever reaches the address, not by what the address is
     for.

9.21.5.  Constants

   The 4 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/physics.c.

   o rampOverride IS [ESP+0x7c], computed once at 0x0048efab from a
     field two pointers deep off instance+0xe4. It read 0 on every
     captured frame, so the ramp was never bypassed that way and
     what it selects is unread. [open]

9.21.6.  Transcribed

   src/physics.c gains physics_brake_force, physics_brake_step,
   physics_apply_brake and physics_brake_gated, and MR_Drivetrain
   gains brakeHoldMs. tools/check_physics.py gains sixteen brake
   cases covering both sides of the ramp and all three dt values,
   and passes.

   o THE COUNTER RESET IS THE PART A PORT WILL GET WRONG.
     physics_apply_brake advances brakeHoldMs but cannot know when
     the brake was released; the original zeroes it at 0x0048f930 on
     the path that skips the block. A reconstruction that forgets
     that has a bike braking at full strength from the first frame,
     six times too hard, and the symptom is "braking feels too
     strong" rather than anything that looks like a missing reset.

9.22.  THE SLOPE TERM - and the drivetrain closes

   The last unexplained term. 9.10.4 read it out of the instructions
   and 9.17.8.3 and 9.21.3 each fitted its magnitude indirectly; this
   is it measured.

9.22.1.  The model

       if (airTime != 0)  slope = 0
       else               slope = -dirY * dtMs * 0.004905
       if (slope > 0.55)  slope = 0.55

   at 0x0048fdac to 0x0048fdee, guarded so it only runs when the
   racer is moving faster than 0.1 or a flag at [ESP+0x88] is clear.

   o 0.004905 IS 9.81/2000 - gravity for the third time in this
     subsystem, after Engine_TorqueAt's kgf.m conversion and the
     air-timer step. DOWNHILL ACCELERATES because dirY is negative
     pointing down and the expression opens with FCHS.

9.22.2.  Verified to 1.00000 on every frame

   Eight consecutive frames rolling down a hill [verified]:

   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | Hit   | slope       | dirY        | dt ms   | speed    | predicted   | ratio    |
   +=======+=============+=============+=========+==========+=============+==========+
   | 1     | 0.025227    | -0.025715   | 200     | 7.6205   | 0.025227    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | 2     | 0.016271    | -0.025715   | 129     | 7.3694   | 0.016271    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | 3     | 0.004036    | -0.025715   | 32      | 7.3080   | 0.004036    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | 4     | 0.004919    | -0.025714   | 39      | 7.2362   | 0.004919    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | 5     | 0.004162    | -0.025715   | 33      | 7.1760   | 0.004162    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | 6     | 0.042591    | -0.263128   | 33      | 7.3188   | 0.042591    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | 7     | 0.043886    | -0.263151   | 34      | 7.0935   | 0.043886    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+
   | 8     | 0.042595    | -0.263151   | 33      | 7.0739   | 0.042595    | 1.00000  |
   +-------+-------------+-------------+---------+----------+-------------+----------+

   o TWO SLOPES TEN TIMES APART AND FIVE FRAME TIMES, which is a
     better test than one long descent would have been - the term is
     confirmed across a range rather than at a single value, and the
     200 ms and 129 ms frames at the start exercise the dt scaling
     far outside the usual 33.

   o SO dirY IS WHAT 9.8.2 AND 9.10.4 SAID IT WAS. The inference
     chain - matOrientation row3 + row2, normalised, Y component,
     used as the bike's forward direction in world space - now has a
     measurement behind it rather than only a consumer.

9.22.3.  Gravity is switched off in the air

   0x0048fdb4 tests airTime with the same sign-masked idiom as the
   drag selector (9.18.5) and leaves the term at the zero stored at
   0x0048fdac.

   o SO A JUMP IS PURE BALLISTICS as far as the drivetrain is
     concerned: no gravity along the forward axis, and 5.33 times
     the drag. Whatever pulls the bike down is elsewhere. That is
     the second thing the air timer gates and it was found by
     reading this block rather than by looking for it.

9.22.4.  The 0.55 clamp is a long-frame guard, not a slope limit

   dirY is a component of a NORMALISED vector, so |dirY| <= 1. What
   it takes to reach 0.55:

   +------------+------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | Frame      | dirY needed            | Verdict                            |
   +============+========================+====================================+
   | 33 ms      | less than -3.40        | IMPOSSIBLE - dirY is a component   |
   |            |                        | of a normalised vector             |
   +------------+------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | 100 ms     | less than -1.12        | impossible for the same reason     |
   +------------+------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | 200 ms     | less than -0.56        | reachable on a steep slope         |
   +------------+------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | 500 ms     | less than -0.22        | REACHABLE - and -0.263 was         |
   |            |                        | observed on an ordinary hill       |
   +------------+------------------------+------------------------------------+

   o IT CANNOT FIRE AT A NORMAL FRAMERATE. At 33 ms the clamp needs
     a dirY of -3.4, which no unit vector has. Anyone reading this
     block statically would reasonably call it dead code.

   o IT IS NOT DEAD - IT IS THE SAME IDEA AS THE dt CAP. As the
     frame stretches the threshold falls, and at the 500 ms cap
     (_DAT_004d1dac, 9.17.1) a dirY of -0.225 is enough. The
     steepest slope in this capture was -0.263, on an ordinary hill.
     So on a stutter, on a slope the player rides every lap, the
     clamp fires. It exists to stop one long frame delivering an
     enormous gravity impulse - exactly what the dt cap twenty lines
     earlier exists for. [inferred - the arithmetic is exact, the
     intent is a reading]

   o AND IT IS ONE-SIDED. Uphill values are not bounded, because the
     comparison is only against the upper limit. A long frame going
     uphill can take away as much speed as it likes.

9.22.5.  The drivetrain is closed

   Every term in maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain's accelerate, brake and
   coast paths is now measured against the running game:

       engine gain    ratio 1.000    9.17.7.2, 9.17.8.1
       torque curve   0.1-0.3 pct    9.17.1, two bikes
       drag           0.999-1.000    9.17.9.3
       grip curve     6 dp           9.19.1
       brake          exact          9.21.2
       slope          1.00000        this section

   o NOTHING IN THE DRIVETRAIN IS STILL A GUESS. What remains
     unwritten is unread code - the gearbox resync's engineSpeed
     side, the second SetLongSpeed, FUN_0048eb40's lerp factor and
     the low-speed guard on the power division - and each is named
     at the point in src/physics.c where it belongs.

9.22.6.  A note on how the last three runs went

   THREE CAPTURES WERE SPENT ON THE SAME MISTAKE and it is worth
   recording because it is not the obvious one. The hook budget is
   eight hits; this block runs EVERY FRAME; so eight hits is a
   quarter of a second, and it is spent wherever the game happens to
   be a quarter of a second after the user unpauses.

   o TWICE THAT WAS A STATIONARY BIKE, and both traces read
     speed 0.0, dirY 0.0, slope -0.0 - perfectly valid data about a
     state nobody wanted to measure.

   o THE FIX IS NOT A BETTER TRACK. It is to arm while the condition
     already holds, which for a per-frame block means pausing INSIDE
     the manoeuvre. The user asked whether the small hills were the
     problem; they were not, and saying so saved a fourth run.

   o AND THE THIRD RUN SUCCEEDED WITHOUT AN UNPAUSE. The capture had
     already happened during the ride TOWARD the hill - the data was
     sitting in the ring while the game sat paused at the top of it.
     Check the trace before asking for another run.

9.23.  THE WHEELIE AND THE AIR TIMER, TRANSCRIBED - and 1.6 was
       wrong

   The drivetrain of 9.20 to 9.22 took three inputs from its caller:
   airTime, wheelieScale and slipAbs. Two of the three are now
   written, so the only value physics.c still asks somebody else for
   is the lateral slip.

9.23.1.  The wheelie in full

   maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie, 0x004660f0, read end to end:

       if (racer+0x30c1 != 0)      RESET - angle, timer, request and
                                   racer+0x31f9 cleared, scale 1.0
       else if (request && timer < 200.0)
           angle += dt * 0.03*PI/2         clamped to PI/2
           timer += dt
       else if (angle > 0)
           angle += dt * -0.016*PI/2
           if it crossed zero:  racer+0x2e95 = -32.0f, angle = 0

       scale = angle != 0 ? (g_codeCheckPassed ? 2.0 : 1.2) : 1.0

   o THE CAP GATES THE RAMP, NOT THE ANGLE. Once the timer reaches
     200 the ramp stops and the DECAY branch runs even with the
     button still held. 9.17.3 saw exactly that live - timer
     200.1001, angle decayed to nothing, thump fired once, button
     never released - and this is the code that does it.

   o THE THUMP IS ONE-SHOT AND IT IS A WRITE TO ANOTHER FIELD.
     racer+0x2e95 takes -32.0f only on the frame the angle crosses
     zero, guarded by the float sign test CMP EAX,0x80000000 / JBE.

9.23.2.  THE MULTIPLIER IS 1.2, NOT 1.6

   9.11.2 and the field map both said "1.6 while the angle is up, and
   2.0 when g_codeCheckPassed is set", and summarised it as "a
   wheelie is worth 60 per cent more engine torque". THE IMMEDIATE AT
   0x004661e8 IS 0x3f99999a, WHICH IS 1.2. 1.6 would be 0x3fcccccd.

   o SO A WHEELIE DOUBLES TORQUE ON A STOCK COPY - 2.0, because
     g_codeCheckPassed reads 1 in ours (9.20.2) - and is worth only
     twenty per cent on a cracked one.

   o WHICH MAKES IT A SIXTH PROTECTION PENALTY AND THE HARSHEST ONE
     FOUND. copy-protection.txt has NbSpeed losing a gear and
     Engine_TorqueAt losing its 1.1x; this is losing HALF the boost,
     and like the others it is silent.

   o HOW IT SURVIVED: "60 per cent more torque" is a plausible
     number, nothing downstream contradicted it, and it was written
     once and then cited rather than re-read. The same failure as
     9.18.2 and 9.19's audio guess. A CONSTANT COPIED OUT OF A
     DISASSEMBLY SHOULD BE CHECKED AGAINST ITS HEX, not against
     whether it sounds right - 0x3f99999a and 0x3fcccccd look alike
     at a glance and differ by a third.

9.23.3.  Four time bases in one subsystem

   +--------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------+
   | Quantity                       | Time base        | Consequence                        |
   +================================+==================+====================================+
   | the wheelie ramp and timer     | SCALED, 3.3      | cap 200 -> 2.00 s                  |
   +--------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------+
   | the brake hold counter         | MILLISECONDS     | threshold 200 -> 0.2 s             |
   +--------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------+
   | the drivetrain, all of it      | MILLISECONDS     | dt*10 at entry, capped at 500      |
   +--------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------+
   | the air timer                  | PER FRAME        | no dt at all - framerate dependent |
   |                                |                  | by construction                    |
   +--------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------+

   o THE WHEELIE AND THE BRAKE BOTH USE A THRESHOLD OF 200 AND THEY
     MEAN DIFFERENT THINGS - 2.00 seconds and 0.2 seconds. Nothing
     in either function says which unit it is in; it has to come
     from the caller, and the drivetrain rescales its own dt at
     entry while the wheelie does not.

   o A PORT THAT NORMALISES THESE "for consistency" CHANGES THE
     GAME. The air timer in particular is framerate dependent on
     purpose - rule 5 - and the wheelie's two-second cap depends on
     the tick's dt being 3.3.

9.23.4.  Constants

   The 2 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/physics.c.

9.23.5.  What is written and what is not

   o WRITTEN, WHOLE: racer_update_wheelie - the crash reset
     included - and physics_air_time, with eight air-timer cases
     reproducing the captured ladder to 2e-5 and eight wheelie
     cases covering the ramp, the cap, the decay with the button
     held, the thump and both scale branches. It takes MR_Racer
     rather than a state of its own, so the timer and the torque
     scale reach their consumers and not only the angle.

   o THE RAMP IS CONFIRMED AGAINST RETAIL frame for frame from a
     matched placement: 0.6220/0.6221, 1.2441/1.2441, then 1.5708
     on both sides. [verified]

   o THE VISIBLE TILT IS NOT THE RAMP. It is the ramp MINUS the
     suspension angle, and the second term is DRIVEN BY THE
     ENGINE. racer_update_suspension chases
     chassisAttitude - headingRef + forkDive, and forkDive is a
     peak hold of longitudinal ACCELERATION - so torque reaches
     the picture. Traced on MOTO from a standing start, holding
     stunt:

     +--------+------+--------+----------+---------+-----------+
     | frame  | gear | engine | forkDive | suspDeg | tilt      |
     +========+======+========+==========+=========+===========+
     | 12-24  |    1 |  4.5-7 |   -0.096 | -11.247 | 56.25     |
     +--------+------+--------+----------+---------+-----------+
     | 27     |    2 |    3.8 |   -0.062 | -10.285 | 55.29     |
     +--------+------+--------+----------+---------+-----------+
     | 42     |    2 |    6.5 |   -0.092 |  -3.219 | 48.22     |
     +--------+------+--------+----------+---------+-----------+
     | 48     |    2 |    7.0 |   -0.032 |  -4.945 | 49.95     |
     +--------+------+--------+----------+---------+-----------+
     | 51     |    3 |    4.5 |   -0.043 |  -3.429 | 48.43     |
     +--------+------+--------+----------+---------+-----------+

     Hard acceleration pins the suspension at its AngleSuspension
     clamp and the bike sits at maximum tilt; nearing the redline
     the acceleration falls, forkDive decays and the tilt
     unwinds; each SHIFT interrupts torque and steps it. The
     RetardCadre lag smooths the whole thing. [verified]

     So a wheelie that looks like it changes angle with the
     gears is doing exactly that, and the chain is
     torque -> acceleration -> forkDive -> suspension -> tilt.

   o BOTH WHEEL POSITIONS MATCH RETAIL through a whole wheelie.
     racer+0x1b4 and racer+0x338 are MR_ModelInstance[1] and [2]
     at +0x0c, so they are where the wheels are DRAWN, and
     measured against racer+0x30 they agree at every frame -
     93.8, 111.8, 122.9, 123.8 up and the same back down. The
     fork and swingarm SOLVERS therefore have correct inputs.

     THE BONE ANGLES ARE DRAW-TIME LOCALS, not racer fields - the
     arm is special-cased out of the pose loop entirely - so they
     cannot be read back. A TRACE HOOK GETS THEM ANYWAY, and this
     is how:

         moto_set_hook  0046031d  len 7  captures
                        ESP+10c:4, ESP+7c:12, ESP+dc:12

     0x0046031d is the first instruction at least five bytes long
     after the arm angle is stored, and nothing pushes between the
     two - so ESP+0x10c holds the atan2 result and its two input
     vectors are still in frame.

     WHAT IT SHOWS. The angle is atan2(|cross|, dot) of two
     vectors: one of unit length at ESP+0x7c, one of wheelbase
     length at ESP+0xdc. Four consecutive frames climbing into a
     wheelie:

     +---------+----------------------------+-----------------------+
     | atan2   | ESP+0x7c   (unit)          | ESP+0xdc   (len 168)  |
     +=========+============================+=======================+
     | 24.81   |  0.326  -0.121  -0.938     |  46.5  -87.9  -134.1  |
     +---------+----------------------------+-----------------------+
     | 23.66   |  0.466  -0.442  -0.768     |  56.9 -129.9   -93.7  |
     +---------+----------------------------+-----------------------+

     THE UNIT VECTOR IS -matBikeRoot ROW 2, exactly. Read on the
     same frame, matBikeRoot row 2 was (-0.4662, 0.4417, 0.7665)
     against the captured (0.4660, -0.4417, -0.7676). So the arm
     is measured against the bike's own backward axis WITH the
     wheelie pitch and the lean already in it. [verified]

     AND WITHOUT THE SUSPENSION PITCH. That frame had
     suspensionAngle pinned at its -11.25 degree clamp; had the
     pivot matrix carried it the axis would have been eleven
     degrees out. The BODY draw does take that pitch, at
     0x0045f6ab, and the pivots do not - so despite appearances
     they are not the same matrix. [verified]

     OUR matBikeRoot IS CORRECT. Row 2 diffed against MOTO through
     a whole wheelie agrees to 0.01 of a degree at every frame, so
     the reference axis is available and right; what the port has
     to get right is passing THAT matrix to the solver rather than
     one with extra transforms composed onto it.

     STILL OPEN: the wheelbase-length vector at ESP+0xdc has not
     been identified, the fork block has not been hooked the same
     way, and nothing has been found that spins the wheels.
     maybe_Racer_PlaceWheels (0x0045c7c0) is spawn-time placement
     only. [open]

     AND riderPosition IS NOT THE BIKE ORIGIN. It sits 67.77 up
     and 10.6 BEHIND racer+0x30, and MOTO leaves matRiderRoot's
     translation at zero while ours writes the draw position
     there. Measuring fork and arm lengths against it makes the
     two sides look different when they are not.

   o STILL AN INPUT FROM OUTSIDE: slipAbs. maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed is
     read (9.19.4) but its DECOMPILE IS UNRELIABLE - the return
     expression uses three locals Ghidra never shows assigned,
     because they are written by the Matrix34_Copy call through a
     pointer to the local block. Transcribing it from that decompile
     would be guessing, so it waits for a careful disassembly read
     rather than being written on the strength of a plausible-looking
     C rendering. [open]

9.24.  maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed - read from the disassembly

   9.23.5 left this as the one input src/physics.c still took from
   outside, and left it deliberately: THE DECOMPILE IS NOT USABLE.
   This is the function read instruction by instruction instead.

9.24.1.  What it computes

       v      = normalize(instance+0x10c .. +0x114) * speed
       result = row0 . v  -  row0 . t

   where row0 is matOrientation's first row, instance+0x98..0xa0.

   o row0 IS THE LATERAL AXIS. structs/MR_Racer.txt records that its
     Y is exactly zero because maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact builds
     the matrix by crossing the wheel-contact vector with world up.
     So the result is HOW FAST THE BIKE IS TRAVELLING SIDEWAYS, and
     the MotoType 1 grip curve reads its magnitude. The name it was
     given from its callers turns out to be exactly right.

   o THE ZERO-LENGTH GUARD IS AN EXACT EQUALITY TEST against 0.0f at
     _DAT_004d1bec, not an epsilon - FSQRT, FCOM, TEST AH,0x40 which
     is C3, equal. A denormal velocity still normalises.

9.24.2.  It builds a 3x4 inverse and reads a quarter of it

   The body transposes the rotation into [ESP+0x44]
   (0x0048d187..0x0048d205), computes all three -(row_i . t)
   translation terms (0x0048d1c9..0x0048d282), calls Matrix34_Copy to
   move all twelve floats to [ESP+0x14], and then multiplies out four
   of them:

   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | Slot             | Holds          | Read at the end?                             |
   +==================+================+==============================================+
   | [ESP+0x14]       | invM[0] = m[0] | YES - 0x0048d29b                             |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x18]       | invM[1] = m[3] | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x1c]       | invM[2] = m[6] | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x20]       | invM[3] = m[1] | YES - 0x0048d293                             |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x24]       | invM[4] = m[4] | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x28]       | invM[5] = m[7] | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x2c]       | invM[6] = m[2] | YES - 0x0048d28b                             |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x30]       | invM[7] = m[5] | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x34]       | invM[8] = m[8] | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x38]       | invM[9]        | YES - 0x0048d2a9, and it is -(row0.t)        |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x3c]       | invM[10]       | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | [ESP+0x40]       | invM[11]       | no                                           |
   +------------------+----------------+----------------------------------------------+

   o EIGHT OF THE TWELVE ARE DEAD. This is a generic matrix-inverse
     idiom being reused, not something with meaning, so src/physics.c
     transcribes the four live terms rather than the machinery. The
     arithmetic is identical and that is the test that matters.

   o THE TRANSPOSE-PLUS-NEGATED-TRANSLATION IS THE STANDARD INVERSE
     for a ROW-VECTOR convention: p' = p.R + t inverts to
     p'.R_transpose - t.R_transpose, and (t.R_transpose)_i is
     row_i . t, which is exactly what 0x0048d1c9 onward builds.

9.24.3.  The translation term is always zero

   matOrientation's translation row - instance+0xbc..0xc4, which is
   racer+0xe0..0xe8 - READ 0, 0, 0 LIVE [verified]. So -(row0 . t)
   vanishes and the whole function is a dot product with row0.

   o IT IS KEPT IN THE TRANSCRIPTION ANYWAY, because the code
     computes it and a non-zero translation would change the answer.

   o AND IT WOULD BE WRONG IF IT WERE NON-ZERO. Including a
     translation means transforming a VELOCITY as though it were a
     POINT. That is harmless only while t is zero, and it is the kind
     of latent error that surfaces if some other code path ever puts
     a translation in that matrix. [inferred]

9.24.4.  Why the decompile could not be used

   Ghidra renders the return as

       return local_60 * (local_60 * fVar1) + local_54 * (...)
              + local_48 * (...) + local_3c;

   using local_54, local_48 and local_3c - THREE LOCALS IT NEVER
   SHOWS ASSIGNED. They are written by Matrix34_Copy through a
   pointer to the local block, which the decompiler does not track
   back into the frame.

   o THE RENDERING LOOKS PLAUSIBLE, WHICH IS THE DANGER. It has the
     right shape - a three-term dot product plus a constant - and a
     transcription made from it would have been subtly wrong in a
     function whose output feeds the one curve verified to six
     decimal places. 9.23.5 declined to write it for exactly this
     reason and that was the right call.

   o THE TELL IS GENERAL: a decompiled expression that reads locals
     with no visible assignment means an aliased write the decompiler
     lost. Treat the whole function as unread until the disassembly
     says otherwise.

9.24.5.  Transcribed

   src/physics.c gains physics_slip_speed, and with it
   physics_tick_drivetrain no longer takes any value it cannot
   compute. tools/check_physics.py gains seven cases - the normalise,
   a pure sideways slide, a pure forward run giving zero, a 45 degree
   slide, a yawed lateral axis, the zero-vector guard and the
   translation subtraction.

   o THESE ARE SELF-CONSISTENCY CHECKS, NOT LIVE ONES, and the
     section says so rather than implying more. The captured slip
     values of 9.19.1 cannot be replayed through this function
     because the matrix and velDir that produced them were not
     captured alongside. What the cases catch is a transposed index
     or a swapped component - which, given the function is nine
     matrix loads in a deliberately confusing order, is the realistic
     failure. [open: a run capturing matOrientation, velDir and the
     returned slip together would close it properly]

9.26.  THE TWO TICK BRANCHES - and the AI is kinematic

   racetick.c owns the fork (9.25.7); this is what is on each side of
   it. The single most useful fact about the pair is that THEY DO NOT
   SHARE A PHYSICS MODEL.

9.26.0.  BOTH BRANCHES OWE THE CRASH A VERDICT

   racer_update_crash returns RACER_CRASH_DONE when the rider has
   finished his flight - the timer went negative, or he stopped
   sliding - and SOMETHING HAS TO ACT ON IT.  Only race.c can:
   putting a racer back on the bike walks the whole field, which
   rule 13 puts in race_reset_crashes.

   THE AI BRANCH CALLED IT AND THREW THE ANSWER AWAY, and the cost
   was invisible in the code and obvious on screen: the timer kept
   decrementing - read live at -14961 ms on two riders in a finished
   race - the latch never cleared, and the bodies were still lying
   on the track a lap later, shovable like scenery.  The human
   branch in race_tick_physics had always tested it.

   o THE TELL IS A CRASH TIMER FAR BELOW ZERO.  It is set to
     CRASH_TIMER_START and only ever counted down, so any large
     negative value means DONE was reported and nobody listened.

9.26.0.1.  THE GRID HOLD IS ONE BIT, AND BOTH BRANCHES READ IT

   g_bPreRace (0x004dc0b4) is what keeps the field on the line while
   the countdown runs, and it reaches the simulation through two
   separate arguments:

       racer_apply_drive   preRace -> buttons |= INPUT_BRAKE
       racer_tick_ai       env->preRace suppresses the wheelie, the
                           steering and the drive

   o SO A BUILD THAT NEVER READS IT LAUNCHES THE WHOLE FIELD on the
     first frame of the countdown - not the player alone, and not
     the AI alone.  Both call sites passed a literal 0 for as long
     as the clock was unwired, and the grid was decoration.

   o THE COUNTDOWN ITSELF STILL RAN, which is what made this hard to
     see: the timer counted, the HUD gate worked, the transition
     fired.  Only the hold was missing.

9.26.1.  The human tick is dynamic, the AI tick is not

   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   |                            | HUMAN 0x0045d580         | AI 0x0046bf80                  |
   +============================+==========================+================================+
   | drivetrain                 | the whole chain          | NONE - speed = engineSpeed     |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | steering                   | maybe_Racer_ApplySteerin | none                           |
   |                            | g                        |                                |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | cornering                  | maybe_Racer_ApplyCorneri | none                           |
   |                            | ng                       |                                |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | position                   | falls out of physics     | FUN_0046b120 moves it along    |
   |                            |                          | the track                      |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | ground                     | UpdateGroundContact      | SNAPPED - position[1] = ground |
   |                            |                          | height                         |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | airTime                    | accumulates              | PINNED AT 0                    |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | matOrientation             | from wheel contacts      | rebuilt from two track samples |
   |                            |                          | every frame                    |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | wheelie                    | yes, if !preRace         | yes, if !preRace               |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | shared tail                | -                        | UpdateLean, FUN_00466940,      |
   |                            |                          | Suspension, BuildShadow,       |
   |                            |                          | EmitWheelFx, UpdateCrash,      |
   |                            |                          | UpdateSkeleton, FUN_00467930   |
   +----------------------------+--------------------------+--------------------------------+

   o AN AI RIDER IS PLACED, NOT SIMULATED. maybe_Racer_TickAI calls
     no ApplyDrive, no ApplySteering, no ApplyCornering and no
     maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain. It moves the rider with
     FUN_0046b120, assigns speed = engineSpeed outright, snaps
     position[1] to the ground height and rebuilds matOrientation
     from two track samples at the wheel contact points.

   o WHICH EXPLAINS WHY EVERY MEASUREMENT SESSION ONLY EVER SAW
     RACER 1. A hook in the drivetrain catches the player and
     nothing else, because nothing else goes through it. That was
     taken as a convenience at the time (9.17.6 records it as "hook
     something on the HUMAN-ONLY path"); it is actually structural.

   o AND AI BIKES NEVER TAKE THE AIRBORNE DRAG. flAirTime is set to
     0.0 explicitly on the ground path, so the 1.6 coefficient of
     9.18.5 is a player-only effect. An AI can still be marked
     airborne, but only when a persoAnim stunt is running.

   o A PORT CANNOT MAKE THE AI BEHAVE BY FEEDING IT THE PLAYER
     PHYSICS. Whatever FUN_0046b120 does IS the AI, and any error in
     the drivetrain is invisible on every rider but one.

9.26.2.  The human tick's call order

   The chain, with the branch conditions transcribed. Written into
   src/ridertick.c as a table rather than as prose so it can be
   walked:

   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | Call                             | Address      | Guard                          |
   +==================================+==============+================================+
   | maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie        | 0x004660f0   | !preRace                       |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive           | 0x00465da0   | !crashLatch                    |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_ApplySteering        | 0x00466240   | !preRace && !crashLatch        |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | FUN_0048d620                     | 0x0048d620   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering       | 0x0045d110   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact  | 0x00468ca0   | returns groundFlag             |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_UpdateAirborne       | 0x00466660   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | FUN_004667e0                     | 0x004667e0   | writes forkDive                |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | FUN_00466940                     | 0x00466940   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | Racer_UpdateSuspension           | 0x00466a80   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_BuildShadow          | 0x00461aa0   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_UpdateLean           | 0x00469c60   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash          | 0x004683e0   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_UpdateSkeleton       | 0x00467b30   | given groundFlag               |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx          | 0x00466d70   | groundFlag                     |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | FUN_00467930                     | 0x00467930   | -                              |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+
   | maybe_Trk_LaneOfRacer            | 0x0046d340   | result -> lane                 |
   +----------------------------------+--------------+--------------------------------+

   o THE ORDER IS THE DELIVERABLE. Ground contact runs AFTER
     cornering, the airborne update immediately after ground
     contact, and the skeleton after everything - so a port that
     builds the chain in a different order gets different physics
     even with every individual function correct.

   o TWO CALLS ARE SUPPRESSED ON THE GRID and one is suppressed
     after a crash. ApplyDrive runs during preRace anyway and does
     its own suppression internally, which is why the guard is only
     on crashLatch.

9.26.3.  What the human tick does itself

   Four small pieces of arithmetic in a function that is otherwise a
   call list, all four now in src/ridertick.c:

   o THE STEP IS GUARDED A SECOND TIME. `if ((int)dt < 1) dt = 1.0f`
     on the float's BITS - so +0.0f and every negative become 1.0.
     Race_TickAllRacers already guarded the same quantity as a float
     compare against zero (9.25.3), substituting the same absurd
     1.0. TWO GUARDS, TWO IDIOMS, ONE VALUE.

   o headingRef (racer+0x2ea5) RECOVERS TOWARD ZERO at
     frameDelta * 0.0025 whenever the brake bit is clear or the bike
     is stopped, and is pinned at zero once it gets there. It only
     ever goes negative: maybe_Racer_UpdateAirborne pushes it down
     by frameDelta * -PI/1600 floored at -PI/16 while the brake is
     held and the bike slides. A brake-induced lean that unwinds.
     [inferred - both writers and the clamp are read, the visual
     meaning is not]

   o forkDive (racer+0x2e39) DECAYS TOWARD ZERO FROM BOTH SIDES by a
     fixed 0.01 PER FRAME, snapping to exactly zero on overshoot.
     FUN_004667e0 pushes it back out from a longitudinal
     acceleration with a peak-hold, so what is seen is the two
     racing. It is FORK DIVE, not steering - 9.7 records how that
     was got wrong three convincing ways at once.

   o fxGate (racer+0x2da0) = onGround && |slip| >= 0.04, so skid
     marks need the bike both down and sliding, at a very low bar.

9.26.4.  A map of maybe_Racer_TickAI, for whoever writes it

   NOT TRANSCRIBED - it needs FUN_0046b120, ClipMoveAgainstWalls,
   Trk_FindSegmentForPoint, Trk_GroundHeightAt and FUN_0046d610
   first, none of which are read. Its shape, so a later reader
   starts from a map:

       1  save prevFrame, remember tickInputWord
       2  FUN_0046b120(dt)          THE LINE FOLLOWER - unread, and
                                    it is where the AI actually
                                    lives
       3  ClipMoveAgainstWalls; on a hit, re-find the segment, scale
          engineSpeed down by 0x004d1018, set f_31d1 = 200 and raise
          riderStateBits bit 0
       4  speed = engineSpeed
       5  a persoAnim / stunt test that can set stuntOrOverspeed or
          leave the bike airborne
       6  the wheelie, if !preRace
       7  GROUND SNAP: position[1] = groundHeight, onGround = 1,
          flAirTime = 0, both contact pairs 1
       8  REBUILD matOrientation from Trk_GroundHeightAt at the two
          wheel contact points, with row3 zeroed
       9  the wheel contact points from HauteurCadre, RoueRayonAv/Ar
          and RoueAxeDistance
      10  lean: a decay toward zero clamped to +-PI/4, with a
          DIFFERENT constant depending on persoAnim, then a lerp
          against leanPrev
      11  the shared tail

   o THE tickInputWord SWAP IS THE ODD DETAIL. The entry value is
     saved, the CURRENT value is passed to maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx,
     and the entry value is written back. So FUN_0046b120 synthesises
     buttons into the field, the effects see them, and the field is
     then restored - the AI's inputs exist for exactly one call.

9.28.  CORNERING - the lateral balance, and a threshold that is
       a step

   maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering (0x0045d110), the fifth of the seventeen
   calls in 9.26.2. It is the LATERAL force balance, where the
   drivetrain is the longitudinal one.

9.28.1.  The threshold is the step

   The function opens by giving up:

       if (airTime > 2.592) { maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed(); return; }

   and 2.592 IS THE AIR TIMER'S PER-FRAME STEP (9.18.4) - the same
   .rdata double at 0x004d0f20, read by both. So the test is not a
   tuned threshold at all:

   o IT READS "HAVE I BEEN AIRBORNE FOR MORE THAN ONE FRAME".
     Cornering runs on the first airborne frame and on none after it.
     [verified - one constant, two readers]

   o THE COMPARISON IS FLOAT AGAINST FLOAT and that matters. The
     original narrows the double to float first, so one frame's
     accumulated 2.5920000076f is EXACTLY EQUAL to the threshold and
