     does not exceed it. tools/check_physics.py compared the float32
     timer against a double 2.592 on its first run and reported the
     opposite. A single-precision equality can hinge on which side
     gets narrowed.

   o AND THE EARLY-OUT CALLS maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed AND DISCARDS THE
     RESULT. That helper never writes its receiver (9.24), so this is
     dead code - the SECOND instance after maybe_Snd_TickRacer's
     (9.19.4). Two dead calls to the same pure function in two
     different subsystems is starting to look like an idiom rather
     than an accident. [inferred]

9.28.2.  Two gravity terms, and they are not the same term

   The 3 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/physics.c.

   o physics_slope IS LONGITUDINAL, taken from the bike's forward
     vector, and feeds the drivetrain. physics_camber IS LATERAL,
     taken by sampling Trk_GroundHeightAt at the bike's position and
     again one unit along matOrientation ROW 0 - the lateral axis -
     and feeds the cornering step.

   o MERGING THEM WOULD BE AN EASY AND WRONG SIMPLIFICATION. They use
     different constants, different geometry and different scales,
     and they answer different questions: how steep is the hill,
     against how banked is the road.

   o THAT IS 9.81 FOR THE FOURTH TIME in this subsystem, from three
     separate .rdata doubles. Recognising it stops each one being
     mistaken for a tuning coefficient.

9.28.3.  The model

       camber      = dy / sqrt(dy*dy + 1.0) * 9.81
       centripetal = (v*v) / radius          MotoType 1 ONLY
       if (driveDisengaged) centripetal *= 2.0
       out         = (camber + centripetal) * dt * 0.01 + slipSpeed
       out         -> pulled toward zero by friction*9.81*dt*0.01,
                      snapping at the crossing

   o dy/sqrt(dy^2+1) IS A SMOOTH BOUNDED SIGN FUNCTION. It saturates
     at +-1, so a cliff edge pulls no harder than a steep bank - a
     raw gradient would run away.

   o THE RADIUS IS FITTED THROUGH THREE POSITIONS - positionPrev2,
     positionPrev, position - by maybe_Math_TurnRadius3Pt, then
     scaled by 1/128. It is FLT_MAX when the bike is going straight,
     which is what identifies racer+0x18 as a turn radius at all:
     v*v/FLT_MAX is zero.

   o driveDisengaged DOUBLING THE CENTRIPETAL TERM is the third thing
     that flag does, after 9.18.7 caught it toggling. A MotoType 1
     bike coasting with no brake corners twice as hard.

   o THE TOWARD-ZERO-WITH-SNAP IDIOM APPEARS FOR THE THIRD TIME,
     after the brake (9.21.1) and the fork dive (9.26.3). All three
     test the float's sign bit and all three snap rather than
     overshoot. RECOGNISING THE IDIOM IS WORTH MORE THAN ANY ONE OF
     THEM - it is how this codebase moves a quantity to a target.

9.28.4.  Constants

   The 6 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/physics.c.

   o TWO OF THE SIX ARE SHARED WITH OTHER FUNCTIONS - 0x004d0f20 with
     the air timer and 0x004d0ed8 with the fork-dive decay. Neither
     sharing is coincidence: the first makes the threshold mean "one
     frame", and the second is simply this codebase's small-step
     constant.

9.28.5.  What was not transcribed, and one thing that must not be

   o THE COPY-PROTECTION CHECK BOLTED INTO THE MIDDLE OF THIS
     FUNCTION IS NOT TRANSCRIBED, and that is a port decision rather
     than an omission. It reads the game's own bytes at 0x00442761,
     which a rebuilt executable does not have. Its payload zeroes
     realSpeedMax AND wheelieScale - two independent kills that leave
     the bike unable to move and making no torque.

     WHAT A PORT MUST NOT DO IS REPRODUCE THE PENALTY WITH THE FLAG
     LEFT AT ZERO. Transcribing the kill without the check would
     brick every bike in championship past index 2. CLAUDE.md says
     this and it is repeated here because this is the function where
     it would happen.

   o THE CALLER IS NOT WRITTEN EITHER. Sampling the two ground
     heights needs Trk_GroundHeightAt, and the radius needs
     maybe_Math_TurnRadius3Pt; physics.c has the three arithmetic
     pieces and the caller supplies their inputs. [open]

   o AND WHAT THE RESULT DOES IS STILL UNKNOWN. It goes to
     FUN_0048d2c0, which is unread. The arithmetic that produces the
     number is complete; the number's consumer is not. [open]

9.29.  UpdateGroundContact - MAPPED, and the protection is out
       of src/

   maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact (0x00468ca0). It rebuilds
   matOrientation, so it is the input to almost everything else in
   this subsystem - physics_camber samples across that matrix,
   physics_slip_speed dots against it, and physics_slope's dirY comes
   from it. Getting it right matters more than most.

   WHICH IS WHY IT IS NOT TRANSCRIBED YET.

9.29.1.  The decompiler lost the stack frame here - RESOLVED

   THIS IS A RECORD OF A DB DEFECT, NOT A LIMITATION OF THE FUNCTION.
   Six set_function_prototype calls on the callees fixed it and
   improved every other caller of those helpers - CLAUDE.md 15.1,
   ghidra-db-repairs.txt.  The table below is what the decompiler
   showed BEFORE that, and is kept because the symptom is worth
   recognising: an empty argument list usually means a missing
   prototype, not an unreadable function.

   +------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | What the decompiler showed         | What it meant                                  |
   +====================================+================================================+
   | pfStack_b4 = (float *)0x468dd2     | a pushed RETURN ADDRESS rendered as an         |
   |                                    | assignment - the frame is lost                 |
   +------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | Trk_FindSegmentForPoint()          | called with no arguments, four times. They are |
   |                                    | on the stack it lost                           |
   +------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | in_stack_00000004                  | a phantom parameter                            |
   +------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | fVar17 = 4.48416e-44               | the integer 32 reinterpreted as a float, then  |
   |                                    | cast back                                      |
   +------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------+

   o SO THE CALL ARGUMENTS ARE NOT SHOWN AT ALL. Four calls to
     Trk_FindSegmentForPoint and five to Trk_GroundHeightAt render
     with empty parameter lists, and those calls ARE the function -
     which point is sampled where is the whole algorithm.

   o SAME CLASS OF FAILURE AS 9.24.4's, AND WORSE. There the return
     expression used three locals with no visible assignment; here
     the frame itself is gone. THE RULE FROM 9.24.4 HELD: a
     decompiled body that reads or writes stack slots with no visible
     provenance is not a transcription source.

   o WHAT IS TRANSCRIBED IS THE ONE BLOCK IT RENDERS CORRECTLY -
     the wheel contact points, as racer_wheel_contacts() in
     src/racer.c. That same arithmetic appears in
     maybe_Racer_TickAI too, so it is worth having once.

9.29.2.  The wheel contact points

       h     = HauteurCadre * 96
       rear  = pos + row3 + row1*(RoueRayonAr * -192 + h)
                          + row2*(RoueAxeDistance * -48)
       front = pos + row3 + row1*(RoueRayonAv * -192 + h)
                          + row2*(RoueAxeDistance * +48)

   The 4 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/racer.c.

   o row1 IS UP AND row2 IS FORWARD, so the frame height lifts both
     points and the wheelbase pushes them fore and aft. The two 48s
     are the half-wheelbase either side of the root joint.

   o 96 IS THE MESH SCALE Load3dfAndMap uses and -192 is twice it,
     which is the third place that number has turned up. These are
     unit conversions, not tuning.

9.29.3.  The shape, for whoever writes it

    1  clear onGroundPair1/2
    2  age the contact history: current -> prev -> prev2, both wheels
    3  onGroundPrev = onGround; re-find the segment
    4  build and normalise the frame's move delta
    5  [a copy-protection check - see 9.29.5]
    6  ClipMoveAgainstWalls, retried against the previous segment if
       the first attempt reports a hit that moves us
    7  ON A WALL HIT: push out along wallNormal, re-find the segment,
       pick a CRASH or a SCRAPE by the incidence of the move on the
       normal, scale speed down, resync the gearbox, clear the stunt
       flags
    8  Trk_GroundHeightAt at the position
    9  AIRBORNE: onGround = 0, clear stuntHeld, rebuild
       matOrientation from the MOVE DIRECTION crossed with world up
   10  GROUNDED: snap position[1] to the ground, onGround = 1,
       airTime = 0, raise both pair flags, rebuild matOrientation by
       sampling the ground at the TWO WHEEL CONTACT POINTS and
       crossing with world up
   11  recompute the contact points  <- the transcribed part
   12  decay f_2e31 toward zero with two different rates by sign

   o THE MATRIX IS BUILT TWO DIFFERENT WAYS depending on contact.
     Grounded, it follows the terrain under the wheels; airborne, it
     follows the direction of travel. That is what MR_Racer.txt means
     by "pitch and yaw come from the two wheel contact points, roll
     cannot" - and it only holds on the ground.

9.29.4.  Three ground flags, not one

   All three cleared at the top, set together on contact [verified,
   hardware watchpoint over a dirt-track replay]:

       0x00468cac  +0x2dc0 = 0     entry, every frame
       0x00468cb6  +0x2dc4 = 0     entry, every frame
       0x004694d5  +0x2db8 = 1     contact
       0x004694df  +0x00f0 = 0     THE AIR TIMER, reset here
       0x004694e9  +0x2dc0 = 1
       0x004694f3  +0x2dc4 = 1
       0x0046974b  +0x2db8 = 0     airborne

   o +0x2dc0 AND +0x2dc4 ARE A CLEAR-THEN-SET PAIR; +0x2db8 IS NOT.
     A frame returning early leaves +0x2db8 stale and the pair clear.

   o AND THIS IS WHERE THE AIR TIMER IS RESET. 9.18.1 found
     maybe_Racer_UpdateAirborne zeroing racer+0x0f0 on the grounded
     arm; this zeroes it too, at 0x004694df. TWO functions reset it
     and only one accumulates it.

9.29.5.  The protection is now out of src/ entirely

   This function carries the g_windowTokenOk check - GetWindowLongA
   (hwnd, GWL_USERDATA) plus [0x00540488] against 0x7b5403 - and on
   failure zeroes racer+0x68, THE ACTIVE FLAG, dropping the local
   rider out of the tick and the draw loop. It is not transcribed.

   THE SAME PASS ALSO REMOVED THE TWO PROTECTION-DERIVED PARAMETERS
   that had crept into physics.c:

   o engine_torque_at's torqueBonus BECAME driveInterlock. The
     original gates its 1.1x on `g_codeCheckPassed && racer+0x88 ==
     0` - half protection, half gameplay. The protection half is
     gone, because a rebuilt executable is legitimate by
     construction; the gameplay gate on racer+0x88 stays, so the
     port behaves exactly like a passing copy in every state.

   o physics_wheelie_tick's codeCheckPassed WENT ENTIRELY. The scale
     is now a flat 2.0 - the branch a legitimate copy always takes.

   o WHAT REMAINS IN src/ IS FOUR COMMENT LINES explaining why the
     constants are what they are, so nobody "restores" a branch.
     The mechanism itself lives in copy-protection.txt, which is
     where it belongs. Nothing in src/ reads a byte of code, checks
     a window token, or can zero a bike.

9.29.6.  First disassembly pass: the call map and three blocks

   0x00468ca0..0x00469c5a, 4026 bytes, __fastcall(MR_Racer *, uint
   axis0). Read from the disassembly, as 9.29.1 requires.

   THE CALL MAP, 58 sites. This is the shape of the algorithm and
   it is worth having before any line of it is transcribed:

   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | Callee                         | Sites | What it is       |
   +================================+=======+==================+
   | Trk_FindSegmentForPoint        |   7   | 0x0040c7e0       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | Vec3_Normalize                 |   7   | 0x0040fcd0       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | Vec3_Copy                      |   6   | 0x0040ff80       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | Trk_GroundHeightAt             |   5   | 0x0040c8b0       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | Vec3_Cross                     |   5   | 0x0046d610       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | Vec3_Sub                       |   3   | 0x004570d0       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_BeginCrash         |   3   | 0x00467e30       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | maybe_Trk_ClipMoveAgainstWalls |   2   | 0x0040ca80       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+
   | rail containment               |   1   | 0x0040cd90       |
   +--------------------------------+-------+------------------+

   Five crosses and seven normalises are matOrientation being
   rebuilt; seven segment lookups and five ground samples are the
   terrain being probed. Both wall routines the nowalls toggle
   patches are called from here, which is why turning the rails off
   changes so much more than collision.

9.29.7.  The wall push-out, and what racer+0x31c5 is

   Two ClipMoveAgainstWalls calls in sequence, at 0x00468fe9 and
   0x0046909f, both on the track object at 0x0052c108 with six
   stack arguments. The second is reached whether or not the first
   reported a hit; only if BOTH return zero does the function jump
   past the block, to 0x004693f9.

   When one does report a hit:

       pos.x = clipped.x + racer[0x31c5] * 32.0
       pos.y = clipped.y + racer[0x31c9] * 32.0
       pos.z = clipped.z + racer[0x31cd] * 32.0

   at 0x004690ae..0x004690f3, with 32.0 the float at 0x004d1024 and
   clipped the vector the clip wrote to ESP+0x8c.

   SO racer+0x31c5 IS A VEC3 AND IT IS AN OUT PARAMETER. Nothing in
   the image stores to it with FSTP; its only references are one
   MOV that zeroes it in FUN_0045bb30 and four LEAs that pass its
   address - here twice, and once each in maybe_Racer_TickAI and
   maybe_Racer_TickRemote. The clip fills it, and 32.0 is how far
   the bike is pushed out along it.

   THIS IS NOT THE GENERAL MOVER, which is the trap to avoid. The
   store at 0x004690eb is the only FSTP to racer+0x30 in the
   function, but it sits inside the wall-hit branch. The ordinary
   advance must go through one of the six Vec3_Copy calls or
   through the clip itself, both of which write via a POINTER and
   so are invisible to a search for FSTP [reg + 0x30]. Do not
   conclude from an operand search that a field has one writer.

9.29.8.  The airborne test, and two fields it settles

   After the wall block, at 0x004693f9, the position goes through
   the rail containment routine and then Trk_GroundHeightAt, and
   the result is compared:

       airborne  <=>  pos.y > groundY + threshold
       threshold = 32 normally, 8 when nAxis0 is set

   The selector is the function's SECOND ARGUMENT, the throttle
   axis - not a stack flag, as this note first had it. racer.c's
   racer_is_airborne already carried that reading.

   the integers loaded at 0x00469427 and 0x00469437 and compared at
   0x00469448. Airborne takes the branch to 0x0046974b.

   ON THE GROUND, two fields this project had only partly named are
   written outright:

       racer+0x2e95  = (racer[0x40] - groundY) * -2.0
                       0x004d1028, at 0x00469470

       racer+0x124   = (float)(int)racer[0x118] * (1.0 / 3.6)
                       0x004d0f70, a DOUBLE, at 0x0046948a

   The first is springCompression, which racer.h had as "the
   wheelie's landing thump writes -32.0 into it" - it is a scaled
   height error, and the -2.0 is where the sign comes from. The
   second is the SPEED field, rebuilt on landing from an integer at
   racer+0x118 rather than carried through the touchdown.

   AND THE 0.277778 IS 1/3.6, WHICH NAMES BOTH FIELDS. racer+0x118 is
   an INTEGER speed in km/h and racer+0x124 is the same speed in m/s.
   So the drivetrain's unit is metres per second - which physics.c
   had assumed and which nothing had confirmed - and the game keeps a
   rounded km/h alongside it, presumably the one the HUD shows.

   Both are gated on racer+0x2dbc being zero.

9.29.9.  The matOrientation rebuild, read

   0x004695f2..0x00469746. Two probe points, each snapped to the
   terrain, then an orthonormal frame off their difference.

   THE SNAP IS THE PART THAT IS EASY TO MISS. Each point's segment
   is found, then Trk_GroundHeightAt is called and its result is
   stored back into THAT POINT'S OWN y:

       00469616  P1seg = Trk_FindSegmentForPoint(trk, seg, P1, 0)
       00469630  P2seg = Trk_FindSegmentForPoint(trk, seg, P2, 0)
       00469642  P1.y  = Trk_GroundHeightAt(trk, P1seg, P1)
       00469656  P2.y  = Trk_GroundHeightAt(trk, P2seg, P2)

   so the pair that builds the frame is not where the wheels are -
   it is where the GROUND IS UNDER THEM. The x and z come from the
   racer, the y comes from the track.

   THE TWO SEARCHES ARE GATED; THE HEIGHT SAMPLES ARE NOT. 004695f8
   reads PersoAnim - loaded once at 00468cd0 from 0x509398 + 292*bike
   into the stack slot the world-up vector later reuses, which is why
   it reads like an uninitialised local - and skips BOTH
   Trk_FindSegmentForPoint calls when PersoAnim is set and the
   low-air argument is clear, leaving both probes on the racer's own
   segment. INACTIVE ON ROAD ONLY: the eight SPEED bikes read
   PersoAnim 0 [verified], so both games always search there and the
   port searching unconditionally costs nothing. THE EIGHT MOTOCROSS
   BIKES READ 1, so the original DOES skip both lookups on dirt and
   the port does not - a live divergence the moment motocross runs.

   THEN THE FRAME, all three normalised:

       00469670  F = P1 - P2
       00469683  F = normalize(F)
       004696b3  L = cross(worldUp, F)
       004696d1  U = cross(F, L)
       004696e4  L = normalize(L)
       004696ed  U = normalize(U)

   worldUp is built inline on the stack as (0, 1.0, 0) at
   0x004696a0. F is P1 MINUS P2, so P1 is the forward point.

   AND THE STORES NAME THE MATRIX ROWS:

       004696f6  racer+0xbc = L      lateral
       00469714  racer+0xc8 = U      up
       0046972e  racer+0xd4 = F      forward

   WHICH CONFIRMS THE PORT, and confirms the reason racer.h gives.
   racer_orient_from_forward already takes its lateral from world
   up, with the comment that terrain can never ROLL the bike and
   only pitch and yaw it. That is exactly what cross(worldUp, F)
   guarantees: L is horizontal by construction whatever the slope,
   so no amount of camber tips the frame. The port had the rule
   right; this is where it comes from.

   It also settles the older half-claim that +0xbc is "yaw only".
   It is not - F carries terrain PITCH, because the two y values
   are ground heights and generally differ. What it has no ROLL,
   which is a different and narrower statement.

9.29.10.  What fills P1 and P2

   0x004694fd..0x004695f1, two structurally identical blocks that
   differ in ONE constant. Each ends in an explicit three-dword MOV
   copy - to ESP+0x5c for the first and ESP+0x44 for the second -
   so the destinations are readable without any ESP arithmetic.

       t = axeDistance * -48.0      0x004d0e94, block one -> P2
       t = axeDistance * +48.0      0x004d0e98, block two -> P1

       P.x = pos.x   + F.x * t + racer[0xe0]
       P.y = groundY + F.y * t + racer[0xe4]
       P.z = pos.z   + F.z * t + racer[0xe8]

   THE Y IS NOT THE RACER'S. It is groundY, the sample taken at
   0x00469423 under the racer's own position, held at ESP+0x2c. So
   both probe points start on the ground plane under the bike and
   are then pushed fore and aft along F - and only afterwards is
   each one's y REPLACED again by the terrain height under where it
   landed (9.29.9). The first pass puts them on the ground; the
   second pass finds what the ground does there.

   F IS LAST FRAME'S FORWARD AXIS. racer+0xd4..0xdc is the forward
   row this same function is about to overwrite at 0x0046972e, so
   the probe direction is the previous frame's heading. That
   feedback is the whole reason the orientation is stable over a
   crest instead of chasing itself.

   AND +-48 * axeDistance IS THE SAME HALF-WHEELBASE the draw uses.
   It is exactly racer_wheel_ground's offset, with +48 giving P1 -
   which confirms independently that P1 is the FRONT point, since
   9.29.9 has F = P1 - P2 pointing forward.

   SO racer.h's LONG-STANDING CLAIM IS NEARLY RIGHT AND NOT QUITE.
   The frame is built from a front/rear pair sampled against the
   terrain, as it says. But the pair is NOT racer_wheel_contacts:
   there is no radius term and no hauteurCadre, the y comes from
   the ground rather than the chassis, and there is a third addend
   the contact code has no equivalent for.

   THAT THIRD ADDEND IS matOrientation'S TRANSLATION ROW. racer+0xbc
   is a 3x4: lateral at +0xbc, up at +0xc8, forward at +0xd4 and the
   translation at +0xe0, ending at +0xec. FUN_0045cb00 settles it by
   copying all six floats from +0xd4 to +0xe8 out of one contiguous
   source block at 0x0045cb52..0x0045cb86 - forward and translation
   written as a unit, which they only would be if they were one
   matrix.

   SO THE PROBE IS A MATRIX TRANSFORM, not a vector plus a mystery.
   pos + F*t + translation is the point (0, 0, t) put through
   matOrientation and offset by the racer position - the same
   m[6+i]*along + m[9+i] + pos[i] that racer_wheel_ground already
   computes.

   AND IT IS ZERO IN NORMAL RIDING. 0x004699d5..0x004699e1 clears all
   three components with EDI, which 0x0046966e set to zero, on the
   frame immediately after the orientation is rebuilt;
   Racer_AiFollowLine clears the same three at 0x0046bb2e, and
   maybe_Racer_TickAI and maybe_Racer_TickRemote each zero +0xe4
   outright. A rebuilt rotation has no translation, so the term
   contributes nothing until something sets it.

   TWO FALSE WRITERS WERE FOUND AND DISCARDED on the way, and the
   reason is worth keeping. FUN_0048d620 and FUN_0046e320 both store
   to [ESI + 0xe0], but neither ESI is a racer: 0x0045d770 calls the
   first with ECX = racer+0x24, the body ModelInstance, and the
   second is reached only from maybe_Game_RenderFrame. AN OPERAND
   SEARCH MATCHES A DISPLACEMENT, NOT A FIELD - always confirm the
   base register before believing a hit.

   THE CONTACT POINTS ARE COMPUTED LATER, not here. The carac
   cluster at 0x004699f3..0x00469aae - hauteurCadre, rayonAr,
   axeDistance, then rayonAv - runs AFTER the frame is stored, on
   the matrix this block just built. That ordering is the thing to
   keep straight: the frame comes from ground probes, and the
   contacts come from the frame.

9.29.11.  The ordinary position advance is not in this function

   IT HAS ALREADY HAPPENED when UpdateGroundContact runs. The wall
   clip at 0x00468fe9 is handed from = racer+0x3c and to =
   racer+0x30, and racer+0x3c is only ever READ here - three LEAs
   and one FSUB, no writer. It is the previous frame's position,
   saved by Racer_SavePrevFrame. So racer+0x30 already holds the
   NEW position, and this function's job is to clip it, snap it and
   rebuild the frame around it - not to produce it.

   THE MOVER IS ModelInstance_Integrate (0x0048d620), and it is a
   method on the BODY INSTANCE, not on the racer:

       0045d770   LEA ECX, [ESI + 0x24]   /  CALL 0x0048d620

   racer+0x24 is the body MR_ModelInstance and instance+0x0c is
   racer+0x30, so moving the instance moves the racer. The step
   itself is a plain Euler add, at 0x0048dc23..0x0048dc55:

       position.x += displacement.x      instance +0x0c += +0xd8
       position.y += displacement.y      instance +0x10 += +0xdc
       position.z += displacement.z      instance +0x14 += +0xe0

   with the displacement computed a few instructions earlier, at
   0x0048db07..0x0048db23, into that same +0xd8..0xe0 triple.

   A CORRECTION THIS COST. 9.29.10 records FUN_0048d620 as a false
   writer of racer+0xe0, on the grounds that its ESI is the
   instance. That is right and it is not the whole story: the
   function IS the racer's mover, one indirection away. Both
   statements have to be held at once - the field it writes is not
   racer+0xe0, and the position it writes IS racer+0x30.

   maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering IS NOT A MOVER EITHER, though it
   looked like one. At 0x0045d39e..0x0045d40d it forms
   pos + lateral + translation, samples Trk_GroundHeightAt there,
   and subtracts - a CAMBER probe one lateral unit to the side.
   That is what feeds physics_camber.

9.29.12.  The player tick order

   Twenty calls, in order, from maybe_Racer_Tick (0x0045d580):

       0045d5bf  Racer_SavePrevFrame
       0045d679  maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed
       0045d6ee  maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie
       0045d713  maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive
       0045d743  maybe_Racer_ApplySteering
       0045d759  FUN_0046c7a0
       0045d770  ModelInstance_Integrate      <- the position moves
       0045d792  maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering
       0045d79c  maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact
       0045d7a6  maybe_Racer_UpdateAirborne
       0045d7ad  maybe_Racer_UpdateForkDive
       0045d7b4  FUN_00466940
       0045d7bb  Racer_UpdateSuspension
       0045d7c2  maybe_Racer_BuildShadow
       0045d7c9  maybe_Racer_UpdateLean
       0045d7d0  maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash
       0045d7d8  maybe_Racer_UpdateSkeleton
       0045d7e4  maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx
       0045d7eb  FUN_00467930
       0045d7f2  maybe_Trk_LaneOfRacer

   Drive and steering run BEFORE the move; ground contact, lean and
   the skeleton run after it. That ordering is what makes the probe
   in 9.29.10 use the previous frame's forward axis - the frame is
   rebuilt after the bike has already gone somewhere.

9.29.12.1.  The wall clip runs TWICE, against two segments

   0x00468fe9..0x004690a6. The first call clips the move against
   the racer's own segment, racer+0x31bd. What happens next is the
   part a single-pass port gets wrong:

       hit = Clip(curSegment, prevPos, pos, ...)
       if (hit && FindSegmentForPoint(hitPoint) == curSegment)
           goto pushOut;              0x0046900b
       Clip(racer+0x31c1, prevPos, pos, ...)   second call, 0046909f
       pushOut:

   So the clip is retried against a SECOND segment whenever the
   first pass did not settle inside the starting segment - and the
   no-hit path at 0x00469047 falls into the same second call, so a
   move that crosses no wall of the current segment is still tested
   against the next one.

   racer+0x31c1 IS THAT NEXT SEGMENT. It reads as curSegment+1 in
   every live sample, which is what makes the retry meaningful: a
   move leaving one segment is checked against the one it enters.

   ON A STRAIGHT THE TWO PASSES ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE, because
   consecutive segments carry the same two wall planes. On a curve
   they are not, and the second pass is the one that actually stops
   the bike. A port that clips once looks correct on every straight
   and fails only where the track bends.

9.29.12.2.  What turns the bike after a wall hit

   Racer_ComposeOrientation (0x0045cb00) is the only thing that
   yaws matOrientation, and a hardware watchpoint on racer+0xd4
   caught it doing so throughout a wall scrape. Its return address,
   captured at the entry, names the callers for the local racer:

       0x0046664f   maybe_Racer_ApplySteering
       0x00469c49   maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact, at its tail

   AND STEER WAS 0.0 AT EVERY ONE OF THOSE CALLS. So ApplySteering
   composes a yaw that does NOT come from the bars, which the
   port's transcription - yaw = -(grip * factor * dt * K * steer) -
   has no term for. [open - find the second term]

   MEASURED: a square hit at 22 m/s, then no input at all. The
   heading swung from -90 to +3.43 degrees at a steady 4.43 to 4.45
   degrees a frame and then STOPPED DEAD on 3.43, which is the
   segment's own heading - a turn toward a target, not a decay. The
   racer never left the ground (onGround 1) and never crashed
   (crashLatch 0), so neither the airborne branch nor a respawn is
   involved.

   flImpactBlend (racer+0x31d1) IS LIVE THROUGHOUT, and it is the
   obvious candidate for gating that second term:

       set to 200.0 on a wall hit    0x004693d5, and by
                                     Racer_ResetCrash and the AI tick
       decays 3.3 a frame            measured; 3.3 is dtScaled
       w = flImpactBlend * 0.005     0x004d100c

   so w is exactly 1.0 at the moment of impact and reaches 0 about
   sixty frames later.

   NOT UpdateLean, WHICH IS THE EASY WRONG ANSWER. UpdateLean does
   read flImpactBlend and decay it, but only to floor the alpha of
   the frame it samples the wheel contacts in - see the plate
   comment on 0x00469c60. That produces a LEAN, not a heading.

   NOT maybe_Racer_TickRemote's FUN_0045e560 EITHER, which reads
   flImpactBlend with the same 0.005 and calls ComposeOrientation.
   A hook on its entry recorded zero hits across a full impact: it
   is the network path and does not run for a local racer.

9.29.12.3.  The pending yaw - what turns the bike off a wall

   racer+0x2e31 IS A PENDING HEADING CORRECTION IN RADIANS, paid
   out over several frames. It is the whole mechanism, and it is
   the reason a bike that hits a rail ends up running along it
   instead of pointing into it.

   SET ON THE HIT, 0x00469111..0x0046914c:

       racer+0x31c1 = FindSegmentForPoint(...)   the entered segment
       racer+0x174..0x17c = a vec3 from the clip
       yaw     = Racer_GetYaw(racer+0xbc)        0x00490220
       pending = FUN_0046ad10(racer, wallNormal, -yaw)

   FUN_0046ad10 (0x0046ad10, 1024 bytes) is UNREAD and is what
   decides the target heading from the wall normal. [open]

   DRAINED EVERY FRAME, 0x00469bb0..0x00469c44, at the tail of
   UpdateGroundContact:

       if ((pending & 0x7fffffff) == 0) skip the whole block
       step     = g_00581014 * (pending > 0 ? K+ : K-)
       pending -= step
       if it crossed zero, pending = 0        0x00469c15
       ComposeOrientation(Ry(step))           0x00469c44

   with K+ the double at 0x004d1208 and K- at 0x004d1210. A FIXED
   RATE, not a decay - which is why the measured recovery held a
   steady 4.43 to 4.45 degrees a frame and then STOPPED DEAD on the
   segment heading rather than easing into it.

   THE FIELD IS MISNAMED IN src/racer.h AS leanTarget. It has
   nothing to do with lean: every access is the sequence above, and
   its only consumer builds a pure-Y rotation from it. The name
   came from reading one slice of the function in isolation.

   AND IT IS NOT ApplySteering. That function does call
   ComposeOrientation unconditionally at 0x0046664a, but its yaw is
   grip * factor * dt * K * (*EDI), and 0x00466409 is
   LEA EDI,[ESI+0x110] - so EDI IS the steering angle and the yaw
   really is zero with the bars centred. The rotation seen with
   steer == 0 comes from the drain above, which shares the same
   0x0040fd20 / 0x0040fd40 / ComposeOrientation tail and so looks
   identical in a caller trace.

9.29.12.4.  What the recovery yaw actually returns - MEASURED

   A hook at 0x00469152, the instruction after the store to
   racer+0x2e31, catches every contact's (heading, normal) against
   the value produced:

   +-------------+------------------+-------------+
   | heading     | wall normal      | stored      |
   +=============+==================+=============+
   |    -90.00   | (-1, 0, 0)       |    -90.00   |
   +-------------+------------------+-------------+
   |    -27.40   | (-1, 0, 0)       |    -27.40   |
   +-------------+------------------+-------------+

   THE PENDING IS THE BIKE'S OWN HEADING when the target is zero,
   so the difference runs BIKE MINUS TARGET. Draining it to zero
   turns the bike onto the wall's own direction, which for a
   normal of (-1, 0, 0) is straight down +Z.

   AND THE SIGN-FLIP TEST NEEDS AN EPSILON IN A PORT. The original
   builds its left vector through the 0x4000-entry angle table,
   where a quarter turn is EXACTLY zero. cos(-pi/2) in float is
   -4.37e-08, so a strict `< 0` flip test fires on a square hit
   that MOTO does not flip.

   THAT ACCIDENT HID THE REAL BUG FOR TWO SESSIONS. The spurious
   flip negated an already-negated formula, so the FIRST impact
   came out exactly right and every later one inverted - which
   reads as "the recovery works but the bike still snags" rather
   than as a sign error. Two contacts in one capture separated
   them; no amount of reading had.

9.29.12.5.  The gate across a whole scrape - MEASURED

   A square hit at 23 m/s produces exactly TWO contacts, and they
   take DIFFERENT branches. Captured at 0x004692e4 with the two
   branch entries marked:

   +---------+----------+-----------+---------+---------+--------+
   | contact | heading  | moveDir   | dot 3D  | dot XZ  | branch |
   +=========+==========+===========+=========+=========+========+
   |       1 |  -90.00  |   -90.00  |  -77.39 |  -93.15 | SCRAPE |
   +---------+----------+-----------+---------+---------+--------+
   |       2 |  -27.40  |   -50.51  |  +30.07 |   -3.41 | BOUNCE |
   +---------+----------+-----------+---------+---------+--------+

   THE SECOND CONTACT IS WHAT LETS THE BIKE LEAVE. The bounce
   assigns moveDir the reflected direction - +50.51 degrees, away
   from a +X wall - and the slip blend then walks it DOWN to the
   heading over the following frames. Without it moveDir only ever
   approaches the heading from below and the bike keeps drifting
   back into the rail.

   SO THE SEQUENCE IS scrape, TURN, bounce, GONE. The first
   contact kills the speed and starts the recovery yaw; the
   recovery turns the bike; the second contact, now oblique,
   bounces it clear. Reading either contact alone gives a
   misleading picture of the gate - the first transcription of it
   was measured on a run where every captured hit bounced.

   AND NOTE dot XZ IS NEGATIVE ON BOTH. Dropping the vertical term
   makes contact 2 scrape, which removes the only bounce in the
   sequence and with it the bike's way out. That is the same
   3D-versus-2D point as 9.29.12, seen through its consequence
   rather than through one impact.

9.29.12.6.  The step damping is a CORNER radius, not a speed

   ModelInstance_Integrate scales its grounded displacement by

       a = 1 - K / *(float *)(*(int *)(instance + 0xe4) + 0x18)
       clamped to [0.5, 1.0], K = 6 for PersoAnim 0 else 11

   and the pointer at instance+0xe4 POINTS AT THE RACER ITSELF -
   read live, racer+0x108 holds 0x00584421, which is racer 1's own
   base. So +0x18 is racer+0x18, turnRadius.

   IT IS THEREFORE A CORNER DAMPING. A tight radius shortens the
   step; a straight line leaves it alone. K being 6 on the road
   and 11 on the dirt means a dirt bike loses more of its step in
   the same corner.

   BUT MOTO HOLDS FLT_MAX THERE AND NEVER WRITES IT. A hardware
   watchpoint on racer+0x18 saw ZERO hits across sixteen frames of
   hard cornering, and the value read 3.403e+38 before and after.
   So on the player's path the damping is a flat 1.0, and whatever
   maintains turnRadius is somewhere else - the AI line, or
   nothing in this build. [open]

   THE PORT MUST SEED IT. A zeroed struct reads as the tightest
   possible corner and halves every step, which is the opposite of
   the intended default. racer_place_from_grid now writes FLT_MAX.

   THE RESULT, measured on the straight approach to a wall at
   three-frame steps - a step size the harness runs exactly:

   +--------+-------------+-------------+---------+
   | frame  | MOTO x      | ours x      | gap     |
   +========+=============+=============+=========+
   |      9 |   -1067.25  |   -1067.23  |    0.02 |
   +--------+-------------+-------------+---------+
   |     27 |     -48.32  |     -48.16  |    0.16 |
   +--------+-------------+-------------+---------+
   |     42 |    1241.22  |    1241.83  |    0.61 |
   +--------+-------------+-------------+---------+

   0.61 units over 2440 travelled, or 0.025 per cent, against 8.0
   before - a thirteenfold improvement from one field. Speeds were
   already identical to four decimals; it was the step LENGTH that
   was short, and only by the ratio a damp of 0.997 against 1.000
   predicts.

   AND IT COST TWO WRONG READINGS. This was transcribed first as
   r->speed and then, after an A/B put our travel at 0.641 of
   MOTO's, as r->speed * 128 - which lands at 0.997 and looks
   right. Both were guesses at a quantity that is not a speed at
   all. A value that is nearly correct is harder to catch than one
   that is obviously wrong; only reading the pointer settled it.

9.29.13.  The displacement, and the instance-to-racer offsets

   0x0048dac7..0x0048db23, immediately before the Euler add:

       displacement = moveDir * speed * 128.0 * a * b

   with 128.0 the DOUBLE at 0x004d1c30, speed read from
   instance+0x100 and moveDir from instance+0x168.

   b IS THE FRAME DELTA, SCALED. At the prologue, 0x0048d625,

       b = arg0 * 0.01           the float at 0x004d1c10

   where arg0 is the first stack argument - dt, in the same
   0.1-scaled millisecond unit the rest of the tick uses.

   SO THE PLAYER AND THE AI RUN THE SAME FORMULA. 128.0 * 0.01 is
   1.28, which is exactly Racer_AiFollowLine's constant at
   0x004d1010. The two integrators differ only in how the number is
   spelled and in the extra factor a:

       AI      pos += moveDir * speed * dt * 1.28
       player  pos += moveDir * speed * dt * 1.28 * a

   a IS A LOW-SPEED DAMPING IN [0.5, 1.0], computed at
   0x0048d955..0x0048d9dd:

       K = (carac[72] == 0) ? 6.0 : 11.0     0x004d1c38 / 0x004d1c40
       a = 1.0 - K / |v|
       clamp a to 1.0 at 0x0048d9be, and to 0.5 at 0x0048d9d5

   Both clamps are done on the FLOAT BIT PATTERN with an integer
   CMP, the same idiom as the crash-timer gate in 9.35.4 - valid
   for positive floats and the reason the branch reads as dead in
   the decompiler.

   So the step is full size at high |v| and tapers to half as |v|
   falls, hitting the floor at |v| = 12 for K = 6 and |v| = 22 for
   K = 11. A zero |v| divides to infinity, which the same clamp
   catches and pins at 0.5, so the degenerate case is safe.

   CARAC 72 SELECTS K and is read as an INT, not a float - the
   field at 0x00509398, stride 292, the last of the 73. Note
   0x00509398 is the g_bikeCarac base 0x00509278 plus 0x120, so
   every [EAX*4 + 0x509398] with EAX = 73*bike reads THIS field
   and not field 0.

   IT IS THE DISCIPLINE FLAG [verified, all 32 CARAC records]:
   0 on every bike in SPEED and SPEEDPKT, 1 on every bike in
   KTMCROSS and KTMPKT. MotoType, carac 0, is NOT the same test -
   it is 2 for road and 1 for dirt and never 0, so a == 0 test on
   it silently answers "dirt" for both.

   v IS *(float *)(instance[0xe4] + 0x18), a pointer field at
   instance+0xe4 = racer+0x108 whose target is unread. [open]

   moveDir IS NORMALISED IN PLACE first, at 0x0048da89..0x0048dac1,
   guarded by a length test against 0x004d1bec, which is plain 0.0
   - so a zero direction is left alone rather than dividing.

   THE OFFSET MAP IS WHAT MAKES ALL OF THIS LEGIBLE. The instance
   base is racer+0x24, so:

   +---------------+---------------+--------------------------+
   | instance      | racer         | field                    |
   +===============+===============+==========================+
   | +0x0c         | +0x30         | position                 |
   +---------------+---------------+--------------------------+
   | +0xd8         | +0xfc         | displacement this frame  |
   +---------------+---------------+--------------------------+
   | +0x100        | +0x124        | speed, m/s               |
   +---------------+---------------+--------------------------+
   | +0x168        | +0x18c        | moveDir                  |
   +---------------+---------------+--------------------------+

   TWO CORRECTIONS FALL OUT OF THAT TABLE, both of things this
   document asserted earlier in the same session.

   o moveDir IS WRITTEN ON THE PLAYER PATH. 9.29 said its nine
     writers were all in the AI and network movers and that
     nothing wrote it for the local player. Wrong: the normalise
     at 0x0048dab3, 0x0048dab5 and 0x0048dac1 writes all three
     components, through instance+0x168 rather than racer+0x18c.
     The search was right about the displacement and wrong about
     the field.

   o 128 IS THE PLAYER'S SCALE TOO, and it is now read rather than
     inferred. port-divergences 2.5 carried a caveat that
     0x004d1010 has only AI and network references, so 128 was
     verified for those and assumed for the human. The human's
     integrator states it outright, as a different constant of the
     same value: 0x004d1c30 = 128.0. The AI writes it as 1.28
     against a 0.1-scaled millisecond; this writes 128.0 directly.

9.29.14.  The airborne branch

   0x0046974b, taken when pos.y > groundY + threshold (9.29.8).
   Three gates, then a frame built from the VELOCITY instead of
   the terrain.

       0046974b  racer+0x2db8 = 0            onGround clear
       00469755  if humanPlayer && soloMode
       00469763      racer+0x31f9 = 0        [open - what this is]
       0046976d  if (speed & 0x7fffffff) == 0 -> 004699e7

   THAT LAST GATE IS THE INTERESTING ONE. A stationary airborne
   racer jumps clean past the frame build AND past the stores, so
   it keeps last frame's orientation rather than being handed a
   degenerate one. The test is on the bit pattern with the sign
   masked off - speed != 0 either way - the same integer-on-float
   idiom as the damping clamps in 9.29.13.

   THE FRAME IS THE SAME CONSTRUCTION, DIFFERENT SOURCE:

       0046979d  F = moveDir - <a vector>
       004697b0  F = normalize(F)
       004697da  L = cross(worldUp, F)

   worldUp is built inline as (0, 1.0, 0) at 0x004697c7, exactly as
   the grounded path does at 0x004696a0. So airborne and grounded
   agree on the RULE - lateral from world up, hence no roll - and
   differ only in where forward comes from: two ground probes on
   the deck, the direction of travel in the air. Which is the only
   thing it could be, with no terrain under the bike to sample.

   IT GUARDS ITS NORMALISES AND THE GROUNDED PATH DOES NOT. At
   0x00469840 and 0x0046987c a Vec3_Length is compared against
   0x004d0e54, which is 0.0, and the reciprocal is skipped when it
   matches. The grounded path calls Vec3_Normalize unguarded. That
   asymmetry is the original's, not a transcription artifact.

   BOTH PATHS CONVERGE at 0x004699cf and store the same three rows:

       0046997f  racer+0xbc = lateral
       0046999d  racer+0xc8 = up
       004699bb  racer+0xd4 = forward
       004699cf  racer+0xdc, then +0xe0..0xe8 zeroed

   The grounded path JMPs there from 0x00469746; the airborne path
   falls into it. The temporaries differ - grounded holds lateral
   at ESP+0x68 and up at ESP+0x38, airborne the other way round -
   which is worth knowing before reading either block as the other.

   NOT FULLY READ: the Gram-Schmidt tail. Between the first cross
   and the stores there is a projection subtract at
   0x00469823..0x00469837 and a second Sub / Copy / Normalize /
   Cross / Cross / Normalize sequence at 0x004698fb..0x00469976,
   selected by the length test at 0x0046987c. The shape is an
   orthogonalisation with a degenerate fallback; the terms are not
   read. [open]

9.29.15.  The three BeginCrash conditions

   All three set racer+0x2dac bit 1 on firing; the first also sets
   bit 0 when it declines. 0x00458ce0 is Game_RandNext, so two of
   the three are PROBABILISTIC - which is the fact that reframes
   this whole block. Falling off is not purely deterministic.

   ONE, 0x00469174:

       if (racer+0x2dbc == 0 && (Game_RandNext() & 3) == 0)
           BeginCrash()

   A flat one-in-four once the gate is open.

   TWO, 0x00469226 - the only unconditional one:

       if (|x| > 0.98 && speed > 27.7778)
           BeginCrash()

   THREE, 0x0046928e - a disjunction:

       if (((Game_RandNext() & 3) == 0 && |x| > 0.5
            && speed > 22.2222)
        || (racer+0x307d != 0 && speed > 5.5556))
           BeginCrash()

   THE SPEED THRESHOLDS ARE ROUND km/h, WHICH CONFIRMS THE UNIT:

       0x004d11d8   27.777778 m/s = 100 km/h
       0x004d11e0   22.222222 m/s =  80 km/h
       0x004d0f50    5.555556 m/s =  20 km/h

   all exactly n/3.6, and the last is the same constant racer.h
   already cites as the 20 km/h stance gate. That is a third
   independent confirmation that racer+0x124 is metres per second,
   after the 1/3.6 conversion in 9.29.8 and the 128 scale in
   9.29.13.

   x IS THE IMPACT COSINE. Built at 0x004691cf..0x004691ef:

       x = normal.x * V.x + normal.y * V.y + normal.z * V.z

   where normal is the vec3 at racer+0x31c5 - the wall normal the
   clip wrote (9.29.7) - and V is the vec3 at ESP+0x20, the clip's
   other out parameter. Both tests mask the sign, so it is |cos|
   of the angle between the wall and the direction of travel:
   near 1 is head-on, near 0 is a glance.

       0.98 -> within 11.48 degrees of head-on
       0.50 -> within 60.00 degrees

   SO THE CRASH MODEL IS HEAD-ON-NESS AGAINST SPEED. Square into a
   wall over 100 km/h and you always go down; clip one at 60
   degrees over 80 km/h and it is one in four; graze it and the
   geometry alone cannot put you off.

   THE EBX TRAP, and it is a nasty one. Reading 0x004691cf in
   isolation, EBX looks like racer+0x124 - the LEA that says so is
   four instructions earlier at 0x0046918d. But that LEA is on the
   OTHER branch. The path that reaches 0x004691cf comes from the
   JNZ at 0x0046915a and carries EBX = racer+0x31c5, set back at
   0x00468fc4. Read as racer+0x124 the dot product becomes speed
   times something, which is plausible enough to believe.

   racer+0x2dbc SELECTS BETWEEN THE TWO CRASH MODELS, rather than
   merely gating one: zero takes the one-in-four at 0x0046915c,
   non-zero takes the dot-product pair. It is also tested in the
   landing block at 0x00469456. Still unidentified, as is
   racer+0x307d. [open]

   AND THE ZERO PATH ALWAYS STOPS THE BIKE, 0x0046918d..0x004691ca,
   whether or not it crashed:

       racer+0x180 = speed * 0.8            0x004d11c0
       if (racer+0x180 < 5.5556) racer+0x180 = 5.5556
       speed = 0

   5.5556 m/s is 20 km/h and appears twice - as the double at
   0x004d10c0 for the compare and as the float immediate
   0x3f0e38e4 for the assignment. So the bike stops dead and
   racer+0x180 carries away four fifths of what it had, floored at
   20 km/h. maybe_Racer_Tick tests that field at 0x0045d775, which
   fits it being the slide the crash then plays out.

   THE ARGUMENT TO Game_RandNext IS IGNORED - its signature is
   __stdcall(int nIgnored) and the body is eight bytes. The two
   call sites pass 0x18b1 and 0x1885, which are 6321 and 6277.
   Distinct, constant, unused, and in the range a source file's
   line numbers would occupy: a __LINE__ tag left in a rand
   wrapper is the natural reading [inferred]. If so they place
   these two sites about forty lines apart in whichever
   translation unit holds them, which is a small but real handle
   on the original layout.

9.29.16.  Trk_ClipMoveAgainstWalls

   0x0040ca80..0x0040cd80, 768 bytes, and the DB already had the
   signature:

       int __thiscall maybe_Trk_ClipMoveAgainstWalls(
               MR_Trk *this, int nSeg,
               float *pFrom, float *pTo,
               float *pHitPointOut, float *pDirInOut,
               float *pNormalOut)

   pDirInOut is the name that matters: the direction goes IN and
   comes back REFLECTED, which is what 9.29.15's impact cosine
   dots against the normal.

   THE WHOLE ALGORITHM:

       if (|from.x-to.x| < 1e-5 &&
           |from.y-to.y| < 1e-5 &&
           |from.z-to.z| < 1e-5)
           return 0;                     nothing moved

       hit = 0
       for plane in (seg+0x60, seg+0x70):
           d = Plane_Distance(plane, to)
           if (d < 0.0) {
               *pHitPointOut = Plane_IntersectSegment(plane,
                                                      from, to)
               *pNormalOut   = plane->normal
               dir -= 2.0 * dot(dir, normal) * normal
               if (|dir| != 0.0) normalize(dir)
               hit = 1
           }
       return hit;

   So it is a MIRROR REFLECTION, not a slide. The bike bounces off
   the rail along the plane normal and the reflected direction is
   renormalised, which is why the caller can treat it as a unit
   vector and why the push-out at 0x004690ae is a separate 32-unit
   nudge rather than part of the clip.

   FOUR CONSTANTS, all floats:

       0x004d02c8   9.9999997e-06   the no-move epsilon, per axis
       0x004d02d0   0.0             the side test and the zero-length
       0x004d02d8   2.0             the reflection factor

   0x004d02cc is FLT_MAX and 0x004d02d4 is 10000.0; neither is used
   here, they are just the neighbours.

   AND IT HANDS OVER THE TRACK SEGMENT LAYOUT, which is the part
   worth having beyond this function. The address arithmetic at
   0x0040caeb is SHL 6 / SUB / SHL 2 - that is (64n - n) * 4:

       segment stride     252 bytes
       wall plane A       segment + 0x60
       wall plane B       segment + 0x70
       plane record        16 bytes: normal[3] then d

   Two planes sixteen bytes apart - the left and right rail of one
   segment.

   BOTH ARE TESTED, NOT JUST THE FIRST. Wall A's hit block ends at
   0x0040cc22 by setting the return flag and then JMPing to
   0x0040cc34 - which is wall B's Plane_Distance, not the epilogue.
   The no-hit path at 0x0040cc2c falls into the same place. So into
   a corner the direction reflects TWICE, the second off the
   already-reflected vector, and the flag is accumulated.

   This note first said "first hit wins", which was wrong and which
   src/trk.c had right all along - trk_clip_move_against_walls
   calls clip_one_wall twice and ORs the results, with a comment
   saying why. A JMP that lands three instructions short of the
   return is easy to read as a return.

   NOT READ: Plane_Distance (0x004b10c0, 38 bytes) and
   Plane_IntersectSegment (0x004b0f60, 342 bytes). Both are needed
   before this can be transcribed - the shape above is the control
   flow, not the arithmetic. Also unread: the exact stack slots,
   deliberately. Five pushes straddle the argument reads and the
   displacements shift under them; the argument identities come
   from the DB signature and the call site, not from counting.

9.29.17.  The two plane primitives

   Both are small, both are textbook, and reading them completes
   the wall chain. They also settle the plane record outright:

       struct { float nx, ny, nz, d; }      16 bytes

   Plane_Distance, 0x004b10c0, 38 bytes, __thiscall(plane, point):

       return n.x*p.x + n.y*p.y + n.z*p.z + plane.d;

   The ordinary signed plane distance, with d the plane constant -
   which is what makes the caller's d < 0.0 mean "beyond the rail".

   Plane_IntersectSegment, 0x004b0f60, 342 bytes, RET 0x1c - seven
   dwords, being an out pointer and two points passed component by
   component rather than by address:

       dir = to - from

       if (|from.x-to.x| < 1e-5 &&
           |from.y-to.y| < 1e-5 &&
           |from.z-to.z| < 1e-5)
           { *out = from; return out; }      degenerate

       den = n.x*dir.x + n.y*dir.y + n.z*dir.z
       if (den == 0.0)
           { *out = from; return out; }      parallel

       t = -(n.x*from.x + n.y*from.y + n.z*from.z + plane.d) / den
       *out = from + dir * t
       return out;

   BOTH DEGENERATE CASES RETURN from RATHER THAN FAILING, which is
   why the caller never checks: a parallel or zero-length move puts
   the hit point back where the bike started, and the reflection
   that follows is harmless.

   THE EPSILON IS THE SAME 1e-5 the clip uses, but it is a SECOND
   COPY - 0x004d2090 here against 0x004d02c8 there, identical
   values in different pools. So the clip's own no-move guard is
   redundant with this one; it exists to skip the work, not to
   protect the maths.

   AND BOTH BUMP A COUNTER AT 0x00709378. Plane_Distance does it
   unconditionally at its first instruction; Plane_IntersectSegment
   does it only on the path that actually intersects. A profiling
   counter left in the shipped build [inferred] - nothing reads it
   anywhere this project has looked. [open]

9.29.18.  Where steering becomes heading

   moveDir HAS EXACTLY ONE WRITER ON THE PLAYER PATH, and it is in
   this function: 0x00469353 copies three consecutive stack dwords
   into racer+0x18c, +0x190 and +0x194. Those dwords are the wall
   clip's pDirInOut - the REFLECTED direction. So the only thing that
   writes the player's travel direction outright is a wall bounce.

   WHICH CANNOT BE THE WHOLE STORY, because the bike steers. The link
   is maybe_Racer_SetLongSpeed (0x0048cbe0, 827 bytes), called on the
   body instance at 0x004693aa here and again at 0x00469499 in the
   landing block, both times as a trio:

       maybe_Racer_SetLongSpeed(instance, speed)
       FUN_0048edd0(instance)
       maybe_Gearbox_SpeedToEngine(...) -> racer+0xec

   9.35.5 already records what SetLongSpeed does from the other end:
   SetLongSpeed(instance, 0.0) zeroes only the FORWARD component and
   leaves the other two, so a bike sliding sideways keeps its drift.
   That is a function that DECOMPOSES a velocity against the
   orientation and reassembles it.

   SO THE CHAIN IS: maybe_Racer_ApplySteering rotates matOrientation,
   SetLongSpeed re-projects the velocity onto the rotated axes, and
   moveDir comes out pointing where the bike now faces - minus
   whatever lateral component survives, which is the slip. Steering
   never touches moveDir directly; it turns the frame and lets the
   projection follow. [inferred - SetLongSpeed is unread]

   THAT IS THE ANSWER TO port-divergences 2.6, and it explains why
   racer+0x110 has no physics reader (9.29 call map): the handlebar
   angle is drawn, the LEAN turns the frame, and the frame turns the
   travel. Reading SetLongSpeed is what closes it.

9.29.19.  Racer_SetLongSpeed, and how a bike turns

   0x0048cbe0..0x0048cf1b, 827 bytes, __thiscall on the body
   INSTANCE with one float argument. It is the whole steering link.

       1.  clamp v to +-instance[0x90]        racer+0xb4, a limit
       2.  dir = normalize(instance[0x10c])   racer+0x130, the
                                              velocity, LAST frame's
       3.  vel = dir * instance[0x100]        racer+0x124, speed
       4.  L   = inverse(matOrientation)      transpose plus the
                                              negated translation,
                                              built inline at
                                              0x0048cca5..0x0048cd93
       5.  local = L * vel
       6.  world = matOrientation * (local.x, local.y, v)

   STEP SIX IS THE WHOLE THING. The third component - the one that
   multiplies row2, the FORWARD axis at instance+0xb0 - is not
   local.z. It is the clamped argument. The lateral and vertical
   components survive the round trip; the longitudinal one is
   replaced outright.

   AND THE TWO FRAMES ARE NOT THE SAME FRAME. The velocity at
   racer+0x130 is what the bike was doing last frame; matOrientation
   has already been turned by maybe_Racer_ApplySteering this frame.
   So decomposing in the new basis and rebuilding with a fresh
   forward speed is exactly a bike changing direction while keeping
   its sideways momentum. THAT IS THE SLIP.

   SO port-divergences 2.6 IS ANSWERED. Nothing converts a handlebar
   angle into a heading, because nothing needs to:

       ApplySteering      turns matOrientation
       SetLongSpeed       reprojects the velocity onto it, keeping
                          the lateral component as drift
       ModelInstance_     normalises the result into moveDir and
       Integrate          steps the position along it

   The handlebar angle at racer+0x110 is drawn and nothing else
   (9.29 call map). The LEAN turns the frame; the frame turns the
   travel.

   9.35.5 said this from the other end long before it was read -
   SetLongSpeed(instance, 0.0) zeroing only the forward component -
   and the reading confirms it exactly.

9.29.20.  The two direction fields, and what joins them

   There are TWO, they are not the same, and nothing copies one to
   the other:

   +---------------+-------------+----------------------------------+
   | instance      | racer       | who touches it                   |
   +===============+=============+==================================+
   | +0x10c        | +0x130      | THE PHYSICS VELOCITY DIRECTION.  |
   |               |             | Racer_SetLongSpeed reads and     |
   |               |             | writes it; TickDrivetrain takes  |
   |               |             | its address at seven sites, plus |
   |               |             | SlipSpeed, Gearbox_TickShift,    |
   |               |             | Racer_LongSpeed and FUN_0048d2c0 |
   +---------------+-------------+----------------------------------+
   | +0x168        | +0x18c      | moveDir. ModelInstance_Integrate |
   |               |             | normalises it and steps along    |
   |               |             | it; the AI and network movers    |
   |               |             | write it; UpdateGroundContact    |
   |               |             | overwrites it after a wall hit   |
   +---------------+-------------+----------------------------------+

   THE JOIN IS INSIDE ModelInstance_Integrate, and it is computed
   rather than copied. At 0x0048d6bf..0x0048d6d6 the function stores
   a locally built vector into instance+0x10c:

       EAX/ECX/EDX = [ESP+0x1c], [ESP+0x20], [ESP+0x24]
       instance+0x10c/0x110/0x114 = those three

   and in the same breath sets EDI = instance+0x168 for the length
   and normalise that follow. So the integrator WRITES the physics
   direction and READS its own - the reverse of the arrangement this
   note assumed, where SetLongSpeed would have fed the step.

   WHAT THAT MEANS FOR THE ORDER. Racer_SetLongSpeed sets +0x10c from
   the reprojected velocity; the drivetrain works on +0x10c;
   ModelInstance_Integrate then reconciles the two, writing +0x10c
   back and stepping along +0x168.

   THE ARITHMETIC THAT RECONCILES THEM IS UNREAD. It is in the
   integrator's body between 0x0048d620 and 0x0048da90, about 1100
   bytes, where +0x168 is read at 0x0048d77c and written at
   0x0048da65 alongside +0x16c and +0x170. That block is the last
   unknown in the player's motion chain. [open]

   A WARNING WORTH KEEPING. instance+0x10c is racer+0x130, but
   racer+0x10c is steerRate - a different field entirely, and
   maybe_Racer_ApplySteering reads it at 0x00466325 and 0x004663a5.
   An operand search for "+ 0x10c]" returns both without
   distinguishing them.

9.29.21.  The integrator's first act, and the closed loop

   ModelInstance_Integrate opens by rebuilding the physics velocity
   direction from the ORIENTATION, 0x0048d643..0x0048d6d6:

       fwd = matOrientation * (0, 0, 1)      0x0048d664
       fwd = normalize(fwd)                  0x0048d683, guarded
       instance+0x10c = fwd                  0x0048d6c5

   The (0, 0, 1) is built inline on the stack at 0x0048d64d,
   0x0048d652 and 0x0048d657 - three consecutive slots holding 0, 0
   and 1.0f. So the third row of matOrientation is FORWARD, which
   agrees with 9.29.9 storing it at racer+0xd4.

   SO THE VELOCITY DIRECTION IS SNAPPED TO WHERE THE BIKE POINTS,
   at the top of every frame. Which looks like it would destroy the
   slip Racer_SetLongSpeed works so hard to preserve - and it would,
   if the two ran in the other order.

   THE TICK ORDER IS WHAT MAKES IT WORK (9.29.12):

       0045d679   maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed         reads +0x10c
       0045d770   ModelInstance_Integrate       RESETS +0x10c to fwd
       0045d79c   maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact
                    -> Racer_SetLongSpeed       WRITES +0x10c with
                                                the lateral kept

   So the slip SetLongSpeed builds at the end of frame N survives
   exactly one frame boundary: SlipSpeed measures it first thing in
   frame N+1, and the integrator then wipes it back to the heading.
   One frame of memory, deliberately - which is why SlipSpeed is
   the FIRST call in the tick and not merely an early one.

   THAT CLOSES THE PLAYER'S MOTION CHAIN:

       ApplySteering       turns matOrientation
       Integrate           dir := forward axis; steps the position
                           along moveDir at +0x168
       UpdateGroundContact clips, snaps, rebuilds the frame
       SetLongSpeed        dir := reprojected velocity, forward
                           component replaced, lateral kept
       SlipSpeed           next frame, reads the difference

   STILL UNREAD, and now the only gap: how instance+0x168 - moveDir,
   the vector the step actually follows - is derived. It is read at
   0x0048d77c and written at 0x0048da65 with +0x16c and +0x170, and
   the arithmetic between is not read. Everything either side of it
   now is.

   A GATE NOT IDENTIFIED: instance+0xcc, which is racer+0xf0, is
   compared against the double 2.592 at 0x004d1c18 immediately
   before the direction stores, and a mismatch jumps to 0x0048d906.
   2.592 is 9.331 km/h if it is a speed, which is not a round
   number in either unit. [open]

9.29.22.  moveDir is the velocity, and this is where gravity is

   0x0048d6e4..0x0048d7c0, with EDI held at instance+0x168 from
   0x0048d6bf. The triple +0x168/+0x16c/+0x170 is not a direction
   that something else computes - it is the VELOCITY, carried
   across frames, and the integrator is what advances it:

       len = |moveDir|
       if (len != 0) moveDir /= len              unit direction
       moveDir *= instance+0x100                 now a velocity
       moveDir.y -= dt * 24.525                  GRAVITY
       instance+0x100 = |moveDir|                the new speed
       moveDir /= |moveDir|                      unit again

   So the field alternates between a direction and a velocity
   within one call, and leaves as a direction with the magnitude
   parked in instance+0x100. That is the same polar arrangement
   Racer_SetLongSpeed uses for instance+0x10c, one field over.

   24.525 IS 2.5 * 9.81, EXACTLY. The double at 0x004d1c20, applied
   against the same dt factor the displacement uses, works out to
   24.525 m/s^2 - two and a half times real gravity. An arcade
   racer making its jumps land quickly, and a number that could
   not be anything else.

   THAT COMPLETES THE PLAYER'S MOTION CHAIN. The two direction
   fields are now both accounted for and they are not redundant:

       instance+0x10c   where the bike POINTS, reset from the
                        forward axis each frame and given its
                        lateral slip back by SetLongSpeed

       instance+0x168   where the bike is GOING - momentum plus
                        gravity, integrated, never snapped to the
                        heading

   The position follows the second. The physics reads the first.
   A bike in the air keeps travelling along +0x168 while +0x10c
   swings with the handlebars, which is exactly what a motorcycle
   in flight does.

   STILL UNREAD in this function: the block from 0x0048d7d6 to
   0x0048d955, between the gravity step and the damping factor of
   9.29.13. It contains a third normalise and the instance+0xcc
   gate. Nothing downstream of the port depends on it. [open]

9.29.23.  ApplySteering's tail - and steer IS a physics input

   RETRACTION FIRST. 9.29.18 and port-divergences 2.6 both said the
   handlebar angle at racer+0x110 has four readers and all of them
   are draw code, so nothing turns the bike with it. THAT IS WRONG.
   maybe_Racer_ApplySteering reaches it through a POINTER -

       00466409   LEA  EDI, [ESI + 0x110]
       00466625   FMUL float ptr [EDI]

   - and an operand search for "+ 0x110]" cannot see the second
   line. That is the FOURTH time this session a displacement search
   has been mistaken for a field search. The rule is now earned:
   after any operand search, look for a LEA of the same field.

   THE TAIL, 0x004664f2..0x0046664a:

       g     = <unread> * 0.5 * dt * 0.01     004d1018, 004d1004
       f     = 1.0, or a clamped value, or 1.6 when BRAKING
               WHILE TURNING - buttons bit 1 and either of 2 or 3,
               tested at 00466604 and 0046660d
       yaw   = f * g * steer
       euler = (0, yaw, 0)                    a pure Y rotation
       matOrientation = matrix(euler)         through FUN_0045cb00

   So the bars turn the frame directly, once per tick, and
   FUN_0045cb00 is the same setter that proves matOrientation is a
   3x4 (9.29.10). racer+0xac - turnBrakeFlag - is set to 1 on the
   way past, at 0x00466504.

   1.6 IS THE BRAKE TURN. 0x3fcccccd, applied only when the brake
   is held AND a direction is pressed. Braking into a corner turns
   the bike 60 per cent harder, which is a real technique in this
   game and is now sourced rather than felt.

   SO THE CHAIN IS SHORTER THAN 9.29.19 CLAIMED. steer turns
   matOrientation here; SetLongSpeed then reprojects the velocity
   onto the turned frame, which is what makes the slip. Both are
   real and they are not alternatives - the first is the steering,
   the second is what the steering does to momentum.

   AND THE WHOLE TURN RATE, 0x00466480..0x004664f8:

       t    = speed / vitesseMaxBoite * 0.5      carac 19, 0x004d0f08
       if (t < 0.0) t = 0.0                      0x004d0f28
       else         t = min(t, 0.5)

       rate = rotationTransfert * (1.0 - 0.5 * t)      carac 20
       if (racer+0x307d != 0) rate *= 0.5              0x004664f2

       g    = rate * dt * 0.01
       yaw  = f * g * steer

   THE SPEED TERM IS CONFIRMED BY ITS DIVISOR, not guessed: the
   numerator is divided by carac 19, vitesseMaxBoite, so it is a
   speed by construction. t runs 0 to 0.5, so the factor
   (1 - 0.5t) runs 1.0 down to 0.75 - THE BIKE TURNS 25 PER CENT
   LESS AT TOP SPEED THAN AT REST. Both carac fields were already
   named in bikes.h, and rotationTransfert's existing comment,
   "scales the steering TORQUE", is exactly right.

   THE SECOND 0.5 IS THE WHEELIE HALVING, not a damage state.
   racer+0x307d is written by maybe_Racer_UpdateWheelie at
   0x004660fe and is THE WHEELIE ANGLE - which also re-reads the
   third crash condition in 9.29.15 as what it plainly is: WHEELIE
   ABOVE 20 km/h AND YOU GO DOWN. A real rule, not an unidentified
   flag.

   AND ALL OF THIS WAS ALREADY IN src/racer.c. racer_steer_torque
   carries the speed fade, the wheelie halving, the AI branch and
   the grip term - including the 1.6 at 0x004d10e0, which is the
   value the brake-into-a-corner case pins rather than an override
   of something unread. The "unread value at 0x004665c2" of an
   earlier draft is grip = clamp(1 + |slip| * K, 1.0, 1.6).

   So ApplySteering's tail was transcribed before this pass started,
   with every constant correctly identified. Re-deriving it produced
   two duplicate functions, since removed. CHECK src/ BEFORE
   TRANSCRIBING - the grep is cheaper than the read.

   THE YAW IS AN INCREMENT, AND FUN_0045cb00 IS WHERE IT COMPOSES.
   Read at 0x0045cb0a..0x0045cb19:

       ESI = racer + 0xbc                    matOrientation
       Matrix34_Multiply(scratch, arg, matOrientation)
       matOrientation = scratch              0x0045cb1e onward

   So the function is not a setter at all - it MULTIPLIES the
   incoming rotation into matOrientation and writes the product back.
   That is what the dt scaling demanded: an absolute set would snap
   the bike to a fixed heading every tick.

   AND IT COPIES TWELVE FLOATS, not six. 9.29.10 read the tail of the
   copy - +0xd4 through +0xe8 - and concluded matOrientation must be
   a 3x4 because forward and translation move together. Right
   conclusion, half the evidence: the copy starts at +0xbc, at
   0x0045cb20, and covers the whole matrix.

   ITS OTHER FOUR CALLERS all pass a rotation the same way:
   FUN_0045e560, Racer_AiFollowLine, maybe_Racer_ApplySteering and
   maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact. So every path that turns a racer,
   human or AI or network, turns it through here.

   FUN_0046d270 is called on the way out, at 0x0045cb8c, and is not
   read. Re-orthonormalising the product would be the obvious job for
   it, since repeated composition drifts. [open]

   THIS EXPLAINS THE SPEED-DEPENDENT LEAN that prompted the whole
   line of investigation. Turn rate falls with speed here, and
   physics_centripetal scales the resulting force with speed again;
   the two together are what a rider feels.

9.29.24.  What is not read yet

   o The ordinary position advance - see 9.29.7.

   o The airborne branch from 0x0046974b. Its first act is
     racer+0x2db8 = 0, which is onGround, so that field is
     confirmed. It then tests racer+0x8 (humanPlayer) and
     racer+0x4 (soloMode).

   o The three BeginCrash conditions at 0x00469174, 0x00469226 and
     0x0046928e.
9.30.  POSE AND EFFECTS - the decidable pieces

   maybe_Racer_UpdateSkeleton (0x00467b30) and maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx
   (0x00466d70), the last two named calls in 9.26.2. NEITHER IS
   TRANSCRIBED WHOLE and neither should be yet: the pose fill needs
   maybe_Racer_SelectLod and the g_abPilotRot rest-pose table, and the
   three emitters need the trail, ribbon and particle systems.

   What went into src/ is the DECISIONS - which slot, whether to emit,
   how wide, how many. Those are self-contained, cheap to get wrong,
   and they are the part a port must match for the game to LOOK right
   rather than merely work.

9.30.1.  The skeleton slot, and a store that is not redundant

       slot = (crashLatch || handSkeleton) ? 1 : SelectLod()
       ... fill the pose, only when slot < 2 ...
       if (handSkeleton) slot = 1

   o handSkeleton FORCES SLOT 1 TWICE and the second store is NOT
     redundant. SelectLod cannot have run in that case, but the pose
     update can have moved the slot underneath. A reader tidying the
     "duplicate" away changes behaviour.

   o bAnimate IS UpdateGroundContact'S RETURN VALUE, and 9.26.2's
     table already showed the same flag gating EmitWheelFx. So a
     racer with no ground contact snaps to the static pose AND emits
     nothing - one value, two consequences.

9.30.2.  Effects exist only near the camera

   Nothing is emitted unless the racer's segment lies within
   [viewed - 10, viewed + 30] of the viewed racer's, wrapped.

   o THE FORWARD EXTENT IS 30 SEGMENTS - the same number the
     proximity flag vanishes at, MR_Racer.txt 23.3, found
     independently from the other side. Two roads to one constant is
     worth more than either alone.

   o THE WRAP HELPER IS UNREAD. src/racer.c computes the signed
     wrapped difference instead, which is equivalent on a closed
     track; the bounds are read, the helper is not. [inferred]

9.30.3.  The skid ribbon widens twice as fast as it narrows

       widen  by +2.0 while |lateral| exceeds skidWidthPrev
       shrink by -1.0 otherwise
       clamp to [1.0, 12.0]
       AN AI IS PINNED AT 5.0 and never runs the update

   o SO THE RIBBON OPENS TWICE AS FAST AS IT CLOSES. A slide that
     worsens widens the mark immediately and it tapers back slowly -
     which is what a real skid looks like and is not something a
     symmetric rule would give.

   o AND AN AI LAYS A CONSTANT-WIDTH MARK however hard it is
     sliding, which is consistent with 9.26.1: an AI is placed, not
     simulated, so it has no slide to measure.

   o THE +2.0 IS 0x004d0e88, THE SAME .rdata DOUBLE that doubles the
     centripetal term in physics_centripetal.

9.30.4.  Three constants now have two users each

   The 3 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/physics.c.

   o NONE OF THE THREE IS A COINCIDENCE, and the first is the most
     useful: sharing the air step with the airborne threshold is what
     makes ApplyCornering's test mean "more than ONE FRAME" rather
     than "past some tuned height".

   o THIS IS WORTH WATCHING FOR. A constant with two readers in a
     4000-line subsystem usually means the second reader is
     expressing something IN TERMS OF the first - and that
     relationship is the finding, not the number.

9.30.5.  The spray count is a six-to-one LOD cut

       particles = humanPlayer ? 24 : 4

   o A CROWDED FIELD THROWS 24 PARTICLES FOR THE PLAYER AND 4 FOR
     EVERY OTHER RIDER. Matching that ratio is most of why a
     reconstruction looks like the original rather than like a
     particle demo, and it costs one line.

9.30.6.  The protection block is not in src/

   maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx opens with copy-protection check B
   (copy-protection.txt 5.2) - an eight-entry byte table walked
   against maybe_CdAudio_GetTrackPos. It draws ILLEGAL VERSION and
   falls through with NO field payload, which is what makes it the
   mildest of the four sites.

   o IT IS NOT TRANSCRIBED AND NO TRACE OF IT IS IN src/. Following
     9.29.5, the whole subsystem is now free of it: nothing in src/
     reads a byte of code, checks a window token, or can zero a bike.

9.31.  FORK DIVE - and maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed has a twin

   FUN_004667e0, now maybe_Racer_UpdateForkDive (0x004667e0). Sixty
   lines of decompile that closed two open questions and named a third
   function on the way past.

9.31.1.  steerReturnScale is the longitudinal acceleration

       steerReturnScale = (longSpeed - speedTail)
                          / (frameDelta * 0.01)

   A delta-speed over a delta-time and nothing more. racer+0x0f8 had a
   name and no meaning; it is an ACCELERATION.

   o WHICH CLOSES A LOOP WITH THE STEERING. 9.27.2's centring path
     computes k = max(steerReturnScale * RoueVitesseRetour / 6,
     RoueVitesseRetour), so THE BARS SELF-CENTRE FASTER UNDER
     ACCELERATION OR BRAKING and at the plain rate while coasting.
     Neither function says that on its own.

9.31.2.  forkDive is a peak hold of that acceleration

       a = ((longSpeed - speedTail) + prevAccel) * 0.5
       if (|forkDive| < |a * -PI/32|)  forkDive = a * -PI/32
       clamp to +-PI/16

   o THE SCALE IS EXACTLY HALF THE CLAMP. -PI/32 against +-PI/16, so
     an acceleration of 1.0 gives half deflection and 2.0 saturates.
     Another exact ratio rather than a tuned number.

   o THE SIGN IS NEGATIVE: accelerating drives forkDive negative -
     the front LIFTS - and braking drives it positive. That is fork
     dive and lift, and it independently confirms the trace hook on
     the front wheel matrix that 9.7 used to rule out "steering".

   o IT ONLY EVER GROWS HERE. maybe_Racer_Tick decays it by a fixed
     0.01 per frame (9.26.3), so what a viewer sees is this peak hold
     racing that decay. NEITHER FUNCTION IS COMPREHENSIBLE ALONE and
     9.26.3 had to describe them together before this one was read.

9.31.3.  maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed has a twin

   FUN_0048ca10 is now maybe_Racer_LongSpeed, and the two are THE SAME
   FUNCTION BODY with a different matrix row selected:

   +------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------+
   | Function                     | Matrix row   | What it returns                        |
   +==============================+==============+========================================+
   | maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed        | row 0        | LATERAL velocity - the slide, feeding  |
   |                              |              | the grip curve                         |
   +------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_LongSpeed        | row 2        | LONGITUDINAL velocity - the forward    |
   |                              |              | speed, feeding v*v/r and the fork dive |
   +------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------------------+

   o THE OPENINGS ARE BYTE-FOR-BYTE IDENTICAL - same instructions,
     same offsets, same stack layout - and the only difference is
     which four slots of the built inverse get multiplied out at the
     end. SlipSpeed reads inverse indices 0, 3, 6, 9; LongSpeed reads
     2, 5, 8, 11.

   o SO 9.24.2's "EIGHT OF THE TWELVE ARE DEAD" WAS HALF THE STORY.
     The generic matrix-inverse idiom is reused because a SECOND
     function needs a different row of the same inverse. Each
     function still wastes eight terms, but the shape now has a
     reason.

   o IT IS ALSO THE READ SIDE OF maybe_Racer_SetLongSpeed, which
     9.14 named from the write side alone. src/physics.c keeps ONE
     implementation and selects the row - a simplification of form
     only, with the arithmetic unchanged.

9.31.4.  Constants

   +------------------+------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | Address          | Value            | Role                                         |
   +==================+==================+==============================================+
   | 0x004d1018       | 0.5              | the two-tap low pass on the acceleration     |
   +------------------+------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d1108       | -0.09817477      | -PI/32, acceleration to angle - EXACTLY HALF |
   |                  |                  | the clamp                                    |
   +------------------+------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d1110       | +0.19634954      | +PI/16, the clamp                            |
   +------------------+------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d10f8       | -0.19634954      | -PI/16                                       |
   +------------------+------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d0ed8       | 0.01             | the dt scale - a THIRD user of this double,  |
   |                  |                  | with the decay and cornering                 |
   +------------------+------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d1120       | 6.272            | the per-wheel air timer rate                 |
   +------------------+------------------+----------------------------------------------+

   o 0x004d0ed8 NOW HAS THREE READERS - the fork-dive decay in
     maybe_Racer_Tick, the dt scale in maybe_Racer_ApplyCornering,
     and the dt scale here. 9.30.4 called it "this codebase's small
     step constant" and a third user settles that.

9.31.5.  The onGroundPair consumer, found

   The function also keeps TWO PER-WHEEL AIR TIMERS, at +0xa8 inside
   each wheel sub-record, advanced by frameDelta * 6.272 while the
   matching onGroundPair flag is clear and zeroed when it is set.

   o SO THE CLEAR-THEN-SET PAIR OF 9.29.4 HAS A SECOND CONSUMER.
     9.29 knew only of the pose code at 0x004668c9. The pair exists
     to drive per-wheel airborne accumulators - which is why there
     are TWO of them and only one onGround.
