   CONSTRUCTION.  racer_frame_from_forward is reused unchanged (9.40);
   only the source of the two points differs, and the AI zeroes row3
   afterwards where the player does not.

   THE HOLES ARE WRITTEN AS COMMENTED CALLS CARRYING THEIR ADDRESSES,
   not dropped, so the shape of the frame is right and what is
   missing is visible:

   +----------------------+------------+--------------------------+
   | Hole                 | Address    | Effect while it is open  |
   +======================+============+==========================+
   | Racer_AiFollowLine   | 0x0046b120 | the opponent never moves |
   +----------------------+------------+--------------------------+
   | FUN_0046c7a0         | 0x0046c7a0 | stands in as 0, which is |
   |                      |            | the not-on-it answer, so |
   |                      |            | both arms stay reachable |
   +----------------------+------------+--------------------------+
   | FUN_00467930         | 0x00467930 | the tail call, unread    |
   +----------------------+------------+--------------------------+
   +----------------------+------------+--------------------------+

   maybe_Racer_UpdateLean WAS A FOURTH HOLE and is closed: its body
   had been inline in race_tick_physics, reachable only by the
   player, and is now racer_update_lean in racer.c.  The move is a
   move, not a rewrite - four race.c statics became parameters (the
   track, the drivetrain speed, the step in milliseconds, and the
   three published yaws, which are diagnostics and may be NULL).

   So an opponent is snapped to the ground, oriented, shadowed and
   drawn, and does not move.  That is the honest state and it is
   worth more than a shell that fakes motion: every step that IS
   written can be diffed against MOTO today.

9.47.5.1.  The ground lift, read off the disassembly

   The decompiler renders this block as a bare __ftol() with no
   argument, because the value being converted is on the FPU stack.
   0x0046c211 has it:

       n = (int)(engineSpeed / realSpeedMax * 8.0)   racer+0xec,
                                                     racer+0xb4,
                                                     0x004d1020
       if (n != 0)  position.y = ground - rand() % n

   gated on persoAnim != 0 AND the 0x0046c7a0 query returning 0.  SO
   A DIRT AI IS SUNK INTO THE GROUND BY A RANDOM AMOUNT THAT SCALES
   WITH ITS SPEED - roughness, and the reason a dirt opponent never
   sits flat.  A bare __ftol() in this subsystem always means go and
   read the two instructions above the call.


9.47.5.2.  First diff against MOTO's racer 2

   THE LINE FOLLOWER IS A HOLE, so a plain side-by-side diverges on
   frame one for a reason already known.  Step MOTO first, copy its
   POST-follow-line position, engineSpeed, speed, moveDir and
   segmentStart into ours, then step ours: steps 3..12 then run on
   the same input on both sides and the comparison is about the code
   that IS written.

   NINE OF TWELVE MATCH EXACTLY - flAirTime, riderStateBits,
   onGround, both contact-pair flags, springCompression,
   segmentStart, flImpactBlend - and matOrientation to 0.004.  Three
   differ, and all three are explained:

   +-------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | Field       | Why                                          |
   +=============+==============================================+
   | rearWheel   | NOT A DIFFERENCE. See 9.47.5.3.              |
   | Contact.y   |                                              |
   +-------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | lean,       | the maybe_Racer_UpdateLean hole. Nothing     |
   | leanPrev    | sets lean each frame, so racer_ai_lean_step  |
   |             | multiplies the same value by 1.2 until it    |
   |             | pins at the pi/4 clamp: -0.7854 against      |
   |             | MOTO's -0.0203. The hole behaving exactly as |
   |             | predicted, not a new defect.                 |
   +-------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | stunt       | racer+0xb4 HAS NO WRITER FOR OPPONENTS in the|
   | OrOverspeed | port. MOTO's racer 2 holds 72.222 and ours   |
   |             | holds 0.0, so the overspeed test reads       |
   |             | 0 < engineSpeed and is always true. This is  |
   |             | the substitution warning in section 4 biting |
   |             | on the first run - it needs                  |
   |             | maybe_Race_SetOpponentSpeeds.                |
   +-------------+----------------------------------------------+

9.47.5.3.  The stored contact pair is overwritten by the DRAW

   Read after the frame, MOTO's racer 2 has rearWheelContact.y = 0.0
   and ours has -5.76.  The formula says -5.76: for carac 0,
   96 * HauteurCadre - 192 * RoueRayonAr is 96*0.58 - 192*0.32.

   HOOKING maybe_Racer_UpdateLean's ENTRY SETTLES IT.  That runs
   immediately after the contact block, and at that point MOTO's
   racer 2 reads

       rearWheelContact = (18.979, -5.760, 10658.5)

   so the port's value is right and the difference appears LATER:
   maybe_Racer_Draw at 0x0045f87c rebuilds racer+0x1B4 from
   racer+0x338 and the wheelbase, with no radius term, which is what
   racer_wheel_ground already records.

   THE FIELD MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS AT TWO POINTS IN THE FRAME -
   a chassis reference during the tick, a ground contact after the
   draw.  Comparing it end-of-frame compares the draw, not the tick.
   READ IT AT THE POINT THE CODE UNDER TEST WROTE IT.

9.47.5.4.  A ZERO-dt FRAME IS NOT A SIMULATION STEP

   race_tick ran the whole physics tick on EVERY RENDERED FRAME,
   including the ones where dt came out zero - and while the port is
   paused it still renders, so that is most of them.  The particle
   tick and the camera beside it already carried a dt > 0 guard; the
   racer tick did not.

   IT HID BECAUSE ALMOST EVERYTHING SCALES BY dt and a zero step is a
   no-op.  racer_ai_lean_step does not: it takes no dt at all and
   multiplies the lean by 1.2 a call, so a paused game drove an
   opponent's lean to the pi/4 clamp within a few hundred idle frames
   and pinned it there.  The AI was simply the first code in the port
   with a dt-free accumulator in it.

   THE SYMPTOM LOOKED LIKE A DEAD CLOCK.  Hooking racer_tick_dispatch
   showed dtScaled = 0 on 40 consecutive calls, which reads as "the
   frame pacer is broken" - and the pacer was fine.  The hook's cap
   was being spent entirely on idle frames before a stepped one
   arrived.  WHEN A HOOK ON A PER-FRAME FUNCTION SHOWS ONE VALUE
   FOREVER, ASK WHETHER THE FRAMES IT CAUGHT WERE STEPS.

   The original calls Race_TickAllRacers once per simulation step,
   never per rendered frame.  race.c now matches.

9.47.5.5.  The last field: UpdateLean divides by racer+0xb4

   With the zero-dt guard in, every field of the racer-2 diff matched
   except the lean - MOTO -0.1235 against ours -0.0771, on identical
   seeded input.

   0x00469cd1 IS THE ANSWER and it is one instruction:

       00469ccb  FLD  float [ESI+0x124]      speed
       00469cd1  FDIV float [ESI+0xb4]       THE RACER's realSpeedMax
       00469cd7  FMUL float [0x004d1020]     * 8.0
       00469cdd  FSUBR float [0x004d0e70]    1.0 - that

   That is racer_lean_blend_weight, the alpha the travel frame is
   built with, and it divides by racer+0xb4 - NOT the carac's
   RealSpeedMax, which this port was passing.

   FOR RACER 1 THE TWO ARE THE SAME NUMBER, because Racer_Create
   copies the carac's RealSpeedMax into +0xb4, which is why the
   substitution survived every player-only comparison this project
   has run.  AN OPPONENT'S COPY COMES FROM THE ComputerSpeed TABLE -
   75.0 against the carac's 80.0 on ile01 - so the blend, the travel
   frame, yawFrame and the lean were all wrong for every AI.

   TWO CALL SITES, ONE INSTRUCTION.  The port models 0x00469cd1
   twice - once in racer_lean_blend_weight for the travel-frame
   alpha and once in racer_lean_delta for the speed gate - because
   the same 1 - speed/realSpeedMax*8 expression is read twice on our
   side.  BOTH HAD TO CHANGE.  Fixing only the first took the gap
   from 0.046 to 0.022 rad and left the rest sitting there, which is
   what a half-applied fix looks like: better, and still wrong.

   MEASURED AT THE AMPLIFIER, hooking 0x0046c6dc and 0x0046c6f3:
   racer 2 enters the AI lean step at 0.29444 and leaves at 0.35333,
   which is exactly x1.2, with flImpactBlend 0 and speed 75.82.  The
   pair of hooks either side of a multiply is the cheapest way to
   tell a wrong INPUT from a wrong OPERATION.

9.47.5.6.  Where racer_tick_ai stands

   ELEVEN OF TWELVE FIELDS ARE EXACT against MOTO's racer 2 on
   seeded input - flAirTime, both wheel contacts, riderStateBits,
   onGround, both contact-pair flags, stuntOrOverspeed,
   springCompression, segmentStart and flImpactBlend, the contacts
   to 1e-4 at 48,000 units out.

   THE LEAN IS WITHIN 3 PER CENT - -0.5292 against -0.5452 on a
   banked section, 0.016 rad at worst.  It arrives through
   racer_lean_delta, which is SHARED WITH THE PLAYER PATH and which
   the street-turn comparison matches to five decimals, so the
   residual is in the delta rather than in anything TickAI owns.
   [open - 0.016 rad of AI lean]

   THE FOUR SESSIONS OF DIFFING FOUND FOUR REAL DEFECTS, and it is
   worth being clear that only one was in the new code:

   +---------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | Defect                    | Where it actually was              |
   +===========================+====================================+
   | segment vs segmentStart   | the new transcription - carried in |
   |                           | from the old slices                |
   +---------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | racer+0xb4 unwritten      | the port, since before the AI      |
   +---------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | the tick ran on zero-dt   | the port, since before the AI      |
   | frames                    |                                    |
   +---------------------------+------------------------------------+
   | the piecewise lean is     | the DOCUMENTATION, for three       |
   | human-only                | sessions - 9.71.0                  |
   +---------------------------+------------------------------------+

   TICKING A SECOND RACER IS WHAT FOUND THEM.  Every one had been
   invisible while only racer 1 was simulated, because racer 1 is the
   one racer for which the carac and racer+0xb4 agree, the one that
   reaches the piecewise arm, and the one whose every term is
   dt-scaled.


9.47.6.  The size of the job, measured

   HOOKED AND CONFIRMED LIVE.  A hook on maybe_Racer_TickAI's entry
   fired eight times in one two-frame step with ECX at 0x0058762a,
   0x0058a833 ... 0x0059d469 and EBX counting 2..9 - so the AI arm
   really does run once per opponent per frame, and EBX is the racer
   index.  Captures of racer+0xb4, +0x124, +0x2e1d and +0x2e21 came
   back 70.175 / 68.72 / -0.518 / -0.518, which confirms 9.47.4:
   leanPrev is written, and tracks lean, on this path only.

   WHAT IS STILL TO IMPORT.  The callee sets of maybe_Racer_TickAI
   and Racer_AiFollowLine, minus everything already transcribed:

   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   | Function                 | Address    | Bytes | What it is      |
   +==========================+============+=======+=================+
   | Racer_AiFollowLine       | 0x0046b120 |  3005 | the only        |
   |                          |            |       | decision made   |
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   | maybe_Racer_TickAI       | 0x0046bf80 |  2076 | the orchestrator|
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   | FUN_0046cd20             | 0x0046cd20 |  1354 | the charge      |
   |                          |            |       | meter, +0x31d5  |
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   | FUN_0046c970 .. 0046cb40 | 0x0046c970 |   944 | four racer-array|
   |                          |            |       | scans it uses   |
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   | FUN_00467930             | 0x00467930 |   499 | unread          |
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   | FUN_0046c7a0             | 0x0046c7a0 |   458 | a track query   |
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   | maybe_Mat34_LookAt       | 0x0046bdc0 |   442 | not camera_look |
   |                          |            |       | _at, 0x0046e140 |
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+
   |                    TOTAL |            |  8778 | ten functions   |
   +--------------------------+------------+-------+-----------------+

   WHAT IS DONE, against that list:

   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   | Function                 | Bytes | State                      |
   +==========================+=======+============================+
   | Racer_AiFollowLine       |  3005 | in, verified end to end    |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   | maybe_Racer_TickAI       |  2076 | in, 11 of 12 fields exact  |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   | Racer_AiLaneStep         |  1354 | in, untested - needs a     |
   | (was FUN_0046cd20)       |       | field to exercise          |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   | FUN_0046c970 .. 0046cb40 |   944 | in, all four               |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   | maybe_Mat34_LookAt       |   442 | in                         |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   | FUN_00467930             |   499 | in, one hole - 9.48.0.6    |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   | FUN_0046c7a0             |   458 | in - 9.48.0.5              |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+
   |                    8778 of 8778, all ten                      |
   +--------------------------+-------+----------------------------+

   AND FOUR THINGS THAT WERE NOT ON THE LIST, because the estimate
   counted the AI's own call graph and not what ticking a second
   racer would expose:

     o race_set_opponent_speeds, the solo arm of 0x0047bc60 - the
       only writer of racer+0xb4 for an opponent;
     o racer_update_lean factored out of race_tick_physics, because
       TickAI calls UpdateLean and the port had its body inline;
     o the FOURTH lean formula, 9.71.0 - the piecewise curve turns
       out to be a live human solo rider's alone;
     o the dt > 0 guard on the whole tick, 9.47.5.4.

   EVERYTHING ELSE TickAI CALLS IS ALREADY IN THE PORT - SpinWheels,
   UpdateSuspension, UpdateWheelie, UpdateSkeleton, UpdateCrash,
   EmitWheelFx, UpdateLean, BuildShadow, SavePrevFrame,
   ClipMoveAgainstWalls and the Trk_ and Vec3_ helpers.  That is most
   of a racer tick and it is done, which is why the number above is
   as small as it is.

   NINE SLICES ARE ALREADY WRITTEN, three out of TickAI and six out
   of AiFollowLine - every one carrying a two-address ORIGIN, which
   rule 15 marks as owing a whole-function import.  Folding them in
   is part of this work and reduces the file rather than adding to
   it.

   THE PORT MUST ALSO TICK MORE THAN ONE RACER.  race.c drives racer
   1 alone today, so Race_TickAllRacers' non-time-attack loop and the
   dispatch fork on humanPlayer come with the job.

   AND c->realSpeedMax STOPS BEING A VALID SUBSTITUTION the moment
   this lands - see the racer+0xb4 note above.


9.48.  THE LINE FOLLOWER - a pursuit model, not a driver

   FUN_0046b120, now Racer_AiFollowLine (0x0046b120), 3005 bytes. It
   is the only place the AI makes a decision; everything else in
   maybe_Racer_TickAI reacts to what it did.

   IT DOES NOT EMULATE A DRIVER. There is no throttle, no brake and
   no steering angle - the AI never touches the player's inputs. Each
   step it picks a point on the racing line ahead, lerps its
   direction toward it, blends its speed by how well aligned it
   already is, and integrates. That is the whole thing.

   THE ONE INPUT IT SYNTHESISES is tickInputWord = 1 at the very top,
   which is "accelerate held". 9.47 recorded the odd swap TickAI does
   around EmitWheelFx without being able to say what was in the
   field; this is what. An AI bike throws dust as if its rider were
   on the gas, because as far as the effects are concerned he always
   is.

9.48.0.  racer+0x198 AND racer+0x1a4 ARE NOT A POINTER

   The DB typed +0x198 as one, which makes the decompiler render
   every access as *(float *)(unk_0198 + 0xc) - pointer arithmetic
   that reads as a separate object and is really just racer+0x1a4.

   READ LIVE off three AI racers:

       racer 2   +0x198 (-0.4876, -0.0969, 0.8677)   |v| 1.0000
                 moveDir (-0.2713, -0.1203, 0.9549)
                 +0x1a4  75.833      engineSpeed 75.768

   |v| IS 1.0000 ON EVERY ONE, and it is NOT moveDir, which sits at
   +0x18c and differs.  So +0x198 is a unit AIM vector and +0x1a4 is
   the AI's target road speed.  Both are named in the DB and in
   racer.h as aimDir and aiRoadSpeed.

   RACER 1 HOLDS (0,0,1) AND 0.0 and always will - nothing on the
   player path writes either, which is why this stayed unread.

   IT ALSO CLOSES A MARKED HOLE.  racer_place_wheels already wrote
   matOrientation row2 + row3 into racer+0x198 at 0x0045c92e with a
   "marked hole" note against it; that IS the aim vector, built the
   same way the line follower builds it.

9.48.0.1.  THE LOOK-AHEAD, and what aiming at nothing does

   0x0046b1d9..0x0046b1f9 computes two segment offsets and adds them
   to segmentStart before the wrap:

       dir = g_nRaceDirection (0x004dc140), +1 or -1

       PersoAnim    far = dir * 9     near = dir * 2
       otherwise    far = dir * 16    near = dir * 4

   THE FAR POINT SETS THE HEADING, at 0x0046b202; the NEAR point, at
   0x0046b5e1, is what the travel direction is pulled toward.  A DIRT
   BIKE LOOKS HALF AS FAR AHEAD AS A ROAD BIKE, which is what lets it
   take a tighter line.

   THE DECOMPILER SHOWS Trk_WrapSegmentIndex() WITH NO ARGUMENTS at
   both sites, so an import taken from the decompile alone aims at
   the CURRENT segment - the point the rider is standing on.  The
   bearing to it is meaningless, the backwards guard then flips
   moveDir and engineSpeed every frame, and the bike walks the track
   in reverse: measured racer 2 going segment 787 -> 786 with
   engineSpeed at -11.4 and aiRoadSpeed collapsing to zero.

   AN EMPTY ARGUMENT LIST IN A DECOMPILE IS A HOLE, NOT A FACT.  Two
   instructions above the CALL had it.

9.48.0.2.  TWO DELTAS, AND MIXING THEM IS A FACTOR OF TEN

   Racer_AiFollowLine is handed the same param_2 maybe_Racer_TickAI
   was - g_frameDeltaScaled, 3.3 at a 33 ms step - and EVERY dt term
   in it is scaled that way: the throttle ramp (1/6), the engine
   bleed (1/18), the airborne gravity (0.24525) and the move (1.28).

   maybe_Racer_UpdateLean IS THE OPPOSITE CASE and divides by
   milliseconds.  Both are reached from the same tick, so the AI
   tick carries both quantities and a call site has to know which it
   is holding.

   PASSING ms TO THE FOLLOW LINE launched an opponent off the grid at
   ten times the right speed - the move alone became 75 * 33 * 1.28
   instead of 75 * 3.3 * 1.28, about 3170 units a frame.  The helper
   parameters are still NAMED dtMs from when they were slices; the
   name is wrong and the value they want is the scaled one.

9.48.0.3.  Verified: both games run the same opponent

   Seed racer 2 from MOTO ONCE - the twenty-six fields the follow
   line reads - then let both run free.  No per-frame reseeding, so
   this measures the whole loop rather than one step of it.

       n |  MOTO seg  eng   | ours seg  eng   | pos err | dir dot
       0 |        97  75.76 |       97  75.58 |    63.7 |  0.9962
       3 |       101  75.77 |      101  75.75 |   172.3 |  0.9991
       7 |       106  75.78 |      106  75.74 |   244.8 |  0.9999
      11 |       111  75.77 |      110  75.77 |   285.4 |  0.9999

   engineSpeed AGREES TO 0.01 and is identical by step 8.  The
   heading dot CONVERGES to 0.9999 - under a degree apart - rather
   than drifting, so the turn is right.  The segment tracks within
   one.

   THE POSITION ERROR IS DECELERATING, not compounding: the
   per-step increments run 49, 33, 27, 20, 16, 14, 22, 8, 20, 6, 7.
   285 units after roughly 7,000 travelled is a 4 per cent
   along-track lag that settles, which is what a small
   first-frame offset looks like once the two loops lock together.
   A diverging follow line would look nothing like this.

   WHAT IS STILL MISSING is the charge meter (FUN_0046cd20), so
   chargeMeter never changes and every AI drives the SAME line
   instead of its own parallel one.  [open]

9.48.0.4.  FUN_0046cd20 IS THE LANE LOGIC, not a charge meter

   The name came from reading only its tail, where a float ramps to a
   ceiling and decays - which is exactly what a charge meter looks
   like.  It is the OUTPUT.  chargeMeter is the rider's LATERAL
   OFFSET from the racing line, ramped toward lane * 240 at 1.0667
   units a step (0x004d1308), and Racer_AiFollowLine adds it to the
   segment's own offset to get the point it aims at.  THREE LANES 240
   APART IS WHAT SPREADS A FIELD ACROSS THE ROAD.

   The decision above it, 0x0046cd60 .. 0x0046d1ad:

   1. IF THE INSIDE IS CLEAR, TAKE IT.  lane > 0 and nobody in
      lane-1 within a half window, and the lane simply decrements -
      so a field collapses back onto the racing line whenever it is
      not fighting.
   2. Otherwise find the nearest rider in MY lane in the window.
      None, and there is nothing to do.
   3. ACT ONLY IF I AM FASTER.  THE SIGN CONVENTION IS INVERTED: the
      comparison yields -1 when OURS is the greater and the code acts
      on -1 only.  Read the natural way it makes an AI try to
      overtake exactly the bikes it cannot catch.
   4. Take the first completely empty lane, if it is within one of
      mine.
   5. Failing that, FUN_0046cb40 returns the lane whose occupant is
      FASTEST - the least bad place to be stuck, not the emptiest -
      and the move needs the blocker at least 7 segments ahead.
   6. STILL STUCK: match his speed.  engineSpeed lerps 1/32 of the
      way (0x004d1158), is trimmed by 0.95 (0x004d1310), and if the
      change exceeds 11.1 m/s (0x004d1318) tickInputWord becomes 2 -
      THE BRAKE.  So an AI closing on a slower bike throws brake dust
      like a rider would, and that is the second thing after the
      accelerate bit that the AI synthesises into the input word.
   7. The ramp, and only on the ground, so a jumping bike holds the
      line it left with.

   THE WINDOW SIZES ARE SWAPPED BY THE REVERSE FLAG.  0x004dc144 is
   the race's own latched copy of it, not a separate setting: it has
   ONE writer, maybe_Game_LoadRace at 0x0047b460, which copies the
   settings dword at 0x00542878 - and MenuPage01_Loading_Tick copies
   that SAME dword into g_reverseEnabled (0x004dc068) at 0x0040b854.
   One source, two latches, and all three read 0 together with
   g_nRaceDirection at +1.

   Forward gives 12 ahead / 0 behind for the same-lane test, 6 and 4
   for the inside check, 12 and 12 for the empty-lane sweep.  REVERSE
   SWAPS AHEAD AND BEHIND THROUGHOUT, which is right - running the
   track backwards makes a higher segment index the way the bike came
   from, so the rider it has to watch for is on the other side of it.

   THE THREE HELPERS are a wrapped window test (0x0046c970), a
   forward segment distance that wraps once and is never negative
   (0x0046c9f0), and a nearest-in-lane scan (0x0046ca10).

   MEASURED ON MOTO, mid-race, racers 2..8:

       lane          2    1    1    0    1    0    0
       chargeMeter 480  162  244    0  240    0    0

   480 is 2 * 240 and 240 is 1 * 240 exactly, and 162 and 244 are
   riders part-way through the ramp - which confirms the lane * 240
   reading from the outside.

   NOTHING ASSIGNS THE STARTING LANES.  Racer_Reset zeroes the field
   at 0x0045bcec - EAX is XORed clear at 0x0045bcca and used for the
   whole run of stores around it - and the only other writers are
   Racer_AiLaneStep, maybe_Racer_Tick and maybe_Racer_TickRemote.
   MOTO'S FIELD STARTS IN LANE 0 TOO; it is spread across three lanes
   only because it has been racing.  Comparing a MOTO mid-race
   against our port on the grid is what made this look like a missing
   assignment, and it was not one.

   THE PLAYER'S LANE IS MEASURED, NOT CHOSEN.  maybe_Racer_Tick calls
   maybe_Trk_LaneOfRacer (0x0046d340) at 0x0045d7f2 and stores the
   answer:

       d = dot(row0, position - origin) - dot(row0, row3)
       lane 0,1,2 at off 0, 240, 480:
           base = off + segment +0xb0
           base - 120 < d < base + 120   ->  that lane
       d < (+0xb0) - 120  ->  0
       (+0xb0) + 600 < d  ->  2
       otherwise          -> -1

   THE LANES ARE 240 WIDE AND THE TEST IS +-120 ABOUT EACH CENTRE -
   the same 240 racer_ai_lane_step ramps chargeMeter by, so the two
   agree on the geometry from opposite ends.  0x004d1320 is the 120
   and 0x004d1324 the 600.

   IT EXISTS SO THE AI CAN SEE THE HUMAN AS TRAFFIC.  Without it the
   player reads as lane 0 forever and every AI treats it as being on
   the inside line.  AND IT CAN RETURN -1, for a bike in a gap the
   three bands do not cover, which the original stores in the lane
   field without comment.

   VERIFIED AGAINST MOTO at ten lateral offsets on the same spot,
   placed identically in both games:

       x      -600 -300 -120    0  120  240  360  480  600  800
       MOTO      0    0    0    0    1    1    2    2    2    2
       ours      0    0    0    0    1    1    2    2    2    2

   Both band edges and both out-of-range fallbacks agree.  AND THAT
   ALSO CHECKS THE TRACK PARSE: the band centres are built on the
   segment's +0xb0 field, so the two games agreeing on where the
   lanes fall means our loader reads that float the same way MOTO
   does - see trk.txt 10, where it is otherwise undecoded.

9.48.0.5.  FUN_0046c7a0 IS A CORNER DETECTOR

   Two chords from this segment's origin - one to the segment `ahead`
   on, one to the half-way segment - flattened into XZ, normalised,
   and compared:

       |dot(halfChord, fullChord)| > 0.995  ->  0, a straight
       otherwise                            ->  1, a corner

   Both look-aheads are multiplied by g_nRaceDirection, so it reads
   the way the race is actually run.  0x004d1300 is the 0.995.

   IT RETURNS 1 FOR A CORNER, which is worth stating because the
   caller uses it to SUPPRESS: maybe_Racer_TickAI clears
   stuntOrOverspeed on a bend, and rolls for a stunt or applies the
   PersoAnim ground lift only on a straight.  AI RIDERS DO NOT PULL
   WHEELIES THROUGH CORNERS.

9.48.0.6.  FUN_00467930 IS THE DASHBOARD, and the speedometer is
           MEASURED

       d = rearWheelContact - rearWheelContactPrev
       speedKmhRaw = (int)( |d| / (g_frameDelta * 0.01) * 0.028125 )

   SO THE NEEDLE READS HOW FAR THE REAR TYRE ACTUALLY WENT over the
   ground, not engineSpeed.  A bike being dragged sideways, or one
   whose wheel is spinning, shows what it is doing rather than what
   the drivetrain thinks.

   AN AI's NEEDLE IS DAMPED AND THE PLAYER's IS NOT:

       rate = (rate + (needle - prev)/g_frameDelta * 100) * 0.5
       rate clamped to [-50, +40]
       needle = prev + (int)(rate * g_frameDelta * 0.01), floored at 0

   racer+0x120 IS A FLOAT, NOT AN INT.  The DB typed it int, which is
   why the decompiler renders the store as (int)(...) and why a live
   dump of MOTO's AI shows values like -1035468800 - that is -50.0f
   read as an integer.  Racer_UpdateDash uses FADD, FSTP and FLD on
   it at 0x004679dc, 0x004679e8 and 0x00467a1e, and the clamps store
   the raw bit patterns 0x42200000 and 0xc2480000 as dwords.  Read as
   floats, MOTO's eleven opponents give 40.000, -25.000, 0.000,
   40.000, -21.513, -0.000, -50.000, -0.002, -0.001, 0.000 and 1.656
   - every one inside [-50, +40], three sitting exactly on a clamp.

   AND THE CLAMP IS ON THE BITS, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL:

       if ((int)bits < 0x42200001)        <= +40.0f
           if ((uint)bits > 0xc2480000)   < -50.0f
               bits = 0xc2480000
       else
           bits = 0x42200000

   WRITTEN AS A VALUE COMPARE IT LETS NaN THROUGH.  NaN fails both
   `>` and `<`, so it survives the clamp, and the next frame's
   rate = (... + NaN) * 0.5 is NaN again - a damped accumulator never
   recovers from one.  Measured with the value compare in: two of
   eleven opponents sat at NaN with every input to them finite.  The
   bit compare catches it for free, because a positive NaN is
   0x7Fxxxxxx and clamps to +40 while a negative one is 0xFFxxxxxx
   and clamps to -50.

   THAT IS THE THIRD TIME THIS SESSION a float-bit test read as a
   value test caused a defect, after the engine bleed at 0x0046b64a
   and the ground-clearance pair.  In this subsystem, an integer
   compare against a constant that looks like a float bit pattern is
   never an integer compare.

   which is what MR_Racer +0x120 has said for a long time without
   saying who wrote it.  ONLY THIS PATH DOES, and only for an AI -
   a human returns early through the tacho.  THE FOURTH FIELD IN THIS
   SUBSYSTEM THAT READ AS DEAD because every capture watched racer 1,
   after 9.18.2, 9.19.3, 9.32.3 and leanPrev.

   THE HUMAN SOLO ARM is one line:

       tachoRpm = (int)(RegimeMax / MoteurVitesseMax * engineSpeed)
                  carac 29 over carac 6

   THE AUTO SHIFT IS DECIDED ON GROUND SPEED, not on the drivetrain's
   number.  The original stages it through the field: save racer+0xec,
   write needle * (1/3.6), call maybe_Gearbox_AutoSelect (0x0048e180),
   put the old value back, then multiply RegimeMax by the rev
   fraction that call returned and floor the result at 800.

   THAT STAGING IS WHY racer+0xec READ AMBIGUOUSLY.  A dump taken
   between frames shows the RESTORED value while the gear and the
   tachometer were computed from the temporary.  The port passes the
   speed as a parameter instead, because nothing here needs the side
   effect - but the negative clamp at 0x00467ac2 is kept, since
   reverse can make the temporary negative.

   THE GEARBOX BANDS ARE IN THE CODE, NOT THE CARAC, doubles at
   0x004d1ca8 on a stride of 16:

       gear   shift up at    range top
        1        0.18          0.23
        2        0.33          0.38
        3        0.48          0.53
        4        0.62          0.67
        5        0.74          0.79
        6         -            1.00

   THE SHIFT POINT AND THE RANGE TOP ARE DIFFERENT NUMBERS, and the
   gap is what stops the tachometer snapping to zero on every change.

   DIRT BIKES HAVE FIVE GEARS, NOT SIX.  The PersoAnim branch has its
   own bands at 0x004d1cf8 - shift at 0.15, 0.30, 0.45, 0.75, tops
   0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.80, 1.00 - and stops at five.  A real
   difference between the two bike families, decided here.

9.48.0.7.  THE LANE QUEUE, seen whole on MOTO

   With the lane logic and the corner query in, a free-run comparison
   of racer 2 showed MOTO slowing to 54 m/s while ours held 72 - and
   that is a ROSTER difference, not a defect.  MOTO's field, sorted
   by segment:

       r5   126  lane 0  road 50.86
       r3   130  lane 1  road 76.29
       r7   132  lane 0  road 51.50
       r8   135  lane 0  road 52.53
       r6   139  lane 1  road 58.39
       r4   140  lane 2  road 78.75
       r2   143  lane 0  road 54.07
       r9   145  lane 1  road 60.18
       r12  149  lane 0  road 55.56
       r11  152  lane 1  road 62.64
       r10  171  lane 0  road 75.00

   LANE 0 IS A QUEUE.  Read it upward - 50.86, 51.50, 52.53, 54.07,
   55.56 - each rider a little slower than the one ahead, all the way
   to r10 at 75.00 with clear road in front of it.  That is step 6 of
   Racer_AiLaneStep compounding down a line of traffic, and it is the
   whole mechanism visible in one snapshot.

   LANES 1 AND 2 ARE THE FAST ONES, 76.29 and 78.75 - above their own
   realSpeedMax, because racer_ai_top_speed adds 2.5 per cent a lane.
   THE OUTSIDE LINE IS FASTER BY DESIGN, which is what makes moving
   out to overtake worth doing.

   VERIFIED WITH MATCHING ROSTERS.  With NbComputerCut set both games
   build eleven opponents and the realSpeedMax multisets are
   identical - 55.56, 61.11, 66.67, 69.44, 72.22, 73.33, 73.61,
   73.89, 74.17, 75.00, 75.00 on both.  Run from a standing grid,
   our field does this:

       r3  seg 227  lane 0  road 55.56  cap 55.56  charge   0.0
       r10 seg 291  lane 1  road 68.33  cap 68.33  charge 240.0
       r2  seg 295  lane 2  road 74.73  cap 78.75  charge 480.0
       r4  seg 295  lane 0  road 71.55  cap 75.00  charge   0.0
       r5  seg 295  lane 1  road 72.90  cap 75.74  charge  66.9
       r8  seg 302  lane 1  road 74.03  cap 74.03  charge 240.0
       r6  seg 306  lane 2  road 77.29  cap 77.29  charge 480.0

   THREE THINGS ARE CONFIRMED AT ONCE:

     o chargeMeter SITS EXACTLY ON lane * 240 for a settled rider -
       0.0, 240.0, 480.0 - and mid-ramp for one that is moving
       (66.9).  The same is true of MOTO's field, which shows 240.0,
       243.5, 391.4 and 98.6 against its own lanes.
     o THE OUTSIDE LANES CARRY THE BONUS: r2 in lane 2 has a cap of
       78.75 against a realSpeedMax of 75.00, and r6 77.29 against
       73.61.  2.5 per cent a lane, as racer_ai_top_speed says.
     o THE QUEUE FIRES.  r4, r5 and r2 are all BELOW their caps with
       a bike 7 to 11 segments ahead in the same lane - which is step
       6 holding them at the blocker's pace.

   THE FIELDS DO NOT LINE UP RIDER FOR RIDER and are not meant to.
   MOTO's has been racing long enough to bunch into 45 segments with
   a slow lane-0 queue; ours has just spread out of a standing grid
   across 91.  The MECHANISM matches; the race state does not.

9.48.0.8.  The dirt AI, checked on the icy mountain track

   The dirt bike is the PersoAnim side of every branch the road bike
   never reaches: the 9/2 look-ahead instead of 16/4, the airborne
   arc, the ground lift, the five-gear bands and the steering angle.
   Rosters match - realSpeedMax 34.72 .. 37.22 on both, eleven values
   identical - and the carac reads persoAnim 1, realSpeedMax 46.00.

   Seeded once, then both run free:

       n | MOTO seg  eng   | ours seg  eng   | pos err | dir dot
       0 |       11  25.95 |       11  25.97 |     2.8 |  1.0000
       5 |       13  31.53 |       13  31.53 |     9.3 |  1.0000
       9 |       15  35.93 |       15  35.93 |    11.2 |  1.0000
      13 |       18  36.35 |       18  37.23 |    30.9 |  0.9996

   THE SEGMENT IS IDENTICAL EVERY STEP and the heading dot is 1.0000
   - so the look-ahead, the turn and the integrator all agree on a
   bike that steers far harder than the road one.

   THE SPEED SPLIT AT STEP 10 IS THE LANE BONUS, not a defect. Both
   racers hold realSpeedMax 35.56, but MOTO's is in lane 1 and ours
   in lane 2:

       MOTO  35.56 * 1.025 = 36.44,  road speed 36.44
       ours  35.56 * 1.05  = 37.33,  road speed 37.33

   EACH SITS EXACTLY ON ITS OWN LANE'S CAP.  racer_ai_top_speed is
   right on both sides; the lane differs because the traffic around
   it does - MOTO's field spans segments 18..49 and ours 13..41.

   THE FIVE-GEAR DIRT BANDS CHECK OUT. With realSpeedMax 46.00 the
   shift points fall at 25, 50, 75 and 124 km/h. MOTO's AI sat at
   needle 78 km/h in gear 4 and ours at 47 in gear 2 - both correct
   for their own needle, which is still climbing at the +40 clamp
   after the start.  regimeMax is 10000 on this bike.

9.48.1.  The target point

       seg    = Trk_WrapSegmentIndex(...)
       offset = segment[seg]+0xb0 + racer.chargeMeter
       target = segment[seg].origin
                + transform((offset, 0, 0), segment[seg].matrix)

   Row 0 of a segment's matrix is its LATERAL axis (trk.h), so
   segment+0xb0 is a PER-SEGMENT RACING-LINE OFFSET and chargeMeter
   is this rider's own displacement from it. A racer with a non-zero
   chargeMeter drives a line parallel to the ideal one, which is how
   overtaking is expressed without any awareness of other bikes.

   NEITHER FIELD IS EXPOSED YET - MR_TrkSegment does not carry +0xb0,
   and chargeMeter is a name inherited from elsewhere that clearly
   does not mean what it says here. The target selection is therefore
   documented and not transcribed. [open]

9.48.2.  unk31d1 is a BLEND FACTOR over 200

       alpha = unk31d1 * 0.005, floored at 0.01

   0x004d100c IS 0.005, WHICH IS EXACTLY 1/200, AND EVERY WALL PATH
   SETS unk31d1 TO EXACTLY 200.0 [verified]. So alpha is 1.0 the
   instant after an impact and decays toward the 0.01 floor as the
   counter runs down.

   It weights the OLD state against the new in BOTH the speed blend
   and the direction lerp:

       alpha = 1      ignore the racing line completely
       alpha = 0.01   follow it almost exactly

   SO A WALL HIT MAKES AN AI STOP STEERING and coast on whatever
   heading it was left with, recovering over the next 200 counts.
   That is why both wall paths - the player's at 0x004690f6 and the
   AI's at 0x0046c02c - set the same 200.0, and it is also why a
   non-zero unk31d1 zeroes an ordinary AI's lean (9.47.3): a bike
   that is not steering should not be leaning either.

   THE FIELD IS RENAMED impactBlend IN THE DB. It was f_31d1, and
   MR_Racer.txt described it only as "set to 200.0 in several places
   and decaying" - true, and it turned out that the 200 was not an
   arbitrary duration but the DENOMINATOR. Finding the reciprocal in
   the code is what turned a counter into a ratio.

9.48.3.  Cornering is one dot product

       c = |dot(moveDir, forward)|, floored at 0.5
       w = forward * (target * c) + moveDir * ((1 - c) * speed)
       speed = alpha*speed + (1 - alpha)*min(|w|, topSpeed)

   MISALIGNMENT COSTS SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE AI'S
   CORNERING. c is 1 when the bike points where it is going and falls
   toward the 0.5 floor as it slides, and the target speed is scaled
   by it - so a sideways AI simply cannot reach its cap.

   No grip curve, no slip angle, no centripetal term, no carac
   lookup. The player's model (9.19, 9.28) is several hundred bytes
   of tyre physics; the AI's is one dot product and a floor. THEY DO
   NOT SHARE A SINGLE LINE, which is the sharpest possible statement
   of 9.47's "placed, not simulated".

9.48.4.  Lane spreads the field by 2.5% a step

       topSpeed = realSpeedMax + lane * realSpeedMax * 0.025

   0x004d12b8 is 0.025 [verified], applied twice - once clamping the
   throttle ramp and once clamping the blended speed. So a field of
   mechanically identical bikes strings out because each rider is
   given a different lane number, and lane 4 is four per cent faster
   than lane 0 at the top end.

   THAT IS THE DIFFICULTY KNOB. It also means AI pace is independent
   of the carac table beyond realSpeedMax, so tuning a bike does not
   tune its opponents.

   OTHER CONSTANTS READ HERE:

   +------------+------------+------------------------------------+
   | Address    | Value      | Role                               |
   +============+============+====================================+
   | 0x004d12b0 | 1/6        | the target-speed ramp, per ms      |
   +------------+------------+------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d12e0 | -0.2/3.6   | engine decay going forward         |
   +------------+------------+------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d12e8 | +0.2/3.6   | engine decay going backward        |
   +------------+------------+------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d12f8 | 9.81/40    | the AI airborne gravity - a        |
   |            |            | different scale from the crashed   |
   |            |            | rider's 9.81/100 (9.35.2)          |
   +------------+------------+------------------------------------+
   | 0x004d1010 | 1.28       | the position step = 128 * 0.01,    |
   |            |            | the crash ballistics pair folded   |
   |            |            | into one constant                  |
   +------------+------------+------------------------------------+

   THE ANGLE WRAPPING uses 0x004d0e58 = 2pi and 0x004d0e60 = pi in
   the ordinary "wrap into [0,2pi) then take the short way round"
   shape. Only persoAnim riders get a steering value out of it; for
   everyone else steering is set to 0 outright, which is consistent
   with the AI never using it.

9.48.5.  Transcribed, and the Ghidra pass

   racer.c gains racer_ai_top_speed, racer_ai_throttle_step,
   racer_ai_blend, racer_ai_speed_blend, racer_ai_steer_toward and
   racer_ai_integrate. MR_Racer gains realSpeedMax and lane.

   NOT TRANSCRIBED: the target-point selection (9.48.1), the yaw
   alignment block, and the airborne persoAnim ballistic arm - which
   is the same construction racer_ai_ground_snap already uses, with
   gravity added. [open]

   A TYPE PASS WENT WITH THIS SECTION, because three functions
   transcribed earlier were still decompiling with raw int
   parameters and that degrades every caller:

   o MR_Trk was CREATED - it did not exist. Only MR_TrkSegment did.
     Two fields, segments and segmentCount, which is all that has
     been read of the object at 0x0052c108.
   o Trk_FindSegmentForPoint and maybe_Trk_ClipMoveAgainstWalls now
     take MR_Trk and named float pointers.
   o Racer_ResetCrash now takes MR_Racer instead of int.

   THE __thiscall ECX PARAMETER NEEDED A SECOND STEP, and the first
   attempt at this section got it wrong. Setting the prototype types
   the STACK parameters and leaves `this` as void*, so
   this->segmentCount still decompiled as *(int *)((int)this + 4).
   Both set_function_prototype and set_parameter_type refuse it with
   "Cannot modify auto-parameter: this".

   THAT ERROR IS THE EXPLANATION. Under __thiscall, `this` is an
   AUTO-PARAMETER - synthesised from the calling convention rather
   than stored - so there is no variable to retype, and no MCP call
   can fix it. It is not an MCP limitation.

   AN AUTO-this TAKES ITS TYPE FROM THE FUNCTION CLASS NAMESPACE.
   Put the function in a GhidraClass whose name matches an existing
   structure and Ghidra types `this` as a pointer to it. Done with
   run_script_inline; both Trk functions now decompile as
   MR_Trk::f(MR_Trk *this, ...) with this->nSegmentCount rendering
   properly.

   THAT IS ALSO WHY THE RACER FUNCTIONS ALWAYS LOOKED RIGHT. Somebody
   had already put them in an MR_Racer class, which is what made
   maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash render as
   MR_Racer::maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash(MR_Racer *this). The difference
   between the two families was never the prototype - it was the
   namespace, and nothing in the prototype API hints at that.

   The procedure is now tools/ghidra_set_thiscall_class.java, with
   the GUI equivalent in its header. See ghidra-db-repairs.txt 11.


9.49.  UPDATELEAN - four formulas, and two closures

   maybe_Racer_UpdateLean (0x00469c60), 4266 bytes. It computes the
   lean into racer+0x2e1d, rebuilds the wheel contacts and
   matBikeRoot, and folds in a wheelie rotation - and it does the
   lean itself FOUR different ways behind three flags.

   Its plate comment already carried a good live confirmation: lean
   is an atan of lateral acceleration, clamped to 1.04 rad = 59.6
   degrees, measured at five states including the one that proves it
   is a lean and not a steering angle - 2.34 degrees coasting through
   a left turn at speed 4, falling away with speed while the bars are
   still over.

9.49.1.  Where impactBlend counts down

   9.48.2 identified impactBlend as a blend factor over 200 and found
   its READER - Racer_AiFollowLine - and every writer that sets it to
   200.0. Nothing found reduced it. THIS IS THE DECREMENTER, at the
   very top of this function [verified]:

       if (impactBlend != 0) {
           impactBlend -= g_frameDelta
           if (impactBlend < 0) impactBlend = 0
       }
       blend = impactBlend * 0.005

   using the SAME 0x004d100c that 9.48.2 identified as 1/200.

   SO THE RECOVERY IS COUPLED TO GAME TIME, NOT FRAME COUNT. The
   counter falls by g_frameDelta, so an impact fades over a fixed
   duration however fast the machine runs. A port that decremented
   per tick instead would make AI recovery frame-rate dependent, and
   the bug would look like "the AI drives differently on a fast PC".

   The value is then used as a FLOOR on the lean's own blend, which
   is why a bike that has just hit something leans less readily.

9.49.2.  leanPrev's zero is finally explained

   The plate comment on this function carried an explicit unresolved
   item:

       +0x2e21 READ 0.0 IN EVERY LIVE SAMPLE, including ones where
       +0x2e1d was -0.21. If that tail ran, the two would be equal.
       So either it is on a path not taken or something later in the
       tick clears it. Unresolved - do not port +0x2e21 as "previous
       lean" on the strength of the disassembly alone.

   IT IS ON A PATH NOT TAKEN. The lerp against leanPrev sits inside
   the branch guarded by humanPlayer AND soloMode AND PersoAnim != 0.
   The samples were of racer 1 on an ordinary bike, so PersoAnim was
   0 and that branch never ran. 9.47.4 found the OTHER writer -
   maybe_Racer_TickAI's lean step - which the same samples missed for
   the same reason, being of the player rather than an AI.

   SO leanPrev IS CORRECTLY NAMED and has two writers, neither of
   which a player-on-a-normal-bike capture can reach. The plate
   comment's caution was right and its worry is now discharged.

   THAT IS THE FIFTH TIME in this document a field that "always reads
   zero" turned out to be about which racer or which bike was
   sampled, after 9.18.2, 9.19.3, 9.32.3 and 9.47.4. The check is
   cheap and should come first: before concluding a field is unused,
   ask what the capture could not have exercised.

9.49.3.  The player's response curve has a gain break

   For an ordinary human player in solo [verified]:

       lean = (yawDelta / dt) * -30.0        0x004d1288, a FLOAT
       if (lean >  0.3) lean = lean*1.4 - 0.12
       if (lean < -0.3) lean = lean*1.4 + 0.12
       clamp to +-1.04

   The 6 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/racer.c.

   THE OFFSET MAKES IT CONTINUOUS, and that is the point of it. At
   exactly 0.3, 0.3*1.4 - 0.12 is 0.3. So the curve is linear near
   upright and 40 per cent steeper past about 17 degrees, with no
   step where the slope changes.

   That is a deliberately shaped control response, not a clamp. The
   bike answers gently when nearly upright and increasingly hard as
   it goes over, which is what gives it apparent weight - and it is
   the kind of thing that would be very hard to recover by feel if a
   port simply used a linear curve and a clamp.

   THERE IS ALSO A dt FLOOR: when leanMode is 0 and the smoothed
   delta at DAT_004dc150 is below 20, the original substitutes 20.
   Without it a fast frame turns a small yaw change into an enormous
   lean.

9.49.4.  Four lean paths, one transcribed

   +----------------------------------+---------------------------+
   | Condition                        | lean =                    |
   +==================================+===========================+
   | human && solo && persoAnim       | atan of the yaw rate x    |
   |                                  | speed, clamped, then      |
   |                                  | lerped against leanPrev   |
   +----------------------------------+---------------------------+
   | persoAnim, otherwise             | delta / (3.0 - speed *    |
   |                                  | 0.045 * 0.5) * -1.3       |
   +----------------------------------+---------------------------+
   | not human, or not solo, or       | -delta, no shaping at all |
   | replaying                        |                           |
   +----------------------------------+---------------------------+
   | human && solo && !persoAnim      | the curve of 9.49.3       |
   +----------------------------------+---------------------------+

   src/racer.c gains racer_lean_impact_decay and
   racer_lean_response, which are the two that are both verified and
   needed for an ordinary player on an ordinary bike.

   NOT TRANSCRIBED [open]: the three other lean paths; the
   matBikeRoot rebuild at racer+0x2e55, which is the same
   front-minus-rear construction racer_frame_from_forward already
   implements but with a WHEELIE rotation composed in when
   wheelieAngle > 0; and the yaw-difference computation feeding all
   four, which needs Racer_GetYaw.

   THE matBikeRoot POINT MATTERS FOR THE PORT and the plate comment
   states it: racer+0x00bc carries NO ROLL - it is a pure rotation
   about Y in every attitude sampled. The lean lives in +0x2e1d and
   maybe_Racer_Draw composes it in. A bike drawn from matOrientation
   alone corners bolt upright.


9.50.  UPDATEPOSE - the glance, and a dead rand

   maybe_Racer_UpdatePose (0x0045d810), 2935 bytes. It stacks
   Anim_BlendAngleFrames passes into one set of joint angles and
   writes them into the racer's pose array.

9.50.1.  Seven blends stacked into one pose

       1  a base pose chosen by SPEED, with variants for the finish
          position - leading, second, third, last all differ
       2  a PITCH pose from the front/rear contact height difference,
          smoothed into poseBlend (racer+0x3051) and sign-selected
       3  the GLANCE - see 9.50.2
       4  the LEAN, two mirrored poses picked by its sign
       5  the STUNT, if one is running
       6  the CRASH, if crashLatch is set
       7  a camera-distance fade back toward the neutral pose

   THE CAMERA ONE IS WORTH NOTING: between camera distances of about
   900 and 1000 the pose is blended back toward g_abPilotRot, the
   neutral rider. Far away, the rider stops emoting - a level-of-
   detail on ANIMATION rather than on geometry.

9.50.2.  The glance over the shoulder

   A racer looks at the nearest rival who is CATCHING him. Every one
   of these must hold [verified]:

   The 2 constants are #defined with their addresses in
   src/racer.c.

   dot is between the bike's forward axis and the direction to the
   rival, both flattened into XZ and normalised. The side is the sign
   of the cross product, choosing one of two mirrored poses, and the
   result runs for 400 ms blended by sin(t/400 * pi) so it eases in
   and out.

   THE |dot| < 0.7 WINDOW IS WHAT MAKES IT A GLANCE and not a stare.
   A rival dead ahead or dead behind gives |dot| near 1 and is
   ignored; the pose only fires when they are roughly ABEAM, within
   about 45 degrees.

   AND THE RISING TEST IS THE GOOD PART. Requiring dot > lastDot
   means the rival must be drawing ALONGSIDE rather than dropping
   back - so the rider looks at somebody overtaking him and not at
   somebody he is pulling away from. One remembered float buys a
   behaviour that would otherwise need a relative-velocity test.

   THE SAME-RIVAL REQUIREMENT is a deliberate one-frame delay: the
   nearest racer is recorded each frame and the glance can only start
   if the same one is still nearest next frame. That stops a glance
   firing at somebody who was closest for a single frame in a pack.

   Transcribed inline in racer_pose_glance. Ours takes the two wheel
   contacts as parameters, because the original keeps them in the
   racer record at +0x32c and +0x338 while our MR_Racer computes them
   on demand through racer_wheel_contacts.

9.50.3.  The stunt rand is a DEAD STORE

   The plate comment noted that the next stunt index is picked "from
   DAT_0050bbc8's bit pattern instead of rand()". The disassembly
   shows why - BOTH are computed, and the second overwrites the
   first, six instructions apart [verified]:

       0045e087  CALL Rand_Next
       0045e08d  IDIV [0x005cf4fc]        signed:   rand % count
       0045e093  MOV  [ESI+0x3061], EDX   stuntNext = that
       0045e099  SUB  EDX,EDX
       0045e09b  MOV  EAX,[0x0050bbc8]    the ms timestamp
       0045e0a0  DIV  [0x005cf4fc]        UNSIGNED: stamp % count
       0045e0a6  MOV  [ESI+0x3061], EDX   stuntNext = that

   SO THE Rand_Next CALL IS DEAD. Its result is divided, stored, and
   immediately replaced. Almost certainly a leftover: somebody
   changed the selection to be timestamp-based and did not delete the
   old line.

   TWO CONSEQUENCES FOR THE PORT. Stunt selection is DETERMINISTIC
   given the clock - the same millisecond gives the same stunt, so it
   is not random at all and will not vary between runs that reach the
   same time. And Rand_Next has one fewer real consumer than the call
   graph suggests, which matters if anyone ever tries to reproduce
   the RNG sequence.

   Note that this is Rand_Next (0x004b1160) and NOT Game_RandNext
   (0x00458ce0) of 9.36.4 - two different functions, and only the
   latter is the CRT wrapper.

9.50.4.  It consumes the crash fields

   The crash blend reads flCrashTimerMs, flRiderSpeed and
   flRiderSpeedRef - the three fields renamed in 9.35.3 after finding
   the DB had the timer and the speed swapped. This is an independent
   consumer that confirms the reading:

       t     = (K - crashTimerMs) * K2, clamped to +-1
       blend the "down" pose by t
       if (riderSpeed < riderSpeedRef * K3)
           u = (riderSpeedRef*K3 - riderSpeed) / (riderSpeedRef*K3)
           blend a second pose by -sin(u*K4 + pi)
       handSkeleton = 1

   THE SECOND BLEND IS DRIVEN BY HOW MUCH SPEED THE RIDER HAS LOST,
   as a FRACTION of his launch speed - which only makes sense if
   +0x30d9 really is the launch reference and +0x30d5 really is the
   current speed. A timer in either slot would give nonsense. So
   9.35.3's correction has a second, independent witness.

   AND handSkeleton = 1 HERE TOO, which is the same conclusion 9.35.1
   reached from maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash: the hand skeleton is "the
   rider is off the bike".

   NOT TRANSCRIBED [open]: the six other blend passes, all of which
   need Anim_BlendAngleFrames and the .ROT pose tables; the stunt
   playback; and the pose-array write at the tail.


9.51.  ANIM_BLENDANGLEFRAMES - the shortest arc

   Anim_BlendAngleFrames (0x0045b4e0), 852 bytes, and every pose pass
   in 9.50 goes through it. The base pose, the pitch, the glance, the
   lean, the stunt, the crash and the camera fade are all this one
   function applied in sequence, each blending the running result
   against another frame.

   ITS DECOMPILE IS CLEAN. No phantoms, no dropped arguments - the
   three unrolled angle bodies are just the compiler unrolling a
   three-iteration inner loop.

9.51.1.  The runtime frame is not the .ROT

       int   count
       float angle[20][3]        244 bytes = 0xf4

   THE 0xf4 IS CONFIRMED FROM THE CALLER: maybe_Racer_UpdatePose walks
   the stunt table as (g_abPilotRot + id * 0xf4), and 4 + 20*3*4 is
   exactly 244. The 20 is confirmed independently by the three
   20-iteration clear loops at the top of that same function.

   THIS IS A DIFFERENT LAYOUT FROM MR_SklPose, and the difference
   matters. A .ROT file holds SHORT angles for up to 18 members
   (formats/skl-rot.txt); this holds FLOATS for up to 20 joints. So
   the file's shorts are expanded at load time and the blender never
   sees the file format.

   A PORT THAT BLENDED MR_SklPose DIRECTLY WOULD READ SHORTS AS
   FLOATS and produce garbage angles - the kind of bug that looks
   like corrupt asset data. src/skl.h now carries MR_AnimFrame as a
   separate type for exactly that reason.

9.51.2.  The blend

       out.count = min(a.count, b.count)

       t <= 0    copy a, only if out != a
       t >= 1    copy b, only if out != b
       else, per joint, per angle:

           y = b.angle
           if (y > x + pi) y -= 2pi
           if (y < x - pi) y += 2pi
           r = x + (y - x) * t
           wrap r into [0, 2pi]

   THE SHORTEST-ARC WRAP IS THE WHOLE POINT. Without it a joint at
   350 degrees blending to 10 sweeps backwards through the entire
   circle. A port that lerps these as plain floats gets a rider who
   occasionally spins a limb the long way round - and it will present
   as a data problem rather than an arithmetic one, which is why it
   is worth stating before anybody writes the naive version.

   THE COUNT IS A MINIMUM, NOT A REQUIREMENT. Frames with different
   joint counts blend to the SMALLER, so a shorter pose truncates the
   result rather than failing. That is the mechanism by which the
   hand skeleton's extra members drop out when blended against a
   body-only pose - which ties back to 9.35.1 and 9.50.4, where
   handSkeleton selects a pose block with hands.

   BOTH ENDPOINTS ARE GUARDED AGAINST SELF-COPY, so blending a frame
   against itself at t=0 or t=1 is a genuine no-op and not an
   overlapping memcpy. Worth preserving - UpdatePose does exactly
   that in its pre-race path, calling the blender with the same frame
   as source and destination.

   OUTPUT IS IN [0, 2pi], NOT [-pi, pi]. Anything downstream that
   compares a blended angle against zero has to know that.

9.51.3.  What it unblocks

   src/skl.c gains anim_blend_angle_frames and src/skl.h gains
   MR_AnimFrame.

   THIS WAS THE GATE ON FOUR OF THE SEVEN PASSES in 9.50 and on the
   AI's pose work. What still stands between here and a posed rider
   is not the blender but the DATA: the .ROT tables at 0x0051xxxx
   have to be located, expanded from shorts to MR_AnimFrame, and
   indexed the way UpdatePose indexes them. formats/skl-rot.txt has
   the file format; the in-memory tables are not yet mapped. [open]

   THE TICK CHAIN IS NOW READ END TO END. From Race_TickAllRacers
   through the dispatch, both tick branches, the drivetrain, the
   crash, ground contact, the wall arms, the AI line follower, the
   lean and the pose - every function in the chain has been read and
   its decidable parts are in src/. What remains inside it is data
   tables and presentation, not logic.


9.52.  THE POSE TABLE - index is load order

   9.51 left the .ROT tables at 0x0051xxxx as the last thing between
   a read tick chain and a posed rider. They are now mapped.

9.52.1.  Neither the table nor the paths are in the image

   THE COMPILED .BPT HAS NO "PilotRot" KEY. maybe_Bike_ReadManifest
   calls Settings_ReadString for one, but that reads the .SPT TEXT
   source; the compiled manifest drops most key strings and carries
   the meaning POSITIONALLY. Decoding MOTOSPD.BPT gives records 0..17
   as the models, 18..48 as the poses, and record 49 as the literal
   "MotoCarac" - one of the few markers that does survive, which is
   why bikes.c can scan for it and a scan for "PilotRot" finds
   nothing. The list therefore starts at record 18 and ends at the
   first record with no '.' in it.

   Bike_LoadPilotRots (0x00410a10) is four instructions of loop
   [verified]:

       count = [0x005191e0]
       EBX   = 0x0051d2a8              the pose table, stride 0xf4
       ESI   = 0x005191e4              the path list, stride 0x40
       loop: Rot_Load(dest=EBX, path=ESI); EBX += 0xf4; ESI += 0x40

   SO THE INDEX IS NOTHING BUT THE LOAD ORDER. There is no
   name-to-slot lookup anywhere - slot i holds whatever file is i-th
   in the list.

   BOTH ARRAYS READ AS ZERO IN THE IMAGE. A static dump of 52 table
   entries gives count 0 and angles 0.0 for every one, and the path
   list is equally blank. They are uninitialised data filled at load
   time, so the mapping simply does not exist statically and no
   amount of reading the binary will produce it.

   THAT ALSO CORRECTS A CLAIM. maybe_Racer_UpdatePose's plate comment
   states that 0x0051fc98 - index 44 - "holds a well-formed record
   (part count 17)". It cannot have come from the image; that was a
   live read, and the comment does not say so. Recorded here because
   the next reader would waste a sweep discovering the zeros.

   The list was therefore read out of a RUNNING GAME, 31 entries.

9.52.2.  The map

   +----+------------+----------------------+---------------------+
   | ix | frame addr | file                 | used for            |
   +====+============+======================+=====================+
   |  0 | 0051d2a8   | static.rot           | the neutral pose,   |
   |    |            |                      | camera-fade target  |
   |  1 | 0051d39c   | gauche.rot           | lean left           |
   |  2 | 0051d490   | droite.rot           | lean right          |
   |  3 | 0051d584   | staticHaut.rot       | the riding base     |
   |  4 | 0051d678   | staticPied.rot       | foot down           |
   |  5 | 0051d76c   | derapGauche.rot      | slide left          |
   |  6 | 0051d860   | derapDroite.rot      | slide right         |
   |  7 | 0051d954   | saut.rot             | pitch up            |
   |  8 | 0051da48   | staticgagne.rot      | leading             |
   |  9 | 0051db3c   | descent.rot          | pitch down          |
   | 10 | 0051dc30   | fin2.rot             | finished 2nd        |
   | 11 | 0051dd24   | fin3.rot             | finished 3rd        |
   | 12 | 0051de18   | fin7.rot             | second to last      |
   | 13 | 0051df0c   | fin8.rot             | last                |
   | 14 | 0051e000   | finautre.rot         | any other place     |
   | 15 | 0051e0f4   | chuteDos.rot         | the crash fall      |
   | 16 | 0051e1e8   | staticDos.rot        | lying on his back   |
   | 17 | 0051e2dc   | podium/a1..f1        | the ceremony,       |
   | .. |            |                      | twelve frames       |
   | 28 | 0051ed58   |                      |                     |
   | 29 | 0051ee4c   | lookdroite.rot       | glance right        |
   | 30 | 0051ef40   | lookgauche.rot       | glance left         |
   +----+------------+----------------------+---------------------+

   EVERY ADDRESS maybe_Racer_UpdatePose USES IS ACCOUNTED FOR. There
   is no frame it reads that this list does not name, and no entry in
   the list it does not read except the podium block, which belongs
   to MenuPage24_Podium_Enter.

9.52.3.  The names confirm the readings

   The French filenames independently confirm several things that
   were inferred from the code alone:

   o derapGauche/derapDroite - DERAPAGE IS A SLIDE. 9.50 read slots 5
     and 6 as the pair selected at higher lean, against 1 and 2 at
     lower. The names say the difference is leaning versus sliding,
     which is exactly the right distinction for a motorcycle and is
     not something the blend weights alone would have revealed.

   o chuteDos, "fall onto the back", and staticDos, "static on the
     back" - the two crash frames of 9.50.4, in the order the crash
     blend uses them: fall first, then lie there.

   o lookdroite/lookgauche - THE GLANCE OF 9.50.2, confirming that
     g_abPoseGlanceA and g_abPoseGlanceB are a left/right pair
     selected by the cross-product sign and nothing more.

   o staticPied, "foot down" - and racer+0x2e0d was already named
     poseStaticPied from the field's use. The name in src/ and the
     filename agree without either having been derived from the
     other.

   THAT IS THE VALUE OF GETTING THE FILENAMES: they are the original
   authors' own words for what these poses are, and they arbitrate
   readings that the arithmetic leaves ambiguous.

9.52.4.  The table is PER BIKE

   The paths are data\speed\..., carrying the BIKE NAME. So the
   pose set is loaded per bike and the table length varies with it.

   THIS RESOLVES THE 32-VERSUS-44 QUESTION. An earlier pass
   recorded "32 frames ending at 0x0051f128" and UpdatePose's plate
   comment observes indices reaching 44, treating the first as a
   read-coverage figure rather than a bound. Both are right and
   neither is a bound: they are two different bikes. A road bike
   loads 31; KTMCROSS loads more because it ships SAUTDELIRE*.ROT
   stunt poses that SPEED does not - which is also why 9.50.3's
   stunt block can never fire on a road bike.

   A PORT MUST THEREFORE LOAD THE TABLE PER BIKE and must not
   hard-code 31, or index 29 will be the glance on one bike and
   something else on another.

   src/skl.h gains the index enum, named from the files rather than
   from the use, so a future reader can see both.


9.53.  THE POSE PASSES - weights and choices

   9.50 listed the seven blends maybe_Racer_UpdatePose stacks and
   transcribed only the glance. With anim_blend_angle_frames (9.51)
   and the pose table (9.52) in place, the rest of the decidable part
   is the WEIGHTS and the pose CHOICES.

   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | pass         | weight                                          |
   +==============+=================================================+
   | base         | below 20 km/h, speed * 0.18 toward staticHaut   |
   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | pitch        | see 9.53.1                                      |
   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | glance       | sin(elapsed/400 * pi), see 9.50.2               |
   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | lean         | see 9.53.2                                      |
   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | stunt        | a run of frames, see 9.50.3                     |
   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | crash        | (400 - crashTimerMs) * 0.02                     |
   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | camera fade  | (dist - 900) * 0.01, only between 900 and 1000  |
   +--------------+-------------------------------------------------+

   THE CRASH WEIGHT'S 400 IS THE VALUE BeginCrash WRITES (9.36.1), so
   it is 0 at the instant of the crash and 1 fifty units later - the
   pose fades in over the first eighth of the crash timer.

9.53.1.  The pitch is one signed number

       p = ((front.y - rear.y) / (axeDistance * 48)) * 4.0
       clamp to [-1, 1]
       poseBlend += (p - poseBlend) * 0.25

   and then the SIGN of poseBlend picks the pose: positive takes
   saut.rot, negative takes descent.rot with the weight negated.

   ONE SIGNED NUMBER SELECTS BETWEEN TWO MIRRORED POSES AND SUPPLIES
   THE BLEND. That is the third time this struct uses a float's sign
   as a selector, and it is worth recognising on sight - the pattern
   is always "compute a signed quantity, smooth it, then branch on
   the sign and use the magnitude".

   THE 0.25 SMOOTHING IS WHY THE RIDER DOES NOT SNAP. The raw ratio
   jumps whenever the two wheel contacts cross a segment boundary and
   the ground height steps; a quarter-step filter turns that into a
   body lean that takes about four frames to arrive. Remove it and
   the rider twitches on every seam - which would look like a track
   data problem.

   THE PASS IS GATED OFF ENTIRELY IN TWO CASES, at 0x0045dbc0:

       if (!isLocal || wheelieAngle != 0)   no pitch pose at all

   so a remote rider never gets it, and A WHEELIE SUPPRESSES IT. The
   bike pitches up from wheelieAngle and the rider holds the base
   stance; blending saut.rot in on top of that is what makes a
   wheelie look wrong. poseBlend also stops advancing while the pass
   is suppressed, because the original writes it only on the live
   branch.

9.53.2.  Local and remote disagree about sliding

   The lean pass blends a LEAN pose against a SLIDE pose - gauche
   against derapGauche, droite against derapDroite - and the two
   paths choose that mix differently [verified]:

       remote/AI   mix = (speed - 30 km/h) * 0.12
                   w   = |lean| * (speed < 60 km/h ? 4.3478 : 2.1739)

       local       w   = |lean| * 2.0
                   mix = (w - 0.46) * 1.0870
                   w  *= 2.1739

   anim_blend_angle_frames copies its FIRST argument at t=0 and its
   SECOND at t=1 (9.51.2), and the two calls pass their poses in
   OPPOSITE ORDERS:

       remote/AI   blend(tmp, SLIDE, LEAN, (speed - 30) * 0.12)
       local       blend(tmp, LEAN, SLIDE, (w - 0.46) * 1.0870)

   so a remote rider moves FROM the slide pose TOWARD the lean pose
   as speed rises, and the local player moves FROM the lean pose
   TOWARD the slide pose as his lean angle grows.

   For the local player, leaning harder shows more slide - the pose
   answers what he is DOING. For everyone else the mix is driven by
   SPEED alone, which is what the AI can afford, because 9.47
   established it never computes a proper lean at all.

9.53.3.  One constant and its reciprocals

       0x004d0fc0  4.3478261   = 2 / 0.46
       0x004d0fc4  2.1739130   = 1 / 0.46
       0x004d0fc8  0.46
       0x004d0fcc  1.0869565   = 1 / 0.92

   ALL FOUR ARE 0.46. The slow rider leans exactly twice as readily
   as the fast one, and the local path's mix divides by 0.92 because
   its input was already doubled.

   Recognising that turns four opaque decimals into one tuning
   number, which is the sort of thing worth checking for whenever a
   run of constants looks arbitrary - the same instinct that turned
   the km/h decimals into round numbers.

9.53.4.  THE POSE ARRAYS ARE NEVER CLEARED BETWEEN BIKES

   The PRE-RACE path blends staticPied (index 4) toward index 31, and
   the low-speed path tests the current pose against index 31 too.
   But g_nPilotRotCount reads 31 on a road bike, so valid indices are
   0..30 and 31 IS ONE PAST THE END.

   Read live on the grid [verified]:

       g_nPilotRotCount        31
       paths 0..30             data\speed\...
       paths 31, 32, 33        data\ktmcross\depart.rot
                               data\ktmcross\depart2.rot
                               data\ktmcross\sautdelire.rot
       pose frame 31           count 17, 22 non-zero angle words
       pose frame 32           count 17

   SO THE ARRAYS HOLD A MIXTURE OF TWO BIKES. The current bike is
   SPEED with 31 entries; slots 31 and beyond are left over from a
   KTMCROSS loaded earlier. Neither
   maybe_Bike_ReadManifest nor Bike_LoadPilotRots clears the arrays
   first - each simply overwrites the first count entries.

   AND INDEX 31 IS THE PRE-RACE POSE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS TARGETED.
   depart.rot is French for the start, and it sits at exactly the
   slot the pre-race blend reads. bikes.h already recorded that
   MOTOSPD and MOTOKTM "agree name for name from record 18 to 30, and
   only the LENGTH of the list differs" - this is what the extra
   length is: KTMCROSS ships depart.rot, depart2.rot and the
   sautdelire stunts, and SPEED ships none of them.

   THEREFORE THE PRE-RACE BLEND IS CORRECT ON A DIRT BIKE AND READS
   STALE DATA ON A ROAD BIKE. On a road bike loaded after a dirt
   bike it blends toward a KTMCROSS start pose; on the first bike
   loaded in a fresh process it blends toward a zero-filled frame.
   What the rider does on a road bike start line depends on what was
   played before it.

   THAT IS A BUG IN THE ORIGINAL, and a quiet one - both wrong
   outcomes are still a plausible-looking rider, which is exactly why
   it would survive testing.

9.53.5.  What the port does instead, and why that is a divergence

   pilot_rots_load memsets MR_PilotRots before loading, so index 31
   on a road bike is a zeroed frame every time rather than sometimes
   a KTMCROSS pose. That is DELIBERATE and it is a divergence from
   the original: it trades an unpredictable wrong pose for a
   predictable one.

   The alternative - reproducing the staleness - would mean carrying
   a global pose array that survives bike changes and never clearing
   it, in order to inherit a defect. Rule 5 says match observed
   behaviour including the ugly parts, and this is the line where
   that stops: a bug whose output depends on play history is not
   behaviour worth reproducing, and it is recorded here rather than
   imitated.

   IF THE PRE-RACE POSE EVER LOOKS WRONG ON A ROAD BIKE, this is
   why, and the fix is not to copy the original - it is to decide
   what a road bike should do when the pose it is asked for does not
   exist. [open]

   The weights are inline in racer_update_pose, except the stance,
   racer_pose_pitch, racer_pose_lean_weight, racer_pose_crash_weight
   pitch, lean and crash weights, which stayed as named helpers.


9.54.  THE POSE CHOICES - stance, finish, stunt

   9.53 transcribed the blend WEIGHTS. This is the pose SELECTION -
   which frame each pass reaches for.

9.54.1.  HAUT means HIGH, and the blend goes the right way

   Above 20 km/h the stance blends from staticHaut (index 3) toward
   static (index 0), weighted by

       (speed - 20 km/h) / (saturation - 20 km/h)

   THAT DIRECTION LOOKED BACKWARDS UNTIL THE FILENAME SETTLED IT.
   Blending away from "staticHaut" as speed rises only makes sense if
   haut is HIGH - staticHaut is the upright stance and static is the
   tucked one, so a rising weight folds the rider down onto the tank.
   Another case of 9.52.3: the French names arbitrate what the
   arithmetic leaves ambiguous.

   THE SATURATION SPEED DEPENDS ON WHO IS WATCHING [verified]. The
   function picks 0xf0 or 0x78 at its top and halves it:

       remote / AI    120 km/h
       local player    60 km/h

   so the player's own rider reaches a full crouch at half the speed
   everyone else needs. Same local-versus-remote split as the lean
   pass (9.53.2), and the same reasoning - detail where it is looked
   at, cheaper elsewhere. A port that used one figure would make the
   player's rider sit up too long or the pack crouch too early.

9.54.2.  Six finish poses, at both ends

       0             staticgagne    he won
       1             fin2
       2             fin3
       count - 2     fin7           second to last
       count - 1     fin8           last
       anything else finautre       the anonymous middle

   THE INTERESTING PART IS THAT BOTH ENDS ARE SPECIAL. The game has a
   distinct animation for finishing SECOND TO LAST, which is not a
   result anybody celebrates and not one a designer would obviously
   pay for. Somebody decided the back of the field deserved its own
   body language.

   Only reached while humanPlayer, raceState is 2 and speed is at
   least 5, blended in over (speed - 5) * 0.1.

9.54.3.  The 1.2 is what makes a stunt HOLD

       t = sin(elapsed / duration * pi) * 1.2, clamped to [0, 1]
       k = min((int)(frameCount * t), frameCount - 1)
       blend frame k-1 against frame k by (frameCount * t - k)

   The sine makes the stunt ease out and back rather than run
   linearly. THE 1.2 OVERSHOOT IS THE DETAIL WORTH KEEPING: sin peaks
   at exactly 1, so scaling past it and clamping pins t at the last
   frame through the middle of the move.

   The rider therefore reaches the extreme of the stunt and STAYS
   there for a while before coming back. Drop the 1.2 and he would
   touch the last frame for a single instant and immediately return -
   the same animation, and it would read as a twitch rather than a
   trick.

   That is the third constant in this subsystem whose only job is to
   make something hold or ease rather than snap, after the 0.25 pitch
   filter (9.53.1) and the sin easing on the glance (9.50.2). The
   original spends real effort on not looking mechanical.

9.54.4.  What is left in UpdatePose

   Transcribed here: racer_pose_stance_weight,
   racer_pose_finish_index and racer_pose_stunt_step.

   WHAT REMAINS IS PLUMBING RATHER THAN LOGIC [open]:

   o THE PODIUM PATH, driven by DAT_005dabd0 through three stages
     against 0x004d0f64 and 0x004d0f68. It blends the podium frames
     17..28 and belongs with MenuPage24_Podium_Enter rather than with
     the race.

   o THE TAIL WRITE. The blended frame is copied into
     pose[skeletonSlot * 0x14].pAngle with a stride of 0x19 floats
     between joints, bounded by min(perSlotArrays[slot], count). The
     destination is not packed triples, so it needs the pose array's
     own layout read before it can be written.

   o THE THREE 20-ITERATION CLEAR LOOPS at the top, which call
     FUN_00415a00 on three frame buffers. Their effect is understood
     - zeroing the working frames - but the callee is unread.

   maybe_Racer_UpdatePose's LOGIC is now read end to end. Every
   decision it makes about which pose to use and how hard to blend it
   is in src/; what is missing is where the answer gets written.


9.55.  THE TAIL WRITE - and the array we do not mirror

   The last piece of maybe_Racer_UpdatePose: what happens to the
   frame after seven blends have been stacked into it.

9.55.1.  Where the blended frame goes

   From the disassembly at 0x0045e305 [verified]:

       if (handSkeleton)  skeletonSlot = 1          racer+0x638
       n    = min(perSlotJointCount[slot], frame.count)
                                                    racer+0x2d4c
       dest = racer + 0x648 + slot * 2000
       for each of n joints:
           copy the angle triple;  dest += 100

   The slot stride comes out of the LEA chain at 0x0045e338 -
   slot*5*5*5 then shifted left four, which is slot * 2000 - and the
   joint stride is the ADD EDI,0x64 at 0x0045e35e.

   SO THE RACER HOLDS ONE 100-BYTE RECORD PER JOINT, twenty joints to
   a slot, with the angle triple at offset 0 and 88 bytes after it
   that this function never touches. 2000 / 100 = 20 confirms
   ANIM_MAX_JOINTS from a third direction, after the 0xf4 stride and
   the three 20-iteration clear loops.

   THREE FIELDS ARE NAMED BY THIS BLOCK: skeletonSlot at racer+0x638,
   a per-slot joint-count array at racer+0x2d4c, and the pose array
   itself at racer+0x648.

   AND THE handSkeleton OVERRIDE IS HERE, not where the flag is set.
   Every place that raises handSkeleton - the crash (9.35.1), the
   stunt, the riding poses - is really just voting for slot 1, and
   this is the line that counts the votes.

9.55.2.  The divergence, and why

   src/skl.c gains skl_apply_anim, which applies an MR_AnimFrame
   straight to an MR_Skeleton. IT DOES NOT REPRODUCE THE COPY.

   The 88 bytes after each angle triple are the original's per-joint
   scratch - composed matrices and mesh bindings - and our
   MR_Skeleton already holds the equivalent in its own shape.
   Mirroring the array would mean growing MR_Racer by 2000 bytes a
   slot to store data we would then have to keep in step with a
   second copy of the same information.

   THE REMAP STILL APPLIES EITHER WAY. An MR_AnimFrame is indexed by
   PART and a skeleton member wears part remap[i], the same as a
   .ROT - so skl_apply_anim and skl_apply_pose now share one angle
   source and one matrix walk, with the only difference being that
   one scales shorts and the other does not.

9.55.3.  The chain is complete

   manifest -> pilot_rots_load -> rot_load -> anim_frame_from_pose
            -> MR_AnimFrame -> anim_blend_angle_frames (x7)
            -> skl_apply_anim -> MR_Skeleton -> mesh vertices

   Every link is now in src/. What is NOT yet written is the caller
   that runs the seven blends in order and hands the result over -
   the weights and pose choices are all transcribed (9.53, 9.54) but
   nothing sequences them yet. That is assembly of parts we have,
   not reverse engineering. [open]


9.56.  THE SEQUENCER - and what writing it caught

   racer_build_pose in src/racer.c runs the blend passes in the
