   original's order and produces one MR_AnimFrame for skl_apply_anim.

9.56.1.  Four passes of seven

   +---+-----------+-----------------------------------------------+
   | 1 | BASE      | present                                       |
   | 2 | PITCH     | present                                       |
   | 3 | GLANCE    | not sequenced - needs the rival, the timer    |
   |   |           | and the remembered dot on MR_Racer            |
   | 4 | LEAN      | present                                       |
   | 5 | STUNT     | not sequenced - needs the running pose and    |
   |   |           | start stamp; no road bike has stunts anyway   |
   | 6 | CRASH     | present                                       |
   | 7 | CAMERA    | present                                       |
   +---+-----------+-----------------------------------------------+

   THE THREE ABSENT PASSES ARE MISSING STATE, NOT LOGIC. Every one of
   them has its arithmetic already transcribed -
   racer_pose_glance (9.50.2), racer_pose_stunt_step (9.54.3)
   and racer_pose_finish_index (9.54.2) - so each switches on when
   its fields are plumbed onto MR_Racer. They are marked in the body
   in sequence position rather than omitted, so the shape of the
   original stays visible.

   THE PRE-RACE PASS IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT for a different reason:
   it targets pose index 31, which a road bike does not have, and
   reproducing it would mean reproducing a bug whose output depends
   on what was played earlier (9.53.4).

9.56.2.  A blend factor does not name its endpoints

   anim_blend_angle_frames copies its FIRST argument at t=0 and its
   SECOND at t=1, and the two calls pass their poses in OPPOSITE
   ORDERS [verified]:

       remote/AI   blend(tmp, SLIDE, LEAN, (speed - 30) * 0.12)
       local       blend(tmp, LEAN, SLIDE, (w - 0.46) * 1.0870)

   so a remote rider moves FROM slide TOWARD lean as speed rises, and
   the local player moves FROM lean TOWARD slide as his angle grows.

   A BLEND FACTOR ALONE DOES NOT SAY WHICH POSE IT SELECTS - that is
   decided by the argument order, six tokens away in the call. "t
   rises with speed" is half a fact until both endpoints are named.


9.57.  Posing is per-draw, not per-tick

   skl_apply_anim rewrites MR_Skeleton.mesh.vertices IN PLACE, and
   every racer is drawn from that one shared pool. A pose is therefore
   valid only until the next call, and racer_build_pose has to run
   immediately before the draw of the racer it describes - not in the
   tick.

   The original has the same constraint and answers it differently:
   maybe_Racer_Draw transforms each part's slice of the bind pool by
   that part's inverse world matrix as it draws, so it never poses a
   shared mesh at all. Ours poses and draws in turn instead, which
   costs one skeleton walk per racer per frame.

   A bike with no pilot rots keeps whatever pose the load path applied.


9.58.  The carac in memory is not in file order

   bikes.c reads MR_BikeCarac by FILE index - realSpeedMax is index
   18, moteurVitesseMax is 6. The game's own g_bikeCarac at 0x5092b0
   does NOT use that order: realSpeedMax reads at memory index 4
   [verified, live]. The stride is 73 floats either way.

   So a field cannot be compared against game memory by assuming
   offset 4*fileIndex. Decode the .BPT instead - the file order is
   what bikes.c uses and what formats/bpt-carac.txt documents.

9.58.1.  physics_speed_to_engine is exact

   SPEED carac1 gives moteurVitesseMax 7, vitesseMaxBoite 61 and
   rapportDeBoite[0] 0.3, so gear 1 tops at 18.3 m/s and

       engineSpeed / roadSpeed = 7 / 18.3 = 0.38251

   The running original at gear 1 holds engineSpeed 1.2351 against
   gearboxRoadSpeed 3.2289 - a ratio of 0.38251 [verified]. The
   formula and race.c's per-frame recompute both match.

   THE GEAR LADDER, rapportDeBoite x vitesseMaxBoite:

       1  66 km/h   2  132   3  198   4  264   5  294   6  318

   capped by realSpeedMax at 288. Gear 1 alone reaches 66, past the
   60 km/h where the rider's crouch saturates (9.54.1).


9.59.  The automatic shift: a power crossover, and a clutch delay

   Gearbox_RequestAutoShift @ 0x0048e550 upshifts on ROAD SPEED
   against a per-gear table, and Gearbox_BuildShiftTable @
   0x0048e3a0 builds that table once by sweeping speed and finding
   where the next gear makes more POWER than the current one. There
   is no red-line fraction in the criterion. Both plate comments
   carry the code and the constants.  [verified]

       T[gear] measured live, stock bike, at racer+0x90:

           T[1] 17.8000   T[2] 34.5000   T[3] 50.8998
           T[4] 65.6996   T[5] 72.8995

       upshift    roadSpeed >  T[gear]
       downshift  roadSpeed <  T[gear-1] - 2.0

   AN EARLIER READING OF THIS SECTION WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH
   NAMING, because the reasoning was sound and the conclusion was
   not. It argued that an automatic shift CANNOT test road speed,
   since physics_speed_to_engine saturates engineSpeed at
   moteurVitesseMax so road speed asymptotes to the gear top and
   never crosses it - and concluded the criterion had to be engine
   speed against zoneRouge. The step it missed is that T[gear] is
   NOT the gear top. It is the power crossover, which sits below
   the asymptote, so the test fires easily: gear 1 reached 17.88748
   against a T[1] of 17.8.

   THE SHIFT IS NOT INSTANT AND THAT IS MOST OF ITS CHARACTER.
   Requesting only sets state and a timer of 200 ms - 150 when
   racer+0x88 is 0 - and Gearbox_TickShift moves the gear when it
   expires. Through the whole delay the engine is pinned at the
   limiter and drag pulls the bike back:

       frame  gear    rpm     speed
          24     1  11978  17.88748    crossed T[1] here
          26     1  12000  17.85331    waiting out the timer,
          28     1  12000  17.81925    and LOSING SPEED
          30     2   5968  18.20472    200 ms up, gear moves

   Six frames at 33 ms is 198 ms, so the dwell IS the timer.
   [verified]

   THE PORT NOW DOES BOTH. gearbox_build_shift_table in physics.c
   sweeps the same crossover, race.c tests roadSpeed against the
   table, and gearbox_request_upshift already set the 150/200 ms
   timer that gearbox_tick_shift waits out - so the dwell falls out
   of machinery that was there all along. What was wrong was only
   the criterion: moteurVitesseMax * zoneRouge, which fires early,
   and with it the invented 0.4 downshift fraction, replaced by the
   original's flat 2 m/s hysteresis.

   IT NEEDED BOTH HALVES. A table alone still skips the loss at the
   limiter; a delay alone shifts at the wrong speed. Neither is a
   constant to tune, which is what made this implementable rather
   than fittable.

   ONE ARM IS UNVERIFIED. The sweep is capped by
   min(realSpeedMax, topGearTop), and the original reads that
   second term through a carac field the decompiler renders as an
   out-of-bounds index. The arithmetic is legible, the field name
   is not, and it did not bind on the stock bike - every threshold
   came from the crossover - so it is transcribed as read and
   marked. It only matters for a bike geared past its top speed.
   [inferred]

9.59.4.  Part 15's override is a slide along the fork axis

   THE STORE AT 0x004613af IS PART 15 AND NOTHING ELSE. ECX holds
   the part index there and reads 0x0f on every hit, with EBP at
   0x5dc = 15 * 100. It is not a loop body that happens to reach
   part 15; it is the override.  [verified]

   PART 15 IS SKL MEMBER 2, via remap. CORPS.SKL's remap is

       member   0  1   2  3   4  5 ...
       part     0  1  15  2  16  3 ...

   so the front bodywork is member 2, vertices 71..94, and its
   parent member 1 is the steering head. Their recentred origins
   give |origin[2] - origin[1]| = 69.4053, which is the L0 of the
   re-anchor formula.  [verified]

   THE OVERRIDE MOVES THE PART ONLY ALONG THE FORK AXIS. Captured
   on page 10, the matrix translation before and after the store:

       +----------+----------------------------+---------+--------+
       | state    | translation                | along u | perp   |
       +==========+============================+=========+========+
       | before   | (-2.5608,  0.0000, 0.1325) | -1.2286 | 45.211 |
       +----------+----------------------------+---------+--------+
       | after    | (-2.8310,  5.2932, -3.4000)| -7.5981 | 45.211 |
       +----------+----------------------------+---------+--------+

   u is the unit vector from member 1's pivot to member 2's,
   (0.0424, -0.8310, 0.5546). The perpendicular distance is
   IDENTICAL to four decimals, so the override is a pure slide:
   -6.3695 along the fork. That equals forkLen - L0 for the
   forkLen of 63.036 measured earlier, confirming the formula's
   shape from the other end.  [verified]

   A AND B, IDENTIFIED. The re-anchored pivot is built at
   0x0045fc29..0x0045fdde from three points in the racer's working
   frame, all captured live on page 10:

       +------------+------------------------------+-------------+
       | slot       | value                        | what        |
       +============+==============================+=============+
       | ESP+0x70   | (-0.25363, 65.88342,38.30714)| part 1      |
       +------------+------------------------------+-------------+
       | ESP+0x1cc  | ( 2.69102,  8.20504,76.79911)| part 15     |
       +------------+------------------------------+-------------+
       | [EBP]      | ( 0.00000,-27.84042,83.97533)| front wheel |
       +------------+------------------------------+-------------+

   so

       A = |part15 - part1|                       = 69.40531
       L = |wheel  - part1|                       = 104.25836
       B = L - 96 * RoueRayonAv                   =  76.41836
       S = |d| * A / B,  d = part15.pos - part1.pos
         = 69.40531 * 0.908228                    =  63.0359

   which reproduces the captured pivot exactly: |T - pivot1| is
   63.036 and its direction matches the fork axis to five
   decimals.  [verified]

   A IS THE REST FORK LENGTH, AND IT IS INVARIANT. ESP+0x70 and
   ESP+0x1cc are the SAME two points d is taken between, so
   A == |d| identically - the original measures one length twice.
   Both sit 41.7598 above the bind pivots in Y, a frame offset
   that cancels in the subtraction. So the whole override is

       S = L0^2 / (L - 96 * RoueRayonAv)

   with L0 = |pivot15 - pivot1| constant from the .skl, and L the
   only runtime term.  [verified]

   96.0 IS AT 0x004d102c, units per metre. RoueRayonAv is read as
   0x005092b4 + bike * 260, bike from [EBX+0x78]. For bike 0 it is
   0.29; only that value reproduces S, so the read is confirmed
   from the output.  [verified]

   THAT 41.7598 OFFSET IS A TRAP. It divides by RoueRayonAv to
   143.9992, which invites A.y = pivot1.y + 144 * RoueRayonAv.
   That is a coincidence: the offset is a frame translation shared
   by both points, it cancels, and it has nothing to do with the
   wheel. Fitting it also fails the rest case by nine units.

   racer+0x338 IS THE REAR WHEEL, NOT THE FRONT. It reads
   (0, -30.72, -84.0): -96 * 0.32 and 84 BEHIND centre, where the
   front wheel above is -96 * 0.29 and 84 in front. The two radii
   differ, so a single wheel offset cannot serve both.  [verified]

   ESP+0xac/0xb0/0xb4 IS SCRATCH, REUSED SEVEN TIMES across the
   function. The write that reaches part 15 is the one at
   0x0046116d/0x0046117b/0x00461189, not the cross product at
   0x00460148 four kilobytes earlier. Reading the first writer
   found by a search gives a vector 64 units wrong.

   THE PORT IMPLEMENTS IT in skl_refork_front (src/skl.c), called
   between skl_build_* and skl_transform_verts from both draw
   paths. Against MOTO's captured values it gives

       +-------+------------+------------+
       | term  | port       | MOTO       |
       +=======+============+============+
       | L0    |   69.40531 |   69.40531 |
       +-------+------------+------------+
       | L     |  104.25277 |  104.25836 |
       +-------+------------+------------+
       | B     |   76.41277 |   76.41836 |
       +-------+------------+------------+
       | S     |   63.04048 |   63.03590 |
       +-------+------------+------------+

   0.007 per cent on S. The residual is the page-10 pose yaw,
   which this check left out and the port applies at run time, so
   the built code should agree more closely still.

   THE ONE OPEN TERM IS THE FRAME LIFT. The pivots sit 41.7598
   above the bind pose in Y where the wheel offsets do not; it
   cancels in A but not in L. On bike 0 that is 144 * rayonAv to
   2e-4, and 144 * rayonAr misses by four units.

   IT CANNOT BE SETTLED FROM SHIPPED DATA. All eight bikes carry
   rayonAv = 0.29 and rayonAr = 0.32 - read live off the loaded
   table at 0x005092b4, stride 292 - so 144 * rayonAv and a fixed
   41.76 agree for every bike the game can select. Swapping bikes
   returns a byte-identical capture, which is how this was found.
   The two forms diverge only on a modded carac, so the choice
   cannot change what a stock install draws.

   POKING rayonAv AT RUN TIME DOES NOT SHOW IT EITHER. The pivots
   and the wheel offsets are baked when the model loads: writing
   0.5 over bike 2's rayonAv left all three captured points
   unchanged. A poke would have to be followed by a page reload.

   THE FRONT WHEEL RIDES THE FORK AXIS TOO. It is not drawn at the
   carac offset at all:

       wheelCentre = pivot1 + B * normalize(pivot15 - pivot1)

   built at 0x0045fc9e (Vec3_Scale) and 0x0045fcb1 (Vec3_Add) into
   ESP+0x10c, then drawn by FUN_0048c400. Reproduces the measured
   draw position to 1e-5.  [verified]

   racer+0x1B4 NEVER POSITIONS ANYTHING. The caller passes
   Vec3_Sub(ESP+0x10c, racer+0x1B4) because FUN_0048c400 adds
   racer+0x1B4 straight back at 0x0048c43a. The carac offset makes
   a round trip and cancels.

   B IS THE PIVOT-TO-AXLE DISTANCE, which is what the construction
   means: L reaches the tyre's contact point, one radius back up
   the fork is the axle. AT REST B == A, so the wheel centre lands
   exactly on part 15's bind pivot - part 15 IS the front wheel
   node, which is why the original calls it RoueAV. Under
   compression they separate, the wheel at B and part 15 at A*A/B,
   reciprocal about A.

   THE LIFT CANCELS IN BOTH. pivot1 and the axis endpoints carry
   the same frame offset, so evaluating either expression on the
   .skl bind pivots gives the same answer. Only L needs the lifted
   frame.

   +----------------------+-----------+-----------+-----------+
   | front wheel, bind    | x         | y         | z         |
   +======================+===========+===========+===========+
   | MOTO, measured       |    2.9886 |  -39.3829 |   80.7009 |
   +----------------------+-----------+-----------+-----------+
   | port, carac offset   |    0.0000 |  -27.8400 |   83.9753 |
   +----------------------+-----------+-----------+-----------+

   So the port sits 11.54 high and 3.27 forward. An earlier attempt
   to fix this by dropping the wheels 41.76 was 30.22 too far and
   looked comical; the offset is not a constant, it is whatever the
   fork geometry gives that frame.

   THE PORT DOES BOTH from one call. skl_refork_front returns the
   wheel centre alongside re-anchoring part 15, since they share
   pivot1, the axis and B. Against the two captures, on the bind
   pose:

       part 15   error 0.0046
       wheel     error 0.0057

   The residual is the page-10 pose yaw, which the port applies at
   run time and this check does not. menu.c takes it at page enter,
   race.c per racer per frame.

   THE REAR WHEEL IS PART 16'S PIVOT, with no adjustment at all.
   Built at 0x00460c43 as R * record[16].pos + T, where R and T are
   the racer's own orientation and position - the same pair every
   part goes through. So the wheel's LOCAL position is just the
   pivot. The carac offset cancels here too: the caller subtracts
   racer+0x338 at 0x00460d52 only because FUN_0048c400 adds it
   back.  [verified]

       record[16].pos measured (0.08641, -32.73438, -71.56070)
       member 4 bind pivot     (0.0864,  -32.7344,  -71.5607)

   THE SWINGARM IS PART 2 = MEMBER 3, twelve vertices, with the
   rear wheel node (part 16, member 4) as its child. IT ARTICULATES,
   AND IT IS NOT DRAWN FROM THE SKELETON'S PART MATRIX.

   MEMBER 3 IS SPECIAL-CASED IN THE PART LOOP:

       004614cf   CMP EDI,0x3
       004614d2   JNZ 0x00461537     <- every other member

   The normal path at 0x00461537 loads record[part].mat and
   transposes it. Member 3 skips that entirely and takes
   0x00461525, LEA ECX,[ESP+0x240] - A MATRIX BUILT SEPARATELY. It
   also reads a pointer at record[part]+0x58 and drops the draw if
   it is null.  [verified]

   THAT MATRIX IS BUILT AT 0x0046067e, composing a model matrix
   with the camera view at ESI+0x68..0x94, then copied to
   ESP+0x238. Captured live it changes frame to frame as the menu
   bike turns, which the part matrices do not.

   THE PROOF IS A NULL RESULT. Negating part 2's pose angle in the
   running game - racer+0x648+2*100, -0.191748 to +0.191748 - moved
   the part matrix from -10.99 to +10.99 degrees and changed
   NOTHING on screen. The swingarm does not read it.  [verified]

   SO A PORT THAT POSES MEMBER 3 FROM THE .rot IS WRONG BY
   CONSTRUCTION, however exactly its matrices match. Ours does, and
   that is why its swingarm sits near horizontal where retail's
   points down.

   THE MODEL MATRIX IS A SINGLE X ROTATION FROM THE COS TABLE.

       004603f2..0046047c   build A at ESP+0x10 from g_cosTable
       004604ba   CALL 0x00403a80   ESP+0x40 = A * [ESP+0x12c]

   Captured live on page 10:

       A = [1  0        0      ]   rotation about X of -7.317 deg
           [0  0.99186 -0.12736]
           [0  0.12736  0.99186]
       [ESP+0x12c] = identity, so ESP+0x40 is just A

   THE TWO LOADS THAT LOOK LIKE CONSTANTS ARE NOT A MISREAD. The
   listing shows absolute FLD [0x005fcb80] and FMUL [0x00608b80],
   and the bytes confirm it - d9 05 80cb5f00. They are cos table
   entry 0 (1.0) and entry 0x3000 (0.0), used as literals to place
   ones and zeros in the matrix. The ANGLE enters through EDI and
   EBP, LEA'd from ECX*4 and EAX*4 off the same table base.

   EAX IS THE 14-BIT SWINGARM ANGLE. Measured 0x2eb3:

       cosTable[0x2eb3] = -0.12736    <- matches A's off-diagonal
       cosTable[0x1eb3] = -0.99186    <- sin, index i-0x1000

   to five decimals, signs set by the FCHS at 0x00460448 and
   0x00460474.  [verified]

   THE REAR WHEEL SHARES THIS ROTATION. The R applied to
   record[16].pos at 0x00460c43 is the same matrix, element for
   element. So the arm and the wheel swing together about the
   pivot - which is what a swingarm IS, and it means the earlier
   reading of that R as a whole-bike display pitch was wrong.

   THE ANGLE IS A REST CONSTANT PLUS A TRACKING ARCTANGENT.

       004602d8..0046030b   FPATAN  -> [ESP+0x10c]
       0046031d             carac[bike] at 0x005092e4 -> [ESP+0x1cc]
       004603b9   FLD  [ESP+0x1cc]
       004603c0   FADD [ESP+0x10c]
       004603c7   FMUL 0x004d0ed0        0x4000 / 2pi
       004603cd   CALL __ftol            truncates

   so

       angle = carac[27] + atan2(|cross(d,w)|, dot(d,w))
       d     = racer+0x338 - [ESP+0x168]
       w     = (0, 0, -1)
       index = ftol(angle * 2607.5945876) & 0x3fff

   Measured on page 10, bike 2, every step:

       carac[27]  -0.550000 rad     read at 0x0050952c
       atan2 term +0.422254 rad     = +24.193 deg
       sum        -0.127746 rad     = -7.319 deg
       index      0x3eb3
       cos        cosTable[0x3eb3] = +0.99186
       sin        cosTable[0x2eb3] = -0.12736

   which is matrix A element for element.  [verified]

   CARAC INDEX 27 IS THE SWINGARM REST ANGLE, in radians, and it is
   NOT PARSED by bikes.c - that file jumps from 16 to 28. Address
   is 0x005092e4 + bike*292, the same stride and base family as
   RoueRayonAv at 0x005092b4 (index 15).

   d IS TAKEN FROM racer+0x338, THE REAR WHEEL CARAC OFFSET. That
   offset positions nothing at draw time - it cancels there - but
   it is real input here, so do not delete it as dead data.

   [ESP+0x168] IS PART 3'S PIVOT, IN WORLD SPACE. Built at
   0x0045ffc8 by FUN_00410010(out, point, matrix), which is
   out = point * matrix with the translation at matrix+0x24. The
   point is racer+0x7b0, and

       0x7b0 = 0x684 + 3*100   ->  record[3].pos

   read live as (-0.01595, -7.95258, 59.63626) against member 5's
   bind origin (-0.01595, -7.95258, 59.63625), error 1e-5.
   [verified]

   PART 3 = MEMBER 5 IS A PURE JOINT, zero vertices. It exists only
   to mark the swingarm pivot. So the whole articulation is

       P     = record[3].pos * racerWorldMatrix
       d     = racer+0x338 - P
       w     = (0, 0, -1)
       angle = carac[27] + atan2(|cross(d,w)|, dot(d,w))
       A     = X rotation by angle, off g_cosTable

   and A drives the swingarm mesh AND the rear wheel together.

   THE 41.76 'MODEL LIFT' IS THE RACER'S WORLD TRANSLATION. That
   same FUN_00410010 is what produced ESP+0x70, and the offset
   between record[1].pos and ESP+0x70 - (0, 41.75979, -0.01234) -
   is simply the matrix's translation column. It is NOT
   144 * rayonAv; that was a coincidence of the menu pose, where
   the bike happens to sit at y = 41.76.

   THIS MAKES src/skl.h's SKL_MODEL_LIFT_K WRONG IN FORM. It is
   right on the bike selection screen and wrong everywhere the
   racer's Y is not 41.76 - which is the whole track. The fork
   scalars A and B need the racer's world matrix, not a constant.

   THE MATRIX IS THE RACER'S WORLD 3x4. Built at 0x0045ff99 by
   FUN_00403740(out, euler, pos), pushed at 0x0045ffab:

       euler = [ESP+0xac]    three angles, each scaled by the
                             double at 0x004d00d0 and looked up in
                             g_cosTable exactly as everywhere else
       pos   = [ESP+0x70]    copied straight into out+0x24..0x2c

   Captured on page 10:

       rotation    identity (euler reads 0 to 12 decimals)
       translation (0.0000000, 41.7597885, -0.0123367)

   which is the offset between record[1].pos and ESP+0x70 to SEVEN
   decimals.  [verified]

   SO THERE IS NO 'MODEL LIFT'. The 41.76 is the racer's world
   position, nothing more. On the bike selection screen the bike
   sits at y = 41.76; on the track it sits wherever the physics
   puts it, and the number changes every frame.

   WHAT THE PORT NEEDS. skl_refork_front takes A and B from points
   in this frame, so it needs the racer's world matrix, not a
   constant. SKL_MODEL_LIFT_K = 144 * rayonAv reproduces the menu
   because 41.76 happens to equal 144 * 0.29 there, and it is
   wrong everywhere else. It was fitted, not read, and all eight
   bikes sharing rayonAv = 0.29 is what let it survive.

   THE RACER'S OWN POSITION AND ORIENTATION ARE ALREADY KNOWN, and
   they are the fields this document has carried all along:

       +0x30     position          reads (0,0,0) for the menu bike
       +0x2e55   bike root 3x4     reads identity, zero translation

   both read live at racer 0x00584421.  [verified]

   THE 41.76 IS NOT A RACER FIELD. It is assembled on the stack for
   this draw. At 0x0045ff62 the position handed to FUN_00403740 is
   built from two terms captured as

       [ESP+0x0dc] = (0.0000000, 26.3997898, -0.0123367)
       [ESP+0x014] = (15.3599997, 0, 0)      15.36 = 48 * rayonAr

   and 26.3998 + 15.36 = 41.7598 exactly - note that is A.y + B.x,
   so the combination is NOT a component-wise Vec3_Add and the
   argument order at that call has not been settled.

   WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE PORT. On the track the world matrix
   carries the racer's real +0x30 and +0x2e55, which the port
   already has as r->pos and the bike root. The menu adds a display
   placement on top, and that placement is what made the bike sit
   at y = 41.76 on page 10 - the number that was mistaken for
   144 * rayonAv.

9.60.  The lean's yawDelta is the slip angle

   Racer_GetYaw (0x00490220) takes a 3x4 MATRIX pointer - rows at +0,
   +0x0c, +0x18 - and returns atan2(row0.z, row2.z). For a 12-float
   m[] that is atan2(m[2], m[8]).

   maybe_Racer_UpdateLean calls it twice, and both paths use the same
   pair [verified]:

       0046a947  LEA ECX,[ESP+0xb8]   the frame just built from the
       0046a94e  CALL Racer_GetYaw    wheel contacts
       0046a957  MOV ECX,EBX          EBX = ESI+0xbc = matOrientation
       0046a959  CALL Racer_GetYaw    (LEA'd once at 0x00469d13)

   0x0046a946 is a RET, so 0x0046a947 is a jump target and the bytes
   before it say nothing about ESP+0xb8. The live capture below
   settles it instead: d equals yaw(matOrientation) - yaw(matBikeRoot)
   exactly, so the local is matBikeRoot's freshly built frame.

   SO yawDelta IS THE SLIP ANGLE - the bike's heading against the
   direction it is actually travelling - not a frame-to-frame
   difference. Reading it as a per-frame yaw difference gives numbers
   about thirty times too small to reach the +-1.04 clamp.

9.60.1.  WHERE THE TRAVEL DIRECTION ENTERS: the contacts

   THE CONTACTS ARE NOT TAKEN IN matOrientation. UpdateLean opens by
   building a frame from a blend of the body's heading and the
   movement direction, and samples the wheels in THAT [verified]:

       0046 9cd6  alpha = 1.0 - (speed/realSpeedMax)*8.0
                  clamped at 0 below, floored at flImpactBlend*0.005
                                     0x004d0e70, 0x004d1020, 0x004d100c
       0046 9d0d  local_18 = matOrientation transformed by (0,0,1)
                  moveDir  = racer+0x18c
       0046 9d3f  blended  = alpha*local_18 + (1-alpha)*moveDir
                  FUN_0046bdc0((0,0,0), blended) -> the travel frame
       0046 9e2b  the wheel contacts, taken IN THE TRAVEL FRAME
                  then ground-snapped, each in its own segment
                  then matBikeRoot = frame from (front - rear)

   THE SAME WEIGHT DOES DOUBLE DUTY: alpha blends the direction, and
   1-alpha scales the delta in 9.60.2. At rest alpha is 1, the travel
   frame IS the heading, and the delta is scaled to zero. Past
   realSpeedMax/8 alpha is 0 and the frame is pure moveDir.

   THIS IS THE STEP THAT MAKES THE TWO YAWS DIFFER AT ALL. Sampling
   the contacts in matOrientation instead yields a rebuilt frame with
   matOrientation's own yaw to three decimals, and a delta that is
   identically zero:

   o the contacts differ by row1*dU + row2*dA, and row1's XZ part is
     parallel to row2's, so front - rear is XZ-parallel to row2;
   o the frame builder takes lateral = worldUp x forward, so
     row0.z = -fwd.x/|fwd.xz| - unchanged by any XZ-parallel scaling;
   o ground-snapping does not rescue it, because snapping moves only
     Y and row0.z does not depend on Y.

   A port that samples the contacts in the body matrix therefore shows
   NO LEAN AT ALL, at any speed, on any terrain.

9.60.2.  The whole chain, verified

   Two constants were missing from the first reading: a x2.0 and a
   SPEED GATE. With them the chain is

       d    = wrap(yaw(matOrientation) - yaw(matBikeRoot))
       gate = 1 - alpha   (alpha as in 9.60.1)
       d    = wrap(d * gate * 2.0)          0x004d1020, 0x004d0f60
       clamp d to +-pi/3                    0x004d1250 / 0x004d1260
       lean = d / dtSmoothed * -30          0x004d1288
       if (lean >  0.3) lean = lean*1.4 - 0.12
       if (lean < -0.3) lean = lean*1.4 + 0.12
       clamp lean to +-1.04

   THE GATE IS A SPEED AUTHORITY, zero at rest and full above
   realSpeedMax/8 - 36 km/h on SPEED. A stopped bike cannot lean.

   VERIFIED against a paused capture at 186 km/h [verified]:

       yaw(matOrientation)  +0.862482
       yaw(matBikeRoot)     +0.686520
       d                    +0.175962
       x gate 1.0, x 2.0    +0.351924
       / 33.0 x -30         -0.319930
       piecewise            -0.327900
       live racer+0x2e1d    -0.327893

   dtSmoothed came out at exactly 33.0 - a 30 fps frame - which also
   confirms DAT_004dc150 is milliseconds, and that the 9.49.3
   piecewise belongs to this same path.


9.62.  The bike is drawn on matBikeRoot, with the lean rolled in

   maybe_Racer_Draw does NOT draw the bike on matOrientation. At
   0x0045f2d7 it copies racer+0x2e55 - matBikeRoot, the frame built
   from the ground-snapped wheel contacts - and then, at 0x0045f2f9:

       FLD  [EBX + 0x2e1d]      the lean
       FCHS                     NEGATED
       Vec3_Set(v, 0, 0, -lean)
       FUN_0040fd40(v)          euler -> matrix
       ... composed onto the copy

   SO THE LEAN IS A ROLL ABOUT Z, the frame's own forward axis, by
   MINUS the lean. Rows 0 and 1 turn about row 2, which tips the bike
   into the corner and leaves the heading alone.

   THIS IS WHY A PORT THAT DRAWS FROM matOrientation HAS A BIKE THAT
   CORNERS BOLT UPRIGHT however good its lean value is - the plate
   comment on maybe_Racer_UpdateLean says exactly that and it is easy
   to read as a note about roll in general rather than as an
   instruction about the draw.

   The composition is gated on racer+0x638 > 1 at 0x0045f2f0, which
   is not read yet. [open]

9.63.  The pose maths is verified; what is left is missing passes

   matBikeRoot now matches the original to 0.0028 rad - 0.16 degrees
   - on every row, once the lean pass is moved AFTER the move so the
   frame it publishes is built from this frame's travel direction
   rather than the previous one.

   THE LEAN ITSELF MATCHES within 1 per cent at matched dt: from
   MOTO's slip 0.2691 the formula gives -0.565, and ours from slip
   0.2670 gives -0.5597. The measured leans differ only because
   g_frameDeltaSmoothed is inflated while stepping (9.61.5).

   EVERY POSE CONSTANT WAS READ BACK OUT OF THE BINARY and matches:
   20/3.6 and 0.18 (base), 4.0 and 0.25 (pitch), 30/3.6, 0.12,
   60/3.6, 4.3478, 2.1739, 0.46 and 1.0870 (lean), 5.0 and 0.1
   (finish), 400 and 0.02 (crash), 900/1000/0.01 (camera). The blend
   operand order is right too - local goes lean toward slide.

   SO A RIDER THAT LOOKS MORE EXAGGERATED THAN RETAIL IS NOT THE
   ARITHMETIC. Two things remain that would show as exactly that:

   o THREE PASSES ARE NOT SEQUENCED - glance, stunt and finish - so
     the lean/slide pair has nothing competing with it and holds the
     body for frames where the original would be doing something
     else. All three are blocked on MR_Racer state, not on logic.

   o THE CAMERA DOES NOT TRACK LIKE THE ORIGINAL'S, which makes any
     side-by-side judgement of body angle unreliable. Worth fixing
     before chasing the pose further by eye.

   The one number here that is INFERRED rather than read is the
   stance saturation: the original picks 0xf0 or 0x78 and halves it,
   giving 120 km/h remote and 60 km/h local. [inferred]

9.64.  Two loops draw the skeleton, and they do not run the same
       number of times

   The tail of maybe_Racer_Draw is a vertex loop followed by a face
   loop, and the ONE-OFF BETWEEN THEIR BOUNDS is the whole answer to
   how the trailing group of a .skl is drawn.

   +-------------+------------------+------------------------------+
   | Loop        | Runs             | Indexed by                   |
   +=============+==================+==============================+
   | vertices    | memberCount      | part = remap[i]              |
   +-------------+------------------+------------------------------+
   | faces       | memberCount + 1  | i, RAW - no remap            |
   +-------------+------------------+------------------------------+

   The +1 is two INC EAX instructions, at 0x00461a06 sizing the entry
   test and at 0x00461a85 sizing the back edge. So the face loop
   visits one group past the last member: the trailing group, which
   the loader parks in slot memberCount (skl-rot.txt 1.3).

   IT NEEDS NO MATRIX, AND THAT IS WHY IT HAS NONE. Every group's
   faces are queued against the SAME vertex base - the pointer
   Screen3D_TransformVertsFlat returned from the i == 0 call, held
   across both loops. The trailing group has no vertex run of its own,
   so the vertex loop never reaches it and never needs to; its 91
   faces index vertices other members already transformed. That is
   exactly what makes it the glue: a face spanning two bones is drawn
   from two bones' output.

   THE POOL IS APPENDED TO, NOT ADDRESSED BY VERTEX INDEX.
   transform_verts_flat returns the base of the slice it just filled
   and the next call continues from there, so a face's shared-pool
   index only lands on the right vertex because the members are
   visited in an order whose runs are ascending and contiguous. The
   runs partition the pool exactly (skl-rot.txt 2). They are indexed
   by MEMBER, so the ordering argument does not depend on the remap -
   which is NOT the identity; see 9.59.4. A reconstruction that
   transforms parts in any other order silently scrambles the glue.

   TWO LOOP INDICES SKIP THE INVERSE, AND THE SECOND IS THE SWINGARM.
   Both hand a prebuilt matrix straight to the transform:

   +---------+--------+---------------+---------------------------+
   | counter | member | part          | matrix, uninverted        |
   +=========+========+===============+===========================+
   | 0       | 0      | 0, corps      | the body matrix at the    |
   |         |        |               | draw position, ESP+0x3cc  |
   +---------+--------+---------------+---------------------------+
   | 3       | 3      | 2, SWINGARM   | the arm matrix composed   |
   |         |        |               | to camera, ESP+0x240      |
   +---------+--------+---------------+---------------------------+

   because remap sends member 3 to part 2 (9.59.4). The test is
   CMP EDI,0x3 at 0x004614cf and the two matrix pointers are the
   LEA at 0x004614ab and 0x00461525. [verified]

   o IT IS NOT "A COPY OF THE BIKE MATRIX", which is what stood
     here, and it is not an identity either - racerdraw.h said that
     and was also wrong. Both readings put the swingarm through the
     wrong transform, and the port has the same fact from the other
     end: model.c measured mat[3] as needing a PRE-INVERSION because
     its own draw inverted everything. Same member, opposite route.

   Every other index builds the transpose inverse of the part matrix
   (section 9.59.3) and composes it. Parts 3..14 compose against the
   RIDER position, everything else against the bike position - the
   test is on the remapped index, not the loop index, and it
   substitutes only the TRANSLATION. The rider orientation comes from
   the second walk root, not from a separate placement matrix.

   WHAT THE PORT DOES. bike_draw_body_parts walks the members in the
   same order, composes inverse(mat[member]) with the placement and
   the view, and appends each slice of the BIND pool - no posed
   model-space mesh in between. skl.c merges all memberCount+1 face
   groups into one MESH at load, so queueing the whole primitive list
   against the first slice's base draws the trailing group exactly as
   the original's second loop does. The loader checks that the runs
   tile the pool and sets runsPacked; a skeleton that fails it falls
   back to the old baked path rather than drawing scrambled glue.

   THE TWO SKIPPED INVERSES ARE NOT REPRODUCED. Ours applies the
   inverse to every member, including loop indices 0 and 3. [open -
   whether mat[0] and mat[3] are identity in practice, which is the
   only way the two agree, has not been measured.]

   NEITHER IS THE PER-PART PLACEMENT SPLIT. The original composes
   parts 3..14 against the RIDER position and everything else against
   the bike position, which is what lets the rider separate from the
   bike in a crash - maybe_Racer_Draw calls Skl_BuildPartMatrices
   twice, once on the bike matrix and once on matRiderRoot. bike_draw
   has one placement for the whole skeleton. Drawing per member is
   what makes expressing this possible at all; it is not yet done.
9.65.  A CRASHED BIKE RUNS NO DRIVETRAIN AT ALL

   maybe_Racer_ApplyDrive (0x00465da0) HAS EXACTLY ONE CALLEE,
   maybe_Physics_TickDrivetrain, and maybe_Racer_Tick branches around
   the whole of it on crashLatch at 0x0045d6fb [verified].  It skips
   maybe_Racer_ApplySteering (0x00466240) at 0x0045d733 the same way.

   So while the rider is down the ONLY thing that touches the bike's
   speed is the crash decay at the tail of maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash:

       speed  *= 0.9        _DAT_004d11b8, humanPlayer only
       f_0180 *= 0.9

   MEASURED, square into the rail at speed 60, from the frame
   crashLatch sets:

       MOTO   11.92  9.72  7.92  6.45  5.26  4.28  3.49  2.85
              a clean 0.9 a tick, 0.8146 a frame

   A PORT THAT SPLITS ApplyDrive MUST SKIP EVERY PIECE.  Feeding the
   drivetrain a zero demand is NOT the same thing: it coasts, which
   held the port at

       ours   13.21 13.22 13.20 13.18 13.16 13.14 13.12
              a ratio of 0.9984 - ordinary drag

   and overwrote the 0.9 every frame.

   THREE PIECES, NOT TWO.  The port's split is racer_apply_drive,
   physics_drivetrain_frame AND physics_drivetrain_tail.  Gating the
   first two still left the slide four times too long, because THE
   TAIL IS WHERE THE DRAG LIVES.  Measured on a shallow-angle wheelie
   crash at speed 70, both games going down at the same point:

       MOTO   34.8 28.4 23.0 18.6 15.1 12.2  9.9  8.0  6.5  5.3
       ours   38.6 18.3 18.2 18.2 18.2 18.1 18.1 18.0 18.0 18.0

       slide from the impact point   MOTO 1364 units, ours 3567

   LINEAR IS THE TELL.  CRASH_DECAY is geometric - a fixed RATIO per
   tick - so a crashed bike losing a FLAT amount per frame (0.033
   here, a ratio of 0.998) is not running CRASH_DECAY at all; it is
   running a drag term that should have been skipped.  Check the
   differences, not the values.

   VERIFIED with all three gated, same crash, same frame count:

       MOTO   34.8 28.4 23.0 18.6 15.1 12.2  9.9  8.0  6.5  5.3
       ours   38.6 31.2 25.3 20.5 16.6 13.4 10.9  8.8  7.1  5.8

       ratio  0.8100 on BOTH sides at every step
       slide  MOTO 1358.6 units, ours 1347.7 - 0.8 per cent apart,
              and the two paths stay within 11 units of each other

9.65.1.  WHERE UpdateCrash RUNS, and the frame it must not miss

   maybe_Racer_Tick calls maybe_Racer_UpdateCrash at 0x0045d7d0 -
   AFTER maybe_Racer_UpdateGroundContact at 0x0045d79c, after
   UpdateLean at 0x0045d7c9, and immediately before the skeleton and
   maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx [verified].

   THE CRASH BEGINS INSIDE UpdateGroundContact.  So a port that runs
   its crash update EARLIER in the tick finds crashLatch still 0 on
   the frame the bike goes down, never applies the first 0.9, and
   carries 1/0.9 too much speed for the rest of the slide.

   MEASURED, both entering the impact frame at 72.22:

       MOTO   72.23 -> 34.815    shed 37.41
       ours   72.23 -> 38.568    shed 33.66,  a ratio of 0.8996

   one whole CRASH_DECAY short.  Every later value then carried a
   constant 1.108 offset while decaying at the correct 0.81 - which is
   the shape to look for: a RATIO that is right with a LEVEL that is
   not is an off-by-one in when the decay starts, not a wrong
   constant.

   IT HIDES IN THE DISTANCE.  The slide still measured 1348 units
   against MOTO's 1359, because displacement per unit of speed came
   out 0.9000 on the nose - the same factor, cancelling.  Two
   compensating errors read as a 0.8 per cent result; only comparing
   the speed FIELD exposes it.

   VERIFIED after moving the call to match:

       impact   MOTO 34.815   ours 34.711    0.3 per cent, was 10.8
       then     28.369/28.116  22.979/22.774  18.613/18.447
       ratio    0.80999 on both sides at every step

   WHAT IS LEFT IS THE RUN-IN, NOT THE CRASH.  The two are already
   about 30 units apart in z by the time they reach the rail, while
   their speeds agree to 0.003 per cent - which is the shape of the
   SEND_INPUT frame-count mismatch (9.18.15), not of a physics
   difference.  They therefore strike the wall at slightly different
   points and shed slightly different amounts.  Do not chase it as a
   crash defect.

   THE OVER-LONG SLIDE IS ALSO WHY BRAKING MADE SMOKE.  The wheel
   spray's gate is speed > 5.0 AND onGround, and the raw button word
   reaches maybe_Racer_EmitWheelFx whether or not the racer is down -
   the original passes the same EBP on both arms of the crashLatch
   branch.  Retail simply falls under 5.0 within about six frames, so
   the window is too short to see.  There is no separate crash test to
   add.

   NOT THE COAST CASE.  Branching around the drivetrain when the
   throttle is merely released is a DIFFERENT and known-wrong change -
   it leaves the engine at zero, because the idle floor and the tail
   resync live inside it.  Coasting runs it with num=0, den=1; a crash
   runs none of it.

9.67.  THE HAND SKELETON - why a crashed rider keeps hold of the bars

   THE MANIFEST NAMES TWO SKELETONS, not one [verified, MOTOKTM.BPT
   and MOTOSPD.BPT decoded]:

       record 2   data\ktmcross\corps.skl
       record 3   data\ktmcross\mHand\corps.skl

   mHand is MAIN - French for hand - and it is a SECOND TOPOLOGY, not
   a level of detail.  Decoding both files, KTMCROSS member 1 carries
   58 vertices in the base and 40 in mHand, and the EIGHTEEN in the
   difference are the rider's two hands.  The base WELDS THEM TO A
   BIKE MEMBER; mHand gives them to the rider's own bones.

   THAT IS WHAT racer+0x319d MEANS.  racer_select_skeleton_slot reads

       slot = (crashLatch || handSkeleton) ? 1 : lodSlot

   and slot 1 IS the hand skeleton.  The whole point of the second
   file is that a rider cannot be thrown clear while his hands are
   part of the bike.

   THE SYMPTOM IF YOU LOAD ONLY THE BASE, which the port did: the
   rider lies on the ground, correctly posed, with one hand still on
   the bars and a single arm stretched the whole way between.  The
   skeleton is not at fault and neither is the crash - every rider
   bone matched MOTO's bit for bit.  It is the VERTEX RUNS:

       part  1   MOTO 40 vertices   ours 58
       part 11   MOTO 13            ours  4
       part 14   MOTO 13            ours  4

   eighteen vertices on the wrong bone, and every firstVertex after
   part 1 shifted by the same eighteen.

   HOW TO RECOGNISE IT.  Comparing bone MATRICES proves nothing here
   - they were identical - and comparing the tree proves nothing
   either, because both files have the same seventeen members in the
   same two trees.  The vertex RUNS are the only place the two
   topologies differ.

   THE PRIMS GO WITH THE SKELETON.  cull_and_queue_faces is handed
   one prim array built FROM the skeleton's mesh, so swapping the
   skeleton without swapping the prims queues the base mesh's faces
   against the hand mesh's vertices.  Both are re-pointed together,
   per racer, immediately before the draw - the slot is per racer and
   one rider can be down while the rest ride.

9.68.  fxGate - one uncalled function, three dead effects

   racer+0x2da0 is set ONCE PER TICK in the inline preamble of
   maybe_Racer_Tick, at 0x0045d676, right after its SlipSpeed call
   and before the drive chain [verified]:

       slip   = maybe_Racer_SlipSpeed(instance)
       fxGate = (onGround && |slip| >= 0.04) ? 1 : 0    0x004d0f40

   THE PORT HAD THE FUNCTION AND NEVER CALLED IT.  racer_fx_gate was
   written, declared and correct; nothing invoked it, so racer+0x2da0
   read zero for the whole of every race and racer_save_prev_frame
   was its only toucher.

   THREE THINGS TEST IT, and all three were dead:

   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | Reader              | What it does with it                      |
   +=====================+===========================================+
   | the skid ribbons    | (fxGate && |slip| > 0.5) - the tyre       |
   |                     | streak a sliding wheel leaves             |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | the wheel spray     | the same test - smoke off the sliding     |
   |                     | rear wheel                                |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | racer_steer_torque  | grip = |slip| * K + 1, clamped - so a     |
   |                     | SLIDING BIKE TURNS HARDER for the same    |
   |                     | bar angle                                 |
   +---------------------+-------------------------------------------+

   BRAKING SETS ITS OWN BIT, which is exactly why the braking effects
   worked and the sliding ones did not - and why this survived so
   long.  On the dirt bike, where holding a turn breaks the back end
   loose, it is the entire effect.

   MEASURED before the fix, gas and right held for forty steps from a
   standstill:

       step |  MOTO fxGate skidWidth |  ours fxGate skidWidth
         18 |     0        1.00      |     0        0.00
         24 |     1        1.00      |     0        0.00
         36 |     1        6.00      |     0        0.00

   with the SLIP ITSELF tracking to two decimals throughout (0.574
   against 0.529 at step 36).  The input to the gate was right and
   nothing read it - which is the shape to look for when an effect is
   missing but the physics driving it is not.

   AND IT FEEDS THE POSE INDIRECTLY.  The slide stances
   (derapGauche.rot, derapDroite.rot) are blended against the lean
   pose, and the lean comes off the steering the grip term above
   scales.  So a pose comparison taken before this is fixed is
   measuring the wrong inputs.

9.69.  matBikeRoot - the wheelbase, and PersoAnim decides its direction

   matBikeRoot is built from fwd = front - rear, and WHICH DIRECTION
   THAT PAIR POINTS IN IS PER BIKE CLASS.  Both measured on MOTO:

       KTM    dot(front - rear, matOrientation row2)  1.00000
       SPEED  dot(front - rear, moveDir)              1.00000

   with |front - rear| = 96 * axeDistance either way.  So the dirt
   bikes lay the wheelbase along the HEADING and the road bikes along
   TRAVEL, and on a road bike matBikeRoot's yaw therefore differs from
   matOrientation's by the slip angle.

   THE GATE IS PersoAnim, the same one that picks the lean curve
   (9.70).  0x00469e17 tests it and jumps to 0x00469f37 when zero,
   skipping 0x00469ed8..0x00469f0d - a block that reads racer+0x338's
   three components and stores racer+0x1b4 as rear plus an offset.

       PersoAnim == 0   contacts stay as the travel frame sampled
                        them                       ROAD bikes
       PersoAnim != 0   front rebuilt off the rear along
                        matOrientation row2        DIRT bikes

   UNGATING IT BREAKS THE ROAD BIKE.  That block stood here without
   the test for a while: it fixed the dirt donut and put every
   road-bike turn 16 to 23 degrees out.  The gate is the whole
   difference.

   A SLIDE CANNOT SEPARATE "ALONG THE HEADING" FROM "ALONG velDir",
   because velDir is nearly parallel to the heading there (0.9993 on
   both). The road bike is what separates them, and 9.71 records the
   two wrong explanations that cost a session before it was read.

9.69.1.  The first reading, kept for its numbers

   matBikeRoot is the matrix the bike is DRAWN on (9.62), and it is
   built from the wheel-contact pair: fwd = front - rear, then
   racer_frame_from_forward.  So whatever direction that pair points
   in is the direction the machine faces on screen.

   MEASURED mid-slide on the dirt bike, both games in a steady donut:

       yaw(matBikeRoot) vs yaw(matOrientation)
           MOTO   1.92668  vs  1.92668     identical
           ours   1.52909  vs  2.24690     0.718 rad apart

       dot(front - rear, matOrientation row2)
           MOTO   1.00000        ours   0.75324
       |front - rear|
           both   139.200  =  96 * axeDistance (1.45)

   THE LENGTH WAS RIGHT AND THE DIRECTION WAS NOT.  The port sampled
   BOTH ends of the wheelbase in the TRAVEL frame, so the pair - and
   with it matBikeRoot - carried the direction of travel instead of
   the heading, 41 degrees out in a hard slide.  The camera was in
   exactly the right place; the bike was turned under it.

   THE ORIGINAL REBUILDS THE FRONT OFF THE REAR.  0x0046a4ec is the
   LAST write to racer+0x1b4 in maybe_Racer_UpdateLean, and the reads
   feeding it at 0x0046a4b8, 0x0046a4c4 and 0x0046a4e4 are the rear
   contact's x, y and z.  So only the ROOT of the wheelbase comes from
   the travel frame; the arm of it is laid along matOrientation.

   WHY THE TRAVEL FRAME STILL MATTERS: yawFrame for the lean comes
   from the travel LookAt directly, NOT from matBikeRoot - which is
   what keeps the slip angle non-zero even though matBikeRoot ends up
   parallel to matOrientation.  The two must not be conflated.

   VERIFIED with the gate in place, dirt donut, walls off on both:

       lean   MOTO 0.87573   ours 0.88331
       speed  MOTO 38.38     ours 38.92
       yaw(matBikeRoot) - yaw(matOrientation)   -0.00000 on both
       dot(front - rear, matOrientation row2)    1.00000 on both

   and the road bike keeps its slip - see 9.70's table.

   MATCH THE WALLS FLAG BEFORE READING ANY OF THIS.  s_race_noWalls is
   a port static and does NOT follow a track change, so a level switch
   can leave one game grinding a rail at flImpactBlend 190 while the
   other circles freely.  That pins the blend weight near 1, which
   collapses the lean to zero and looks exactly like a lean defect.

   and the wheelie is untouched by the reorder - wheelieAngle
   identical at every step (0.31102, 1.24407, 1.57080) with the nose
   lift within 0.1 unit (98.33/98.33, 115.97/115.97).

   IT IS NOT A DIRT-BIKE PATH.  There is no per-bike branch anywhere
   in the block - the only gates are racer_travel_frame succeeding and
   the two surface tests - so EVERY bike was drawn on the travel
   frame.  The size of the error is the SLIP ANGLE itself:
   dot(fwd, row2) is its cosine, so a bike going where it points had
   no error at all and a road bike in an ordinary turn had a small
   one.  That is why it only ever showed on the dirt bike.

   THE LEAN SATURATION WAS THE WRONG FORMULA - see 9.70.

   THINGS THAT LOOKED LIKE THE CAUSE AND WERE NOT, all measured:

     o the rider pose - every one of the seventeen bone angles matched
       to five decimals, both legs included;
     o the camera - within 2 units of MOTO's on every axis when
       expressed in the bike's own frame;
     o velDir against moveDir - maybe_Racer_UpdateLean reads +0x18c,
       moveDir, so racer_travel_frame is right.  The port carries a
       real warning about that pair elsewhere and it does not apply
       here;
     o world part matrices - useless for this.  Forty steps into a
       donut the two bikes sit at different headings, so part 0
       differs and every child inherits it.  Only the LOCAL angles
       are pose-meaningful.

9.70.  THE LEAN CURVES, AND PersoAnim PICKS BETWEEN THEM

   maybe_Racer_UpdateLean HAS FOUR LEAN FORMULAS (9.71.0).  PersoAnim picks
   between the first two below; the third, at 0x0046abdf, is the
   PersoAnim path for anyone who is not the human solo player and is
   written up in 9.71.1.

   +-------------+-----------+---------------------------------------+
   | Store       | Formula   | Hits in 44 frames of donut, straight, |
   |             |           | braking, wheelie and turning          |
   +=============+===========+=======================================+
   | 0x0046a8ab  | arctangent| EVERY FRAME                           |
   +-------------+-----------+---------------------------------------+
   | 0x0046ac4f  | piecewise | ZERO                                  |
   +-------------+-----------+---------------------------------------+

   ON THE DIRT BIKE the hits above are the KTM.  ON THE ROAD BIKE THE
   TWO SWAP - same hooks, same manoeuvre, the atan store takes zero
   and the piecewise one fires every frame.

   VERIFIED ON BOTH, same turn, after splitting the branch:

       step |        lean         |  bikeRootYaw - orientYaw
          9 | 0.60837 / 0.60920   |   0.28614 / 0.28647
         18 | 0.62262 / 0.62270   |   0.29175 / 0.29178
         44 | 0.90123 / 0.90142   |   0.40120 / 0.40127

   on the ROAD bike, with the pose at 0.0001 and the particle mix 7
   against 6.  The dirt bike's own verification is in the table above.

   THE PORT HAD ONLY THE PIECEWISE ONE, which is right for the road
   bikes and wrong for the dirt bikes.  racer_lean_response is that
   curve - (yawDelta/dt) * -30, a gain break at 0.3, clamp 1.04.

   THE GATE IS REAL AND WAS DOCUMENTED CORRECTLY ALL ALONG.  A note in
   racer.c said the arctangent was "gated on PersoAnim != 0 and every
   shipped bike reads 0, so the original never takes it".  The FIRST
   half is exactly right - EDI comes from g_bikeCarac field 0 at
   0x00469c75 and 0x0046a641 skips the block when it is zero.  Only
   the SECOND half is wrong: the KTM sets PersoAnim.  Rewriting the
   whole note as "backwards" threw away a correct fact with the
   incorrect one, and briefly collapsed two real branches into one.

   THE LIVE CHAIN, verified by hook on four consecutive donut frames
   and exact to five decimals every time:

       yawDelta = wrap(yawOrient - yawFrame)     0x0046a823, a plain
                                                 wrap into (-pi, pi]
       lean     = atan( (-yawDelta / dt)
                        * min(speed, 50.0)       0x42480000
                        * 1.445912045170292 )    0x004d1220, a DOUBLE
       clamp lean to +-1.04                      0x004d1228/0x004d1238

       yawDelta  cappedSpeed    lean      predicted
       -0.71370     39.6791   0.89246     0.89246
       -0.71378     39.7855   0.89382     0.89382
       -0.71418     39.8901   0.89537     0.89537
       -0.71456     39.9929   0.89688     0.89688

   IT IS SPEED-DEPENDENT and the piecewise curve is not, which is the
   single biggest behavioural difference: the same slip angle leans
   the bike further the faster it is going.

   AND THE DELTA IS A PLAIN WRAP.  racer_lean_delta's speed gate, its
   doubling and its pi/3 clamp ALL belong to the dead branch - the 2.0
   at 0x004d0f60 is read at 0x0046ab00, inside it.  Stacking them put
   the port at BOTH clamps at once, leanDelta pinned at pi/3 and lean
   at 1.04, which is why the value carried no information about how
   wrong the input was.

   VERIFIED end to end through a donut ramp:

       step |   MOTO lean  speed |   ours lean  speed |   delta
         20 |    0.35269  19.24 |    0.35599  19.24 | +0.00330
         35 |    0.69116  27.36 |    0.69654  27.48 | +0.00538
         50 |    0.78871  32.04 |    0.78638  31.98 | -0.00233
         74 |    0.89029  39.57 |    0.89892  40.17 | +0.00863

   0.5 degrees at worst, against 9.4 degrees pinned at the clamp
   before - and it now FOLLOWS the ramp instead of saturating.

   THE RESIDUAL IS THE SPEED, NOT THE FORMULA.  Dividing the speed
   back out - tan(lean)/speed, which the formula makes a constant -
   gives 0.031219 on MOTO against 0.031301 here at the steady state,
   a ratio of 1.0026.  The two donuts simply drift apart in speed.

   TWO CLAMPS IN SERIES HIDE THE CAUSE.  Reconstructing the input from
   a saturated output cannot work, and three attempts at it here
   produced three different wrong answers.  The hook was what settled
   it in one step: capture the operands, do not infer them.

9.71.  PersoAnim IS A THREE-WAY GATE, and it is plainly in the asm

   Everything PersoAnim controls in maybe_Racer_UpdateLean is visible
   in one function, from one register, in four instructions.  It is
   loaded ONCE at the top:

       00469c68  MOV EAX,[ECX+0x78]           the bike index
       00469c6f  LEA EDX,[EAX+EAX*8]          \  EAX * 73, the carac
       00469c72  LEA EAX,[EAX+EDX*8]          /  record in dwords
       00469c75  MOV EDI,[EAX*4 + 0x509398]   carac 72, PersoAnim

   and then tested three times, each time skipping a block:

   +------------+------------------+----------------------------------+
   | Test       | JZ target        | What is skipped when PersoAnim=0 |
   +============+==================+==================================+
   | 0x00469e17 | 0x00469f37       | the front-contact rebuild,       |
   |            |                  | 0x00469ed8..0x00469f0d - front = |
   |            |                  | rear + offset                    |
   +------------+------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 0x0046a641 | 0x0046a943       | the whole ARCTANGENT lean block  |
   +------------+------------------+----------------------------------+
   | 0x0046abb7 | 0x0046abee       | a third lean store at 0x0046abdf |
   |            |                  | - speed * K, and an early RET    |
   +------------+------------------+----------------------------------+

   MEASURED: carac field 0 reads 1 on the KTM and 0 on SPEED.

   READ THE BRANCH, DO NOT INFER IT FROM BEHAVIOUR.  Two wrong
   explanations were built and shipped before this was looked at -
   "MOTO lays the wheelbase along the heading" (true only of the dirt
   bikes) and "the backwards guard fires in a slide" (it fires on
   neither; dot(row2, moveDir) measured +0.755 on both).  Both took
   longer than reading four instructions would have.

9.71.0.  FOUR LEAN CURVES, NOT THREE - the piecewise is HUMAN ONLY

   The gate at 0x0046abee..0x0046ac15, which sits between the
   PersoAnim test and the piecewise store, is three more conditions:

       humanPlayer != 0
       soloMode    != 0
       g_dwReplayMode (0x004e9834) not 1 and not 2

   and anything failing them jumps to 0x0046acf3, which is three
   instructions and an epilogue:

       0046acf3  FLD  float [ESP+0x7c]      the delta
       0046acf7  FCHS
       0046acf9  FSTP float [ESI+0x2e1d]    lean = -delta
       0046acff  ...epilogue, RET

   NO dt, NO GAIN, NO CLAMP.  So the whole rider RESPONSE - the
   piecewise curve, its 0.3 gain break and its 1.04 clamp - belongs
   to a LIVE HUMAN SOLO RIDER and nobody else.  An AI, a replay and a
   demo all take the geometric lean straight, which is the right
   thing for something being played back rather than ridden.

   THE FULL PICTURE, then:

       PersoAnim && humanPlayer && soloMode    arctangent 0x0046a8ab
       PersoAnim, failing either                the divide 0x0046abdf
       !PersoAnim && humanPlayer && soloMode
                 && replayMode not 1 or 2       piecewise 0x0046ac4f
       !PersoAnim, failing any                  -delta    0x0046acf3

   THIS IS WHAT THE LAST 18 PER CENT OF THE AI LEAN WAS.  With every
   input seeded identical - speed, realSpeedMax, leanPrev,
   frameDelta, matOrientation, moveDir - MOTO's racer 2 produced
   -0.355337 and ours -0.293408.  The DELTA was within two per cent
   the whole time; the formula applied to it was the human's.

   AND THE HOOK THAT FOUND IT WAS THE ONE THAT DID NOT FIRE.  Armed
   on 0x0046ac41, the piecewise divide, it caught racer 1 twice and
   racer 2 never - on a bike whose PersoAnim is 0 and which therefore
   "had to" take that store.  A BRANCH AN AI PROVABLY DOES NOT REACH
   IS A GATE NOBODY HAS READ.

9.71.1.  The third gate - the AI lean on a PersoAnim bike

   THREE LEAN CURVES, not two, and the arctangent's gate is an AND of
   THREE conditions at 0x0046a62b..0x0046a643:

       humanPlayer  &&  soloMode  &&  PersoAnim   ->  arctangent
                                                      0x0046a8ab
       PersoAnim, failing either of the first two ->  the divide
                                                      0x0046abdf
       PersoAnim == 0                             ->  the piecewise
                                                      0x0046ac4f

   THE THIRD CURVE, read off 0x0046abbb..0x0046abdf with its constants
   out of memory:

       lean = yawDelta * -1.3 / (3.0 - speed * 0.045 * 0.5)

       0x004d1270  0.045       0x004d0f08  0.5
       0x004d1278  3.0         0x004d1280  -1.3

   A SPEED-SOFTENED DIVIDE WITH NO CLAMP.  The divisor falls from 3.0
   toward zero as speed rises, so the gain GROWS with speed - the
   opposite shape to the arctangent, which flattens.  It returns
   immediately after storing, so nothing later in UpdateLean touches
   the lean on that path.

   WHO TAKES IT, measured: hooking 0x0046abdf on the dirt track gave
   four hits with ESI at 0x0058762a, 0x0058a833, 0x0058da3c and
   0x00590c45 - racers 2, 3, 4 and 5 exactly, against a base of
   0x00581218 and a stride of 0x3209.  Racer 1 took the arctangent
   every frame.  THE ESI IN A TRACE IS WHO, and it is the cheapest way
   to tell a player-only branch from an AI one.

   TRANSCRIBED AS racer_lean_ai AND UNEXERCISED.  The port ticks only
   racer 1, which is human and solo, so this never runs today.  It is
   written so it is already right when the AI does, and it has had no
   live comparison.  [open - no AI to measure against]

9.72.  UPDATEPOSE IS NOW ONE FUNCTION

   maybe_Racer_UpdatePose 0x0045d810 is imported whole as
   racer_update_pose. The control flow - all seven passes, the
   pre-race jump over passes 2 to 5, the slot choice and the
   write-back - is one function matching the original. Six leaf
   formulas stay as named helpers, each carrying the address of the
   block it came from.

   WHAT THE MERGE TURNED UP, none of which the slices could show:

   +--------------------------+----------------------------------+
   | pass                     | what was wrong or missing        |
   +==========================+==================================+
   | 2 pitch                  | ungated - see 9.53.1             |
   | 3 glance                 | absent                           |
   | 5 stunt                  | absent                           |
   | 1 pre-race, podium       | absent                           |
   | 6 crash                  | only the first of two blends     |
   +--------------------------+----------------------------------+

   THE SECOND CRASH BLEND, 0x0045e277, had no slice at all. It takes
   how far the rider has slowed below three quarters of
   flRiderSpeedRef, through a half-sine, and applies staticDos
   NEGATED so the pose unwinds as he stops sliding.

9.72.1.  The countdown ramp is three poses, and it is not dirt-only

   Below 20 km/h the base stance normally blends poseStaticPied ->
   staticHaut. When poseStaticPied is pose 31 - which the pre-race
   pass sets - it instead runs a three-stage ramp through the
   pointer pair at 0x004ea4d8 and 0x004ea4dc [verified, live read]:

       k = 0     pose 31 -> pose 32
       k = 1     pose 32 -> pose 3, staticHaut

   where k is the integer part of speed * 0.18 * 2, clamped to 1.
   0x004ea4d4 reads NULL and is unreachable, because the index that
   would select it is clamped first.

   BOTH POSES ARE PRESENT ON A ROAD BIKE, so this path is live on
   tarmac. An earlier note had it as dirt-only and skipped the whole
   pre-race pass on that basis.

9.72.2.  What is still inert

   Transcribed, running, and taking the false edge because the port
   has no source for the state:

   o THE PODIUM RAMP. env->podiumMode and env->podiumTimer stand in
     for DAT_00504ea0 and DAT_005dabd0, and nothing writes them.

   o THE STUNT DURATION AND HAND FLAG. MR_PilotRots already carries
     the stunt list, but only poseIndex and frameCount are parsed
     from the manifest; durationMs and handSkeleton are the two
     integers FUN_00425080 reads either side of the frame count and
     are still unparsed, so racer_pose_stunt_step returns 0. [open]

   o nowMs. The original reads g_framePrevMs; the port passes the
     racer clock. Every timer it feeds is a delta against a stamp on
     the same racer, so the substitution is safe, but it is not the
     same number.

9.72.3.  poseStaticPied comes from the constructor, and zero is wrong

   Racer_Ctor 0x0045baf4 and FUN_0045bb30 0x0045bc4f both store
   0x0051d678 - staticPied, index 4 - into racer+0x304d before the
   racer is ever ticked. Only two other sites write it, both inside
   UpdatePose: 0x0045d97d puts it back to staticPied above 20 km/h,
   and 0x0045d8bb sets it to pose 31 on the pre-race pass.

   IT IS THE FRAME THE LOW-SPEED STANCE BLENDS FROM, and below 20
   km/h the weight is speed * 0.18 - zero at a standing start, which
   makes the blend a straight COPY of it. A port that leaves the
   field at 0 therefore poses the whole rider from static.rot
   instead of staticPied.rot at every standing start, with the bike
   parts still correct because they come from the skeleton bind.

   The measured signature is distinctive: parts 0..3, 15 and 16
   exact, and every rider part wrong by a whole pose rather than by
   a small amount.

10.  Open questions

   o ANSWERED - WHAT DRAWS THE BIKE'S SHADOW.
     maybe_Racer_DrawShadow (0x00461f70) queues two quads over six
     ground-projected vertices the racer owns at +0x2ea9, refilled
     every tick by maybe_Racer_BuildShadow.  Not baked into the
     track - ile01's palm shadows are painted into the texture pages
     and every ground face near palmier_006 is ordinary kind 16
     [verified] - and not one of the five meshes at 0x0053a6a8.
     Both are reconstructed in racerdraw.c.

     THE PORT SORTS IT BY ITS NEAREST CORNER AND THE ORIGINAL BY ITS
     FARTHEST - Screen3D_CullAndQueueFacesEx's fourth argument, 0
     here against 1 at 0x004620a3.  A deliberate divergence.

     The shadow is one quad about 250 units long and a road face is
     shorter, so it STRADDLES SEVERAL.  Keyed on its farthest corner,
     the moment that corner crosses a seam the key is taken in the
     segment AHEAD and exceeds the key of the face the TAIL is lying
     on - so that face draws after the shadow in the back-to-front
     walk and paints the tail out, snapping as the key crosses
     rather than fading.  Sorting by the nearest corner puts the
     shadow in front of every face it overlaps.

     THE SIDE CAMERA HID IT, and that is the confirming evidence:
     the inversion needs the shadow's LONG AXIS along the view, so
     that its far corner reaches past a seam while its tail sits on
     a nearer face.  From the side that axis is perpendicular to the
     view, every corner is at nearly the same camZ, and there is no
     inversion to have.

     [open] WHY RETAIL DOES NOT SHOW IT.  It passes 1 and should
     invert the same way.  Its z-buffer being off by default is the
     likeliest reason and is not established.

   o The record is 12809 bytes and eight fields are known. This is the
     largest struct left in the project and the one that would improve
     the most decompilation - nearly every gameplay function indexes it.

   o RESOLVED. Racer_Create is __thiscall on the record, and agrees with
     maybe_Game_CreateRacers on the offset of the active flag, which
     confirms the base. See section 4.

   o RESOLVED. Racer_Create has been read and contributed 20 verified
     field offsets, plus the array base. See section 4.

   o The table at 0x00508054, stride 0x24, decides which racers exist
     when not networked. It looks like the track or bike roster and has
     not been identified.

   o DAT_0053b8c4 is a pointer to the default racer name. Where it
     points, and whether the name is localised, is unknown.
   o maybe_ModelInstance_Init's three stack arguments are unidentified.
     Resolving them would name most of the 0x184 record and give the
     racer three more mapped regions.

   o RESOLVED. DAT_0053a6a8 is g_meshSkl, the rider's body skeleton
     mesh, and it is element 0 of a five-mesh run. See section 6.1.
